# Implementation plan

Step-by-step plan derived from `docs/` (mainly `docs/milestones.md`). Work top to bottom. Every `- [ ]` line is one step; everything else is context. See CLAUDE.md for how to mark steps and what to do after each one.

Legend: `[ ]` todo · `[x]` done · `[?]` implemented, awaiting manual verification by a person · `[-]` dropped (say why)

Steps marked *(manual)* need a person (real browsers, phones, VRChat, real OIDC providers). Do everything an agent can (test pages, scripts, instructions written under the step), then mark the step `[?]`.

Each milestone ends with a **gate** step. The gate always stops the run so that Siggi can review the milestone.

---

## Before M0 — prerequisites

- [x] Locate the author's HTTP stack: `io.siggi:SiggiHTTP` (Maven artifact on the author's repo; repository settings in `~/.m2/settings.xml`, e.g. `https://repo.siggi.io`). Java 8 target, JPMS module `io.siggi.http`, depends on `io.siggi:ZipLib:1.0`.
- [x] Settle the open questions in `docs/README.md#open-questions` (all resolved 2026-11-08; see the notes log and the README).
- [x] Set up dependency resolution in `pom.xml`: `io.siggi:SiggiHTTP:1.11-SNAPSHOT` and `io.siggi:SimpleWebsocket:1.1-SNAPSHOT` resolve only from the local `~/.m2` (their sessions `mvn install` new builds there); declare the author's repository too, for released versions (the `<id>` must match the `<server>`/mirror entry in `~/.m2/settings.xml` if credentials are needed; never commit credentials).
- [x] Check the dev machine has `ffmpeg` (with libx264), `ffprobe` and `yt-dlp`, and note the versions here. Check Docker is available for Testcontainers (PostgreSQL). (Node isn't needed: `frontend-maven-plugin` downloads it.)
  - Checked 2026-11-08 (macOS arm64): **ffmpeg 6.1.1** and **ffprobe 6.1.1** (Homebrew, `/opt/homebrew/bin`, built with `--enable-libx264 --enable-gnutls`; a 720p30 `libx264 -preset veryfast -bf 0 -g 60 -flags +cgop` test encode works and ffprobe reports `has_b_frames=0`). **yt-dlp 2026.03.17** (`/usr/local/bin`; metadata/format selection on a YouTube video works, but it's 8 months old, so run `yt-dlp -U` if YouTube extraction breaks). **Docker 29.9.2** (Docker Desktop 4.98.0, linux/arm64), socket at `/var/run/docker.sock` -> `~/.docker/run/docker.sock`, so Testcontainers needs no extra config; `postgres:16-alpine` (PostgreSQL 16.15) pulls and runs. Also present: Java 21.0.3, Maven 3.9.3.

### Notes on SiggiHTTP and SimpleWebsocket (from reading their sources, 2026-11-08)

We build against **SiggiHTTP 1.11-SNAPSHOT** and **SimpleWebsocket 1.1-SNAPSHOT**. Both have their own Claude sessions that fix bugs and reinstall the snapshots into `~/.m2`. The notes below were made against SiggiHTTP 1.10 and are confirmed by the M0 steps. If something is missing or buggy, follow CLAUDE.md ("Upstream libraries"): report it, don't change those projects.

- **Don't use `HTTPResponse.handleRequestWithFile`** (nor `VirtualFileSystemResponder`, which calls it): in 1.10 an `OPTIONS` request falls through to the `PUT` branch and can truncate or overwrite the file. It's fixed in 1.11, but we don't need it: use `returnFile(file, contentType)`.
- Range: `returnFile(...)` parses `Range` and serves partial content. Check open-ended, suffix (`bytes=-N`), `416`, `HEAD` and `Content-Range`.
- Streaming responses: chunked by default (`setContentLength(-1)`). The `.ts` output needs a **close-delimited** body (no `Content-Length`, no `Transfer-Encoding`), like VRCDN. Check how quickly a disconnect surfaces as a write failure.
- WebSocket (SimpleWebsocket 1.1-SNAPSHOT): SiggiHTTP is a `provided` dependency of it, so we declare SiggiHTTP ourselves. Relevant behaviour for a blocking `read()` loop:
  - `read()` answers pings itself.
  - Keepalive ping after 5 s idle; the 15 s read timeout only fires if the peer stops responding.
  - permessage-deflate negotiated by default; `maxMessageSize` (default 1 MiB, closes with 1009) applies after decompression.
  - `send()` throws once closed; writes time out after 15 s.
  - `close()` waits up to 5 s for the peer's close reply.
  - `getCloseInfo()` explains why `read()` returned null.
  - `WebSocketAcceptor` holds shared settings (`setMaxMessageSize`, `setAllowedOrigins`, …).
- Large uploads: `HTTPRequest.getInputStream()` gives the raw body.
- Virtual threads: `HTTPServerBuilder.setExecutor(Executor)`.
- Per-route hooks: none in SiggiHTTP; build a hook chain in our `http/` adapter layer.
- Client IP: leave SiggiHTTP's handling as is. It trusts `X-Forwarded-For` from loopback, Cloudflare ranges and `server.isIPTrusted(...)`; feed `http.trustedProxies` into the latter. A future SiggiHTTP will require the app to list trusted ranges explicitly.
- SiggiHTTP's own `Sessions` are not used (sessions live in our DB per `docs/auth.md`).

## M0 — Skeleton

- [x] Maven project (`pom.xml`): `io.siggi:TogetherTunes`, base package `io.siggi.togethertunes`, `maven.compiler.release` 21, single runnable jar (shade or assembly plugin, with `Main-Class`), Surefire for JUnit 5 (Failsafe for Testcontainers tests if they should run separately); package layout from `docs/architecture.md#code-layout-suggested`.
- [x] `.gitignore` for build output, `data/`, IDE files, `node/`, `node_modules/`.
- [x] Frontend build: TypeScript sources in `web/src`, compiled by `tsc` (strict, ES modules, no bundler) during `mvn package` via `frontend-maven-plugin` with pinned Node and TypeScript versions; compiled output plus static assets packaged so the server can serve them.
- [x] Config loading: JSON config file (Gson) + environment-variable overrides, `output.profile` defaulting to `720p30`, every key from `docs/architecture.md#configuration`; `http.publicBaseUrl` required; fail fast on bad values.
- [x] Integrate SiggiHTTP: router, request/response adapters, per-route hook chain (auth, CSRF, rate-limit placeholders). Request handlers on virtual threads.

HTTP stack checks, each with tests:

- [x] Range requests through `returnFile` (`bytes=a-b`, open-ended, suffix) → `206` + `Content-Range`; `416` for unsatisfiable; `HEAD`.
- [x] Endless streaming responses: close-delimited (for `.ts`) and chunked; fast client-disconnect detection.
  - Unblocked 2026-11-08: SiggiHTTP 1.11-SNAPSHOT `4be3e04` (or later) adds `HTTPResponse.closeDelimited()` and no longer logs a client leaving mid-body (`0ac2a37`). Use it: `setContentType(...)`, `closeDelimited()` before the headers are sent, `setBuffered(false)`, then `write` + `flush` per chunk. It drops Content-Length and Transfer-Encoding, sends `Connection: close`, closes the connection when the response ends (also when `close()` is called from another thread after `openResponse()`), and works for HEAD. Don't use the old `Content-Length`/`deleteHeader`/`keepAlive(false)` workaround. Earlier findings: chunked endless streams work (`contentLength(-1)`, `setBuffered(false)`, `flush()` per write); a disconnect surfaces as a write `IOException` within about 30 ms on loopback. Still to do: a streaming helper in `http/Response` plus tests (chunked, close-delimited with raw-socket header checks, HEAD, disconnect detection time, no SiggiHTTP error logged when the client leaves). Aborting a blocked response from another thread is still an open question in SiggiHTTP (only needed in M4).
- [x] Streaming multi-GB request bodies to disk.
- [x] WebSocket via SimpleWebsocket 1.1-SNAPSHOT: one `WebSocketAcceptor` with `setMaxMessageSize(65536)` and an origin check matching `publicBaseUrl` (`setAllowedOrigins`; this is the WebSocket CSRF check from `docs/auth.md`). One virtual thread per socket with blocking `read()`; log `getCloseInfo()` when a socket ends.
- [x] Client IP: use SiggiHTTP's resolution, with `http.trustedProxies` (CIDRs, IPv4 and IPv6) fed into `isIPTrusted`; unit-test the CIDR matching.
- [x] Static file serving for `web/` (content types, caching headers, no directory traversal), using `returnFile`, never `handleRequestWithFile` or `VirtualFileSystemResponder`. Test that `OPTIONS`, `PUT` and `DELETE` leave files untouched.

Database:

- [x] `Dialect` interface (upsert, `RETURNING`, case-insensitive search) with SQLite and PostgreSQL implementations.
- [x] Connection handling: SQLite WAL / `foreign_keys=ON` / `busy_timeout=5000`, single write connection (or small pool with retry); a small hand-written PostgreSQL pool (no pool library).
- [x] UUIDv7 generator; epoch-ms time helper.
- [x] Migration runner: `db/migrations/{sqlite,postgres}/NNNN_name.sql`, one transaction per file, `schema_migrations` table.
- [x] Initial schema for **both** dialects: every table in `docs/data-model.md`.
- [x] Settings storage (`settings` table, typed accessors, defaults from `docs/auth.md#server-settings`).
- [x] Secret key handling: `<data>/secret.key` (0600, generated on first start) or env var; AES-GCM helper for `*_enc` columns.
- [x] Test that runs all migrations against SQLite and against PostgreSQL (Testcontainers) and checks the two schemas are equivalent.

Rest of the skeleton:

- [x] Data dir layout created on startup (`db/`, `media/`, `cards/`, `rooms/`, `tmp/`).
- [x] Test page in `web/` and a test WebSocket echo endpoint.
- [x] Fill in the **Commands** section of CLAUDE.md (build, test, run, run against PostgreSQL).
- [x] *(manual)* Browser test of the WebSocket echo in Chrome, Firefox and Safari: connect, send messages, leave idle for over a minute, close tabs, reload. Write the test steps under this item. Report anything odd (unexpected disconnects, close codes, exceptions, with `getCloseInfo()` output) to the SimpleWebsocket session.
  - **Agent pre-check (2026-11-08):** `mvn verify` passes. Raw-socket client against the jar (`/ws/echo`, correct `Origin`): echo works, the server sends a ping (`SimpleWebsocket keepalive`) every 5 s, the socket survives 75 s idle (no SiggiHTTP 60 s read timeout after the upgrade), echo works after the idle period, and the server logs `ws-2 on /ws/echo closed after 75 s: WebSocketCloseInfo{remote=1000 "bye", local=1000 "", clean=true, initiatedByPeer=true, error=null}`. Nothing odd so far, so nothing reported to SimpleWebsocket. Real browsers still need a person.
  - **Setup:** `mvn package -DskipTests`; `cp config.example.json config.json`, set `http.publicBaseUrl` to `http://localhost:8080`; `java -jar target/TogetherTunes-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar 2>&1 | tee ws-test.log`. Open `http://localhost:8080/test/ws-echo.html` (use exactly the `publicBaseUrl` host: `127.0.0.1` instead of `localhost` gives a different Origin, so the upgrade is refused with 403). Open devtools (console + network, WS filter). Run every step below in **Chrome, Firefox and Safari** (Safari: enable Develop menu). After each step, compare the page log with the server's `ws-<n> on /ws/echo closed after … s: WebSocketCloseInfo{…}` line.
  - **1. Connect:** click Connect. Expect status `open`, an `open` log line, the negotiated extensions shown (note whether `permessage-deflate` is offered/accepted per browser), no console errors.
  - **2. Messages:** send `hello`, a non-ASCII text (e.g. `héllo 🎵`), Text 16 KiB, Binary 1 KiB, Binary 64 KiB, Burst of 100. Expect every echo to match (mismatch 0, pending back to 0).
  - **3. Limit:** Binary 64 KiB + 1. Expect close code **1009** on the page and `local=1009` in the server line; then Connect again works.
  - **4. Idle:** connect, send one message, leave the tab **in the foreground** untouched for 2 min, then send again. Expect no close, echo works. Repeat with the tab **in the background** (another tab focused) for 2 min, and with the window minimised. Browsers answer the server's 5 s pings themselves (not visible in the page; Chrome shows them nowhere, Firefox devtools shows control frames). Note any close that happens and its code.
  - **5. Periodic:** tick "Send a message every 10 s", leave it for 2 min (also in a background tab; background timer throttling may slow it down but must not close the socket).
  - **6. Close button:** click Close (1000). Expect page `1000`, `wasClean=true`; server `remote=1000`, `clean=true`, `initiatedByPeer=true`.
  - **7. Close tab:** connect, then close the tab. Expect a server line with `remote=1001` (going away) and `clean=true`, or at worst an unclean close with `error` set; note which. Same for closing the whole window and quitting the browser.
  - **8. Reload:** tick "Connect on page load", connect, reload (normal and hard reload) several times. Expect each old socket to log 1001 on the server and the new one to open; no socket left open (count opens vs. closed lines). Also try navigating away and coming back with Back (Safari/Firefox bfcache: the page must reconnect or show closed, not a stale `open`).
  - **9. Server restart:** with a socket open, stop the server (Ctrl+C). Expect the page to show close **1001**. Start it again; Connect works.
  - **10. Network drop (optional):** with a socket open, switch Wi-Fi off for ~30 s, then on. Note what the page and the server log (unclean close expected; check it's detected and logged, not left hanging).
  - **Report:** anything unexpected (closes during idle, close codes other than the ones above, `error=` set where a clean close was expected, exceptions/stack traces in `ws-test.log`, echo mismatches) goes to the `SimpleWebsocket` session via `SendMessage`, with browser + version, the step, the page log and the server's `getCloseInfo()` line. Record results per browser here, then flip this to `[x]`.
  - **Result (2026-11-08, Siggi + agent):** ran the unattended page `/test/ws-auto.html` (main `f0d3253`) in Chrome 156, Firefox 159 and Safari 27 on macOS: **10/10 checks pass in all three** (closed tab, connect, messages, 64 KiB + 1 → 1009, close 1000, removed frame, 8 parallel sockets, 150 s idle, a message every 10 s for 120 s, reload). Every tab close, frame removal and reload reached the server as a clean `remote=1001`. All three negotiate `permessage-deflate`. The first run found a bug in our settings (not SimpleWebsocket's): Chrome and Firefox compress even incompressible messages, so a 64 KiB binary message arrived as a 65557-byte frame and the 65536-byte frame limit closed it with 1009 (Safari sends such frames uncompressed); fixed by `WebSockets.MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD` = 64 KiB + 1 KiB. Not tested (optional, can't be automated from a page): server restart, network drop, Back/Forward cache.

## M1 — Identity and accounts

- [x] Actor resolution: every request → user (`sid` cookie / bearer), guest (`gid` cookie or IP key), or anonymous.
- [x] Setup flow (`/api/setup`): setup page until the first account exists; first account gets `admin`.
- [x] Local accounts: register (respecting `registration.mode`), login, logout; Argon2id via Bouncy Castle (m=64 MiB, t=3, p=1, encoded string, rehash on login when params change); min length 10.
- [x] Sessions: 256-bit opaque tokens, SHA-256 at rest, `sid` cookie flags, 30-day sliding expiry; bearer tokens (`/api/auth/token`); `/api/me/sessions` list/revoke (no IP).
- [x] CSRF: `Origin` must match `publicBaseUrl` for state-changing cookie-authenticated requests (incl. guest cookie) and for WebSocket upgrades.
- [x] Browser baseline iOS 15 / Safari 15.0 (see CLAUDE.md, Frontend): lower `web/tsconfig.json` so tsc catches what Safari 15.0 lacks (e.g. `target` ES2021 and `lib` ES2021 + DOM, or whatever combination proves to work; class fields, private `#` members and top-level await are fine in 15.0, so keep them if the target allows), fix any existing TypeScript that no longer compiles, and add a test (e.g. in `WebBuildTest`) that fails if the compiled JS uses class static blocks or calls `.at(`, `Object.hasOwn`, `structuredClone`, `findLast`. Document the baseline in `docs/architecture.md`.
- [x] Rate limiting: login per IP and per username with exponential backoff; generic per-IP limiter reusable for `/live/` later. Failures to `audit_log`.
- [x] Roles and permissions: built-in `admin` (all, immutable) and `user` (default); custom roles; permission union; full permission list from `docs/auth.md#roles-and-permissions`; feature switches checked before permissions.
- [x] Admin API + pages: users (incl. admin-set temporary password, disable), roles and role permissions, server settings.

Guests:

- [x] Lazy guest creation on first identity-requiring action; `POST /api/me/guest`, `PATCH /api/me/guest`, `/api/me` for guests/anonymous.
- [x] Guest cookie mode (`gid`, 1-year sliding) and IP mode (IPv4 full, IPv6 /64; SHA-256 key; `ip_last`).
- [x] Generated guest names: bundled ~300-word adjective and noun lists, uniqueness among active guests, numeric suffix after 5 tries.
- [x] Custom guest names (when allowed): length 2–32, allowed chars, trim/collapse, NFKC + case-insensitive clash check against usernames/display names, optional admin blocklist.
- [x] Guest badge in actor serialisation (`isGuest`).
- [x] WebSocket `identityTicket` (single-use, 60 s) issuance (consumption lands in M3).

IP policy and tests:

- [x] `IpAccess` service: the only reader of IP columns, checks `ip.view`; `/api/admin/guests`, `/api/admin/audit`, `/api/admin/ip/sessions/{userId}` with IP only for `ip.view`.
- [x] DTO IP-leak test: serialises every DTO type and fails on anything that looks like an IP address.
- [x] Permission-matrix test harness (anonymous / guest / user / admin per endpoint), extended in each later milestone.
- [x] Minimal web UI: setup, login/register, account page (sessions), admin users/roles/settings.
  - The admin pages already exist (`/admin/`, from the admin API step); link them for users holding an `admin.*` permission. Login must handle `user.mustChangePassword` (an admin-set temporary password): send the user to a change-password form (`POST /api/me/password`) before anything else.
  - Done 2026-11-08 at main `445e33b`: new pages `/login.html`, `/register.html`, `/password.html`, `/account/` (profile display name via `PATCH /api/me`; sessions list with revoke, "log out everywhere else", load more; no IP). Shared `web/src/session.ts`: `/api/me` types, site nav (Account, Administration for any `admin.*` permission, Log out; or Log in / Register), `requireUser()` gate used by the front page, account and admin pages: anonymous → `/login.html?next=…`, `mustChangePassword` → `/password.html?next=…` before anything else (login also goes there directly). `?next=` accepts only same-site paths (`/…`, not `//…`). Registration mode isn't exposed to anonymous clients, so the Register link is always shown and a closed/invite-only server answers with its error message (no API change). `WebBuildTest` checks the pages are packaged and wired through `session.ts`. `mvn verify` passes (474 unit + 145 integration). Also driven end to end in headless Chrome over CDP on a fresh data dir: setup → admin, nav, sessions, display name, admin creates a user with a temporary password, logout, login redirects (incl. `#hash`), forced password change on every page, non-admin refused on `/admin/`, revoke others / current session, registration closed (error) and open (works).

New HTTP-level `AdminApiTest.rolePermissionsCombineAsAUnion`: one user given `helpdesk` (`admin.users`, `rooms.create`), then `helpdesk` + `ip` (`ip.view`, `rooms.create`) + an empty role + `user`; `/api/me` lists the union and both `/api/admin/guests` and `/api/admin/ip/sessions/…` answer 200 on the same session (`/api/admin/settings` still 403); emptying `ip`'s permissions drops `ip.view` at once while `rooms.create` stays via `helpdesk`; only the empty role → no permissions, 403. No production code changes.
- [x] A guest gets an identity on first need, in cookie mode and in IP mode.

Coverage: `MeApiTest.lazyCreationOnFirstIdentityRequiringAction` sends a test route through `Guests.requireIdentity`. An anonymous request becomes a guest, and later reads in the same request see that guest. The `gid` cookie returns the same guest with no new row or cookie, and a user stays a user. In IP mode no cookie is set and repeat requests from one address get the same guest. With guests disabled the route answers 403. `cookieModeGuestIsCreatedOnceAndThenRecognised` and `ipModeGuestIsKeyedByTheClientAddress` test the same through `POST /api/me/guest`, with `/api/me` anonymous until then. Further tests cover the cookie flags, the 1-year sliding cookie, IPv6 /64 keys, `ip_last` and concurrent IP-mode creation making one guest. One full `mvn verify` run hung for over 20 minutes in `PostgresAccountsIT.setUp`, which is not a guest test: the JDBC connection to the Testcontainers Postgres stalled during authentication (seen in a jstack). Run on their own, the guest ITs passed.
- [x] Custom-name rules are enforced.

Rules live in `GuestNameRules.check` (NFKC, trim/collapse, 2–32 code points, allowed characters with at most 3 combining marks, at least one letter or digit → 400 `invalid_name`; case/width/spacing-insensitive clash with any username or display name → 409 `name_taken`; compact-substring blocklist → 400 `name_blocked`), applied by `POST /api/me/guest` (with a name), `PATCH /api/me/guest` and `PATCH /api/me`, and only when `guests.allowCustomNames` is on (else 403 `custom_names_disabled`). Covered by `GuestNameRulesTest` (form, key, blocklist), `MeApiTest` over HTTP (`customNames`, `customNamesCantMatchAccounts`, `customNameBlocklist`, banned/anonymous can't rename), and a new `GuestsContract.customNameRulesAreEnforced` on SQLite and PostgreSQL, which adds the one missing case: a **disabled** account's username and display name are also taken. No production code changes.
- [x] IP-leak test passes across all DTOs.

## M2 — Ingest, libraries, retention

Media plumbing:

- [x] Tool wrappers: ffprobe / ffmpeg / yt-dlp subprocess runner with timeouts, stderr capture to job log, `-progress pipe:1` parsing.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `b8cd1ab`, in `ingest/`: `Tools` (from `Config`; `ffmpeg(...)` prepends `-hide_banner -nostdin`, `ffprobe(...)` `-hide_banner`; default limits ffprobe 5 min, ffmpeg 4 h, yt-dlp 2 h, idle 5 min for all) returns a `ToolCall` (fluent, runs once): overall `timeout` and `idleTimeout` (no stdout/stderr bytes for that long), interrupt of the calling thread also stops the tool; stopping sends SIGTERM to the process **and its descendants**, SIGKILL after 3 s. stdin closed; stderr split into lines (`\n`/`\r`) into a `JobLog` (bounded 64 Ki-char tail with a dropped-lines marker, for `ingest_jobs.log`) between a `$ command` line and an `[tool] exit N after x s` line; last 20 stderr lines go into `ToolResult`/`ToolException` (kinds `NOT_AVAILABLE`, `START_FAILED`, `EXIT_CODE`, `TIMEOUT`, `IDLE_TIMEOUT`, `OUTPUT_TOO_LARGE`, `OUTPUT_FAILED`). stdout: discard (default), `stdoutToString(max)`, `stdoutToFile`, `stdoutLines`, or `ffmpegProgress(listener)`, which adds `-progress pipe:1 -nostats` and parses blocks into `FfmpegProgress` (`out_time_us` → `out_time_ms` → `out_time`; `N/A`/negative = -1; `fraction(durationUs)`). `secret(...)` redacts a string (e.g. an RTMP key) from the logged command, stderr and messages (invariant 6). A missing default tool stays a bare name in `Config`, so running it fails with `START_FAILED`. Tests: `ToolCallTest` (shell-script fake tools: capture, exit codes, both timeouts, process-tree kill, interrupt, redaction, line splitting, progress), `FfmpegProgressParserTest`, `JobLogTest`, `ToolsTest` (real ffmpeg encode with progress and ffprobe JSON; skipped if not on PATH). `mvn verify` passes (508 unit + 146 integration).
- [x] **The profile constant**: one `List<String>` of x264 args (preset veryfast, no B-frames, closed 60-frame GOPs, forced IDR every 2 s), per output profile (720p30 / 1080p30). Used by every encode.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `f07ff2d`: `media/EncodeProfile` holds `VIDEO_ARGS_720P30` / `VIDEO_ARGS_1080P30` (immutable `List.of`, encoder options only: `-c:v libx264 -preset veryfast -profile:v high -level:v 4.0|4.1 -pix_fmt yuv420p -bf 0 -g 60 -keyint_min 60 -sc_threshold 0 -force_key_frames expr:gte(t,n_forced*2) -x264-params open-gop=0 -crf 20 -maxrate 3M|6M -bufsize 6M|12M`), `videoArgs(OutputProfile)` returning the same instance, `GOP_FRAMES`/`GOP_MS`, and `scaleFilter(profile)` for mezzanines (`scale=…:force_original_aspect_ratio=decrease:force_divisible_by=2,pad=…:(ow-iw)/2:(oh-ih)/2,setsar=1,fps=30`; `force_divisible_by=2` is an addition to the doc's command so odd scaled sizes, e.g. from portrait sources, stay 4:2:0-friendly). Size and rate are not in the arg list: inputs reach the profile size/30 fps via the filter, the card image size + `-framerate 30`, or the mezzanine itself (overlay). `EncodeProfileTest` (7) checks the arguments, and with real ffmpeg per profile encodes a 24 fps landscape, a 60 fps portrait source and a still card: all High/yuv420p/SAR 1:1/30 fps/`has_b_frames=0`, IDR exactly on every 60th frame and only there, constant frame spacing, byte-identical extradata across the three, and different extradata between 720p30 and 1080p30. `mvn verify` passes (516 unit + 146 integration).
- [x] Reference card per profile + its avcC as the invariant reference.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `dd5190a`: `ingest/ReferenceCard.ensure(tools, cardsDir, profile, log)` renders a black Java2D PNG at the profile size, encodes it with the card command (`-loop 1 -framerate 30 -i png -t 2 -vf <scaleFilter> <profile args> -an`), reads the avcC from ffprobe's `extradata` hex dump (sanity-checked: version 1, High, ≥1 SPS) and stores `<data>/cards/<profile>/reference.mp4` + `reference.avcC` (atomic writes). On later starts the **stored** avcC stays the reference (the library was encoded against it), but a fresh card is still encoded and compared: `encoderChanged()` (e.g. after an ffmpeg/x264 upgrade) makes `Main` log a warning that new encodes will fail the check; a missing `reference.mp4` is restored when the encoder still matches; a malformed stored avcC is an error. `matches(bytes)` / `verify(bytes, what)` (throws `AvcCMismatchException` with both hex strings) are what ingest and the overlay encoder will call. `Main` creates/checks the reference for `output.profile` at startup, sets `java.awt.headless`, and keeps starting (with a warning) if ffmpeg/ffprobe fail; the ingest queue (TODO) will take it. **Deviation:** cards also go through `EncodeProfile.scaleFilter` — a Java2D PNG has no `pHYs` chunk, so its SAR is unknown and x264 leaves the aspect ratio out of the VUI, giving a 1-byte-shorter SPS than the mezzanines; `setsar=1` (in the filter) fixes it. The docs' card command is updated. The next step's MP4 box walker can replace the ffprobe hex-dump read. `ReferenceCardTest` (8): hex-dump parsing, avcC checks, per profile create/reuse/restore with a 60-frame High card whose avcC matches a mezzanine-style encode of another source, level byte 40/41, 720p30 ≠ 1080p30, stored reference wins on encoder change, missing tools. `mvn verify` passes (524 unit + 146 integration); jar smoke test creates `cards/720p30/` on first start and is quiet on the second.
- [x] MP4 box walker to extract avcC (`moov/trak/mdia/minf/stbl/stsd/avc1/avcC`).
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `5d06a88`: `media/Mp4Boxes` reads box headers from a `SeekableByteChannel` and seeks past payloads (moov before or after a multi-GB mdat costs a few small reads). `readAvcC(Path)` returns the avcC payload of the first `trak` whose `stsd` has an `avc1` entry (skips the 8-byte stsd header and the 78-byte VisualSampleEntry fields; other child boxes like `pasp`/`colr` before avcC are fine); `find`/`children` and the `Box` record (start, size, header size, payload range) are public for later use. Handles 64-bit `largesize`, size 0 (top level only, to EOF) and `uuid` headers; throws `Mp4FormatException` (an `IOException`, message names the file) on a missing moov/avc1/avcC, truncated headers, sizes below the header or past the parent, and avcC > 64 KiB. `ReferenceCard` now reads avcC with the walker (`readAvcC(mp4)` + `checkAvcC`) instead of ffprobe's hex dump, which is gone. `Mp4BoxesTest` (8): synthetic files (moov first/last, largesize mdat, uuid, audio trak before video, size-0 last box, malformed cases) and real ffmpeg encodes with audio mapped first, with and without `+faststart`, byte-identical to ffprobe's extradata; an audio-only m4a has no avcC. `Mp4Boxes.Box` is classified INTERNAL in `DtoIpLeakTest`. `mvn verify` passes (529 unit + 146 integration).
- [x] Java2D card renderer: item card (artwork, title, artist, album; shrink then ellipsize), server filler cards (Paused, Queue is empty, Loading…).
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `4a5da04`: `ingest/CardRenderer(profile)` returns `TYPE_INT_RGB` images at the profile size; layout in 720p design units scaled with the height (1080p = 1.5×). **Item card** (`renderItem(ItemCard(title, artist, album, artwork))`): rounded 440-unit artwork tile on the left (artwork cover-scaled = centre-cropped, so square art on a black-barred 16:9 YouTube thumbnail comes out as just the art; halving steps for big reductions; transparency flattened onto the tile colour; no artwork → a shape-drawn note placeholder), background = the artwork blurred (32 px wide, upscaled) and darkened 72 %, or a dark gradient without artwork; to the right, vertically centred: title bold 56→32, artist 40→26, album 32→22 (light grey). Blank title → "Untitled", blank artist/album lines are dropped; control characters/line breaks become spaces. **Fit:** each line keeps the largest whole size ≥ min that fits, otherwise is ellipsized at the min size at grapheme boundaries (`BreakIterator`, so emoji/flags aren't split), trailing space removed before `…`. **Fillers** (`Filler.PAUSED/QUEUE_EMPTY/LOADING`, ids `paused`/`idle`/`loading`, labels "Paused", "Queue is empty", "Loading…"): centred shape-drawn icon (pause bars, list, still spinner) above the bold label (72→36); `renderMessage(filler, subtitle)` adds an optional second line, meant for the room idle card with the room name (M3/M5). Also `writePng`, `readImage` (ImageIO; returns null for formats it can't read, e.g. WebP thumbnails, which ingest must convert with ffmpeg first). Text uses the logical `SansSerif` font for its fallback chain: **the Docker image (M7) must install fonts incl. CJK** (e.g. fontconfig + Noto). Surefire/Failsafe now run with `java.awt.headless=true`. `CardRendererTest` (18): fit (keeps size, shrinks to the largest fitting size, ellipsizes at min, no split surrogates/flags, ellipsis-only), cleaning, sizes per profile, artwork/backdrop pixels, placeholder, text within the margins incl. CJK, distinct centred fillers, determinism, cover crop, PNG round trip; `-Dcards.out=<dir>` writes sample PNGs (looked at for 720p30 and 1080p30). `CardRenderer$ItemCard`/`$Fitted` classified INTERNAL in `DtoIpLeakTest`. `mvn verify` passes (548 unit + 146 integration).
- [x] Card encoder (`-loop 1 … -t 2`) and server filler cards under `cards/`.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `afdb11b`: `ingest/CardEncoder` holds the card command (`encodePng`, moved out of `ReferenceCard`, which now delegates to it) and `encode(tools, image, out, reference, log)`: writes the PNG and the encode to dot-temp files next to `out`, **verifies the avcC against the reference card** (`AvcCMismatchException` otherwise) and only then renames it over `out` (atomic where possible); on any failure `out` is untouched and the temp files are gone. Other image sizes are letterboxed by the scale filter. `ingest/FillerCards.ensure(tools, cardsDir, reference, log)` keeps `cards/<profile>/paused.mp4`, `idle.mp4`, `loading.mp4` (ids from `CardRenderer.Filler`), each with a `<id>.key` fingerprint (SHA-256 over profile, scale filter, encoder args, reference avcC and the rendered pixels); a card is re-encoded only when the file or key is missing, the key differs (renderer/fonts/args changed) or its avcC no longer matches. With a changed encoder (`encoderChanged()`), current cards are kept but a needed re-encode fails the check (the published card stays). `Main` creates them after the reference card (warning and continuing on failure, like the reference card; the packager takes them in M4). `ReferenceCard.ensure` now first deletes dot-temp files left in the profile directory by an encode killed during startup (seen in the smoke test). Cards are 7–10 KB at 720p30 (simple graphics), well under the doc's 40–100 KB estimate. Tests: `CardEncoderTest` (6: command, item card per profile with ffprobe structure check — High/yuv420p/SAR 1:1/30 fps, 60 frames, I then 59 P, constant spacing from 0 — letterboxing, mismatch not published, missing tools), `FillerCardsTest` (5: per profile encode once + reuse + temp sweep, missing/stale/damaged re-encode, encoder change, fingerprint). `mvn verify` passes (558 unit + 146 integration; one run hit an unrelated timing flake in `ToolCallTest.killsTheWholeProcessTree`, 500 ms timeout under load, which passed on rerun). Jar smoke test: first start encodes the reference and 3 filler cards, second start is quiet.
- [x] AAC silence frame from `anullsrc`, after encoder priming, generated at startup.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `9a7fce9`: `ingest/SilenceFrame.generate(tools, log)` runs `ffmpeg -f lavfi -i anullsrc=r=48000:cl=stereo -frames:a 16 <AUDIO_ARGS> -f adts pipe:1` (in memory, no file). It parses the ADTS stream (every header must be AAC-LC, 48 kHz, stereo, one raw block; CRC headers handled), drops the priming packet (ffmpeg's first packet, with the "Lavc" fill element), the first real frame (MDCT overlap with the priming) and the last (flush), and requires the 13 frames in between to be byte-identical; that frame (raw, without ADTS header; 6 bytes with ffmpeg 6.x: `211004608c1c`) is kept. New `EncodeProfile.AUDIO_ARGS` (`-c:a aac -b:a 192k -ar 48000 -ac 2`) plus `AUDIO_SAMPLE_RATE`/`AUDIO_CHANNELS`/`AAC_FRAME_SAMPLES`: the audio half of the profile, for ingest to use too. `Main` generates it after the filler cards (warning and continuing on failure, like the cards; the packager takes it in M4). `SilenceFrameTest` (6): command uses `AUDIO_ARGS`, ADTS parsing with/without CRC, rejection of other configs/broken streams, priming/edge dropping and steady-state check, real ffmpeg: deterministic, and 50 repeats wrapped in the packager's fixed ADTS header decode to exactly 50×1024 all-zero stereo samples; missing ffmpeg. `mvn verify` passes (564 unit + 146 integration; the first run hit the known `ToolCallTest` timing flake, `idleTimeoutFiresOnlyWhenTheToolGoesQuiet`, passed on rerun). Jar smoke test on a fresh data dir starts with no warning.
- [x] Index builder: ffprobe packet CSV → `index.bin` (format in `docs/library-and-ingest.md#sample-index-indexbin`), dropping `D` / negative-PTS packets; index reader with unit tests.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `f7e974b`: `media/SampleIndex` is the format (writer `toBytes`/`write` via dot-temp + rename; reader `read`/`parse`) and an immutable in-memory index: per track primitive arrays of offset/size/pts/duration/flags, `track(kind)`, `file(track)`, `sampleAtOrBefore(pts)` (binary search), `keyframeAtOrBefore/AtOrAfter`, `endPts`, plus avcC. **Format details the doc left open (doc updated):** profile u16 codes `1`=720p30, `2`=1080p30 (`SampleIndex.profileCode`, never renumber); file-ref table = `file count u8`, then per file `name length u16 | UTF-8 name`, a plain file name in the item directory (no separators, not `.`/`..`); version 1. The reader rejects bad magic/version/profile/kind, unsafe names or bad UTF-8, out-of-range file refs, timescale 0, sample counts that can't fit, size/duration > 2^31-1, PTS not strictly increasing per track (so binary search is valid), truncation and trailing bytes. `ingest/IndexBuilder.build(tools, profile, video, audio, out, log)` (video = `mezz.mp4` or, for audio items, `card.mp4`; files must sit next to `out`) runs one ffprobe per file: `-select_streams v:0|a:0 -show_entries stream=time_base:packet=pts,dts,duration,size,pos,flags -of csv` (**deviation:** the doc's packet command plus the stream time base in the same call; `-of csv` keeps section names to tell the lines apart), streaming stdout line by line. Drops packets with `D` or negative PTS, takes the timescale from a `1/N` time base (anything else is rejected), ignores the extra empty field ffprobe adds for packets with side data and unknown sections, derives a missing duration from the next PTS, fails on no stream/no packets. avcC comes from `Mp4Boxes.readAvcC`. It doesn't check avcC vs the reference or GOP spacing (that's the verify step). Tests: `SampleIndexTest` (10: round trip, byte layout, profile codes, lookups, builder limits, malformed files) and `IndexBuilderTest` (9: CSV parsing incl. the AAC priming line; real ffmpeg: a 5 s mezzanine-style video item gives 150 frames with IDRs exactly every 60, every video sample at its offset is length-prefixed NALs filling it exactly, audio starts at pts 0 in 1024 steps and every sample's bytes equal ffmpeg's own ADTS stream copy after the priming frame; an audio item with its card; a file with no audio stream fails without writing). `mvn verify` passes (583 unit + 146 integration).

Ingest pipeline:

- [x] DB-backed ingest queue (`ingest_jobs`), worker pool (`ingest.workers`), survives restart, progress + log tail.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `d608f54` (no schema change: `ingest_jobs` was in the initial schema). `ingest/IngestJob` (row record; `Kind` upload/youtube/reencode/refetch, `State` queued/running/done/failed, lowercase wire names). `ingest/IngestJobs` (repository, one short transaction per transition): `enqueue(itemId, kind)` joins the caller's transaction and **returns the item's unfinished job if there is one (any kind)**, so an item has at most one queued/running job and a second request attaches to it; `claimNext()` (oldest `created_at, id` first; conditional `UPDATE … WHERE state='queued'`, so concurrent workers/processes never get the same job; counts the attempt, resets progress); `saveProgress` (clamped 0..1, running jobs only); `complete`/`fail`/`release`; `get`, `latestForItem`, `list(state, limit)`. **Item status follows the job** for every kind except `reencode`: `pending` on enqueue (error cleared), `processing` on claim, `ready` on done, `failed` + `items.error` (cut to 2000 chars) on failure; a `reencode` job leaves the item `ready` with its old files, also when it fails. **Restarts:** `recoverInterrupted()` at startup requeues jobs a crash left `running` (log line added), or fails them once they have been started `MAX_ATTEMPTS` = 3 times (a job that kills the server can't loop forever); a graceful shutdown `release`s running jobs back to `queued` without counting the attempt. `ingest/IngestQueue(jobs, workers, handler)`: `ingest.workers` virtual threads; `start()` recovers then starts them; `enqueue` wakes a worker at once, and workers also poll the table every 10 s; the `Handler` gets a `Context` (`job()`, `log()` = a `JobLog` resumed from the stored tail via new `JobLog.resume`, so a retried job's log continues; `progress(fraction)` stores progress + log tail at most once a second; `flush()`). Handler returns → done; throws → failed with the exception message (`IngestFailure`, `ToolException`, `IOException`, `AvcCMismatchException` are logged without stack trace, anything else with one; the worker carries on). Start/done/failed/stopped lines go into the job log. `close()` interrupts the workers (ToolCall stops the tools) and waits up to 10 s; a job stopped that way is released. `Main` starts the queue after the cards/silence frame and closes it in the shutdown hook before the database; **its handler is a placeholder that fails every job** ("The ingest pipeline isn't implemented yet") until the next step plugs in the job steps. `IngestJob`/`IngestJobs$Recovered` classified INTERNAL in `DtoIpLeakTest` (the job endpoint, a later step, builds its own view). Tests: `IngestJobsContract` (11, `SqliteIngestJobsTest` + `PostgresIngestJobsIT`: enqueue/attach, claim order, done/failed/reencode item status, error cutting, progress clamping, release, crash recovery incl. give-up after 3, enqueue rolled back with the outer transaction, 6 threads claiming 30 jobs each exactly once), `IngestQueueTest` (9: outcomes and log, unexpected exception, at most `workers` at once, progress visible mid-job, throttling, polling without wake, shutdown requeue + next start finishes it, crash recovery on start, start once), `JobLogTest.resumesAStoredTail`. `mvn verify` passes (604 unit + 157 integration). Jar smoke test: clean start/stop; a job inserted as `running` is requeued on start and then failed by the placeholder handler with the expected log.
- [x] Job steps: probe → (download) → loudnorm pass 1 → encode → extract `audio.m4a` → card render+encode → index → verify → publish (atomic directory swap).
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `6d4aa44`: `ingest/IngestPipeline` is the queue's handler (replaces the placeholder in `Main`). **Source contract for later steps:** an upload job reads `tmp/ingest/<itemId>/source[.<ext>]` (+ optional `cover.<ext>`, preferred over the embedded picture); `youtube`/`refetch` download `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=<youtube_id>` with the doc's yt-dlp command (+ `--newline --no-mtime`, `--ffmpeg-location` when ffmpeg is an absolute path, `--` before the URL; `[download] N%` lines become progress) into `tmp/jobs/<jobId>/`. Metadata (`-J`), live/duration rejection before download and cookies stay with the YouTube step. Steps: `MediaProbe` (ffprobe JSON; attached pics/still-image codecs are covers; no audio / over `ingest.maxDurationSeconds` / unreadable → `IngestFailure`), `Loudness.measure` (pass 1; JSON collected from stderr; `-inf` → encode without normalisation, `loudness_lufs` null), encode (video item → `mezz.mp4` with the shared video+audio args, then `audio.m4a` stream-copied; audio item → `audio.m4a` only), cover → `cover.jpg` (≤1200 px; failures only logged), `CardRenderer` + `CardEncoder` (avcC checked), `IndexBuilder`, `IngestVerifier`, publish (build in `media/.ingest-<jobId>`, swap by two renames via `media/.old-<itemId>-<jobId>`; `IngestPipeline.recoverDirectories` at startup deletes leftovers / restores an `.old` dir whose item dir is missing), then `IngestItems.published` writes kind, `duration_ms` (of `audio.m4a`), `output_profile`, `loudness_lufs` and clears `purged_at`; the staging dir is deleted (kept on failure for a retry). **Deviations/decisions (doc updated):** pass-2 filter ends with `aresample=48000:first_pts=0` so a late audio start is padded and `audio.m4a` starts at 0; pass 1 appends `aresample=48000` because loudnorm's 192 kHz output makes ffmpeg's progress `out_time` ~30× too small; the duration check compares the end of the encoded audio with the source audio's end relative to the file start (Matroska has no stream duration, so ffprobe reads the packets of the last 30 s — loudnorm's decoded length was 60 ms off). **Overlap with later M2 steps:** audio-only vs video item layout and the full verify step are already implemented here (needed for a working pipeline); those steps remain for their own tests/edge cases. Originals are **not** kept yet (every staged source is deleted after publish; the Originals step changes that for audio uploads). `reencode` jobs fail with "isn't implemented yet" (item stays `ready`). Without a reference card (ffmpeg missing at startup) every job fails with a clear message. Tests: `IngestStepsTest` (17: probe/loudnorm parsing, verify on synthetic indexes, commands, staging lookup, swap, crash cleanup, id checks), `IngestPipelineTest` (8, real ffmpeg + SQLite + queue: FLAC with embedded cover → audio item at −14±1 LUFS; 24 fps video with audio 0.3 s late re-ingested over an old dir; YouTube job with a fake yt-dlp; silence; no audio / too long / unreadable / missing source; avcC mismatch publishes nothing and keeps the old files; no reference / reencode), `IngestJobsContract.ingestItemsReadAndPublish` (SQLite + PostgreSQL). New records classified INTERNAL in `DtoIpLeakTest`. `mvn verify` passes (630 unit + 158 integration). Jar smoke test: a staged MP3 + queued job inserted with sqlite3 became a `ready` audio item (6000 ms, −22.25 LUFS) with `audio.m4a`, `card.mp4`, `index.bin`; staging dir gone.
- [x] Originals (`docs/library-and-ingest.md#originals-audio-uploads`): audio uploads keep `original.<ext>` with `original_codec`, `original_web` (codec whitelist + duration within 50 ms) and `original_gain_db = min(−14 − I, −1 − TP)` from loudnorm pass 1; originals of video uploads and YouTube downloads are deleted after ingest.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `48e59d0`: `ingest/Originals` + `IngestPipeline`: for an `upload` job that makes an audio item, the staged source is hard-linked (copy if linking fails) into the work dir as `original.<ext>` after verify, so it is published with the atomic directory swap; the staging dir (and with it the upload) is deleted after publish as before, also for video uploads; YouTube sources live in the job's scratch dir and are deleted with it. `IngestItems.published(…, original)` writes `original_file`, `original_codec`, `original_web`, `original_gain_db` and **clears them (null/0) when there is no original** (video upload, YouTube, re-ingest of an audio item as video). **Decisions (doc updated):** `original_codec` = `<codec_name>/<format_name>` from ffprobe (e.g. `flac/flac`, `aac/mov,mp4,m4a,3gp,3g2,mj2`). The extension comes from the probed container, not the upload name (`mp3`, `flac`, `wav`, `aac` (ADTS), `opus`/`ogg`, `m4a` for MP4/MOV; Matroska keeps `.webm`/`.mka`/`.mkv` from the upload name, else `mka`; anything else keeps a plain `[a-z0-9]{1,10}` upload extension, else `bin`), so the media endpoint can pick the content type from it. Whitelist: `mp3` in MP3; `aac` in MP4/M4A or ADTS; `flac` in FLAC; `opus` in Ogg, or in Matroska only as a `.webm` upload; WAV with `pcm_u8`/`s16le`/`s24le`/`s32le`/`f32le`. The duration compared with `audio.m4a` is the container duration (what a player shows), falling back to the audio end; `original_gain_db` is rounded to 0.01 dB and null when pass 1 measured silence. Tests: `OriginalsTest` (6: extension, whitelist, 50 ms window, gain formula), `IngestPipelineTest` (FLAC with cover keeps a byte-identical `original.flac` with the formula's gain from an independent pass-1 measurement; silence WAV keeps `original.wav` with null gain; MP3 (also with no extension), Opus in Ogg/WebM, AAC in M4A/ADTS are web-playable, Vorbis, AIFF, ALAC and Opus in `.mkv` kept but not; video upload and YouTube keep none; video re-ingest drops an earlier original), `IngestJobsContract` on SQLite and PostgreSQL. `mvn verify` passes (638 unit + 158 integration).
    - **Found for the verify step:** a WMA (`wmav2`) upload fails verify: ffprobe reports 3.000 s but the decoded/encoded audio is 3.072 s (the decoder outputs the last block's padding). The doc says such originals are kept, so the verify step should handle it (e.g. take the source's audio end from the decoded length for such codecs).
- [x] Audio-only items: `audio.m4a` + `card.mp4`, no `mezz.mp4`. Video items: `mezz.mp4`, `audio.m4a`, `card.mp4`.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `98ded94`. The layouts themselves came with the job-steps step; this step added the edge cases and fixed two bugs they found. **Kind detection:** `MediaProbe.isStillImage` — an image-codec video stream (`mjpeg`, `png`, …) without `attached_pic` is a cover only if it has at most one frame (`nb_frames`, else Matroska's `NUMBER_OF_FRAMES[-lang]` tag; if unknown, `duration`/`DURATION` tag ≤ 0.5 s, nothing known → still). Before, a Motion JPEG camera AVI became an audio item. **Profile fix (invariant 1):** x264 copies the source's colour tags and chroma location into the SPS VUI, so a BT.709-tagged source (every real YouTube download), full-range, BT.601 or MJPEG (centre chroma) failed the avcC check. `EncodeProfile` video args now add `-color_range tv -colorspace bt709 -color_primaries bt709 -color_trc bt709 -chroma_sample_location left`, and `scaleFilter` converts with `:out_color_matrix=bt709:out_range=tv` (doc updated; HDR is not tone-mapped). **This changes the reference avcC:** an existing data dir logs the "no longer reproduces" warning at startup and its new encodes fail until `cards/<profile>/` (and media encoded before) is deleted/re-ingested. Tests: `IngestStepsTest.imageCodecsAreVideoOnlyWithMovingPictures`, `EncodeProfileTest.colourTagsOfTheSourceDontChangeTheExtradata` (BT.709, interlaced BT.601 MPEG-2, full-range, MJPEG, 10-bit HDR → identical avcC and BT.709 tags), `IngestPipelineTest.itemLayoutFollowsTheKind` (one item: MP3 with JPEG cover → audio layout; re-ingest from MJPEG AVI → `mezz.mp4`/`audio.m4a`/`card.mp4`/`index.bin`, streams video+audio / audio / 60-frame card, avcCs match; back to M4A with cover → no `mezz.mp4` left; index files per kind). `mvn verify` passes (641 unit + 158 integration).
- [x] Verify step: audio duration within 50 ms of source, avcC byte-identical to reference, keyframes exactly every 2 s.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `500891f`. `IngestVerifier` (from the job-steps step) already checked all three; this step fixed the WMA case found by the originals step and added tests. **Audio length:** the pipeline keeps the largest `out_time_us` of loudness pass 1's progress (the decoded audio's end; `aresample=48000` after loudnorm keeps it accurate, e.g. 3.064 s for a 3.000 s WMA whose encode is 3.072 s). `checkAudioDuration(index, sourceUs, decodedUs)` passes if the encode is within 50 ms of the container's audio end (preferred) **or** of the decoded end, and fails if both are unknown; the failure message names both; `verify` returns the matched value and the job log says `decoded …` when the decoded one was used. Doc updated (`library-and-ingest.md`, Verify). Tests: `IngestStepsTest.verifyAcceptsTheDecodedLength` (WMA-like, unknown container length, truncated encode still rejected) and updated duration tests; `IngestPipelineTest.originalsOfAudioFormats` now includes a 44.1 kHz WMA upload (ingests, `original.wma` kept, not web-playable); new `publishedFilesHoldTheVerifiedProperties` checks the published files independently of `index.bin` with ffprobe for a 60 fps portrait and a 29.97 fps video: `audio.m4a` within 50 ms of the source, mezzanine avcC = reference, keyframe packets exactly at 0, 2, 4, … s and one per 60 frames. `mvn verify` passes (643 unit + 158 integration).
- [x] Re-encode jobs when `output.profile` changes: audio uploads from their original, YouTube items by refetching, video uploads from their old mezzanine.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `868a06f`. `IngestJobs.enqueueReencodes(profile)` queues a `reencode` job for every `ready` item with another `output_profile` and no unfinished job (purged/pending/processing/failed skipped); `Main.startIngest` runs it before the queue starts (when a reference card exists) and prints "Queued N re-encode jobs…". **Decision:** `claimNext` orders `reencode` jobs after every other kind (`ORDER BY CASE WHEN kind = 'reencode' …, created_at, id`), so a library re-encode doesn't block new uploads. `IngestPipeline` handles `reencode` (`SourceKind` STAGED/DOWNLOAD/ORIGINAL/PREVIOUS): YouTube items download again and **fall back to their old files if the download fails** (deviation, so an item whose video is gone doesn't stay on the old profile); items with their `original_file` present re-encode from it (loudness pass 1 again; original linked into the new dir; `original_*` recomputed, same values); everything else from the old `mezz.mp4`, else `audio.m4a`, **without loudnorm** (already normalised) and keeping the stored `loudness_lufs`. `cover.jpg` of the item is the staged cover for the card. `ingest.maxDurationSeconds` isn't applied to re-encodes. A job for an item that is no longer `ready` or already on the current profile ends `done` with a log line and changes nothing. The staging dir isn't touched by re-encodes. `IngestItems.Item` gained `outputProfile`, `originalFile`, `loudnessLufs`. **For M4:** while a re-encode is pending the item stays `ready` with old-profile files; the packager must check `index.bin`'s profile code and treat a mismatch as not ready (doc updated). A failed re-encode is retried at the next start. Tests: `IngestJobsContract.enqueueReencodesQueuesReadyItemsOfAnotherProfile` (SQLite + PostgreSQL: which items, idempotent, claim order), `IngestPipelineTest.reencodeUploadsForANewProfile` (720p30 → 1080p30: FLAC from its byte-identical original with same gain; video from its old mezzanine at 1920×1080 without loudnorm, loudness kept, still ~−14 LUFS, IDRs every 2 s; both stay `ready` during the jobs; then nothing left to queue and a stale job is a no-op), `reencodeYoutubeItemsByRefetching` (fake yt-dlp download; broken yt-dlp → fallback to old mezzanine back at 720p; purged item skipped), `reencodeAudioWithoutOriginalFromAudioM4a` (also: duration limit not applied). `mvn verify` passes (647 unit + 159 integration). Jar smoke test: an MP3 ingested at 720p30, restart with `TOGETHERTUNES_OUTPUT_PROFILE=1080p30` → "Queued 1 re-encode jobs", item `ready`/`1080p30`, original kept, 1920×1080 card; a further restart queues nothing.
- [x] Chunked resumable uploads (`/api/uploads…`): chunks to `tmp/`, streaming SHA-256, size limit, `complete` with dedupe (incl. re-ingest into a purged row), ffprobe rejects (no audio, unparseable, too long), metadata from tags/filename, embedded cover.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `d8c654e`. **Schema:** migration `0003_uploads` (both dialects; doc'd in `data-model.md`): `uploads(id, user_id|guest_id, room_id NULL, filename, size, created_at, updated_at, item_id NULL, deduplicated)`. `library/Uploads` (service) + `library/UploadsApi` (routes, all behind `library.upload`; guests/anonymous 401 until the guest-upload step, a `roomId` gives `404 room_not_found` until rooms exist). `POST /api/uploads` → `201 {uploadId, chunkSize: 8 MiB, size}` (`400 invalid_filename`/`invalid_size`, `413` over `ingest.maxUploadBytes`); bytes go to `tmp/uploads/<id>.part`. `PUT` with `Content-Range: bytes s-e/size` (`*` total ok): appended in order, already-received bytes skipped (repeat/overlap idempotent), start past the received count → `409 upload_offset_mismatch`, a short body keeps what came (`400 chunk_length_mismatch`), one request per upload at a time (`409 upload_busy`). Received = part-file size (survives restarts); the SHA-256 is updated per write and recomputed from the file when the in-memory state doesn't match (restart, write error). `GET` → `{receivedBytes, size, itemId?}`. **complete** (`{title?, artist?, addToLibrary?}`, body optional) → `{itemId, deduplicated}`, repeatable (the row keeps the result): hash lookup among `source='upload'` items under a lock (two concurrent completes of one file make one item); ready/pending/processing → reuse; **purged or failed → re-ingest into the same row** (metadata kept, `deduplicated: true`; decision: failed counts like purged so a re-upload retries); else ffprobe: `422 unreadable_media` / `no_audio` / `too_long` (shared `IngestPipeline.checkSource`), the refused upload is deleted; no ffprobe → `503 ingest_unavailable`. New item: kind from the probe, title = uploader's → tag `title` → file name stem (dirs and last extension stripped, `_` → space) → "Untitled"; artist = uploader's → `artist`/`album_artist`/…; album from tags (`UploadMetadata`, cleaned: control chars/whitespace collapsed, ≤300 code points). `MediaProbe` gained `tags` (lower-case keys; container first, then first audio stream's, for Ogg/Opus). The file is moved to `tmp/ingest/<itemId>/source.<ext>` (plain upload extension, else bare `source`; stale staging leftovers removed first), and item + `upload` job + library entry (`via='upload'`, users only, unless `addToLibrary:false`) + upload result go in one transaction (file moved back if it fails); `last_referenced_at` = now (also bumped on dedupe/re-ingest); the queue is woken. Embedded cover: handled by the pipeline (tested end to end). **Cleanup:** `Uploads.sweep()` at startup and at most every 10 min on create deletes uploads idle for 24 h (+ part files) and part files without a row. `Main.router` takes the `UploadsApi` (test helper `TestUploads`). Tests: `UploadMetadataTest` (7: tags/file name/overrides, cleaning, extensions, probe tags, Content-Range parsing), `UploadsContract` (9, `SqliteUploadsTest` + `PostgresUploadsIT`, over HTTP with real ffmpeg: resumable/idempotent chunks continued on a restarted service with the right hash, every error case, tagged MP3 → item row/job/staging/library + dedupe by another user without library entry, metadata fallbacks incl. Windows path and video kind, the three rejects, purged and failed re-ingest into the same row, 4 concurrent completes → 1 item, an uploaded FLAC with embedded cover ingested to `ready` with `cover.jpg` and byte-identical `original.flac`, sweep), `PermissionMatrixTest` rows for the 4 routes, `DtoIpLeakTest` (routes seeded incl. a dedupe complete; new records classified INTERNAL), schema contract lists `uploads`. Docs updated (`api.md`, `library-and-ingest.md#upload`, `data-model.md`). `mvn verify` passes (663 unit + 168 integration). Jar smoke test: an MP3 uploaded with curl in two chunks → `ready` audio item "Smoke Song"/"Smoke Artist", 5000 ms, `original.mp3` web-playable; `tmp/uploads` and `tmp/ingest` empty.
- [x] YouTube: URL → 11-char ID normalisation (all forms in the doc), cache lookup (ready / pending+processing attach / purged → refetch same row / failed retry after 10 min / miss → create), `yt-dlp -J` metadata (reject live, too long), download from canonical URL, title/artist/thumbnail rules, optional cookies file, `presentAsAudio` at import.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `aa60e3f`. **Schema:** migration `0004_present_as_audio` (both dialects): `items.present_as_audio INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (doc'd in `data-model.md`). `library/YoutubeIds` (normaliser): `watch?v=` (any parameter order, `t=`/`list=`/`si=`/… ignored, also `watch/?v=`, percent-encoded query), `youtu.be/<id>`, `/shorts/`, `/embed/`, `/live/`, `/v/`, `youtube-nocookie.com/embed/`; hosts `youtube.com`, `www.`, `m.`, `music.` (case-insensitive, trailing dot); with/without `http(s)://` or `//`; anything else (other hosts incl. look-alikes and `user@host` tricks, playlists, channels, 10/12-char IDs, a **bare ID**, non-http schemes, > 2048 chars) is rejected. `library/YoutubeMetadata` parses `-J`: title = `track` else `title`; artist = `artist`, else yt-dlp's `artists` list joined with ", ", else `uploader` (else `channel`) **with a trailing " - Topic" removed** (small addition to the doc's rule); album = `album`; cleaned with `UploadMetadata.clean` (≤300 code points), blank title → the video ID; live = `is_live` or `live_status` ∈ {`is_live`, `is_upcoming`, `post_live`}. `library/YoutubeImports` + `YoutubeImportsApi` (`POST /api/imports/youtube {url, presentAsAudio?, addToLibrary?}` → `{itemId, cached, status}`, behind `library.youtube`, so `ytdlp.enabled` off → `403 feature_disabled`; guests/anonymous 401 until the next step). Lookup by `youtube_id`: ready → `cached: true`, no yt-dlp; pending/processing → same item (the job is shared via `IngestJobs.enqueue`'s attach); purged → `refetch` job on the same row (title etc. kept); failed → new `youtube` job if the last job ended ≥ 10 min ago, else `422 youtube_failed` + `Retry-After`; miss → `yt-dlp -J --no-playlist --no-warnings -- <canonical URL>` **synchronously in the request** (2 min timeout; took ~19 s in the smoke test with the old yt-dlp), `422 youtube_live` / `too_long` / `youtube_unavailable` (yt-dlp's last `ERROR:` line without prefix/ID), `502 youtube_bad_metadata`, `503 ytdlp_unavailable`, `504 youtube_timeout`; then item (`pending`, `video`, duration from `-J`, `metadata_json` `{"youtube":{id, uploader, durationSec}}`) + `youtube` job + library entry (`via='youtube'`, users, unless `addToLibrary:false`) in one transaction, queue woken. Every accepted import bumps `last_referenced_at` and adds the library entry. Imports of one ID are serialised (64 lock stripes), so concurrent requests run `-J` once. **Decisions (doc updated):** `-J` uses the canonical URL too, so the pasted URL never reaches yt-dlp; `presentAsAudio` is applied only when the import creates the item or revives a purged/failed one, and ignored for existing items (that's a metadata edit); `cached` = the item was ready. **Pipeline:** the download adds `--write-thumbnail -o "thumbnail:<scratch>/cover.%(ext)s"` (the best thumbnail, WebP from YouTube) which is the staged cover → `cover.jpg` + card art (a re-encode keeps preferring the item's `cover.jpg`). **Cookies:** `IngestPipeline.cookiesFile(setting, warn)` → `--cookies <file>` on `-J` and the download when `ytdlp.cookiesFile` names a readable file, read on every call (Main passes a `Supplier` over `Settings`, now created before the ingest queue); a missing/unreadable path is logged (job log / server log) and ignored. Tests: `YoutubeIdsTest` (4: 33 accepted forms, 30 rejected), `YoutubeMetadataTest` (9), `YoutubeImportsContract` (9, `SqliteYoutubeImportsTest` + `PostgresYoutubeImportsIT`, fake yt-dlp that records calls: new item + job + library; other link forms while pending/processing/ready reuse the item with one yt-dlp call total; presentAsAudio/addToLibrary/guest adder; purged → refetch same row; failed refused until exactly 10 min after the failure then retried without `-J`; invalid links never reach yt-dlp; live/upcoming/too long/unavailable/bad JSON/missing tool; cookies passed or skipped; 4 concurrent imports → 1 item, 1 call), `IngestStepsTest` (download/metadata args, cookies file checks), `IngestPipelineTest.youtubeJobDownloadsThenIngests` (fake yt-dlp writes a WebP thumbnail → `cover.jpg` is it; `--cookies` passed; a missing cookies file is reported and skipped on a refetch), `PermissionMatrixTest` row, `DtoIpLeakTest` (cached import seeded; new records INTERNAL), schema contract column list. `mvn verify` passes (688 unit + 177 integration). Jar smoke test with the real yt-dlp 2026.03.17: `watch?v=jNQXAC9IVRw&t=5s` → pending, `youtu.be/` form while processing → same item, then `ready` video "Me at the zoo" / "jawed", 19012 ms, `mezz.mp4`/`audio.m4a`/`card.mp4`/`index.bin`/`cover.jpg` from the WebP thumbnail; `/shorts/` form → `cached: true` in 11 ms with still one job; `example.com` → 400; nothing left in `tmp/`. yt-dlp warned it is >90 days old and that no JS runtime (deno) is installed ("some formats may be missing"); the download still worked, but consider `yt-dlp -U` and installing deno on the server.
- [x] Permissions for adding media (`library.upload`, `library.youtube` + `ytdlp.enabled`); guest paths stubbed until rooms exist; guest upload quota (per guest and per IP).
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `aa9aeef`. **Schema:** migration `0005_guest_upload_starts` (both dialects; doc'd in `data-model.md`): `guest_upload_starts(id, guest_id FK, ip_key_hash NULL, created_at)`, one row per upload a guest starts. `library/AddMediaPolicy` replaces the plain permission hooks on `/api/uploads…` and `/api/imports/youtube`. Order: YouTube → `ytdlp.enabled` first (`403 feature_disabled`, everyone); users → `library.upload` / `library.youtube` (`403 forbidden`); guests/anonymous without a room → `401 not_authenticated`; with a room → `403 guests_disabled`, `403 guest_banned`, `403 guest_uploads_disabled` / `guest_youtube_disabled`, then the room half `rooms/RoomGuestRules` (capabilities UPLOAD/YOUTUBE). **Stub:** `Main` wires `RoomGuestRules.NO_ROOMS` (every room `404 room_not_found`), so guests can't add media until M3 replaces it with the room settings (and room guest bans); a user's `roomId` still gets `404 room_not_found` directly (TODO M3). Guests' YouTube links stay off the REST import (`401`); M3's `queue.add` should call `AddMediaPolicy.requireYoutube(actor, roomId)` and then `YoutubeImports.importUrl`. `POST /api/uploads` for a guest: policy → filename/size validation (`Uploads.validate`) → quota pre-check (IP only for an anonymous visitor) → **only then** `Guests.requireIdentity` (refused visitors don't become guests) → `Uploads.create(…, roomId, clientIp)`, which stores `room_id` and checks + records the quota in the same transaction under the quota lock. Chunks/status/complete for guests need the server guest rules again (`requireUploadOwner`: switching guest uploads off stops uploads in progress; the matrix's guest column for those routes is now `403 guest_uploads_disabled` instead of `401`). **Quota** (`library/GuestUploadQuota`, decisions, doc updated in `library-and-ingest.md#who-can-add-media` and `api.md`): counts upload **starts** in a rolling hour per guest and per IP key hash (IPv4 / IPv6 /64, SHA-256 like `guests.key_hash`, no IP stored; IPv4-mapped = IPv4), whichever is hit first → `429 upload_quota_exceeded` with `Retry-After` until the oldest counted start leaves the window; starts count even if the upload is later refused/abandoned/deduplicated, refused requests don't; size over `maxItemBytes` → `413`; `perHour: 0` → `403 guest_uploads_disabled`; `Uploads.sweep` deletes starts older than an hour. Tests: `GuestUploadsContract` (9, `SqliteGuestUploadsTest` + `PostgresGuestUploadsIT`, over HTTP with a test room rule: room required, `NO_ROOMS` stub → 404, anonymous becomes a cookie guest only after all checks, ownership, room asked after the server rules, switching off mid-upload, bans, IP mode, users need the permission and have no quota, per-guest across IPs, per-IP across guests incl. the `Retry-After` value and the rolling window, IPv6 /64 and IPv4-mapped, size limits and bad requests don't count, abandoned starts count and are swept, 8 concurrent starts can't overshoot, YouTube policy order incl. `feature_disabled` for guests), `PermissionMatrixTest` rows updated, schema contract lists the table. `mvn verify` passes (696 unit + 186 integration). Jar smoke test: anonymous upload without room → 401, with room → `guest_uploads_disabled`, after the admin enables guest uploads → `404 room_not_found` with no guest and no quota row created; YouTube anonymous → 401; admin upload → 201.

Adding bumps `last_referenced_at`. `DELETE` → `204` / `404 not_in_library`, sets the item's `last_referenced_at` to now (never backwards) — the later `last_referenced_at` step should keep/cover this. No index on `library_entries(user_id, added_at)` (per-user libraries are small; the PK prefix finds the user's rows). `Main.router` takes the `LibraryApi` (test helper `TestUploads.libraryApi`). Changes are audited as `item.updated` (details = changed fields) in the same transaction; a no-op edit writes nothing. `GET /api/items/{id}/job` (any actor) → latest job `{id, kind, state, progress, attempts, createdAt, startedAt?, finishedAt?, log?}`, `log` only for callers who may edit; `404 job_not_found`. **Purge** (`DELETE`, `library.purge` hook → 401/403) → `204`, idempotent (an already purged item keeps its `purged_at`, leftover files are deleted): in one transaction the item row is locked (`FOR UPDATE` on PostgreSQL), a `PurgeCheck` callback runs (API: audit `item.purged` with `previousStatus`; **the sweeper step should pass its "still unreferenced" re-check here**), queued jobs are set `failed` (cancelled), a running job → `409 item_busy` (rollback), `status='purged'`, and `media/<id>` plus the staging dir `tmp/ingest/<id>` are renamed aside to `.purge-<name>-<uuid>` (`IngestPipeline.moveAside`; moved back if the transaction fails); deleted after the commit. `IngestPipeline.recoverDirectories` now also deletes `.purge-*` in `media/` and `tmp/ingest/`. Library/playlist entries stay ("media expired"). **Deviation from the sweeper order in `libraries.md`** (files then mark): mark + move aside atomically, delete after, so a crash never leaves a `ready` item without files. **Open for M4 (doc'd in `libraries.md`):** a metadata edit doesn't re-render `card.mp4` (nor the audio item's `index.bin`, which indexes the card), so cards show the old text until a re-encode; cover replacement isn't implemented. - [x] Playlists + entries (fractional positions, duplicates allowed, move with `beforeEntryId`).
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `6b66ffb` (no schema change; the M0 tables are used as they are). `library/Playlists` (service) + `library/PlaylistsApi` (routes; users only, guests/anonymous `401 not_authenticated`; another user's playlist is `404 playlist_not_found` for every route, so existence doesn't leak; `no-store`). `GET /api/playlists?cursor=&limit=` → `{items:[{id, name, description?, entryCount, createdAt, updatedAt}], nextCursor?}`, **decision:** most recently changed first (`updated_at` desc, id desc; cursor `<updatedAt>_<id>`; every rename/entry change bumps `updated_at`, a no-op PATCH doesn't). `POST {name, description?}` → 201. `GET /api/playlists/{id}` → the playlist + all `entries:[{id, item: Library.ItemView, addedAt}]` in order (no paging; **decision:** limits of 10 000 entries per playlist → `409 playlist_full`, 1000 playlists per user → `409 too_many_playlists`, the latter a soft cap on PostgreSQL under concurrent creates). `PATCH {name?, description?}`, `DELETE` → 204. Entries: `POST {itemId, beforeEntryId?}` → 201 entry (duplicates make a new entry), `PATCH /entries/{entryId} {beforeEntryId: id|null}` (required; `null` = to the end; before itself = no-op) → 200 entry, `DELETE` → 204. Errors: `400 invalid_name` / `name_too_long` (> 100 after cleaning like item metadata) / `invalid_description` / `description_too_long` (> 2000; **decision:** descriptions keep line breaks, at most two in a row, other control/format chars except ZWJ removed) / `invalid_item_id` / `invalid_before_entry_id` / `unknown_field`, `404 entry_not_found` / `item_not_found`, `409 before_entry_not_found` (the entry isn't in this playlist, e.g. a stale client view). **Positions** (REAL/DOUBLE, never sent to clients): append = floor(max) + 1 (1 when empty); before X = midpoint of X and its predecessor, X − 1 at the front; no room for a midpoint (~50 inserts into one gap) → renumber 1..n (order by position, id) in the same transaction and retry once. Every entry change runs in one write transaction that first locks the playlist row (`FOR UPDATE` on PostgreSQL). **Item rules (decision, mirrors `PUT /api/library/{itemId}`):** pending/processing/ready items can be added; a purged/failed upload → `409 media_expired` / `item_failed`; a purged/failed YouTube item is fetched again via the new `YoutubeImports.useExisting` (like `addExisting` without a library entry; refetch/youtube job, 10-min rule → `422 youtube_failed`), after `AddMediaPolicy.requireYoutube` (`403 feature_disabled`). Adding does **not** add to the library. **Retention:** adding sets the item's `last_referenced_at` = now; removing an entry, and deleting a playlist (for all its items), sets it to now (never backwards) — the later `last_referenced_at` step should keep this. For M3's `queue.addPlaylist`: `Playlists.get(ownerId, playlistId).entries()` gives the ordered items. `Main.router` takes the `PlaylistsApi` (test helper `TestUploads.playlistsApi`). Tests: `PlaylistsContract` (11, `SqlitePlaylistsTest` + `PostgresPlaylistsIT`: list order + cursor paging across `updated_at` ties, a change moves a playlist to the front, name/description cleaning + limits + update/no-op/clear, privacy of every operation, the playlist cap, order with appends/inserts/duplicates + entry shape + no library entry + `last_referenced_at`, moves (front/end/later/no-ops/foreign entries), 150 inserts into one gap and 120 moves through renumbering with distinct positions, remove/delete marking items, item status rules incl. refetch/refused check/10-min rule, the entry cap, 16 concurrent adds (half before the first entry) land in distinct positions), `PlaylistsApiTest` (3, HTTP: JSON shapes, order after add/move, paging, every 400/404/409 code, 401s, other user's 404s, `415`, `feature_disabled` then refetch), `PermissionMatrixTest` rows for the 8 routes, `DtoIpLeakTest` (all routes seeded incl. duplicate entries, a move, a removal and a deleted playlist; `Playlists$Playlist/PlaylistPage/Entry/Page` DTOs, `Edit`/`ItemRow` INTERNAL). `api.md` and `libraries.md` updated. `mvn verify` passes (733 unit + 213 integration). Jar smoke test (SQLite): playlist created, an item added twice (second before the first), the first moved to the end → order correct, anonymous `GET /api/playlists` 401, delete 204.
- [x] Sharing (`/api/shares`), adds to recipient's library, notifications (`seen`).
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `6323ff5` (no schema change; the M0 `shares` table is used as it is). `library/Shares` (service) + `library/SharesApi` (routes; users only, guests/anonymous `401 not_authenticated`; no permission needed, any user may share any item since items are visible by direct link; `no-store`). `POST /api/shares {itemId, toUsername, message?}` → `201 share` (sender's view). Recipient: enabled user by case-insensitive username (`Dialect.equalsIgnoreCase`), else `404 user_not_found` (also for disabled accounts; re-checked inside the write transaction); `400 cannot_share_with_self`. In one write transaction: insert-or-ignore the recipient's `library_entries` row (`via='share'`, `shared_by_user_id`; **decision:** an existing entry, whatever its `via`, is left unchanged, but the share row and its notification are still created), insert the `shares` row (`seen=0`), item `last_referenced_at` = now (never backwards). **Item rules (decision, mirror `PUT /api/library/{itemId}`):** pending/processing/ready OK; purged/failed upload → `409 media_expired` / `item_failed`; purged/failed YouTube item → `AddMediaPolicy.requireYoutube` of the **sender** then `YoutubeImports.useExisting` (refetch/youtube job, 10-min rule `422 youtube_failed`). **Decision: rate limit** of 100 shares per sender per rolling hour (`Shares.MAX_PER_HOUR`, counted from `shares`; `429 too_many_shares` with `Retry-After` = until the oldest share in the window leaves it), checked before any refetch and again in the transaction (soft cap on PostgreSQL under concurrency), so notifications can't be used to flood someone. Message: `UploadMetadata.cleanMultiline` (the playlist description cleaner, moved there from `Playlists.description`, which now delegates), ≤ 500 code points (`400 message_too_long`), blank → none. `GET /api/shares?direction=in|out&cursor=&limit=` → `{items:[{id, item: Library.ItemView, from: actor, to: actor, message?, createdAt, seen?}], nextCursor?, unseenCount}`, newest first (`created_at` desc, id desc; cursor `<createdAt>_<id>`; `direction` default `in`, `400 invalid_direction`; `limit` 1–200, default 50). **Privacy decision:** `seen` only in the recipient's view (`null`/omitted for the sender), and nothing about whether the recipient still has the item. `unseenCount` = the caller's unseen received shares in both directions' answers (the badge). **Notifications = unseen shares:** `POST /api/shares/seen {ids:[…]}` (≤ 200, distinct; others' / unknown ids ignored) or `{all:true}` → `{unseenCount}`; `400 invalid_ids` (neither/both, not an array of strings, too many) / `invalid_all` / `unknown_field`. No live push yet: **for M3**, the WebSocket can send a `share.new` (or similar) to the recipient's sockets after `Shares.share` commits; nothing else needs to change. `Main.router` takes the `SharesApi` (test helper `TestUploads.sharesApi`). Tests: `SharesContract` (6, `SqliteSharesTest` + `PostgresSharesIT`: library entry + `sharedBy` + `last_referenced_at`, existing entries kept (incl. `manual`), removal keeps history, recipient lookup (case, unknown, disabled, self, odd/long names), message cleaning/limit, item status rules incl. refused YouTube check and refetch, paging both directions across `created_at` ties, sender view without `seen`, mark seen by ids/all incl. foreign ids, the hourly limit with `Retry-After` and no refetch when over it), `SharesApiTest` (3, HTTP: JSON shapes/keys for both views, library row, paging via `nextCursor`, every 400/401/404/409/415 code, `feature_disabled` then refetch, 429 with `Retry-After: 3600`), `PermissionMatrixTest` rows for the 3 routes, `DtoIpLeakTest` (shares created through the route instead of a direct `library_entries` insert, both list directions swept; `Shares$Share/Page` DTOs, `ItemRow` INTERNAL). `api.md` and `libraries.md` updated. `mvn verify` passes (742 unit + 219 integration). Jar smoke test (SQLite): admin shares a sqlite3-inserted item with `Bob` → 201; bob's `GET /api/shares` shows it unseen (`unseenCount` 1) and his library has it `via:"share"` with `sharedBy`; `seen {all:true}` → 0; admin's `direction=out` has no `seen`; anonymous 401.
- [x] `library.viewAll` endpoints for other users' libraries and playlists.
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `41e2559` (no schema change). `library/UserLibraries` (service, read-only) + `library/UserLibrariesApi` (routes, every one behind `permissions.hook(LIBRARY_VIEW_ALL)`: guests/anonymous `401 not_authenticated`, users without it `403 forbidden`; `no-store`). **Decision: a user search was added** (`GET /api/users?q=&cursor=&limit=`), since "searchable by user" needs a way to find user ids without `admin.users`: `{items:[{id, username, displayName, disabled, libraryCount, playlistCount}], nextCursor?}`, ordered by `lower(username)`, id (cursor `<lowerUsername>_<id>`, split at the last `_`); `q` = one case-insensitive substring of username or display name (literal wildcards, ≤ 100 chars → `400 invalid_query`); disabled accounts are listed and marked (their entries still keep items). No email, roles or anything IP-related. `GET /api/users/{id}/library?q=&kind=&cursor=&limit=` and `GET /api/users/{id}/playlists?cursor=&limit=` delegate to `Library.list` / `Playlists.list` (same shapes, parameters, errors as the owner's own routes); **also added** `GET /api/users/{id}/playlists/{playlistId}` (→ `Playlists.get`, so a viewer can open a playlist's entries; another user's playlist under this id is `404 playlist_not_found`). Unknown user (or a guest's id) → `404 user_not_found`. Viewing doesn't touch `last_referenced_at`. Even a viewAll holder's own id needs the permission on these routes (the own-library routes are separate). `Main.router` takes the `UserLibrariesApi` (test helper `TestUploads.userLibrariesApi`). Tests: `UserLibrariesContract` (4, `SqliteUserLibrariesTest` + `PostgresUserLibrariesIT`: order, counts, disabled flag, search by username/display name incl. literal `%`/`_`, paging + malformed cursors, another user's library with q/kind/paging and no reference bump, playlists list/paging/get, foreign playlist and unknown user 404s), `UserLibrariesApiTest` (3, HTTP: JSON keys, same entry/playlist shapes, paging via `nextCursor`, 401/403 for all 4 routes, 404 codes, 400 codes), `PermissionMatrixTest` rows for the 4 routes (admin-only by default), `DtoIpLeakTest` (routes seeded and swept; `UserLibraries$UserPage/UserSummary` DTOs). `api.md` and `libraries.md` updated. `mvn verify` passes (749 unit + 223 integration). Headers `Cache-Control: private, no-cache` (re-encodes replace files under the same URL; guest access can end) and `nosniff`.
