Simulated mute (analyser reads forced to zero): `webaudio-mute` after exactly 10 s, overlay "Tap to restore sound"; clicking it rebuilt the pool without Web Audio, the item went on from the timeline (drift −9 ms), overlay hidden, no errors. Simulated stuck seek: `seek-stuck` → `handoff` v0 → v1, playing again at drift −16 ms. `?webaudio=0`: `context: "none"`, drift −7 ms. All four reports reached the server log (10–25 KB).
- [x] iOS Web Audio sample-rate mismatch, and a per-device Web Audio setting (added 2026-11-10, from Siggi's retest at main `a78e5a8`). On the **iPod touch 7 (iOS 15.1)** the stutter and mute come back with Web Audio on and are gone with `?webaudio=0` (`c12x2t`: no gaps); the **iPhone X on the same iOS 15.1** doesn't stutter or mute with Web Audio. Report `uhmxlu` (iPod, Web Audio on; 8080 `server.log`) shows why: the context reports `sampleRate: 44100`, but its clock runs **1086–1091 ms per 1000 ms of wall clock** (`ck` in every health sample) = 48000 / 44100 = 1.088, so the hardware pulls 48 kHz while the graph renders at 44.1 kHz; the output then has **5–11 gaps (dropouts) per second** (`out[3]`) while sync is perfect (drift +16 ms, no seeks). This is the known iOS WebKit sample-rate mismatch (the AudioContext's rate doesn't match the audio session's hardware rate, e.g. after a media element changed the session to 48 kHz; cf. WebKit bug 221334 and related reports).
  1. **Detect it at runtime** (no UA sniffing): within the first ~3 s of the context running, compare the `ClockWatch` ratio with 1000 (mismatch if it stays more than ~2 % off over 2–3 samples), and also treat a steady gap rate (e.g. ≥ 3 gaps/s for 3 s while the element plays and is audible) as a failing Web Audio path. Record the verdict in diagnostics.
  2. **Fix, in order of preference:** (a) if the clock runs at a different rate, create a new `AudioContext({ sampleRate: <rate the clock implies, e.g. 48000> })` and move playback onto fresh pool elements routed through it (a media element can only ever have one `MediaElementAudioSourceNode`, so the old elements can't be re-routed; hand off in sync as the stuck-seek handoff does), then re-check; check Safari 15.0 support for the `sampleRate` option (MDN) and that it really fixes the ratio; (b) if that doesn't fix it (or isn't supported), fall back to the direct path (no Web Audio) for this device automatically, in sync, and remember that for this browser (localStorage) so the next visit starts on the direct path at once. Show a small note ("Sound: direct (no volume control)") so it's visible.
  3. **Per-device setting (Siggi's request):** a "Web Audio" setting in listen mode, stored per browser in localStorage like "Audio only": **Auto** (default: Web Audio, with the detection/fallback above), **On**, **Off** (= what `?webaudio=0` does now). `?webaudio=0/1` still overrides it for testing. Explain in the UI what Off costs (no crossfade on the audio-only switch, no loudness adjustment for originals, where the browser can't set the volume).
  Tests (Node) for the detection thresholds and the setting's precedence; smoke test in headless Chrome with an emulated 1.088 clock ratio and gaps. Then update the retest notes under the 50 ms manual step: iPod touch 7 with Auto (should detect and fix or fall back within seconds, no lasting stutter), with On (to confirm the fix (a) if implemented), and the iPhone X / iPad unaffected. Siggi's server on 8080 (from a jar copy in the scratchpad, with `./data`) stays untouched.
  - **Done (2026-11-10, main `1341143`).** `mvn verify` passes (unit + 305 integration). Design written up in `docs/rooms.md` ("Sample-rate mismatch and the Web Audio setting").
    - **Detection (`WebAudioCheck`, `audiohealth.ts`):** `clock` when `ck` is more than 20 ms per 1000 off in 3 health samples in a row while the context runs (samples outside 500–2000 ignored), implied rate = reported rate × median ratio, snapped to a standard rate within 1.5 %; on the first `uhmxlu` samples it trips at the third running sample (≈ 3 s) with 48000 (a Node test replays them). `gaps` when ≥ 3 gaps/s for 3 s while the element should sound, only with the clock measured fine (so the mismatch's own gaps go to the rate fix first) and only in seconds with ≤ 20 % silent windows (**deviation/decision:** the click track from the 50 ms test has "gaps" around every click, but nearly all its windows are silent, so it doesn't trip). `ok` after 5 clean clock samples. Verdicts go to the `webaudio-check` event, `wa` in health samples, and `debug().webAudioCheck`.
    - **Fix (a):** a new `AudioContext({ sampleRate })` (MDN: the options argument is in Safari 14.1 / iOS 14.5, so 15.0 has it; if it throws, a plain context) and a fresh pool routed through it; the current entry starts on the new element at the timeline's position (like the stuck-seek handoff: a short gap, no crossfade). Once per page. A confirmed fix is remembered (`{rate}`), so the next visit creates the context at that rate at once. Whether WebKit then really runs the clock at 1000 is the open question for the device test.
    - **Fallback (b), Auto only:** if the clock is off again after (a), the new context isn't running 4 s later (WebKit may want a gesture for a new context), or on `gaps`, the pool is rebuilt without Web Audio and `{direct: true}` is remembered (also after a "Tap to restore sound" under Auto); the next visit starts direct. The memory is forgotten after 30 days or whenever the setting is changed. **On** and `?webaudio=1` do (a) but never (b) (`webaudio-kept`). **Decision:** spare elements (4 per kind) are touched in every "Click to listen" gesture so a pool rebuilt without a gesture can play on iOS; if iOS still refuses, the usual overlay asks for a tap.
    - **Setting:** "Web Audio" select (Auto / On / Off) next to "Audio only", `localStorage` `togethertunes.webAudio`; choosing Off shows what it costs; the note shows "Sound: direct (no volume control)" or "Sound: Web Audio at 48 kHz" (plus "(?webaudio=… in the URL)" when overridden). Precedence `?webaudio=0/1` > setting > Auto's memory (`webAudioPlan`, Node-tested). The report adds `webAudioSetting` and `webAudioMemory`.
    - **Smoke test (headless Chrome 157, own server on 18091 with a scratch data dir, stopped afterwards):** contexts made without a rate were forced to 44100 with `currentTime` × 1.088 and 300 zeroed samples per analyser window. *Fix works* (a 48000 context runs normally): `clock` (ratio 1.089 → 48000) at 4.0 s, rebuilt at 48 kHz, drift −35 ms, `ok` 6 s later, memory `{rate: 48000}`; next visit started at 48 kHz, `ok`, no rebuild. *Fix doesn't work:* `clock` again 4 s after the fix → direct, drift −10 ms, note "Sound: direct (no volume control)", next visit started direct (`remembered`). *Gaps with a normal clock:* `gaps` (25/s) at 4.0 s → direct. *On:* fix tried, then `webaudio-kept`. *`?webaudio=1` with memory "direct":* Web Audio (URL wins). *Setting:* On → Off rebuilt direct in the change gesture (hint shown), Off → Auto back to Web Audio, 8 spares touched. No page errors; reports reached the server log.
- [x] Use Web Audio only where it's needed (added 2026-11-10, from Siggi's Mac Safari test at main `1341143`): **Mac Safari (26.1) with Web Audio plays the sound 0.5–1 s behind the video** while the video is in sync (report `arqrbq`: drift +36 ms, context running at 48 kHz, clock ratio 1.000, no gaps, `outputLatency` reported as **0**: the delay is invisible to the page, so it can't be measured or compensated). With the Web Audio setting **Off** the sound is on time (`7oik3f`: drift +24 ms). Both reports are in the 8080 `server.log` (`grep 'diagnostics from [a-z]*: {"ref":"arqrbq"'`). So WebKit's `createMediaElementSource` path adds a large hidden output delay.
  1. **Auto = direct path where the page can set the element's volume.** Detect at runtime (no UA sniffing) whether `HTMLMediaElement.volume` is settable (set it to e.g. 0.5 on a spare element and read it back; iOS keeps 1). Where it is (desktop Safari, Chrome, Firefox, Android), Auto plays straight from the elements: gain and the 150 ms video ↔ audio-only crossfade via `el.volume` (the volume-ramp code path that already exists for no-Web-Audio). Only where volume can't be set (iOS/iPadOS) does Auto use Web Audio, with the existing sample-rate check and fallback. The On/Off setting and `?webaudio=` still force a path.
  2. **Gains above 0 dB on the direct path:** `el.volume` can't exceed 1, so an original whose `originalGainDb` is positive can't be brought up to `audio.m4a`'s loudness. In that case play `audio.m4a` instead of the original (it is loudness-normalised); a negative gain is applied as volume. Record the choice in `debug()`/diagnostics.
  3. **Check whether iOS has the same hidden delay:** add a note to the retest steps asking Siggi to compare sound against picture on the iPhone X and the iPad with Web Audio (Auto) and Off. If iOS is also late, the follow-up decision (accept no volume control on iOS, or an estimated offset) is Siggi's; don't decide it here.
  Update `docs/rooms.md` (why the direct path is preferred), Node tests for the detection and the path/gain choice, smoke test in headless Chrome. Run only the tests that cover the change (see CLAUDE.md). Siggi's server on 8080 (from a jar copy in the scratchpad, with `./data`) stays untouched. Then update the retest notes under the 50 ms and the video ↔ audio-only manual steps (Mac Safari: sound on time with Auto; the crossfade now uses volume ramps on desktop).
  - **Done (2026-11-10, main `c8ae132`).** Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`, `mvn test -Dtest='ListenJsTest,WebBuildTest'` (21 tests; `ListenJsTest` runs the Node tests `audiohealth.test.mjs` and `listensync.test.mjs`). Design in `docs/rooms.md` ("Direct path or Web Audio (Auto)").
    - **Detection (`volumeSettable`, `audiohealth.ts`):** the room page sets `volume = 0.5` on a spare `<audio>` once and reads it back (within 0.01), then restores it; a throwing setter counts as not settable. Node tests: a plain object (settable), a getter that always reads 1 and ignores writes (iOS), a throwing setter, a rounding setter.
    - **Plan (`webAudioPlan(override, setting, memory, volumeSettable)`):** `?webaudio=` > setting > Auto. Auto with a settable volume → direct, `why: "volume"` (Auto's memory is ignored then: it only matters on iOS); otherwise as before. The plan carries `volumeSettable` (in `debug().webAudioPlan`, the `player` event and the report, which also has a top-level `volumeSettable`). Node-tested, including On / Off / URL still forcing a path with a settable volume.
    - **Gain (`chooseSource(..., maxGainDb)`, `DIRECT_MAX_GAIN_DB = 0`):** on the direct path (no context) an original with `originalGainDb > 0` plays `audio.m4a`, marked `originalSkippedGainDb` (in `debug()` and the `load` event, which now also has `gainDb` and `original`); exactly 0 or a negative gain plays the original, the negative one as `volume`; no `audio.m4a` → the original anyway. **Decision:** this applies on every direct path, also iOS Off / fallback (there `volume` can't be set at all, so a positive gain was never applied before either; a negative gain still can't be applied on iOS, as the Off hint says).
    - **Note / hint:** where the volume can be set, the note is empty for Auto's direct path and "Sound: direct" when forced (Off, `?webaudio=0`); "Sound: direct (no volume control)" only where it can't be set. The Off hint now says the costs apply where the browser can't set the volume (iOS).
    - **Smoke test (headless Chrome 157, own server on 18091 with a scratch copy of a data dir, stopped afterwards; Siggi's 8080 server untouched):** two FLAC uploads, `hot` (gain −4.82 dB) and `quiet` (+35.96 dB), plus a video item. *Auto:* `webAudio: false`, `webAudioOff: "volume"`, note empty; `hot` plays the original at `volume` 0.574 (= 10^(−4.82/20)); `quiet` plays `audio.m4a` with `originalSkippedGainDb: 35.96`, volume 1; video → audio only crossfaded by volume steps 0.10, 0.21 … 1.00 over ~150 ms (outgoing 0.90 … 0), drift +27 ms after, and back. *Emulated iOS* (`volume` getter fixed at 1, writes ignored): Auto → Web Audio (`why: "default"`, context running), both originals play with their gains through the GainNodes, crossfade as before. *On* and *`?webaudio=1`:* Web Audio. *Off:* direct, note "Sound: direct", same gain choices as Auto. Changing the setting live: Auto → On rebuilt with Web Audio, On → Auto back to direct (`volume`). No page errors; the report (ref `fkjsds`) reached the server log with `volumeSettable: true`.
  - **Answer to part 3 (Siggi, 2026-11-10):** on iOS, if Web Audio adds any delay it's imperceptible. So iOS keeps Web Audio under Auto (with volume control); no offset or other change is needed.

  - **Checked by Siggi 2026-11-10 (main `c8ae132`): "not perfect, but close enough".** Passed in Brave (Mac), Quetta (Pixel 7 Pro), Reynard (iPhone X), Mac Safari, iPhone X and iPad Pro after the Safari seek-mode, sample-rate and direct-path fixes. iPod touch 7 out of scope.

  - **Agent pre-check (2026-11-09, main `5d9e6ff`, no code changes):** `mvn -DskipITs verify` passes. Two separate headless Chrome 157 instances (own profiles) on this Mac, logged in as the owner, `/rooms/sync-room?mode=listen`, "Click to listen" clicked in each; browser B's page clock skewed by +2.5 s (`performance.now` and `Date.now` patched before page scripts) so clock sync has real work to do. A third client drove the room over the WebSocket. Every 250 ms both element positions (`togetherTunesPlayer.debug().positionMs`) were read together with the real (unpatched) clock and brought to the same instant; both browsers share the OS clock, so A−B is the true difference in element position. Clock sync: A offset −0.2 ms, B −2500.0 ms (skew recovered to 0.1 ms). Results, |A−B| median / p95 / max: MP3 original from the start, 30 s incl. the initial catch-up (rate 1.05 for the first second or two) 1.9 / 69 / 96 ms; after an owner seek (3 s to settle) 1.8 / 4.8 / 4.9; after pause → play 4.2 / 5.8 / 8.2 (paused positions 58172 vs 58171 ms); video item `mezz.mp4` 1.2 / 2.5 / 2.7 (both steady at drift −30…−33 ms, inside the 40 ms band, so they agree with each other); the same item audio-only (`audio.m4a`) 2.0 / 4.5 / 4.7; FLAC original 3.6 / 6.3 / 7.0. No page exceptions. The driver is a scratch script (not in the repo); the same measurement for real devices is below.
  - **After the Safari sync fixes (2026-11-10, main `94434e3`): what changed, and what to retest.** `mvn verify` passes (913 unit, 304 integration; `listensync.test.mjs` covers the new rate/seek logic and `diagnostics.test.mjs` the rings).
    - **Two sync modes.** *Rate* (Chrome, Firefox, other Chromium/Gecko): drift > 40 ms starts a fixed nudge of ±3 % (±5 % above 300 ms) that holds until the drift is within 10 ms, so the rate changes twice per correction instead of almost every tick. *Seek* (WebKit: Mac Safari and **every** iOS/iPadOS browser): the rate always stays 1 (Safari raised the pitch and stalled the video on every rate change), and drift > 60 ms is corrected by a seek, at most every 2.5 s, backing off while seeks miss. Drift > 750 ms hard-seeks in both. `debug().mode` shows which one is used; `?sync=rate` / `?sync=seek` on the room URL forces one for testing.
    - **Seek lead.** Each element learns how late its seeks land (`debug().element.leadMs`) and aims that far ahead, re-seeking at once if a landing is > 150 ms off. This targets the "a second late, then fast and high-pitched" symptom.
    - **iPhone X not syncing (cause found by reasoning, not on a device).** The old loop judged drift only at `readyState ≥ HAVE_FUTURE_DATA` (3). iOS can report less while playing, so the element was never corrected and kept playing from 0. iOS also drops a seek made before playback starts, which was the only seek the old code made. Now an element that advances counts as playing, a dropped seek just shows up as drift and is redone, a seek still pending after 5 s is redone, the loop also runs on `playing`/`seeked`, and it can no longer die silently (errors go to the diagnostics ring). Reproduced in headless Chrome by emulating both iOS behaviours: the old code (main `447ec78`) stayed at −20 000 ms drift with 12 dropped seeks; the new code settled within ±10 ms (rate mode) or ±20 ms (seek mode), recovered from a 300 ms push in one seek (seek mode) or one ±5 % nudge (rate mode), and re-centred within ±12 ms after an owner seek. Not verified on a real iOS device, so this is the main thing to retest.
    - **Retest:** the whole procedure above on all three Safari devices plus one Chromium browser; listen especially for stutter in video, pitch changes (there should be none in Safari now), and whether the iPhone X / iPod touch 7 start at the room's position (not from 0) after a reload. Also try `?sync=rate` once in Mac Safari to confirm the pitch problem is real there (if it isn't, rate mode might be preferable).
    - **Send diagnostics from each device:** in listen mode, tap **Send diagnostics** (next to the "Audio only" checkbox, above the player) (works for logged-in users, guests and anonymous listeners). The page shows `Sent (ref xxxxxx)`; note the ref. The server log then has one line `Client diagnostics from <user|guest id|anonymous>: {"ref":"xxxxxx",...}` with the user agent, sync mode, `debug()`, the clock state, the last 200 sync-loop decisions, the last 150 player events (seeks and where they landed, rate changes, `play()` failures, media events) and page errors since load. Send one from each device right after anything looks or sounds wrong, and tell me the refs. (Find it with `grep 'ref":"xxxxxx' server.log`.)
  - **After the seek-mode follow-up (2026-11-10, main `cc039be`, from diagnostics `s0otvo` / `n15p1p`): what changed, and what to retest.** In both reports every seek seemed to land within a few ms at `seeked`, but WebKit then held the clock still for ~300 ms, so each seek ended ~300 ms behind, the loop seeked every 2.5 s, and the backoff then parked the player at −210 ms (iPad) / −104 ms (iPhone) for 30 s at a time. Now seek mode judges a seek on the drift ~1.4 s after `seeked` (once it has come to rest), learns the lead from that (expect `debug().element.leadMs` ≈ 300–400 ms on Safari), backs off only when seeks scatter (at most 8 s), and after a good seek only corrects drift above 80 ms (each WebKit seek is a ~300 ms audible stall, so it should now happen about twice after a start/owner seek and then rarely, minutes apart).
    - **Retest (Safari devices):** the procedure above on the iPad Pro, iPhone X, iPod touch 7 and Mac Safari. Expect after a reload or an owner seek: one or two audible hiccups, then `debug().driftMs` steady within about ±30 ms, `seekCooldownMs` 2500 and `seekBandMs` 80, and no further hiccups for minutes. Listen for repeated hiccups every few seconds (the old symptom) and check that two Safari devices side by side don't flam. Send diagnostics (below) from each device after ~1 minute of playing, and also right after anything sounds wrong; the events now show `seeked` (drift at `seeked`) and `landed` (settled drift, `stable`, the lead, cooldown and band) per seek.
    - **Still unverified:** whether ~300 ms is the same on every WebKit device and for audio-only elements (`<audio>`, originals); the lead is learnt per element, so a different value should just converge to it.

    - **A (default, Web Audio):** open the room in listen mode, play the video item `justfortonight` (and then an MP3) for **5+ minutes**. Listen for stutter. If it goes mute, within ~10 s the page should show **"Tap to restore sound"**; tap it and the sound should come back (without Web Audio) within a second or two, in sync. Tap **Send diagnostics** right after a stutter, right after the mute (before tapping restore is best, then once more after), and at the end; note the refs. In the report, `health` shows per second `out` (peak/RMS dB; `null` = exact silence), `gaps` (dropouts), `ck` (audio clock, 1000 = normal), `wk`/`vq` (decoded bytes, dropped frames), and `events` the `webaudio-mute` / `restore`.

Note: without Web Audio iOS can't set the gain, so the audio-only switch has no crossfade (an instant swap) and originals play without their loudness gain; on iOS the element path also ignores the ringer/silent switch. Send diagnostics at the end.

Before each run, set the setting as described (changing it also clears what Auto learnt), and don't add `?webaudio=` to the URL.

  - **What this doesn't cover (needs a person):** separate devices with their own clocks and real Wi-Fi (asymmetric latency is the one thing clock sync can't see), Safari/iOS 15 and Firefox element timing, and audio *output* latency (`currentTime` is the decoder position; speakers, AirPods/Bluetooth (often 150–250 ms) and some USB DACs add delay after it that the page can't know). The 50 ms target is about what is heard, with wired/built-in speakers.
  - **Setup:** `mvn package -DskipTests`; `config.json` with `http.publicBaseUrl` set to an address both devices reach (e.g. `http://<mac-lan-ip>:8080`, `http.bind` `0.0.0.0`); start the jar. Make a click track and upload it to the library: `ffmpeg -f lavfi -i "aevalsrc='lt(mod(t\,1)\,0.02)*0.8*sin(2*PI*1000*t)':s=48000:d=180" -c:a flac click.flac` (one 20 ms click per second, 3 min; FLAC so the original is played). Also queue a normal music video and an MP3. Create a room, queue the items, press play.
  - **Devices:** at least these pairs, each on Wi-Fi, side by side, built-in speakers (no Bluetooth): Chrome (Mac) + Firefox (Mac, or another computer); Mac Safari + iPhone X (iOS 15.1); iPad Pro (iPadOS 26.1) + iPod touch 7 (iOS 15.1). Open the room in listen mode on both (log in or join as guest), tap "Click to listen". On iOS, turn the ringer/silent switch to ring (Web Audio follows it on iOS 15).
  - **1. Console check (desktop browsers; iOS via Safari Web Inspector from the Mac):** run `togetherTunesPlayer.debug()` a few times on each device. Expect `driftMs` within ±40 ms (steady) once settled, `rate` 1 most of the time, `context: "running"`. `togetherTunesClock.offsetMs` should stay stable (changes of a few ms). Two devices each within ±25 ms of the server timeline are within 50 ms of each other.
  - **2. Listen check:** with the click track playing, stand between the devices. In sync (≲ 20 ms) the clicks merge into one; 30–50 ms sounds like a slightly thick click; a clear double click ("flam") means more than ~50 ms. Note which device is ahead (mute one briefly to tell).
  - **3. Measure (optional, gives a number):** record both devices with a third phone (Voice Memos) for ~20 s, equidistant from both speakers, open the recording in Audacity and measure the gap between the two onsets of a few clicks (zoom in; 1 ms resolution is easy). Record the result here.
  - **4. Events:** with both playing, as owner: seek (forward and back), pause and play, skip to next, let an item end naturally into the next one, toggle audio-only on one device. After each, both should be back within ~50 ms within about 2 s (hard seek at >750 ms, rate nudge above 40 ms).
  - **5. Long run:** leave both playing for 10+ minutes (one with the screen locked / app in the background, on iOS this may pause audio, which is expected; on unlock it must catch up). Check again with steps 1–2.
  - **Report:** per pair, the measured or heard offset and anything that drifts beyond ~50 ms or keeps hard-seeking (`debug()` output and browser version). If it holds, flip this to `[x]`.
- [x] *(manual)* Video ↔ audio-only switching has no audible gap in Chrome, Firefox and iOS Safari.
  - **Checked by Siggi 2026-11-10 (main `c8ae132`):** no gap in Brave and Firefox. Safari doesn't switch smoothly on any device (Mac, iPhone X, iPad); Siggi's decision: Safari/WebKit is best effort and doesn't block this check (see CLAUDE.md, Frontend). iOS users will get smooth switching from the native app later.
  - **Partly checked by Siggi 2026-11-10 (main `c8ae132`):** no gap in Brave and Firefox. Still to check: Mac Safari, iPhone X and iPad Pro. (Firefox on macOS showed the OS microphone permission dialog when audio started, with no site permission prompt; the page never asks for the microphone and creates no AudioContext on the direct path, so that comes from Firefox's audio backend, not from TogetherTunes.)
  - **iPod touch 7 is out of scope (Siggi, 2026-11-10):** test on the iPhone X and the iPad for iOS; the iPod is optional.
  - iOS devices: iPad Pro M1 (iPadOS 26.1), iPhone X (iOS 15.1) and iPod touch 7th gen (iOS 15.1, can't update). Test on all three. No 15.0 device is available, so 15.1 is the oldest version actually tested.
  - **Agent pre-check (2026-11-09, main `5d9e6ff`, no code changes):** `mvn verify` passes (899 unit + 304 integration). A 120 s 720p video whose audio is continuous pink noise plus a 440 Hz tone (−16 dBFS) was ingested and played in listen mode; a script injected before the page's own scripts routed every `connect(destination)` through a recording `ScriptProcessor` (256-frame blocks, ~5 ms), so what the page sends to the speakers was measured, not element positions. The "Audio only" checkbox was then toggled 12 times (6 each way, 4.5 s apart) and the deepest 20 ms window around each switch compared with the steady level (steady noise itself dips to about −2 dB in 20 ms windows). **Headless Chrome 157** (CDP): crossfade started 0.48–0.68 s after the click; deepest dip −2.3 … −6.8 dB, always inside the 150 ms ramp; no 5 ms block more than 20 dB below the steady level, i.e. no gap. **Headless Firefox 159** (WebDriver BiDi): crossfade 0.50–1.12 s after the click; dip −2.0 … −7.0 dB, no block below −20 dB. Both: no page errors; `audio.m4a`/`mezz.mp4` requests alternate as expected; the now-active element's drift after each switch was within ±35 ms. Safari wasn't automated (Remote Automation isn't enabled, and iOS needs a device anyway). The driver scripts are scratch (not in the repo).
  - **What the dip is:** the ramp is linear on both gains, so mid-fade each deck is at 0.5. `audio.m4a` and the mezzanine's audio are the same sound, but at the fade they are up to ~40 ms apart (the gate's steady band; here typically 10–20 ms), so they partly cancel (comb filtering) for those 150 ms: −3 dB for uncorrelated content, up to ~−6 dB at some frequencies. On music it may be heard as a short "phasey"/hollow moment, not a dropout. If Siggi hears that as a gap, the fix would be an equal-power curve (`setValueCurveAtTime` with sin/cos) or a tighter sync band before the fade; not done here.
  - **Not covered (needs a person):** hearing it on real speakers; iOS Safari, where it's unknown whether iOS 15 lets a `<video>` and an `<audio>` play at once (if it pauses one, the switch cuts instead of fading; listen for a hard cut or a short silence).
  - **Test steps (each of: Chrome and Firefox on the Mac, Mac Safari, iPad Pro (iPadOS 26.1), iPhone X and iPod touch 7 (iOS 15.1)):**
    1. `mvn package -DskipTests`; `config.json` with `http.bind` `0.0.0.0` and `http.publicBaseUrl` `http://<mac-lan-ip>:8080`; start the jar. Upload a music video with continuous sound (no silence around the test point) and, for a sharper test, a video with a sustained tone or pink noise: `ffmpeg -f lavfi -i testsrc2=s=1280x720:r=30:d=120 -f lavfi -i anoisesrc=color=pink:amplitude=0.15:d=120 -ac 2 -c:v libx264 -c:a aac -shortest noise.mp4`. Queue it in a room and press play.
    2. Open `/rooms/<slug>?mode=listen` on the device, tap "Click to listen". On iOS, ringer switch on ring; use the built-in speaker or wired headphones (Bluetooth adds its own latency but shouldn't add gaps).
    3. Tick "Audio only" and listen: the sound should continue without a dropout or click; the video disappears (cover art shows) about 0.5–1 s after the tick, when the fade happens. Untick it: the video appears and the sound continues. Repeat 5 times each way, also once mid-song with music.
    4. Optional: in the console (Safari Web Inspector for iOS) run `togetherTunesPlayer.debug()` during a switch: `switching` shows `from`, `to`, `fading` and `toDriftMs`; after it `source` is `audio.m4a` (audio-only) or `mezz.mp4`, `driftMs` within ±40 ms.
    5. iOS specifically: check the outgoing element isn't paused by the incoming one (a hard cut or a silent moment right at the tick or at the fade would show that), and that the video starts playing (not a frozen frame) when switching back.
    6. Optional measurement: record the device with another phone and look at the waveform in Audacity around the switch: a gap shows as a flat line, a fade dip as a short lower-amplitude stretch of ~150 ms.
  - **Report:** per browser/device: gap / cut / phasey dip / clean, with `debug()` output if anything is off. If all are clean, flip this to `[x]`.
  - **Since main `c8ae132` (Web Audio only where it's needed):** on desktop (Mac Safari, Chrome, Firefox) Auto plays straight from the elements, so the crossfade is now a `volume` ramp (stepped every 15 ms over 150 ms; headless Chrome showed 0.10, 0.21 … 1.00 on the incoming element) instead of GainNode ramps; iOS/iPadOS still use the GainNodes. Test the desktop browsers with the "Web Audio" setting on **Auto** (and in Mac Safari also check the sound stays in step with the picture after the switch back to video). The ~150 ms comb-filter dip described above applies to both ramps.

Code path: `chooseSource(..., audioOnly)` is the only place the player picks a URL for current, switch and preload alike, and returns `audio.m4a` for video items when audio-only is on; `#clear` removes `src` and calls `load()` so a dropped `mezz.mp4` element stops downloading. The room page uses `mezz.mp4` nowhere else (only the item page's player does). Control: unticking audio-only did request `mezz.mp4` (2 range requests), so the capture works; ticking it again, then seek + next, again made 0 `mezz.mp4` requests.

  - **Test steps (Chrome, Firefox, Mac Safari, and one iOS 15.1 device via Safari Web Inspector → Network):**
    1. Start the jar, queue two or three video items in a room, press play.
    2. Open `/rooms/<slug>?mode=listen`, tick "Audio only", then reload the page (so the page starts in audio-only and nothing from before the switch is listed). Open the network panel, clear it, filter on `mezz`. Tap "Click to listen".
    3. With the filter on, do: seek forward and back, pause and play, next, previous, and let one item play to its end into the next (the next item is preloaded ~10 s before the end). Expect **no rows** for `mezz.mp4`; with the filter changed to `media`, expect only `audio.m4a` and `cover.jpg`.

- [x] An audio upload plays from its original, at the same loudness as `audio.m4a` (within about 1 dB).
  - Done 2026-11-09 at main `11456c0`. **Found a gap:** `original_gain_db = min(−14 − I, −1 − TP)` assumed `audio.m4a` always lands at −14 LUFS, but loudnorm's `linear=true` falls back to dynamic mode when the gain doesn't fit under −1 dBTP or the LRA is above 11 LU. A 30 s quiet source with sharp peaks (−31.4 LUFS, −3.2 dBTP) became −15.1 LUFS in `audio.m4a`, while the original with its +2.23 dB gain measured −28.8 LUFS: 13 dB quieter. **Fix (decision):** after the encode, ingest measures `audio.m4a` with the same loudnorm pass 1 and stores `original_gain_db = min(I_m4a − I, 0 − TP)` (cap at 0 dBTP, since the browser clips above full scale; −1 dBTP isn't needed for the original). If the cap leaves the original more than 0.5 dB quieter than `audio.m4a` (a quiet, peaky source loudnorm compressed), the original is kept but `original_web` = 0, so clients play `audio.m4a`; the job log says "not played in browsers: matching audio.m4a needs +x dB, but its peaks allow only +y dB". Silence: no gain, still web-playable, as before. The client side was already right (gain `10^(g/20)` on the element's `GainNode`, Node-tested in `listensync.test.mjs`). Items ingested before this keep their old gain until re-ingested (no migration; for the usual linear case the old and new gains differ by only the AAC encode's few hundredths of a dB). Tests: `OriginalsTest.gainMatchesAudioM4aCappedAtZeroDbtp` + `describe`; new `IngestPipelineTest.originalPlaysAsLoudAsAudioM4a` ingests four FLACs (quiet → linear, loud master → negative gain, wide loudness range → dynamic, quiet with peaks), applies the stored gain to the original in float (as Web Audio does) and checks it measures within 1 dB of `audio.m4a` with true peak ≤ 0 dBTP, and that the peaky one is not web-playable; the FLAC and milestone-set tests now expect the new formula. `docs/library-and-ingest.md#originals-audio-uploads` updated. `mvn verify` passes (900 unit + 304 integration).
- [x] A guest can vote and request according to policy, and gets 403 on a non-current, non-next item when guest downloads are off.
  - Checked 2026-11-10 at main `1113b2b` (test only, no code change). **Live run** (shaded jar, fresh SQLite data dir, five 20 s MP3 uploads through the API, a public `vote` room; owner over the WebSocket with `auth`, a cookie guest from `POST /api/me/guest` redeeming its identity ticket on the socket and using the `gid` cookie for HTTP; Node 24 driver, scratch): guest `GET /media/{id}/audio.m4a` → `206` for the current and the next item, `403` for a queued item further down, an item in no room, `original` of a queued item, and anonymously; the guest's upvote moved that entry to next, after which it was `206` and the former next `403` (the live set follows votes at once); guest `queue.add` → `queued` and the owner got `request.new` with `by.isGuest`; guest `next` → `403 forbidden`; room `guests.canVote` off → `403 cannot_vote`; room `guests.canRequestLibrary` off → `403 cannot_request` (with uploads/links also off the guest has no request path at all, so it is `cannot_request` rather than `guest_library_requests_disabled`, as `ControlPolicy.requireSource` documents) and request search `403`; server `guests.canVote` off → `cannot_vote`, back on → vote accepted; `guests.download` on → `206` for any item, guest and anonymous; off again → `403`. No exceptions in the server log. **Regression test:** `RoomSocketTest.guestsVoteAndRequestByPolicyAndOnlyStreamCurrentAndNext` (the test router now also registers `MediaApi` with `registry.playingItems()`): the same over a real socket, plus the vote-skip at the default 0.5 threshold (1 of 2 present skips to the voted-up entry, and the skipped item is `403` at once), `requestsPerActor` (`409 request_limit` on the 4th), `guest_youtube_disabled` by default, server `guests.canRequestLibrary` off, `guests.enabled` off (the guest socket gets `401 session_ended` and its media `403`; after re-enabling, the same cookie reconnects), `owner` mode (request → `suggested`, vote `409 wrong_control_mode`, pause `forbidden`), `open` mode (pause/play allowed), and `next` (the played item `403`, the new current and next `200`). `mvn verify` passes (901 unit + 304 integration).

## M4 — Program output (all three outputs)

- [x] H.264/AAC helpers: AVCC → Annex B (with AUD, SPS/PPS before IDR), ADTS header writer; unit tests.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `3aaa2c2`. `media/AnnexB` (built from the avcC: NAL length size 1/2/4, SPS/PPS lists; `accessUnit(sample, off, len)` → AUD `00 00 00 01 09 F0`, then SPS+PPS from the avcC if the sample holds an IDR NAL (type 5, detected from the NALs, not the index flag), then the sample's NALs with 4-byte start codes; `isIdr(...)`) and `media/Adts` (`writeHeader`/`header`/`wrap`, fields from `EncodeProfile`'s 48 kHz stereo, AAC-LC, fullness 0x7FF, max payload 8184). **Decision:** in-band AUD, SPS and PPS NALs in a sample are dropped (as the overlay re-encoder will do), so every access unit has exactly one AUD and parameter sets appear only before IDRs, always from the avcC (identical across encodes per invariant 1). Malformed avcC or samples throw `AnnexB.H264FormatException` (an `IOException`). Tests: `AnnexBTest` (synthetic samples incl. offsets, 1/2-byte prefixes, multiple SPS/PPS, malformed input; plus a real 5 s profile encode converted sample by sample whose Annex B stream decodes to framemd5-identical frames, with SPS/PPS before exactly the 3 IDRs) and `AdtsTest` (bit fields, known bytes, limits; headers byte-identical to ffmpeg's ADTS muxer on every frame of a real `audio.m4a`). `mvn -DskipITs verify` passes (942 unit tests).
- [x] Playback survives navigation (added 2026-11-10, Siggi's request; belongs with M3's web playback, placed here so it lands before M4/M5 add more pages): **navigating to another page of the site must not interrupt listen-mode playback, unless it's to another room.** Today every page is a separate HTML document, so following a link from the room page (library, playlists, account, admin, item page, rooms list, room management) unloads the player. Build it without a framework or bundler (CLAUDE.md):
  - **A persistent shell:** once a room is playing in listen mode, same-origin navigation to non-room pages happens client-side: intercept link clicks and form-driven navigations to site pages, fetch the target page, swap the page's main content and title, run the target page's module (pages need a mount/unmount entry point instead of running on load; refactor them so each page module exports e.g. `mount(root, params)` / `unmount()`), and update the URL with `history.pushState`; handle back/forward (`popstate`), scroll position and focus. The room's WebSocket, clock sync and the media elements (and the "Click to listen" unlock) stay alive.
  - **A mini player** shown on every page while a room plays: room name (link back to the room page), current item, play state, "Audio only" toggle and a stop/leave button. Going back to the room page reattaches the full player view without restarting playback.
  - **What still interrupts:** navigating to a *different* room (leave the old one, join the new one), logging out, a full reload (the browser needs a new "Click to listen" tap; say so in the mini player if the page is reloaded), and links to other sites / downloads. Remote mode is unaffected (nothing plays).
  - Keep plain multi-page loading working when JavaScript isn't playing anything (deep links, reloads); the shell only takes over while something plays (or always, if that's simpler and every page works both ways).
  Tests: JS (Node) tests for the router/link interception rules (same-origin, other room, downloads, modifier keys, `target`), `WebBuildTest`/`MainRouterTest` updates, and a headless-Chrome smoke test: start playing in a room, navigate library → playlists → account → back, check the media element never paused or reloaded (no new `load` events, drift stays steady) and the WebSocket stayed open; then navigate to another room and check the old one was left. Run only the tests that cover the change (CLAUDE.md). Siggi's server on 8080 stays untouched.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `ae84d6d`, web only (no server change). **Approach:** pages stay separate HTML documents (deep links/reloads unchanged); the room page is the shell (`web/src/shell.ts`). Instead of refactoring every page into `mount()/unmount()`, the shell re-imports the page's own module under a fresh `?mount=N` (a new module instance starts exactly like on a page load, finding its elements in `#shell-page`); unmount = remove the DOM and abort `pageSignal()` (`nav.ts`), which the admin and room-management pages now pass to their `hashchange` listeners; `onReady` silences failures of a page taken away mid-start, and the shell drops unhandled rejections whose stack points to an old `?mount=`. `admin.ts` now uses `onReady` (it waited for a `DOMContentLoaded` that had already fired). **Rules** (`router.ts`, pure): shown in place: `/`, `/index.html`, `/rooms/`, `/library/`, `/playlists/` (incl. `?id=`), `/account/`, `/admin/`, `/items/{id}`, `/rooms/{ref}/manage` (any room); the playing room (`id`, slug, or the ref it was opened with) → its view again; everything else to the browser: other rooms, login/register/password/setup (they change who's listening), API/media, `/r/…`, test pages, other origins/schemes, `download`, non-`_self` `target`, modifier keys / non-main button, already `defaultPrevented`; same path+query with a fragment → left to the browser. Pages' own navigations go through `navigate()` / `reloadPage()` (playlists create/open/delete, room delete, make-owner reload, leave room); plain GET form submits are intercepted too (none exist today). **Mini player** (fixed bottom bar, body gets `has-mini-player` padding): state (▶ Playing / ⏸ Paused / Nothing playing / Stopped), room name linking back, item title (or the kick text), "Audio only" synced with the room's checkbox, Leave (forgets the room and `location.replace`s the shown page). Room titles aren't written over other pages' titles; the room's `?mode/?sync/?webaudio` are read from the load-time query (`PAGE_SEARCH`). **Reload note:** while another page is shown, `sessionStorage` `togethertunes.playingRoom` holds the room; a full load of any page (via `renderNav`) shows a once-only bar "Playback of {room} stopped … click "Click to listen" to listen again" with a link; cleared by the room page, logout, Leave and returning to the room view. **Found and fixed:** Chrome kept the room page in the back/forward cache **with its WebSocket open**, so after going to another room the old room still counted the user as listening (also true before this step for any plain navigation away); the room page now `conn.stop()`s and stops the player on `pagehide`, and reloads on a persisted `pageshow`. Tests: `router.test.mjs` (6 cases) via the new `ShellJsTest` (also: router has no DOM, the shown pages' ids don't clash with `room.html`'s apart from `site-nav`, page modules start via `onReady`, window/document listeners use `pageSignal()`, no `location.href =`/`reload()`; room/shell wiring), `MainRouterTest.pagesTheShellShowsInPlaceServeHtmlWithOneModule`; ran `mvn package -DskipTests` and `mvn test -Dtest='ShellJsTest,MainRouterTest,WebBuildTest,*JsTest'` (54 tests, pass). **Headless-Chrome smoke test** over CDP against the jar (port 18097, temp data dir; script not kept): MP3 uploaded and playing in "Lounge"; library → playlists → (create playlist → `navigate()` to `?id=`) → account → back ×4 → room → forward → mini-player room link → library: same document throughout, one WebSocket still open, no new `loadstart`/`emptied`/`pause` on the media elements, element playing, drift steady at ~1–12 ms; titles and mini player text right; mini "Audio only" toggles the room's and back; rooms list → link to "Other Room" = full load, and Lounge presence drops to `listening 0` within ~10 ms (it stayed at 1 before the `pagehide` fix); reload on `/library/` shows the stopped note once, then not again; in remote mode a nav link is a plain page load. No page exceptions. Not checked in Firefox or Safari (Safari is best effort per CLAUDE.md); the M3 manual browser checks can cover Firefox.
- [x] Sources: `VideoItemSource`, `AudioItemSource` (incl. `presentAsAudio`), `FillerSource` (Paused / Idle / Loading). Source abstraction must not assume file-backed or known-duration sources (future live input).
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `d0cc6ba`. Package `output`: `ProgramSource` is pull-based: `nextVideo()` (a `VideoSample(avcc bytes, pts90k relative to the source start, idr)`, null = no more video, hold the last picture) and `nextAudio()` (raw AAC frame, frame *k* implicitly at `k·1920`; null = the source has ended). Reads may block; nothing asks for a file or a duration, so a live input fits. Item sources (`ItemSource`: `itemId`, `requestedStartMs`, `mediaStartMs`) read through `SampleData` (`FileSampleData`: per-file `FileChannel` opened lazily, positional reads). `VideoItemSource` starts at the keyframe at or before `startMs`, video PTS `(srcPts − keyframePts)·90000/timescale` (rounded, kept strictly increasing), audio from the AAC frame **nearest** the keyframe's time (≤ ~11 ms off; earlier one on a tie). `AudioItemSource` starts audio at the frame containing `startMs` and repeats a `CardGop` (in-memory 60-frame GOP, checked: one IDR first, none after; frame *n* = `frame(n % 60)` at `n·3000`). `FillerSource(Filler.PAUSED/IDLE/LOADING, card, silence)` repeats card and silence forever. `ItemSources.forItem(itemId, videoItem, presentAsAudio, index, data, card, startMs)` picks the source; a passed card wins (room overlay card), an audio item falls back to the card in its index. **Note:** a video item's `card.mp4` and the filler/room cards aren't indexed, so `CardGop.load` indexes them once with ffprobe (`IndexBuilder`); callers should cache them (loading/caching is for the index-loading step). Overlay GOP substitution in `VideoItemSource` is left for M5. Tests: `SourcesTest` (synthetic indexes) and `RealSourcesTest` (real profile encodes, needs ffmpeg). `mvn -DskipITs verify` passes (953 unit tests).
- [x] Unit builder + timestamp rules: audio-driven boundaries (1920-tick frames), video follows audio, card GOP repetition and truncation, fillers in whole units, clock start at 900000, 33-bit wrap, DTS = PTS.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `2a7a90a`. Package `output`: `UnitBuilder` (`play(source)` between units, `next()` → `Unit`, `nextUnitStart()`, `switchPoint()`, `sourceEnded()`; owns and closes the source it switches away from), `Unit(startPts, boundaryPts, audioEndPts, video, audio, source, sourceEnded)` with `Unit.Video`/`Unit.Audio` (`dts()` = `pts()`, `pts33()`/`dts33()`), `OutputClock` (`START` 900000, `WRAP` 2³³, `wrap()`). Timestamps stay unwrapped `long`s internally and are masked only on the wire (wrap after arithmetic, e.g. `wrap(dts − 63000)` for the PCR). A unit runs from one of the source's IDRs to its next and holds the audio frames whose PTS falls in that span; audio frame *k* at `S + k·1920`, video at `S + relPts`, video ≥ `E_n` dropped (card GOP / long video truncated). **Decisions:** (1) since 2 s is 93.75 audio frames, a unit's last audio frame usually straddles its boundary; on a switch the new source starts at `switchPoint()` = where the emitted audio ends (`S_{n+1} = E_n`, ≤ 1919 ticks past the boundary), and when a source ends right after its last IDR's 2 s the unit runs to `E_n`: so a unit can be up to 1919 ticks (21.3 ms) longer than 2 s (HLS `EXTINF` 2.021 still rounds to the target duration 2). (2) Video shorter than audio: the last frame is held to the end of that 2 s unit; any further unit (only when the audio outlasts the video by more than the rest of a GOP) repeats the source's last IDR once at its start, so every unit still starts with an IDR. (3) A source with no audio at all gives no unit (`next()` → null, source ended). (4) Contract violations (first video sample not an IDR at 0, non-increasing video PTS) → `IOException`. Tests run: `UnitBuilderTest` (11: clock start, filler whole units with the 94/94/94/93 audio split, audio item ending mid-unit with card truncation, video item PTS = S + rel, video longer/shorter than audio, switch at a boundary, many switches incl. seeks, 33-bit wrap, no-audio source, contract violations; a checker asserts on every unit: IDR at start, contiguity, strictly increasing video PTS, audio deltas exactly 1920, DTS = PTS), plus `SourcesTest` and `RealSourcesTest`.
- [x] Applying timeline changes at unit boundaries; natural end mid-unit; >3 s drift re-sync at item boundaries; unknown state → Paused.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `58190a9`. Package `output`: `TimelineFollower` (wraps the `UnitBuilder`; `update(ProgramCue)` from any thread, `nextUnit()` on the packager thread, never null) and `ProgramCue` (state — `null` = a state the packager doesn't recognise —, current/next `Entry(entryId, itemId, videoItem, presentAsAudio, durationMs)`, anchor, `playedOut`; `ProgramCue.of(Timeline, …)`). Sources come from a `SourceFactory` (`item(entry, startMs)` → null when not loadable yet; `filler(Filler)`) and output PTS map to server time through a `ServerClock` (both supplied by later steps: index loading, pacing). **Rules as built:** at each unit boundary, positions are taken at the builder's `switchPoint()` (where a new source would start); same entry + same cue or a re-anchor that moves the position by < 100 ms (`SAME_POSITION_MS`) → keep, otherwise seek → item at the timeline's position; `PAUSED` and unknown/`null` cue → Paused filler; `IDLE` → Idle filler; unplayable (factory null or throws, logged) → Loading filler, retried every boundary. **Natural end:** if the room hasn't advanced yet, the output starts `next` at 0 at `E_n` itself (Idle filler if there is no next) and later *adopts* the room's advance without restarting if output and timeline are ≤ 3 s apart. **Decisions:** (1) `ProgramCue.playedOut` = the entry the timeline advanced away from because it reached its end; while the output still plays that entry (output behind) it plays it to its end instead of cutting it (no correction mid-item). The room wiring must set it from `EntryStateChanged(id, PLAYED)` + the advance and keep it for later cues of the same timeline. (2) Drift > 3 s **behind** at an item boundary → next item at the timeline's position for it; > 3 s **ahead** (item ended in the output while the timeline still has > 3 s of it, e.g. media shorter than `durationMs`) → Loading filler until the timeline moves on, then the next item at its position. (3) An entry that ended in the output isn't replayed while the cue still shows it (same cue or same motion); a real seek into it plays it again. (4) A timeline already at/after the current entry's end (room about to advance) → `next` from 0 (or from the overshoot if > 3 s). (5) A source that gives nothing (e.g. a seek past the media end) is re-decided, at most 4 times, then the Paused filler. Wiring this to `PackagerHook` (building cues from room state) is left to the packager lifecycle step. Loaded per item: the `SampleIndex`, a `FileSampleData` (positional `FileChannel.read(buf, pos)`), and the card in memory (audio item: from its index; video item played as audio: `card.mp4` via a `CardLoader`, `ffprobeCardLoader` = `CardGop.load`, loaded only when some entry needs it). `filler(f)` serves the in-memory filler cards (`loadFillerCards(FillerCards, CardLoader)`) and the silence frame. **Not loadable** (retried no sooner than 5 s later, each distinct failure logged once): missing/unreadable `index.bin`, index for another profile (re-encode pending, `docs/library-and-ingest.md`), avcC ≠ reference (invariant 1), no audio track. An item dropped while loading is discarded when the load finishes. **Not done here:** a stale loaded index isn't detected (an item stays loaded only while current/next/playing, and re-encodes for the current profile don't happen; card re-render after metadata edits is still the open M4 note in `docs/libraries.md`); two rooms playing the same item load it separately (a few MB each). Tests: `ProgramMediaTest` (10: current+next only and eviction as the room advances/idles, on-demand load with positional reads, card for presentAsAudio loaded once, missing index retried after backoff, other profile, avcC mismatch, dropped while loading, close, bad item id, follower → Loading then the item). `mvn -DskipITs verify` passes (995 unit tests).
- [x] MPEG-TS muxer: PIDs as VRCDN (SDT 0x11, PMT 0x100, video 0x101, audio 0x102), SDT/PAT/PMT at each unit start and PAT/PMT about every 400 ms, RAI on IDR, PES for video (unbounded) and audio (~5 frames), interleave by DTS, PCR on every video PES, continuity counters for packager lifetime, stuffing. Each unit's bytes produced once as an immutable `byte[]`.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `089a690`. `output/TsMuxer` (one per packager, packager thread only): `new TsMuxer(avcC, provider, serviceName)` (or just `avcC`: both names "TogetherTunes"), `byte[] mux(Unit)` returns a fresh array the muxer never touches again (shared by every consumer; Java can't make it immutable, so don't modify it). **As built:** PAT (TS id 1, program 1 → PMT 0x100), PMT (PCR PID 0x101; 0x1B @ 0x101, 0x0F @ 0x102, no descriptors), SDT (original_network_id 0xFF01 as ffmpeg, service 1, service_descriptor type 0x01 with provider/name, printable ASCII only, max 64 chars each), version 0, CRC-32/MPEG-2. Each unit: SDT, PAT, PMT, then PES in DTS order (video first on a tie, so the IDR comes first); PAT+PMT again before the first PES whose DTS is ≥ 36000 ticks after the last tables (5 times per 2 s unit: 0, 0.4, … 1.6 s; SDT only at the unit start). Video: one PES per access unit via `AnnexB.accessUnit`, `PES_packet_length 0`, `data_alignment_indicator 1`; first packet's adaptation field has the PCR (`wrap(DTS − 63000)·300`, extension 0) and RAI on IDRs only. Audio: up to 5 ADTS frames per PES (fewer if it would pass 65535 bytes), PTS of the first; groups restart at each unit. Continuity counters per PID for the muxer's lifetime, no discontinuity flag ever. Last packet of each PES padded with adaptation-field stuffing; PSI padded with 0xFF. A bad AVCC sample or an AAC frame > 8184 bytes → `IOException`. Tests: `TsMuxerTest` (11, synthetic units parsed back: CRC, table layout and order, PES content vs `AnnexB`/`Adts`, PCR/RAI per video PES, DTS order and 400 ms tables, CC across 12 units, stuffing only at PES ends for many sizes, 33-bit wrap of PTS/PCR, large audio frames splitting PES, errors) and `RealTsMuxerTest` (real profile encodes through `UnitBuilder`: video item to its natural end, Paused filler, seek, item as audio, Idle filler → `ffmpeg -v error … -f null -` prints nothing; ffprobe sees h264 0x101 / aac 0x102, PCR PID 257, service name/provider, every frame, video PTS strictly increasing, DTS = PTS, audio deltas exactly 1920). `TsMuxer$Pes` classified internal in `DtoIpLeakTest`. `mvn -DskipITs verify` passes (1007 unit tests).
- [x] Pacing thread (one platform thread per active output, LEAD 1000 ms); per-consumer bounded queues, slow consumers disconnected.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `3746fa6`. Package `output`: `Packager(name, UnitProducer, Muxer, PacingClock, TimeSource)` runs one platform thread `packager-{name}` (daemon; `name` is for logs, e.g. the room id, never a token). Loop: `due = clock.nanoTimeAt(producer.nextUnitStart()) − LEAD`; sleep until then; **then** build (`nextUnit()`), mux once and publish a `TsUnit(sequence, startPts, boundaryPts, audioEndPts, ts)` to every consumer. Waiting before building means timeline changes are applied as late as possible (seek visible about one unit + LEAD later); a unit after a source switch starts up to 1919 ticks after `nextUnitStart()`, so it can go out at most 21 ms more than LEAD early. `TimelineFollower` now implements `UnitProducer` (new `nextUnitStart()` = the builder's). `PacingClock` (also the follower's `ServerClock`): `START` ↔ the time source's now (monotonic ns for pacing, server-clock ms for `serverMsAt`, so a server-clock step neither bursts nor stalls the output). `TimeSource.system(Clock)` is real time; tests use a virtual one. **Decisions:** (1) a stall: if the thread is more than `MAX_LAG_MS` = 4000 behind a unit's send time, the pacing clock is moved forward by the lag (`rebase`, logged) instead of bursting the missed output; smaller lags are caught up (timestamps never change either way, invariant 3). (2) If building or muxing fails (`IOException`, runtime error), the packager logs it, records `failure()`, stops and closes every consumer; the lifecycle step decides about restarting. (3) The producer is closed on the packager thread when it ends (`close()` interrupts and joins up to 5 s; before `start()` it closes it directly; idempotent). **Consumers:** `UnitSink` (`deliver` must not block; false → removed and closed) for the HLS ring later; `ConsumerQueue` (default capacity 4) for `.ts` clients and the RTMP pusher via `subscribe(joinLatest)` / `subscribe(capacity, joinLatest)`: `join=next` gets the next emitted unit, `join=latest` gets `latest()` queued at once; a unit arriving at a full queue closes it (`OVERFLOW`), drops what it held and runs its `onClose` action (meant to close the client's `StreamBody`, which also frees a writer blocked on a stalled client: the open M4 note from 2026-11-08), also when the packager stops (`STOPPED`); `take()`/`poll(ms)` return null once closed; `leave()` (`CONSUMER`) removes it without running `onClose`. All consumers share the same `byte[]`. `consumerCount()` for the lifecycle step. `TsUnit` classified internal in `DtoIpLeakTest`. Tests run: `PackagerTest` (13: send times exactly `max(0, due − 1000 ms)`, never more than LEAD early with odd unit lengths, slow consumer dropped after 4 queued units while another gets every unit in order, join next/latest and shared bytes, 10 s stall → pacing moved by 8 s and 2 s spacing after, 3 s lag caught up without moving, producer failure closes consumers and the producer on `packager-room1`, close (platform thread, sinks/queues/producer closed, idempotent, later sinks closed at once), close before start, consumer leave, `take()` woken by stop, real-time pacing with 100 ms units and a 50 ms lead, `TimelineFollower` + `TsMuxer` + `Packager` end to end on a virtual clock), plus `TimelineFollowerTest`, `UnitBuilderTest`, `TsMuxerTest`, `DtoIpLeakTest`; `PackagerTest` run 5 more times, no flakes.
- [x] Packager lifecycle: start on enable or first request; stop 60 s after last consumer unless RTMP enabled.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `ec775d3`. `output/ProgramOutputs` (one per server, the registry's `PackagerHook`; Main wires it, `snapshotsFrom(registry)` after the registry exists, `close()` on shutdown before the registry) and `output/ProgramPipeline` (the `OutputFactory`: each start opens a `ProgramMedia`, a fresh `PacingClock`, `UnitBuilder`, `TimelineFollower`, `TsMuxer` and a started `Packager`, so a new start resets the output clock and CCs; filler cards are ffprobe-indexed on the first start and cached; without reference card / filler cards / silence frame every start fails with an `IOException`). **API for the endpoint steps:** `acquire(roomId)` → `Lease` (`.ts` connection, RTMP pusher; `lease.packager()` to subscribe, `close()` when the consumer leaves), `touch(roomId)` (output enabled, HLS playlist/segment fetch: starts if needed, counts as activity now), `keepRunning(roomId, rtmpEnabled)` (pins it running / unpins with the 60 s counted from then), `stop(roomId)` (every output disabled: at once, consumers closed), `running(roomId)` (never starts), `addStartListener` (HLS ring / pusher attach on each (re)start). Checking the server/room switches and tokens stays with the caller (outputs API step). **Stop rule:** a sweep every 5 s stops an output with no leases, not pinned, and no activity for `IDLE_STOP_MS` = 60 s (so it stops 60–65 s after the last consumer); a packager that stopped by itself (build/mux failure) is cleaned up and restarted by the next acquire/touch, or by the sweep while pinned (every 5 s at most). **Following the room:** `PackagerHook` got a default `roomChanged(RoomState, RoomChange)` that the executor now calls; `ProgramOutputs` copies a `Snapshot` (version, timeline, current entry, `Playback.nextEligible`, playedOut) on the executor thread and, for rooms that have an output, resolves it into a `ProgramCue` (item kind and `present_as_audio` from `items`, `itemKindsFrom(db)`) on a virtual thread, newest version wins, and calls `Output.update`. `playedOut` = the entry of an `EntryStateChanged(PLAYED)` in a change that moved the timeline, kept until the next timeline change. An output started before any change was seen reads the room once through a read-only mutation on its executor (changes nothing; invariant 4). A current item that can't be looked up → unknown cue (Paused filler); a next one → left out. Room deleted → its output stops (on a virtual thread, not the executor). **Not done:** an edit of `present_as_audio` on the playing item takes effect at the room's next change. Tests run: `ProgramOutputsTest` (11: start on first request and shared, stop exactly 60 s after the last lease, enable/touch then idle stop, RTMP pin and unpin, restart gives a new packager, `stop` with RTMP, failed packager restarted by request and by the sweep, start failures, close, snapshot → cue resolution, room read once), `ProgramOutputsRoomsTest` (SQLite + real `RoomRegistry`: start reads the room, pause/play/natural end/skip reach the output as cues with kinds and `playedOut`, delete stops it), plus `DtoIpLeakTest` (3 new records classified internal), `SqliteRoomExecutorTest`, `SqlitePlaybackTest`, `PackagerTest`, `TimelineFollowerTest`, `ProgramMediaTest`, `MainTest`, `MainRouterTest`; `mvn package -DskipTests` builds; the jar starts and stops cleanly on a spare port.

Now: video less than 3000 ticks before `E_n` is dropped (the unit's IDR stays); a source whose audio would end less than 3000 ticks after its next IDR ends in the unit before (up to 3000 + 1919 ticks over 2 s; reads up to ~3 audio frames ahead); a source with less than 3000 ticks of audio gives no unit (was: none at all). Tests passing: `LiveTsApiTest` (9), `LiveTsTargetsTest` (2), `MainRouterTest`. **Blocker:** when a client stalls (connection open, not reading) the writer thread blocks in `HTTPResponse.write`; the queue overflows and the packager drops it, but nothing can end the connection, so the handler thread and the lease (which keeps the output running) are held until TCP gives up. `HTTPResponse.close()` from another thread flushes first (`finishBody`/`lingeringClose`) and so can't unblock it. Confirmed by `LiveTsApiTest.stalledClientIsDisconnectedWhenItsQueueOverflows` (fails today; `@Disabled`). Reported to the `SiggiHTTP` session with `SendMessage`: requested a never-blocking `abort()` callable from any thread that closes the socket at once, makes the blocked write throw and logs nothing. **To finish:** once the new 1.11-SNAPSHOT is installed, implement `LiveTsApi.abortFor` with it (the queue's `onClose` already calls it), remove the `@Disabled`, run `LiveTsApiTest`, tick.
  - **Done 2026-11-10 at main `4f153b0`:** SiggiHTTP 1.11-SNAPSHOT added `HTTPResponse.abort()` (upstream `7fa8d70`: callable from any thread, never blocks, resets the socket with SO_LINGER 0, the blocked write throws, nothing logged). `LiveTsApi.abortFor` calls it: a connection whose 4-unit queue overflows is reset at once; when the output stops, a connection is reset only if a write is in progress, otherwise the body ends cleanly (a reset can make the client discard received-but-unread bytes, which broke `bodyEndsWhenTheOutputStops`). `LiveTsApiTest.stalledClientIsDisconnectedWhenItsQueueOverflows` is enabled; on macOS loopback the client sometimes never saw the reset, so the test writes a byte on each quiet read to provoke the kernel's reset (passed 6/6 for the agent, 3/3 again after). `DtoIpLeakTest` classifies `output.LiveTsApi$Target` as internal. Tests: `mvn -DskipITs verify` (1050 unit tests, 0 failures); `LiveTsApiTest` + `DtoIpLeakTest` 3 more times. Finished by the main session after the step agent stopped before committing.
- [x] HLS segmenter: ring of 8, playlist of 6, real `EXTINF`, correct headers.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `3c8d9e5`. `output/HlsSegmenter` (one per room, a `UnitSink` on the packager) and `output/LiveHlsApi` (`GET /live/{token}/index.m3u8`, `GET /live/{token}/seg/{seq}.ts`, HEAD like GET without body; `targetsFrom(db, settings)` = enabled `hls` row in `stream_outputs` behind `outputs.hls.enabled`, else `404`; wired in `Main` next to `LiveTsApi`). **Decisions:** (1) **real `EXTINF`** = next unit's `startPts` − this unit's `startPts` (exact also after a switch, where the next unit starts at `audioEndPts`), so a unit becomes a segment when its successor arrives; units are emitted LEAD (1 s) early, so a segment is listed about 1 s after its real-time start, never ahead of the live edge. (2) **Media sequence numbers belong to the segmenter**, not `TsUnit.sequence`, and continue across packager restarts (a polling player never sees them go backwards or a URL reused); the first segment after a restart (new output clock) or a delivery gap carries `#EXT-X-DISCONTINUITY`, and `#EXT-X-DISCONTINUITY-SEQUENCE` appears once such a segment has left the playlist. When the packager stops the ring is emptied. (3) `EXT-X-TARGETDURATION` 2, never decreasing (it would only grow if a segment rounded above 2 s, which the unit rules don't allow; max is 2.055 s). (4) The ring follows a room's packager only from the first HLS fetch on (attach also primes with `Packager.latest()`, deduplicated by unit sequence) and is re-attached on a packager restart (`ProgramOutputs.StartListener`) only if HLS was fetched in the last 60 s, so `.ts`/RTMP-only rooms hold no ring. (5) Each playlist/segment fetch is `ProgramOutputs.touch` (activity, starts the output); a fresh output's first playlist request waits up to 5 s for the first segment, else `503` + `Retry-After: 1`. (6) Headers: playlist `application/vnd.apple.mpegurl`, `Cache-Control: no-cache`; segments `video/mp2t`, `Content-Length`, `Cache-Control: no-cache` (numbers restart with the server, so a segment URL isn't forever cacheable), `Range` ignored; expired/future segment `404`. No CORS header (VRChat doesn't need it; add if a browser HLS player on another origin should work). Per-room segmenter state (a few hundred bytes once the ring is empty) stays for the process lifetime. Tests: `HlsSegmenterTest` (format, ring 8 / list 6, EXTINF incl. 2.021/0.500/2.055, target duration, duplicates/gaps, restart discontinuity + discontinuity sequence, stop empties the ring, real packager attach), `LiveHlsApiTest` (raw sockets: headers, Content-Length, ring bounds via HTTP, HEAD, 404s for bad paths/tokens, 500/503, first-segment wait, restart continues the sequence, re-attach only while HLS is fetched, tokens never logged), `LiveTsTargetsTest` (+ HLS targets), `LiveTsApiTest`, `MainRouterTest`, `MainTest`; `mvn package -DskipTests`.
- [x] RTMP pusher: supervised ffmpeg (`-c copy -f flv`), backoff 1/2/5/10/30 s, status with last stderr line.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `8da3997`. `output/RtmpPusher` (one per room push, supervisor on a virtual thread `rtmp-{roomId}`) and `output/RtmpPushers` (server registry: `start(roomId, rtmpUrl, key)` replaces an existing push, `stop`, `status`, `statusListener`, `close`; constructed in `Main` when ffmpeg is configured and closed on shutdown before the program outputs; nothing starts a push yet, that is the outputs API step's job, which also checks switches and decrypts the key). **As built:** command `ffmpeg -hide_banner -nostats -loglevel warning -progress pipe:1 -analyzeduration 1000000 -f mpegts -i pipe:0 -c copy -f flv -flvflags no_duration_filesize <rtmpUrl>/<key>` (trailing slashes of the URL dropped; no key → URL alone). `-analyzeduration 1 s` so ffmpeg opens the output after the first unit instead of probing ~5 s of real-time input; `no_duration_filesize` silences the end-of-stream header warnings. Each attempt: `ProgramOutputs.acquire` lease + `subscribe(join=latest)` 4-unit queue (the newest unit, starting with an IDR, goes out at once), units written to ffmpeg's stdin; the push pins the output (`keepRunning(roomId, true)`), `stop` unpins. **Status** `Status(state, detail, failures, sinceMs, retryAtMs)`, states `CONNECTING` → `CONNECTED` (first `progress=` line from `-progress`, which ffmpeg only writes once the RTMP output is open) → `RETRYING` / `ERROR` (a retry is pending at `retryAtMs`; `ERROR` once the backoff reached 30 s, i.e. 5 failures in a row, or when ffmpeg or the program output can't be started at all; still retried) → `STOPPED`. `detail` = the last non-empty stderr line (max 300 chars), else "The program output restarted" (packager stopped, ffmpeg finished cleanly), "The RTMP connection fell behind the program output" (queue overflow), or "ffmpeg exited with code N". **Backoff** 1, 2, 5, 10, 30, 30, … s; an attempt that stayed connected ≥ 60 s (`STABLE_MS`) starts it over. **Ending an attempt:** ffmpeg exiting by itself leaves the queue (wakes the writer); queue overflow (ffmpeg not taking the stream, e.g. a stalled connection) kills ffmpeg at once, as does a packager stop while a write is pending; otherwise stdin is closed and ffmpeg gets 5 s to finish before it is killed. `close()` closes stdin, interrupts the supervisor (also during a backoff wait), kills ffmpeg if it doesn't finish, waits for the supervisor. **Invariant 6:** the key is masked (`…` + last 4, just `…` under 8 chars) in every stderr line, status detail and log line; it is on ffmpeg's command line (no other way to give it to ffmpeg), so local users who can list processes can see it. Also classified the HLS step's `HlsSegmenter$Segment` record in `DtoIpLeakTest` (the HLS step didn't run it; it failed on that alone), and `RtmpPusher$Status` as internal (the outputs API decides what reaches clients). Tests run: `RtmpPusherTest` (11: target/command, key masking, backoff sequence with RETRYING ×4 then ERROR and the masked last stderr line in status and logs, stdin gets exactly the units and progress means connected, packager stop → clean end and retry after 1 s, stable connection resets the backoff, short one doesn't, exit code as detail, never-reading ffmpeg killed on overflow, close while connected (process gone) and during a backoff wait, launch failure and output start failure → ERROR; real ffmpeg: "Connection refused" without the key, then a push to an ffmpeg `-listen 1` RTMP server whose FLV has h264 + aac and ~6 s), `RtmpPushersTest` (2: pin + one lease per push, replace, stop unpins so the sweep stops it 60 s later, close), `DtoIpLeakTest`, `ProgramOutputsTest`, `MainTest`, `MainRouterTest`; the two RTMP classes 3 more times; `mvn package -DskipTests`.

- [x] *(manual)* RTMP to VRCDN (or local nginx-rtmp) plays in VRChat and recovers from network cuts.
  - Agent part done 2026-11-10 at main `7bd10c7` (no code change). **Checked locally** (jar on a spare port, scratch SQLite data dir, `tiangolo/nginx-rtmp` in Docker on port 19350, a room with an RTMP output `rtmp://127.0.0.1:…/live` + key, a 4-minute 720p test clip (~3.3 Mbit/s) uploaded and queued over the WebSocket): `RtmpPusherTest`, `RtmpPushersTest`, `StreamOutputsApiTest`, `OutputStatusFeedTest` pass. Push goes `connecting` → `connected` within about 1 s; pulling `rtmp://…/live/<key>` back from nginx gives H.264 High 1280×720 30/1 + AAC LC 48 kHz stereo and decodes cleanly (`ffmpeg -v error … -f null -`, also after each cut below). **Network cuts** (pushed through a small local TCP relay so the link could be broken): (1) nginx restarted (connection reset): `retrying` with "Error closing file: Broken pipe", reconnected after the 1 s backoff. (2) Blackhole for 50 s (relay stops moving bytes, connections stay open, i.e. packets dropped): detected after about 15 s as "The RTMP connection fell behind the program output" (queue overflow → ffmpeg killed); reconnect attempts during the blackhole hang in the RTMP handshake and are killed the same way (backoff 1, 2, 5, 10 s); 11 s after the link came back it was `connected` again (the pending 10 s backoff). (3) Relay killed for 20 s (connection refused): because the previous connection had lasted under 60 s, the failure count went on from 4 → 5 = `error` with a 30 s wait, reconnected 30 s after the drop. So recovery after a cut takes up to 30 s (by design: backoff 1/2/5/10/30 s, reset only after 60 s connected). Neither the RTMP key nor the bearer token appeared in the server log. Docker Desktop's port forwarding buffers a lot: `docker pause` of the nginx container is *not* a usable blackhole test with low-bitrate filler (the push stays `connected`), hence the relay. Each reconnect is a new RTMP publish session (FLV timestamps start over, as with any publisher reconnect).
  - **Still to check by hand (Siggi):** (a) In a room, Outputs panel: add RTMP with the VRCDN ingest URL and key (or a local nginx-rtmp reachable from the VRChat machine, e.g. `docker run -d --rm --name tt-rtmp -p 1935:1935 tiangolo/nginx-rtmp`, ingest `rtmp://<host>/live`, key anything, viewer URL `rtmp://<host>/live/<key>`), set the room's RTMP viewer URL (for VRCDN its player URL), queue something. (b) In VRChat (PC, AVPro player; Quest too if handy) open the viewer URL: picture and sound play, cards show when paused/idle. (c) Cut the network of the machine running TogetherTunes (Wi-Fi off or cable out) for about 30 s, then restore: the panel shows `retrying`/`error` with ffmpeg's line, then `connected` within about 30 s of the network coming back; in VRChat the player resumes (it may need a reload of the URL; note whether it recovers by itself on VRCDN). Moderators see no key line and no target URL line at all. The edit form keeps its password field; when editing, prefill nothing (empty still keeps the key). Fix the form's help text ("stored encrypted and never shown again" is no longer true). The key field's placeholder says "Stream key (if needed)" instead of "(optional)" (Siggi: it isn't always optional).

- [x] Keep the source of every video item (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "Perhaps we should keep the original so that files can be re-encoded easily without generational loss."). Today a video upload's file is deleted after ingest and a YouTube download is thrown away (docs/library-and-ingest.md). Change: keep the source of **every** item as `media/<itemId>/original.<ext>`: video uploads (as audio uploads already are) and YouTube downloads (the merged `src.mkv`, so a re-encode doesn't depend on YouTube still having the video). Same extension rules as audio originals (from the container ffprobe finds). The audio-original rules for the web player (`original_web`, `original_gain_db`) stay audio-only: a video original is never served to the web player; it only feeds re-encodes. Re-encodes (profile change, the bitrate and preset steps below) use the original when there is one; items made before this step without one keep today's fallback (YouTube refetch, else the old mezzanine). Retention and purge delete the original with the rest of `media/<itemId>/`; a purged YouTube item refetches as now. Dedupe for uploads already hashes the original. Storage roughly doubles for video items: say so in the docs (and in the admin storage view if there is one). Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (Originals section becomes all items; the step "4. ... deleted once ingest has finished" and the re-encode sources), `docs/data-model.md` if columns change. Tests: the ingest ITs that cover uploads and YouTube (fake yt-dlp) keep the original; a re-encode uses it.

Extension rules as for audio, but for a video item MP4/MOV → `mp4` (or `mov`/`m4v` from the name) and Matroska → `mkv` (or `webm`), so a YouTube download is `original.mkv` (`original.webm` if yt-dlp left a WebM). No schema change (`original_file`/`original_codec` now set for every item). Re-encode source order: original if the file exists → YouTube refetch (older items; the new download is then kept) → previous encode (clears `original_*`). `MediaApi` serves `/media/{id}/original` only for audio uploads (a video/YouTube original is `404 file_not_found`); `RoomViews` was already audio-upload-only.
