- [x] Constant bitrate video, configurable (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "It should be constant bitrate (preferably configurable), and card view must also be at that constant bitrate. This may mean you may somehow have to pad junk data if the cards are at a lower bitrate."). Every x264 encode that feeds the packager (mezzanines, cards incl. the reference card and filler cards, and later M5's overlay re-encodes) becomes CBR: `-b:v R -minrate R -maxrate R -bufsize <B>` with `-x264-params nal-hrd=cbr` (keep `open-gop=0`). x264 then pads with **H.264 filler data NAL units (type 12)**, which is the standard way to pad: checked by Siggi's session on 2026-11-10 with ffmpeg 6.1.1: a 4 s still 1280×720 card at 3 Mb/s came out 1.46 MB, 1.45 MB of it filler NALs, and `-c copy` from MPEG-TS to FLV (what the RTMP pusher does) keeps them, so RTMP stays CBR. The packager must pass filler NALs through unchanged (they're the padding; check the AVCC→Annex B path, the indexer and anything that inspects NAL types), and pacing stays by timestamp. Audio stays AAC 192 kb/s.
  - **Config:** `output.videoBitrateKbps` (server config / admin settings like `output.profile`), default 3000 at 720p30 and 6000 at 1080p30 when unset (today's caps). Pick `bufsize` (today 2× the rate) after a quick check that the CBR output is steady per GOP; note the choice. `nal-hrd=cbr` puts the bitrate into the SPS (VUI HRD parameters), so the bitrate is part of the avcC: the reference card is per profile **and** bitrate, and changing the bitrate re-encodes every item, exactly like a profile change (from originals, previous step). The index's profile code/check must cover the bitrate too, so the packager never splices an item encoded at another bitrate (treated as not ready, as for a profile change). Filler cards and room cards re-encode via their fingerprints.
  - Invariant 1 in `CLAUDE.md` (on `plan`) gains: all encodes are CBR at the configured bitrate. Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (profile table: rate control row, reference command, cards: their size is now bitrate × 2 s, ~750 KB at 3 Mb/s), `docs/program-output.md`, `docs/overlay.md` (overlay re-encodes use the same CBR arguments), config docs. Tests: an encode is CBR (bytes per 2 s GOP within a small tolerance of R×2 s/8, including a card), filler NALs reach the `.ts` output, a bitrate change triggers re-encodes; the affected ingest ITs.
  - Done 2026-11-10 (main `6a72dce`). `EncodeProfile.videoArgs(OutputEncoding)`: `-b:v/-minrate/-maxrate R`, `-bufsize R/2`, `-x264-params open-gop=0:nal-hrd=cbr:vbv-init=1`, no CRF. **Bufsize choice** (measured with ffmpeg 6.1.1, 720p30 at 3 Mb/s, bytes per 2 s GOP vs 750 000): 0.5 s with `vbv-init=1` (buffer starts full) gave a still card +0.1 %, `testsrc2` ±0.01 %, a noisy fractal source −1…+2 %; without `vbv-init=1` a card was −2.4 %; 1 s spread −2…+6 % (noise) / −5…+10 % (`testsrc2`); 2 s −10…+18 %. `vbv-init` doesn't change the avcC; the bufsize and rate do. **Encoding identity:** new `config/OutputEncoding` (profile + `videoBitrateKbps`, id like `720p30-3000k`) replaces `OutputProfile` wherever encodes are made or checked: `items.output_profile` stores the id (no schema change; pre-CBR rows hold the bare profile name, so every existing item gets one startup re-encode to CBR), cards live in `cards/<encoding>/` (new reference card per bitrate; old directories are left alone), filler-card fingerprints include the args, `index.bin` is version 2 with a `video bitrate u32` after the profile code (version 1 is rejected → not ready until re-encoded), and `ProgramMedia` refuses an index of another encoding. Config `output.videoBitrateKbps` / `TOGETHERTUNES_OUTPUT_VIDEO_BITRATE_KBPS`, default 3000 (720p30) / 6000 (1080p30), range 500–25000 (level 4.0 High MaxBR); config only, like `output.profile` (there is no admin setting for the profile either); `config.example.json` shows 3000. Packager: `AnnexB` already passes every NAL but in-band AUD/SPS/PPS, so filler NALs (type 12) go through unchanged; nothing else inspects NAL types. Docs: library-and-ingest (rate control row, why CBR, buffer measurements, reference command, card size ~750 KB at 3 Mb/s, `cards/<encoding>`, index format v2, re-encodes on bitrate change), architecture (config key, cards dir), program-output (filler NALs pass through, RTMP copy keeps them), overlay (args are CBR), data-model, README; `CLAUDE.md` invariant 1. Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `ConfigLoaderTest` (key, env, default per profile, range), `EncodeProfileTest` (args; a real moving encode and a card each within 3 % of R×2 s/8 per GOP, card full of filler NALs; another bitrate gives another avcC), `SampleIndexTest` (v2 layout, bitrate round trip, v1 rejected, bad bitrate), `ReferenceCardTest` (per-bitrate references), `CardEncoderTest` (card size = CBR), `RealTsMuxerTest` (every filler NAL of the samples reaches the `.ts`, count and bytes), `IngestPipelineTest` (new `reencodeForANewBitrate`: 720p30 3000k → 2000k re-encodes from the original, new avcC, 500 000 B per GOP ±3 %), `IngestJobsContract` via `SqliteIngestJobsTest` (another bitrate and pre-CBR names queue re-encodes), `FillerCardsTest, IndexBuilderTest, IngestStepsTest, AnnexBTest, Mp4BoxesTest, ProgramMediaTest, RealSourcesTest, SourcesTest, OfflineHarnessTest, PackagerGoldenFileTest, CardRendererTest, MainTest, WebBuildTest` and the Sqlite library/upload/YouTube/settings tests; ITs `PostgresIngestJobsIT, PostgresUploadsIT, PostgresYoutubeImportsIT, PostgresGuestUploadsIT, PostgresLibraryIT, PostgresPlaylistsIT, PostgresSharesIT, PostgresUserLibrariesIT, PostgresSettingsIT`. Not checked here: a real RTMP push staying CBR end to end (Siggi's session already confirmed `-c copy` to FLV keeps filler NALs); Siggi's server on 8080 will re-encode its whole library on its next restart (one-off, from originals where kept).
- [x] Admin-selectable x264 presets: quick encode first, quality re-encode for library items (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "Allow the server admin to choose the preset ... two options -- one for quick encoding, to get a recently requested item to play as quickly as possible, and then another for slower encoding to get better quality, which can start if the room owner (or if someone else on the same server happens to paste the same YouTube link) decides they want to add the item to their library.").
  - **Settings:** `ingest.quickPreset` (default `veryfast`) and `ingest.qualityPreset` (default `slow`), in the config and the admin settings page. Allowed: `veryfast`, `faster`, `fast`, `medium`, `slow`, `slower`, `veryslow` (not `ultrafast`/`superfast`: they turn off CABAC/8×8 transform, which changes the PPS).
  - **Keeping one avcC across presets** (invariant 1: the packager splices without re-encoding, so SPS/PPS must stay byte-identical). Measured by Siggi's session on 2026-11-10 (ffmpeg 6.1.1, 720p CBR 3 Mb/s, `-bf 0 -g 60 -profile:v high -level:v 4.0`): presets differ in the SPS by `num_ref_frames` and in the PPS by `chroma_qp_index_offset` (x264 lowers it by 2 when psy-rd is active, i.e. subme ≥ 6: preset `fast` and slower). With `ref=4` pinned and `chroma-qp-offset=-2` for veryfast/faster but `chroma-qp-offset=0` for fast…veryslow, **all seven presets gave byte-identical SPS+PPS**. So: the shared argument constant stays the single source of every encode's arguments, plus a per-preset table for the preset name and its chroma offset; `ref=4` is part of the constant (it also fits level 4.1's DPB at 1080p). Don't hard-trust the table: at startup, encode the reference card's GOP with each **configured** preset and compare the avcC with the reference; if a preset doesn't match (e.g. a different x264 build), log it clearly and fall back to the quick preset for that tier (or refuse that admin setting with a clear error), never ingest with a mismatching avcC. The per-encode avcC check stays.
  - **When each runs:** an item is first encoded with the quick preset if any room has it queued, current or suggested when its job starts (a request, a paste into a room); otherwise (e.g. an upload or paste straight into a library) it goes straight to the quality preset. A quick-encoded item gets a **quality re-encode job** as soon as it's in any library (added by the room owner, by anyone pasting the same YouTube link into their library, by an upload dedupe, …), from its original (step above). Record per item which preset its files were made with (`items.encode_preset` or similar, migration for both databases), so nothing re-encodes twice and a re-encode is skipped when the presets are equal. Quality jobs are low priority: they only run when no quick/normal ingest job is waiting, and a new request never waits behind them. Changing a preset setting doesn't re-encode existing items automatically.
  - **Swap while playing:** a quality re-encode publishes like today's re-encode (item stays `ready` with its old files until the swap). An item playing in a room, on the web or in the program output during the swap must not glitch: say how (e.g. the packager keeps reading the open old files until that play ends; the web player keeps its URL). Test it.
  - Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (profile table "x264 preset", the "Why the preset is part of the profile" paragraph, the measurement above, jobs and priorities), `docs/data-model.md`, `docs/api.md` (admin settings), `CLAUDE.md` invariant 1 wording (one shared argument constant **plus** the preset table, avcC still byte-identical). Tests: the avcC of a quick and a quality encode match; the job choice (in a room → quick then quality once in a library; library-only → quality directly; equal presets → no second job); the priority; the startup preset check (a forced mismatch falls back); the affected ingest ITs.
  - Done 2026-11-10 (main `49bd2b2`). **Presets:** `config/X264Preset` (the seven presets with their `chroma-qp-offset`), `EncodeProfile.videoArgs(encoding, preset)` adds `ref=4:chroma-qp-offset=N` to `-x264-params`; cards and the reference card always use veryfast. Re-measured here (ffmpeg 6.1.1, 720p30 and 1080p30, black still and `testsrc2`): all seven presets byte-identical SPS/PPS. Pinning `ref=4` changes every avcC, so `OutputEncoding.id()` gained `-v<ARGS_VERSION>` (`720p30-3000k-v2`): **every existing item re-encodes once at the next start** (library items with the quality preset, so this costs real CPU on Siggi's server), new `cards/720p30-3000k-v2/`. Config `ingest.quickPreset`/`ingest.qualityPreset` (env `TOGETHERTUNES_INGEST_QUICK_PRESET`/`..._QUALITY_PRESET`) are the defaults of admin settings of the same names (like `ingest.maxDurationSeconds`); the admin page renders them as enums automatically. **Check:** `ingest/EncodePresets` encodes the reference black card with each configured preset at startup (`ReferenceCard.presetMatches`, cached); a mismatch is logged and falls back (quick → veryfast, quality → quick); `AdminApi.settingCheck` refuses such a preset with `400 invalid_setting`. **Tiers:** migration 0008 adds `items.encode_preset`, `items.encode_quality` and `ingest_jobs.tier`. Deviation from the plan's wording: "in a room when the job starts" can't be seen reliably, because RoomQueue writes the queue entry only after the import/upload has queued (and a worker may already have started) the job; so the caller records the tier at enqueue time (`library ? quality : quick` in Uploads/YoutubeImports; a room request, guest or playlist is quick) and the job still switches to quick if a room has the item queued/suggested/playing when it starts. `reencode` uses quality for a library item no room wants, else quick. `encode_quality` = made with the quality preset of the time (audio items always 1), so a preset change never re-queues quality encodes. **Quality jobs** (`Kind.QUALITY`, doesn't own the item status): `IngestJobs.enqueueQualityIfNeeded` for a ready video item in a library with `encode_quality = 0` and another preset, called when any job finishes (same transaction), from a new `LibraryAddListener` on Library/Shares/Uploads/YoutubeImports (inside the insert's transaction), and at startup (`enqueueQualityReencodes`, also retries failed ones). A quality job re-encodes from the original (YouTube without one downloads; with neither it just marks the item, since re-encoding the quick encode only loses quality); presets already equal → just marks. **Priority:** claim order new-item jobs → `reencode` → `quality`; `IngestQueue` makes a running `reencode`/`quality` give way (interrupt + release without counting the attempt; it restarts from scratch later) when a new-item job is queued and all workers are busy (checked on every enqueue via a new `IngestJobs.enqueueListener` → `wake()`), and a worker that just claimed a re-encode while a new-item job waits puts it back. (The plan only asked this of quality jobs; profile re-encodes give way too, since they now may run the slow preset.) **Swap while playing:** `reencode`/`quality` jobs wait before the publish while the item is current or next in any room (`RoomRegistry.playingItems()`, poll 5 s, at most 1 h, then publish anyway), so web players' range requests never mix old and new `mezz.mp4` bytes under one URL; the packager is safe regardless (it reads the files it opened; rename keeps them readable; same avcC). An item played from the library page outside a room can still see the swap. Also fixed: `DtoIpLeakTest` failed since the CBR step (`OutputEncoding` unclassified); now INTERNAL. Docs: library-and-ingest (profile table, "Why the preset…", new Presets section with the measurement, tiers, priorities, swap; reference command; Re-encodes), data-model, architecture (config keys, cards dir), auth (settings), api (admin settings 400), README, overlay; `CLAUDE.md` invariant 1. Tests run: `mvn package` (all 1130 unit tests pass; incl. new `EncodePresetsTest`, `EncodeProfileTest` (`presetsChangeOnlyThePresetAndTheChromaOffset`, `everyPresetSharesTheExtradata` for both profiles), `ReferenceCardTest.presetsMatchTheReference` (real check + forced mismatch fallback), `IngestJobsContract` via `SqliteIngestJobsTest` (tiers, quality follow-up rules, startup sweep, priority, enqueue listener), `IngestQueueTest` (give-way), `IngestPipelineTest` (`quickForRoomsThenQualityForLibraries`, `equalPresetsNeedNoQualityJob`, `aQualitySwapWaitsWhileTheItemIsPlaying` incl. an open handle reading the old bytes after the swap, `qualityWithoutAnOriginalKeepsTheQuickEncode`), `AdminApiTest`, `ConfigLoaderTest`, `SettingsContract`, Library/Shares/Uploads/YoutubeImports contracts); ITs `PostgresIngestJobsIT, PostgresMigrationIT, PostgresInitialSchemaIT, PostgresSettingsIT, PostgresUploadsIT, PostgresYoutubeImportsIT, PostgresLibraryIT, PostgresSharesIT, PostgresGuestUploadsIT, PostgresUserLibrariesIT, PostgresItemsIT, PostgresRetentionSweeperIT`. Smoke start of the jar with `TOGETHERTUNES_INGEST_QUALITY_PRESET=veryslow` on a fresh data dir: migration 0008 applied, preset check passed, no temp files left, server stopped.
- [x] Stop the RTMP push when the room has been idle too long (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "if the queue is empty for more than a few minutes (10 by default, make it configurable by room owner, and a maximum time set by admin), it should stop the RTMP push, and only start again when the room owner turns it back on."). "Idle" = nothing playing and nothing queued, i.e. the program output shows the idle ("Queue is empty") card; paused with an item doesn't count. Once the room has been idle continuously for the limit, the server turns the room's RTMP output **off** (the same as the owner pressing Turn off: `enabled = false`, the push stops, the output stays configured with its URL and key), records why, and it stays off until the owner turns it on again (a new request or play doesn't restart it). Any activity (an item starts playing or is queued) resets the timer.
  - **Settings:** room setting `outputs.rtmp.idleStopMinutes` (default 10, owner-editable in Manage room → Settings → Outputs; minimum 1); server setting `outputs.rtmp.maxIdleStopMinutes` (admin settings page; default 60): the effective limit is `min(room setting, admin max)`, and the room settings form rejects or clamps values above the max (say which). Validation like the other room settings (docs/rooms.md table).
  - **What people see:** the RTMP output's status/API carries the reason (e.g. `stoppedReason: "idle"` with the time), and the room page's outputs panel tells the owner "Stopped after N minutes with an empty queue. Turn on to start again." (moderators see that it's off, as for any off output). An audit-log entry like the owner's turn-off, attributed to the system. Only RTMP: `.ts` and HLS are pulled by viewers and aren't affected.
  - Docs: `docs/rooms.md` (settings table, outputs), `docs/program-output.md` (RTMP), `docs/api.md`, admin settings. Tests: idle for the limit → output off and push stopped (use a fake clock/short limits, no real ffmpeg needed if the existing push tests avoid it); activity resets the timer; paused doesn't count; the admin max clamps the room setting; turning it on again works and starts a fresh timer.
  - Done 2026-11-10 (main `92d4b8b` + `a1aa8b5`). `output/RtmpIdleStop`: idle state per room from `ProgramOutputs` (new `IdleListener`, called on the room executor after every committed change and every snapshot read; idle = timeline `IDLE` AND no `queued` entry, so paused isn't idle and suggestions don't count), push start/stop from `StreamOutputControl.reconcileRtmp` (a fresh timer only when a push starts, not when only its URL/key changed); timer start = max(went idle, push started). `StreamOutputControl.checkIdle()` runs on its 10 s timer before each reconcile and calls `roomChanged(room, false)` for each room turned off (push stops; the program output stops if no other output is active). `StreamOutputs.stopIdle` sets `enabled = 0` plus `stopped_reason = 'idle'`, `stopped_at`, `stopped_idle_minutes` (migration `0009_rtmp_idle_stop`, sqlite + postgres) and audits `room.output_updated` with no actor/IP and `reason`/`idleMinutes`; any later change of `enabled` clears them. Settings: room `outputs.rtmp.idleStopMinutes` (default 10, 1..1440), server `outputs.rtmp.maxIdleStopMinutes` (default 60, 1..1440, admin settings page via `Settings.KEYS`). **Decision: clamps, not rejects:** a room value above the server max is saved and acts as the max (`RtmpIdleStop.effectiveMinutes`), also covering an admin lowering the max later; `GET …/server-switches` now has `outputs.rtmp.maxIdleStopMinutes` and Manage room shows "This server stops idle pushes after at most N minutes[, so N applies.]" next to the field. API: `stopped?: {reason, at, idleMinutes}` on the RTMP output, owner only; the owner's panel shows "Stopped after N minutes with an empty queue. Turn on to start again.", moderators "Off". Docs: program-output.md (new "Idle stop" section, RTMP + lifecycle pointers), rooms.md (settings table, outputs), api.md (outputs, server-switches), auth.md (server settings), data-model.md (columns). Tests run: `StreamOutputsApiTest` (+4: idle for the limit → off, push and program output stopped, URL/key kept, `stopped` for the owner only, system audit without URL/key, activity after the stop doesn't restart it, turn on → fresh timer and stops again; activity resets the timer; admin max clamps the room setting; `.ts`/HLS untouched), `ProgramOutputsRoomsTest` (+1: queued-but-not-playing, playing, paused → not idle; natural end with empty queue → idle), `RoomSettingsTest`, `RoomsApiTest`, `SqliteSettingsTest` + `PostgresSettingsIT`, `OutputsJsTest` + `RoomManageJsTest` (Node tests for the panel line and cap note), `DtoIpLeakTest` (`StreamOutputs$Stopped` internal), `SqliteInitialSchemaTest`/`SqliteMigrationTest` + `PostgresInitialSchemaIT`/`PostgresMigrationIT`/`SchemaEquivalenceIT`, `ProgramOutputsTest, OutputStatusFeedTest, MainTest, MainRouterTest, WebBuildTest, LiveTsApiTest, LiveHlsApiTest, RoomSocketTest, RtmpPushersTest, PermissionMatrixTest, RoomsPageJsTest, RemoteJsTest, AdminApiTest`; `mvn package -DskipTests`. Not checked in a real browser (panel text, Manage room note).
- [x] Minimum free disk space (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "we should have an admin configurable free space minimum. If free disk space drops below the minimum, all uploads are blocked."). Server setting `storage.minFreeBytes` (admin settings page, entered in GiB there; default 5 GiB; 0 disables the check), measured on the file system of `data.dir` (`FileStore.getUsableSpace()`, checked fresh on each decision; a short cache is fine).
  - **Blocked while below the minimum:** starting an upload (`POST /api/uploads`): refused when free space minus the declared upload size would fall below the minimum; upload chunks of an upload already started: refused once free space is below the minimum (the client shows the error; the partial upload is cleaned up as uploads already are). Also new **YouTube fetches** (they write media just the same; Siggi said "uploads", this reading includes every way of adding new media: note it so Siggi can narrow it). Error: `507 insufficient_storage` with a message for the user ("The server is low on disk space; adding media is paused."); the web UI shows it where uploads and YouTube links are added (library, room).
  - **Not blocked:** playback, rooms, the program output, deleting/purging. Ingest jobs already queued and re-encodes (profile/bitrate changes, the quality re-encodes from the presets step) **wait** while below the minimum instead of failing, and continue when space is free again (retention/purge or the admin freeing space); a job that runs out of space mid-encode fails cleanly as today.
  - **Admin view:** the admin page shows free space on the data volume, the minimum, and a warning while below it; log a warning once when crossing below (and an info when back above).
  - Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md`, `docs/api.md` (uploads, YouTube, admin settings, the 507), `docs/architecture.md` if it lists settings. Tests: a fake free-space source (no real disk filling): an upload start below/above the threshold, the size-aware check, chunk refusal, a YouTube request refused, a queued job waits and resumes, 0 disables.
  - Done 2026-11-10 (main `6dc1f61`). New `storage/DiskSpace`: usable/total bytes of `data.dir`'s `FileStore` (2 s cache), the minimum read from the settings on each decision (a DB error keeps the last value), `requireRoomFor(bytes)` → `507 insufficient_storage` "The server is low on disk space; adding media is paused." (new 507 reason phrase in `Response`); warning logged once when crossing below, info when back above; space that can't be measured blocks nothing (logged once). Setting `storage.minFreeBytes` is a new `bytes` setting type (Long, 0..1 PiB, API type `bytes`, group `storage`); the admin page shows and takes it in GiB (unchanged text keeps the exact stored bytes). **Uploads:** `Uploads.validate` (start, also the guest path before an anonymous visitor becomes a guest) checks free − declared size ≥ minimum; `writeChunk` refuses once below the minimum (bytes kept in the part file; the 24 h sweep cleans up as before; `complete` isn't blocked). **YouTube:** `YoutubeImports` refuses before yt-dlp `-J` for a new video and before queuing a purged refetch / failed retry, so it covers `POST /api/imports/youtube`, room `queue.add` and library/playlist adds of purged items; ready/pending/processing items are still returned. **Decision for Siggi to confirm:** "uploads" was read as every way of adding new media, YouTube fetches included (narrow it to uploads if not wanted). **Ingest waits:** `IngestQueue.pauseWhile(diskSpace::low)`: workers don't claim while low (jobs stay `queued`, no attempt counted; re-encode give-way is skipped while paused), re-checked at every wake and 10 s poll; running jobs carry on. **Admin view:** `GET /api/admin/storage` (`admin.settings`) → `{freeBytes?, totalBytes?, minFreeBytes, low}`; the settings tab has a Storage box with free/total, the minimum and a warning while low (`web/src/storage.ts`, DOM-free helpers). **Web upload client:** 507 isn't retried (stops with the server's message) and the upload stays resumable (Library shows Retry); the room page and YouTube forms already show the server/WS error message. `docs/architecture.md` lists only config keys, so no change there. Docs: library-and-ingest (new "Free disk space" section, originals storage note), api (general 507 note, uploads, chunks, YouTube, admin settings `bytes` type, `/api/admin/storage`), auth (Storage row in server settings). Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `DiskSpaceTest` (new: threshold, size-aware, 0 disables, minimum re-read, unreadable minimum, unmeasurable volume, cache, real volume), `StorageJsTest` + `storage.test.mjs` (new: GiB parse/format, storage box text, 507 not retryable), `SqliteUploadsTest` (`lowDiskSpaceRefusesNewUploadsAndChunks`: start above/below, size-aware, chunk refused then resumed, 0 disables), `SqliteYoutubeImportsTest` (`lowDiskSpaceRefusesNewFetchesButNotReadyItems`), `IngestQueueTest` (`queuedJobsWaitWhileDiskSpaceIsLowAndResumeAfterwards`, `aPauseSupplierThatThrowsDoesNotStopIngest`), `AdminApiTest` (`storageMinimumAndFreeSpace`, route permissions), `SqliteSettingsTest` (default, whole-byte values), `DtoIpLeakTest, PermissionMatrixTest, WebBuildTest, MainTest, MainRouterTest, SqliteGuestUploadsTest, RoomSocketTest, LibraryApiTest, PlaylistsApiTest, SharesApiTest, RequestBodyTest, RouterTest`; ITs `PostgresSettingsIT, PostgresUploadsIT, PostgresYoutubeImportsIT`. Smoke start of the jar on a fresh data dir (port 18093): starts, `/api/admin/storage` answers 401 unauthenticated, server stopped. Not checked in a real browser: the admin Storage box and the GiB field, and the 507 message in the library/room upload and YouTube forms.
- [x] Encode jobs page, for admins and for users' own items (added 2026-11-10, Siggi; moved here from M7's "Admin: failed jobs view + retry", which it replaces and widens). Siggi: "Also allow non-admins to see encodes for items that appear in their own library."
  - **Admin view** (`/admin/`, a "Jobs" tab; needs a permission like `admin.jobs`, or reuse the closest existing admin permission and say which): every ingest job that is **queued, running, or failed** (plus recently finished ones, e.g. the last 24 h, collapsed or filterable), each with the item (title, link to its item page), kind (`ingest`/upload, YouTube, `reencode`, `quality`) and tier/preset, state, progress, attempts, created/started/finished times, and the job log for failed ones. Ordered as the workers will take them (running first, then the queue in claim order from the priority step below), so the page shows what runs next. Actions: **Retry** a failed job, **Cancel** a queued job (a quality or re-encode job only drops that job; cancelling a new item's only ingest leaves the item `failed`/retryable, say which), audited. Updates live (WebSocket push if the client already has a suitable channel, else a short poll while the page is visible; say which).
  - **User view:** a page for any signed-in user (e.g. "Encodes", linked from the Library page and the nav) listing the same kind of rows, **only for items in the user's own libraries** (libraries they own; not shares from others, not other people's items; Siggi: a shared item shows up only once the user adds it to their own library, which the rule already covers), without logs or other users' details, no IP anything (invariant 5), and no actions except what the user may already do on the item (e.g. retry a failed ingest of their own upload if that exists today). Guests don't get it.
  - **API:** e.g. `GET /api/admin/jobs?state=&cursor=` (fill in the existing `docs/api.md` placeholder row) and `GET /api/jobs` (the user's), `POST /api/admin/jobs/{id}/retry`, `POST /api/admin/jobs/{id}/cancel`. Leave `/api/admin/ytdlp/update` to M7.
  - Docs: `docs/api.md`, `docs/library-and-ingest.md`, `docs/auth.md` (the permission). Tests: admin sees all, a user only their own library's items (another user's item and a shared item are absent), retry/cancel, ordering matches the claim order, guests refused.
  - Done 2026-11-10 (main `16bc182`). New permission **`admin.jobs`** (admin holds it automatically). `JobList` (lists) + `JobsApi` (`GET /api/admin/jobs?state=active|failed|done`, `GET /api/admin/jobs/{id}` with the log, `POST …/retry` → 201, `POST …/cancel`, `GET /api/jobs`), registered in `Main` beside the outputs API (not in `Main.router`, so not in the permission matrix; `JobsApiTest` covers the refusals). Queue order: one shared `IngestJobs.CLAIM_ORDER` used by `claimNext` and the list (the next step must change both); each queued row has its 1-based `position` in the whole queue, also in a user's list. **Cancel** marks the job `failed`; a new item's ingest leaves the item `failed` ("Cancelled by an administrator", retryable), a `reencode`/`quality` job is only dropped (requeued at the next start if still needed). **Retry**: same kind and tier, only the item's latest job, not for purged items. Failed list = unresolved failures (item's latest job, item not purged). Audited `job.retried` / `job.cancelled`. Live updates: **3 s poll** while visible (no suitable push channel). UI: admin Jobs tab (`/admin/#jobs`, Retry/Cancel/Log) and `/encodes/` (nav + Library link; read only); shared `web/src/jobs.ts`, text in `jobtext.ts`.
  - **Deviation (user's words win):** the user view includes items **shared with** the user while they are in their library, because Siggi's request is "items that appear in their own library" and a shared item does; this step's text said "not shares from others". To leave shares out, change `JobList.IN_LIBRARY` to add `AND le.via <> 'share'` and flip the assertions in `JobsApiTest`/`IngestJobsContract`.
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; full `mvn test` (all unit tests, incl. new `JobsApiTest`, `JobsJsTest`, `SqliteIngestJobsTest` additions, `PermissionTest`, `AdminApiTest`, `DtoIpLeakTest`, `ShellJsTest`, `MainRouterTest`, `WebBuildTest`); `PostgresIngestJobsIT`. Smoke: `/encodes/` 200, `/api/jobs` and `/api/admin/jobs` 401 anonymously. Not clicked through in a browser.
- [x] Encode jobs for queued items go first (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "Items that in a queue should also be prioritized."). Today workers take new-item jobs, then `reencode`, then `quality` (docs/library-and-ingest.md "Priorities"). Change the claim order so a job whose item a room is **waiting on** (any room's current item, its queue, or the suggestions that would play next) comes before every job for an item no room wants, of any kind: e.g. a `reencode` or `quality` job of a queued item (a re-encode makes the program output show "Loading…" for that item until it finishes) goes ahead of an unqueued upload. Within the queued group: the current item first, then by how soon it plays (position in its room's queue, the earliest across rooms); then the existing kind order. The give-way rule (a new job interrupts a running re-encode/quality job when all workers are busy) follows the same order: a queued item's job may interrupt a job for an item no room wants, never the reverse. The order is decided at claim time from the rooms' live state (not frozen at enqueue time), so queueing an item later moves its pending job forward. For a `quality` job of a queued item, a re-encode while it's playing is still safe (the swap step), so no special case. Docs: library-and-ingest "Priorities". Tests: the claim order with items queued in rooms vs not, across kinds; a later queue add moves a job forward; the give-way rule in both directions. The jobs page above shows this order.
  - Done 2026-11-10 (main `31f890b`). **Ranking source:** `LivePlayingItems` (already the rooms' current/next live set) now also implements the new `ingest.WantedItems`: each room executor publishes its whole wish list ranked by how soon it plays (current entry 0, queued entries in play order 1, 2, …, then suggestions oldest first; an item's earliest rank wins, also across rooms); the merged map is rebuilt on change and a change listener calls `IngestQueue.wake()` (wired in `Main` via `RoomRegistry.wantedItems(listener)`). **Claim order** (`IngestJobs.claimOrder`, built at every claim and used by `claimNext` and the jobs page's `JobList`, which now takes the `IngestJobs`): `want` (live rank; else 1 000 000 if the item has a `queued`/`suggested`/`playing` entry in any room in the database, covering rooms not loaded since a restart; else 1 000 001), then the old kind order, then age. At most 500 live ranks go into the query (the rest count as "in a queue"). **Give-way:** `IngestJobs.Priority(want, newItem)`; the first queued job interrupts a running `reencode`/`quality` job only if it comes strictly before it (age ignored): wanted beats unwanted (never the reverse), sooner beats later, and among equals a new-item job beats a re-encode (old rule; a `reencode` still never interrupts a `quality` job). Victim: the running re-encode that comes last (most recently started among equals). The claim-then-put-back check uses the same comparison (it used "any new-item job queued", which would loop now). New-item jobs are never interrupted (unchanged). Docs: library-and-ingest "Priorities" (rewritten), jobs page list order, Re-encodes "Order".
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `SqliteIngestJobsTest` (new `jobsOfItemsRoomsWaitOnComeFirstAcrossKinds`, `aLaterQueueAddMovesAPendingJobForward`, `aFailingWantedSourceDoesNotStopClaims`, `priorityGoesBefore`), `IngestQueueTest` (new `aQueuedItemsJobInterruptsAnUnwantedReencode` incl. a later queue add, `anUnwantedJobNeverInterruptsAQueuedItemsReencode`), `LivePlayingItemsTest` (`ranksMergeAcrossRoomsAndTellTheListener`), `SqlitePlaybackTest` (`wantedItemsRankTheWholeQueueByHowSoonItPlays`), `JobsApiTest, DtoIpLeakTest, IngestPipelineTest, SqliteRoomExecutorTest, RoomSocketTest, MainTest, MainRouterTest`; ITs `PostgresIngestJobsIT, PostgresPlaybackIT`. Smoke start of the jar (port 18094): starts, `/api/admin/jobs` 401, stopped.
- [x] More server settings in the admin panel (added 2026-11-10, Siggi): `output.profile`, `output.videoBitrateKbps`, `ingest.maxUploadBytes`, `ingest.workers`, `overlay.workers` and `overlay.cacheMaxBytes` become admin settings, the way `ingest.maxDurationSeconds` and the presets already are: the config file / env var gives the default, the admin setting overrides it (docs/auth.md server settings). Stay config-only (Siggi agreed): `http.bind`, `http.port`, `http.publicBaseUrl`, `http.trustedProxies`, `data.dir`, `db.url`, `tools.*`.
  - **Take effect without a restart:** a profile or bitrate change triggers the same re-encode of every item as a changed config at startup (new reference card, filler cards, queued `reencode` jobs; the program output switches encoding cleanly: say how running outputs handle it, e.g. items of the old encoding count as not ready, and the cards of the new one are used from the next unit). The admin page asks for confirmation first, saying how many items will re-encode. `ingest.workers` resizes the ingest pool live (shrinking lets running jobs finish); `overlay.*` are read by M5 code when it exists (store and validate them now; M5 uses them). `ingest.maxUploadBytes` applies to new uploads. Sizes show in GiB like `storage.minFreeBytes`.
  - Validation as in the config (ranges, enum values); an invalid value → `400 invalid_setting`. Audited like other setting changes. Docs: `docs/auth.md` (server settings table), `docs/architecture.md` (config keys: which are defaults for admin settings), `docs/api.md` if needed. Tests: each setting round-trips and overrides its config default; a profile/bitrate change queues re-encodes and swaps the reference/filler cards without a restart; worker resize; validation.
  - Done 2026-11-10 (main `849fd2c`). **Settings:** `output.profile` (enum), `output.videoBitrateKbps` (nullable integer 500..25000: unset = the profile's default; a configured bitrate equal to its profile's default counts as unset, so switching the profile alone gives that profile's bitrate), `ingest.workers` (1..256), `ingest.maxUploadBytes` (bytes, 1..1 PiB), `overlay.workers` (1..256), `overlay.cacheMaxBytes` (bytes, 0..1 PiB); `Settings(db, config)` takes all their defaults from the config (sizes capped at 1 PiB). New admin group `output` ("Output encoding"); new `SettingKey.optionalInteger`; on the admin page an empty integer field now resets to the default. **Live encoding switch:** new `ingest/OutputAssets` holds the current encoding with its reference and filler cards; `follow(settings::outputEncoding)` (run on a virtual thread from the admin settings listener, one switch at a time, the latest setting wins) makes the new cards first while the old stay in use, then swaps; if the reference card can't be made the old encoding stays (logged). Listener (`Main.encodingChanged`): `EncodePresets.forget()` (presets re-checked against the new reference), `enqueueReencodes(new)`, wake, `ProgramOutputs.restartRunning` (**decision:** running outputs restart on the new encoding at once instead of mixing encodings in one stream, which invariants 1/3 forbid: `.ts` connections end and reconnect, the RTMP push reconnects, HLS sees a new stream; afterwards old-encoding items show Loading until re-encoded). `ProgramPipeline` gained an `EncodedOpener` so each start reads sources, cards and muxer avcC from the same encoding; filler-card indexes are cached per encoding. `IngestPipeline.reference(Supplier)` (read once per job), `Uploads.outputEncoding/limits`, `YoutubeImports.outputEncoding/maxDurationSeconds` read the current values. A job running during a switch finishes for the old encoding and `IngestJobs.currentEncoding` queues its `reencode` when it finishes. **Confirmation:** new `GET /api/admin/encoding?profile=&videoBitrateKbps=` (admin.settings) → `{current, encoding, reencodeItems}` (`IngestJobs.countStale`); the admin page confirms on Save and on Default for either setting, naming the item count. **Workers:** `IngestQueue.resize(n)` (slot-based: growing starts workers at once, a worker whose slot is gone stops before its next job). **Fix on the way:** `ingest.maxDurationSeconds` was an admin setting but uploads, imports and the pipeline still used the config value; they now read the setting per upload/import/job. `overlay.*` are stored and validated only (M5 reads them). Docs: auth (settings table, intro), architecture (config keys marked *admin*, which stay config-only), api (`/api/admin/encoding`), library-and-ingest (new "Changing the encoding at runtime", Re-encodes "When"), program-output (Lifecycle), README. Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `MainEncodingTest` (new; real ffmpeg: bitrate + workers changed in the settings → new reference/filler cards in `cards/720p30-2000k-v2/`, both items get `reencode` jobs, pool resized, reset switches back), `OutputAssetsTest` (new), `IngestQueueTest.resizesTheWorkerPoolLive`, `SqliteIngestJobsTest` (`aJobThatFinishesForAnOldEncodingQueuesAReencode`, countStale), `ProgramOutputsTest.restartRunningRestartsEveryRunningOutput`, `SqliteSettingsTest` (round-trip + config defaults + validation), `AdminApiTest.outputEncodingAndWorkerSettings`, `DtoIpLeakTest`, `PermissionMatrixTest`, `IngestPipelineTest`, `SqliteUploadsTest`, `SqliteYoutubeImportsTest`, `SqliteGuestUploadsTest`, `ConfigLoaderTest`, `EncodePresetsTest`, `ReferenceCardTest`, `FillerCardsTest`, `JobsApiTest`, `MainTest`, `MainRouterTest`, `ProgramMediaTest`, `RealSourcesTest`, `ProgramOutputsRoomsTest`, `LiveTsApiTest`, `LiveHlsApiTest`, `WebBuildTest`, `JobsJsTest`, `StorageJsTest`; ITs `PostgresSettingsIT`, `PostgresIngestJobsIT`. Smoke: jar on a fresh data dir, setup admin, `GET /api/admin/encoding`, PATCH bitrate 2000 + workers 3 → new cards made, "Output encoding changed … queued 0 re-encode jobs", "Ingest workers: 6 -> 3"; server stopped.
- [x] Versioned media files, so re-encodes never wait for a play to end (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "Do it", after asking why a re-encode can't replace files in use). Today a `reencode`/`quality` job that finishes while its item is current or next in a room waits (up to 1 h) to publish (`IngestPipeline.waitUntilNotPlaying`), because a web player fetching `mezz.mp4` by byte range mid-play would get bytes of the new file at the old file's offsets (decode error, freeze or jump); the program output itself is fine (it keeps its open handles). That wait deadlocked a room once (fixed for old encodings in main `bc06506`). Replace the wait with versions:
  - **Layout:** each encode publishes into its own directory, `media/<itemId>/v<N>/` (`mezz.mp4`, `audio.m4a`, `card.mp4`, `index.bin`, `cover.jpg` if per encode), with `items.media_version` (migration, both databases) pointing at the current one. The kept original stays at `media/<itemId>/original.<ext>` (not per version). Existing items: the flat layout counts as version 0 (or move it into `v0/` at startup; pick one, say which; it must survive a restart halfway).
  - **Publish:** a re-encode writes `v<N+1>/`, then switches `media_version` in the database; it never waits. New plays (web and program output) use the new version from then on; a web player in the middle of the item keeps fetching the old version's URL and finishes on it; the program output keeps its open files as now.
  - **URLs:** the web client gets versioned URLs (e.g. `/media/{id}/v/{N}/mezz.mp4`, or `?v=N` if simpler; say which) wherever it gets media URLs today (room state, item pages, library, listen mode), so a play started on version N stays on version N. Versioned responses can be cached as immutable. Keep the unversioned `/media/{id}/{file}` working (current version) for anything that still uses it. `docs/future-ideas.md` "Mobile apps" lists file versions as something to keep open: this is that; check it fits.
  - **Clean-up:** an old version is deleted once nobody can still be playing it: when the item isn't current or next anywhere **and** at least the item's duration plus a margin (e.g. 5 min) has passed since the switch; plus at startup (no player survives a restart: delete every non-current version, and any half-written `v*` without a database switch). Purge and retention delete the whole `media/<itemId>/` as now. The free-space minimum counts old versions (they're real disk use until deleted); nothing special needed beyond deleting them on time.
  - **Remove** `waitUntilNotPlaying` and the publish-wait settings/test hooks, and the "publishing once it has finished" log line; quality swaps no longer wait. Keep the publish atomic from the database's point of view.
  - **M5 note:** overlay caches key on the item's files; include the media version in their key when M5 builds them (add a line to the M5 overlay cache step).
  - Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (publish, re-encodes, storage layout), `docs/architecture.md` (storage layout), `docs/api.md` (media URLs), `docs/data-model.md`. Tests: a re-encode while the item is playing publishes at once; a reader holding version N's URL keeps getting version N's bytes after the switch; the old version is deleted after the play ends and the grace period, and at startup; the flat (version 0) layout keeps playing and re-encodes into a version; purge removes all versions; the affected ingest ITs and migration ITs.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `7b273cd`. Migration `0010_media_version` (`items.media_version INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, both databases). **Layout choice: the flat layout counts as version 0 and is not moved** (nothing to survive halfway); an item's next encode becomes `v1/`, and version 0's files are deleted later by name (`MediaVersions.ENCODE_FILES`), leaving the original. Version numbers only grow (`max(media_version, highest v* present) + 1`; a purge keeps the number), so a refetch after a purge gets a new number too. **Publish** (`IngestPipeline.publish`): a newly kept original moves to `media/<id>/original.<ext>` (a re-encode from the original renames a link of the same file over itself, then drops the link), the work dir is renamed to `v<N>`, then one `UPDATE` sets `media_version` with the other publish columns (`IngestItems.published(..., version)`); a failed switch deletes `v<N>` again. An old original under another name is deleted after the switch. **URLs: path form** `/media/{id}/v/{N}/{file}` (`MediaApi.url`), `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable`; `N` must be `0` or a plain number (else `404 not_found`), a gone version `404 file_not_found`; `original` is not versioned (served the same under any N, `no-cache`); the unversioned route serves the current version. Room state `coverUrl`/`audioUrl`/`videoUrl` and the item page (`ItemPage.mediaVersion`, `mediaPath(id, file, version)`) use versioned URLs; the item page keeps a player that is playing when a newer version shows up. The Library page plays nothing, so nothing to change there. **Program output:** `ProgramMedia.ItemDirs` resolves the current version at every load (Main reads `media_version`); a loaded item keeps its open files; a bad item id now fails the load (Loading filler) instead of throwing. **Clean-up:** `MediaVersions` (sweeps every minute; `inUse` = rooms' current/next live set) deletes a retired version once the item isn't current/next and the new encode's duration + 5 min have passed since the switch; pending deletions are in memory only; at startup (`Main.startIngest`, after `recoverDirectories`) `MediaVersions.cleanUpAtStartup` deletes every non-current version of every non-purged item, including `v*` left without a switch (unknown and purged items' dirs are left to the purge). Removed `waitUntilNotPlaying`, `inUse`/`publishWait` on the pipeline and the publish-wait constants and log lines; the pre-version `.old-*` recovery stays for directories left by an older release. Known edge: re-ingesting an audio upload over itself replaces `original.<ext>` in place (same name), which is unversioned; only a retried upload does that. future-ideas "File versions" fits (noted there: `media_version` is the comparable version; a content hash could still be added). M5 overlay cache line was already there. Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `IngestPipelineTest` (24, incl. new `aQualitySwapPublishesAtOnceWhileTheItemIsPlaying`: switch while playing, old bytes untouched and readable through an open handle, kept while playing and until duration + grace, then deleted, original stays; `videoUploadReplacesTheOldDirectory` now covers the flat version 0 → v1), new `MediaVersionsTest` (layout, retire/sweep timing incl. version 0, startup clean-up), `IngestStepsTest` (publish into versions, leftover v* skipped, failed switch), `MediaApiTest` (new `versionedUrlsKeepServingTheirVersion`: Range on an old version after the switch, immutable header, bad numbers, original, guest rule, purged), `ProgramMediaTest` (`eachLoadUsesTheCurrentVersion`), `ItemsContract` (purge removes every version and the original), `RoomViewsTest`, `RoomSocketTest`, `ItemsApiTest`, `UploadsContract`, `PermissionMatrixTest`, `DtoIpLeakTest`, `InitialSchemaContract`, then all unit tests except `IngestPipelineTest` (1493, green after one JSON expectation fix); ITs `PostgresMigrationIT`, `SchemaEquivalenceIT`, `PostgresInitialSchemaIT`, `PostgresIngestJobsIT`, `PostgresItemsIT`, `PostgresUploadsIT`, `PostgresRetentionSweeperIT` (77, green). Jar smoke test on a scratch data dir: migration 0010 applied, `/media/{id}/v/1/…` routed (403 for anonymous, `item_not_found` for an unknown cover); server stopped.
- [x] Live feedback while a YouTube link is looked up and fetched (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "When I paste a YouTube link in, it says \"Looking up the video\" but does not seem to update its status until it's added to the queue. Having additional feedback can alleviate issues with patience."). Today the room page (`room.ts` YouTube form) shows a fixed "Looking up the video…" for the whole `yt-dlp -J` lookup, which took **15.5 s** for one video when Siggi's session timed it (2026-11-10, `--verbose` printed `[youtube] Extracting URL`, `…: Downloading webpage`, `…: Downloading android vr player API JSON`, then the format line), and once queued the entry only shows a "processing" badge until it's ready.
  - **During the lookup:** the server reports stages while `yt-dlp -J` runs, read from its stderr progress lines (drop `--no-warnings` or add `--verbose` only if needed, and keep the output parsing robust: unknown lines are ignored, wording changes never fail the lookup), mapped to plain text: "Contacting YouTube…", "Reading the video page…", "Getting the video details…", "Checking formats…". Delivery: for the room's `queue.add`, a progress message on the requester's socket tied to the request id (e.g. `{type: "request.progress", id, stage, text}`), sent only to that socket; for the REST import (`POST /api/imports/youtube`, Library page) pick the simplest fit (e.g. make the lookup async with a lookup id the page polls, or stream stages): say which. The client also shows the elapsed seconds ("Looking up the video… 6 s") so it visibly moves even when yt-dlp prints nothing for a while. An item already known (no lookup) answers at once as now.
  - **After it's queued, until it's ready:** queue entries and suggestions whose item isn't ready show what its ingest job is doing: "Waiting to download" (queued, with its place if the jobs page/claim order gives it cheaply), "Downloading 45 %", "Encoding 30 %", "Finishing…", "Failed: <short reason>" (from the job's step and progress, which the jobs page step exposes). Send it with the room state (throttled, e.g. at most once a second per room, and only while some entry's item isn't ready); no IPs or logs (invariant 5). The Library page's existing job status uses the same wording.
  - Docs: `docs/api.md` (WebSocket messages, the import API change), `docs/rooms.md`. Tests: a fake yt-dlp that prints the stage lines slowly → the requesting socket gets the stages in order and nobody else gets them; unknown lines ignored; entries carry job progress while not ready and stop once ready; throttling; `ListenJsTest`-style or room web test for the elapsed counter and stage text.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `fa12139`. **Lookup stages:** `yt-dlp -J --no-playlist --no-warnings --verbose` (without `--verbose`, `-J` is quiet; with `--no-quiet` the progress lines would go to stdout and mix with the JSON; `--verbose` puts them on stderr and adds `[debug]` lines, which are ignored). New `library/YoutubeLookupStage` (`contacting`/`page`/`details`/`formats`, texts as specified; lenient matching on `[extractor] …` lines, `[debug]`, warnings, errors and unknown lines ignored; a `Tracker` reports each stage once and never backwards; a throwing listener is ignored). `ToolCall.stderrLines(consumer)` (new; redacted lines, exceptions ignored). Because verbose prints a traceback after `ERROR:`, which can push it out of the 20-line stderr tail, the lookup keeps yt-dlp's last `ERROR:` line itself for the `422 youtube_unavailable` message. **Room:** `RoomQueue.add(…, listener)`, `RoomSocket` sends `{type:"request.progress", rid, stage, text}` to the requesting socket only (field is `rid`, matching `ack`, not `id`). **REST: streaming chosen** (simpler than a lookup id + poll): with `Accept: application/x-ndjson`, `POST /api/imports/youtube` switches to a chunked NDJSON stream at the lookup's first stage (`X-Accel-Buffering: no`): progress lines, then `{type:"result",…}` or `{type:"error", status, error:{code,message}, retryAfter?}`; a known item or an error before the lookup gets the plain JSON answer (the client checks `Content-Type`); without that `Accept` nothing changes. **Job steps:** `IngestQueue.Context.stage(step, from, to)` (memory only) and `IngestQueue.liveStep(jobId)`; the pipeline names `download` (0..35 % of a download job), `encode` (probe + loudness pass 1 + encode) and `finish` (after the encode). `JobList.Steps` feeds `step`/`stepProgress` into the jobs rows (`JobsApi`) and `GET /api/items/{id}/job` (`Items.jobSteps`). **Room ingest progress:** new `ingest/IngestProgress` (from `JobList.latest(itemIds)`: the latest job per item with its claim-order `position` and live step; `failed` → item error's first line, ≤ 120 chars) and an `ingest` message from `RoomBroadcaster`: `{type:"ingest", items:{itemId:{state, kind?, position?, step?, progress?, error?}}}` without a `version`, to every socket in the room, looked up at most once per second per room and only while some current/queued/suggested entry's item is `processing`/`failed`, sent only when changed, re-sent after a join's/sync's full state, and once with `items:{}` when nothing waits any more (wired in `Main` via `RoomSocket.ingestProgress`). **Web:** `jobtext.ts` gains `ingestText` ("Waiting to download (3rd in line)" / "Waiting to encode" for uploads, "Downloading 45 %", "Encoding 30 %", "Finishing…", "Starting…" before a step is named, "Failed: <reason>"), `jobIngestStatus`, `lookupText` ("Looking up the video… 6 s" → "Reading the video page… 9 s", ticking every second) and `JsonLines` (NDJSON splitter). Room page: the YouTube form shows the stage + elapsed seconds; entry badges (`data-ingest-item`) show the ingest text and update in place on `ingest`. Library page: the import form streams the stages with the same counter; row badges, the activity list and the item page badge use `ingestBadge`/`ingestText`; the jobs page shows the step words for running jobs that have one. Checked the real yt-dlp 2026.10.26 with the final arguments: stderr gives contacting → page → details → formats, stdout stays the JSON.
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `RoomSocketTest` (new `aYoutubeLookupSendsItsStagesToTheRequestingSocketOnly`: slow fake yt-dlp, stages in order before the ack, unknown/debug/repeated lines ignored, the other socket gets none, a known video gets none; `entriesWhoseItemIsNotReadyCarryTheirIngestProgress`: running/queued with position, live step + rounding, both sockets get it, throttling (≤ 7 messages for 1 s of 5 ms changes at a 200 ms interval), quiet when unchanged, failed with short reason, final empty `ingest` once nothing waits), new `YoutubeImportsApiTest` (stream timing, plain JSON for a known item and for errors before the lookup, error line, Accept parsing), new `YoutubeLookupStageTest`, new `IngestProgressTest`, `SqliteYoutubeImportsTest` (new stage order, unknown output + throwing listener, ERROR followed by a 40-line traceback), `IngestQueueTest.aRunningJobsStepIsLive`, `ToolCallTest` (stderrLines), `JobsApiTest` (step fields), `SqliteItemsTest` (job view step), `IngestStepsTest`, `DtoIpLeakTest`, `JobsJsTest` (`jobs.test.mjs`: ingest words, job → words, lookup line seconds, NDJSON split across chunks; wiring of room/roomclient/addmedia/library), `WebBuildTest`, `ListenJsTest`, `ShellJsTest`, `IngestPipelineTest`; then all unit tests but `IngestPipelineTest` (1512, green); ITs `PostgresYoutubeImportsIT`, `PostgresIngestJobsIT`, `PostgresItemsIT`. Jar smoke start (port 18097): starts, `/api/imports/youtube` 401 anonymously, `/js/jobtext.js` served; stopped. Not clicked through in a browser.
- [x] Speed up `IngestPipelineTest` (added 2026-11-10, Siggi agreed): it takes ~79 s on its own (real ffmpeg encodes; slower since CBR, filler cards and the quality preset) and is in most ingest steps' test runs. Find where the time goes (per test method: surefire report times) and cut it without losing coverage, e.g.: encode fixtures at the smallest size/duration that still exercises the path (short clips, low source resolution; the output profile itself can't shrink because of the reference avcC, but a test-only output encoding at a low resolution/bitrate may be possible if `OutputEncoding` allows it: check that every invariant check still runs), build shared fixtures once per class (`@BeforeAll`) instead of per test, use the quick preset (veryfast) wherever a test isn't about the quality preset, and move the few inherently slow cases (e.g. the quality-preset swap, the 1080p re-encode) into a separate class (e.g. `IngestPipelineSlowTest`) that still runs in `mvn test`/`mvn verify` but that steps can leave out unless they touch those paths. Goal: the main class under ~25 s. Same for `EncodeProfileTest` (~18 s) and `RtmpPusherTest` (~17 s) if there's an easy win. No behaviour changes. Tests: the moved/changed classes pass; report before/after times in the notes.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `d4caad9`. **Where the time went:** no single hog; 24 methods of 0.3–11 s each (per-job overhead: ffprobe, loudness pass, 720p encode, card, index, verify), the biggest `originalPlaysAsLoudAsAudioM4a` 10.9 s (four 12 s noise sources), `milestoneSetIngestsWithOneAvcC` 9.9 s (1080p/60 fps/portrait sources), `originalsOfAudioFormats` 9.3 s (11 sequential jobs), `reencodeUploadsForANewProfile` 7.0 s, `unreferencedItemsArePurged…` 6.2 s, `reencodeYoutubeItemsFromTheOriginal` 5.5 s. The per-test reference card is ~0.3 s (kept). **Changes (tests only, same coverage):** (1) JUnit parallel execution, opt-in per method (`src/test/resources/junit-platform.properties`: enabled, default `same_thread` for classes and methods, fixed parallelism 6): the four classes are `@Execution(SAME_THREAD)` and their test methods `@Execution(CONCURRENT)`, so methods run side by side within the class while classes still run one after another (a class-level `CONCURRENT` would also let the class overlap with other classes, tried and dropped: timing-sensitive tests elsewhere could get flaky under the load). Every method already had its own `@TempDir`, database and queues; no static state in the code under test. (2) New `IngestPipelineSlowTest` with the inherently slow cases: `reencodeUploadsForANewProfile`, `reencodeYoutubeItemsFromTheOriginal`, `reencodeAudioWithoutOriginalFromAudioM4a` (1080p30), `quickForRoomsThenQualityForLibraries` (slow preset), `aQualitySwapPublishesAtOnceWhileTheItemIsPlaying` (medium preset), `originalPlaysAsLoudAsAudioM4a`, `milestoneSetIngestsWithOneAvcC`; it runs in `mvn test`/`mvn verify` like any `*Test`. Set-up and helpers moved unchanged to `IngestPipelineTestBase` (abstract, both classes extend it). **Run `IngestPipelineSlowTest` too when a step touches re-encodes/profiles, the quality preset, originals/loudness or the avcC check.** (3) `originalsOfAudioFormats` stages and enqueues its 11 cases on a 4-worker queue, then checks each (same assertions). Shrinking fixtures / a test-only output encoding weren't needed. **Times (class alone, this Mac, 12 cores):** `IngestPipelineTest` 79.0 s → 12.9–13.0 s (17 tests), `IngestPipelineSlowTest` 15.0–16.5 s (7 tests), `EncodeProfileTest` 17.8 s → 9.3–10.0 s, `RtmpPusherTest` 17.0 s → 5.1 s (its three 5 s cases wait for `RtmpPusher.EXIT_WAIT_MS`, production behaviour, now overlapped). Tests run: `IngestPipelineTest`, `IngestPipelineSlowTest`, `EncodeProfileTest`, `RtmpPusherTest` (twice, all pass), plus `WebSocketsTest`, `RoomSocketTest`, `IngestQueueTest`, `ToolCallTest`, `LiveHlsApiTest`, `MainRouterTest`, `FillerCardsTest` to check that the JUnit parallel config (tests now run on JUnit's worker threads) changes nothing for same-thread classes.
- [x] Retry a failed YouTube ingest from its item page and from the room queue (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "If a YouTube yt-dlp item fails to ingest, there should be a retry button both on its page and where it's listed in the queue."). YouTube failures are often temporary (network, YouTube throttling, an outdated yt-dlp), so a **Retry** button appears on a failed YouTube item's page and on its queue entry / suggestion in the room (where the entry shows "Failed: <short reason>", from the live-feedback step). Retry queues a new ingest job for the item (same as the admin jobs page's retry: `409` if a job is already queued/running or the item was purged; respect `ytdlp.enabled` and the free-space minimum). **Who:** on the item page, anyone who may edit the item (`library.editAny`, or the user who added it while it's in their library) plus admins with the jobs permission; in a room, the room's owner and moderators, plus the person who requested that entry (a user or a guest, while the room's guest rules let them add YouTube links). A retried entry stays where it is in the queue and turns back into "Waiting to download…"; when it becomes ready it's eligible again (the not-ready rules). Rate-limit retries per item (e.g. one per 30 s) so a button can't hammer YouTube. Audited (`item.ingest_retried` or the jobs page's audit action). Uploads that failed aren't covered (their file isn't kept on failure); say so if the button would be misleading for them. Docs: `docs/api.md` (the endpoint, e.g. `POST /api/items/{id}/retry`, and a room WS message or the same endpoint from the room), `docs/rooms.md`, `docs/library-and-ingest.md`. Tests: retry from the item page and from the room by each allowed role (and refused for others), a second click while running → 409, the rate limit, the entry keeps its place and plays once ready.

Queues a `youtube` job (a `refetch` if the failed one was), tier decided at job start (so quick while a room waits); the item turns `pending` and the rooms hear it through the jobs' status listener. Audited `item.ingest_retried` (target item; details `jobId`, `kind`, `roomId` from a room). Unlike the import path it doesn't wait `RETRY_FAILED_AFTER` (10 min). `ytdlp.enabled` is checked by the callers (`403 feature_disabled` for everyone). yt-dlp being installed isn't checked up front (the job fails as before; the rate limit bounds it). **Item page:** `POST /api/items/{id}/retry` → `202 {itemId, jobId, status}`; users who may edit it or hold `admin.jobs`; `you.canRetry` added to the item page (`Items.Rights` gained `retryAny`/`youtube`, old two-arg constructors kept). **Room:** WS `queue.retry {entryId}` (queued entries and suggestions; `ControlPolicy.requireRetry`: moderates, or `added(entry) && canYoutube`, so a guest needs the server's and the room's `guests.canYoutube`), run as a slow action like `queue.add`; ack `{itemId, status}`. The room state's `item` now carries `source`, so the client shows Retry only on failed YouTube entries (`jobtext.canRetryEntry`); a retried badge reads "Waiting to download" at once instead of the stale "Failed" until the next `ingest` message. - [x] Check that a not-ready next entry is skipped, in the room and in the program output (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "when the current item finishes, if the next item in the queue is not ready yet, it should remain in the queue, but skip over to find the next item that can be played"). This is already the rule (`docs/rooms.md` "Entries that aren't ready", main `72dea8f`): verify it end to end and fix what doesn't hold. Cases: (a) current ends, the first queued entry is processing, the second is ready → the second plays, the first stays in the queue at the top and plays when ready (after the second ends, if it's ready by then); (b) the same with a failed entry (stays, marked failed, never chosen until retried); (c) all queued entries not ready → idle (Idle card) until one becomes ready, then it starts as today; (d) skip (`playback.skip`) behaves the same as a natural end; (e) the **program output**: an item the room treats as ready but whose files the packager can't use (e.g. an old encoding waiting for its re-encode, or `index.bin` missing/unreadable) must not leave the stream on "Loading…" for the whole item: the room should treat such an item as not ready for choosing (one notion of "playable" shared by the room and the packager, e.g. item status `ready` AND files of the current encoding), so it's skipped like a processing one; and suggestions follow the same rules. Also check the web listen mode doesn't stall on (e). Tests: `RoomSocketTest`/`ProgramOutputsRoomsTest`-style cases for (a)–(e). Docs: `docs/rooms.md`, `docs/program-output.md` if (e) changes anything.

New `ingest.PlayableFiles`: usable = `output_profile` is the encoding in use AND the current media version's `index.bin` reads and passes `PlayableFiles.requireUsable` (encoding, reference avcC, audio track, an audio item's card track; **`ProgramMedia.readIndex` now calls the same method**), the files it names exist, and a video item has `card.mp4`. Positive results cached per item/version/encoding (LRU 4096; published versions are immutable), failures re-checked on each choice and logged once. `QueueEntry` gains `filesUsable` (in `ready()`; `RoomStore` reads `i.kind, i.output_profile, i.media_version` with the entries and asks the check only for `ready` items; `RoomState.ItemInfo` carries it), so such an entry shows as `processing` (entries and suggestions), is skipped, and is never the cue's preloaded `next`. `RoomRegistry.playableFiles(check)` (default `Check.ANY`: status only, for tests without media files), wired in `Main`. **Notifications:** a finished `reencode`/`quality` job now calls the `ItemStatusListener` too (new files may make the item playable; an idle room starts it); an admin encoding switch calls `RoomRegistry.allItemsChanged()` (every loaded room re-reads its entries). **Not changed:** an entry already current when its files become unusable (encoding switch mid-play) keeps playing (output on Loading for it, as documented); `resume()` still checks the raw status. A ready item with a broken index stays queued as "processing" with nothing fixing it on its own (logged; an admin can re-ingest). **Web listen mode** isn't affected: it plays `mezz.mp4`/`audio.m4a` by the item's own `ready` status and URLs (`RoomViews.item`), and never gets such an item as current. Docs: `docs/rooms.md` ("Ready" means playable by the program output too), `docs/program-output.md` (Loading filler), `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (re-encodes, encoding switch).

- [x] One history per room: the play history replaces the request history (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "We should have just one history -- it should be play history. In the play history, anything that's not in the current user's library has an \"Add to Library\" button. For the room owner/moderators, all items in the play history have Add to Queue button. For non-owner/moderators, follow the \"No repeat with last N items\" setting for the Add to Queue button.").
  - **Remove the request history** as a separate view (the owner's request history in Manage room / the room page, its API `…/requests` and docs: `docs/libraries.md` "Room owner's request history", `docs/rooms.md` "Requests and the owner's request history"). Keep `room_requests` rows only if something else still needs them (e.g. `requestsPerActor`, audit, guest moderation); otherwise stop writing them and drop the table with a migration (both databases). Say which.
  - **The play history** is every entry that started playing in the room, newest first, persistent (survives restarts; today's room state carries only the last N played entries) and paged in the UI (e.g. 50 at a time, "Show more"). Kept for the server setting `rooms.requestHistoryDays` (rename it to `rooms.historyDays` with the old key read as a fallback, or keep the name; say which). Each row: title/artist (purged items keep their row, so they stay readable), when it played, who added it (display name at the time; guest badge; no IPs, invariant 5), how (library / upload / YouTube), and whether it was skipped. Visible to everyone in the room (as today's history), so it must not reveal more than the queue already does.
  - **Add to Library** on every row whose item isn't in the **viewing user's** library (signed-in users only; guests have no library), adding it to their own library (the existing library-add path, so retention protection and the quality re-encode trigger follow). Rows already in their library show "In your library" instead. A purged item can be added too if a purged YouTube item can be refetched (as library adds already do), else no button.
  - **Add to Queue:** the owner and moderators get it on every row (exempt from the request rules, as today). Everyone else sees it only where they may request: their usual request rules apply (`requestsPerActor`, guest capabilities, control mode: in `owner` mode it becomes a suggestion), and **`noRepeatWindow`**: for an item that started playing within the last N entries the button is disabled with the reason ("Played within the last N songs"); the server enforces the same rule. No request scope check (Siggi: "If an item is available on the server, it's fair game"; see the next step, which removes `requestScope`).
  - **Retention:** the request history was the owner's way to keep requested items from being deleted; with one history, the owner (and anyone) adds them from the play history instead. Items requested but never played (removed from the queue before playing) are no longer listed anywhere: note it in `docs/libraries.md`.

- [x] Upload pre-check by content hash, and uploads only where library requests are allowed (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "Let's add the hash checking, and then prevent a state where upload is on but library searching is off. If the user attempts to turn upload on while library searching is off, turn library searching on as well and inform the user. Same with turning library searching off while upload is on, turn upload off as well and inform the user. No hand-written SHA-256. The server is set up with HTTPS or the feature is disabled. If someone asks me for help with this on their instance, I'll just tell them HTTP non-TLS is not a supported use case."). Comes after the step that adds the `libraryRequests` room setting.
  - **Why the coupling:** with a pre-check, anyone may *claim* to hold a file by sending its hash and get the existing item without uploading it, which would bypass `libraryRequests` off. So uploads are only allowed where library requests are.
  - **Content ID computed in the browser with `crypto.subtle` only** (no hand-written SHA-256). `crypto.subtle.digest` needs the whole input in memory, which doesn't work for multi-GB files, so the content ID is a **chunked SHA-256**: SHA-256 of each consecutive 8 MiB piece of the file (the last one shorter), then SHA-256 over the concatenation of those digests, plus the file size; e.g. `sha256c8m:<hex>`. Both steps use `crypto.subtle.digest`, reading the `File` with `slice()` piece by piece (bounded memory; show "Checking the file… 40 %"). The server computes the same ID (Java `MessageDigest`, no extra dependency) for every completed upload and stores it on the item (new column, e.g. `items.content_id`, migration for both databases), next to `content_sha256` (kept: dedupe on completion still uses both). Backfill the ID for existing items that have an original file (a background job at startup, low priority; purged items without files keep only `content_sha256`). Note the scheme in `docs/library-and-ingest.md`.
  - **Pre-check:** `POST /api/uploads` takes an optional `contentId`. If it matches an item that is ready, pending or processing, the server answers without an upload (e.g. `200 {deduplicated: true, itemId, status}`) and does exactly what completing that upload would have done (library entry, room queue entry/suggestion with the same request rules, `last_referenced_at`), with the same permission and quota checks as a real upload start (a guest's pre-check counts as an upload start). A purged or failed match, or no match, → a normal upload (the server re-hashes on completion; a wrong claimed ID just means no shortcut). Never reveals whose libraries hold the item.
  - **HTTPS only:** the pre-check is on only when `http.publicBaseUrl` is `https://` **and** the page is a secure context (`window.isSecureContext`, `crypto.subtle` present); otherwise the client uploads normally without a content ID and the server ignores the feature. Not supported over plain HTTP (Siggi's call): say so in the docs (deployment notes / README), no fallback hasher.
  - **Coupling, room level:** add a room setting `uploadRequests` (default on; whether non-owner/moderators may request by upload in this room; users still need `library.upload`, guests `guests.canUpload` server + room). Invariant: `uploadRequests` on ⇒ `libraryRequests` on. In Manage room: turning upload on while library requests are off also turns library requests on, and turning library requests off while upload is on also turns upload off; either way show a notice saying what else changed and why ("Uploads need library requests: turned library requests on too."). The server enforces the same on `PATCH` (a patch that would break it applies the same companion change and reports it in the response, e.g. `adjusted: ["libraryRequests"]`), and stored states that break it (older rows) are read as upload off.
  - **Coupling, guests:** the same rule for `guests.canUpload` ⇒ `guests.canRequestLibrary`, both for the **room** guest settings and the **server** guest settings on the admin page (same companion change + notice). Effective checks also enforce it at request time (an upload request is refused where a library request would be), so no combination of server and room switches can let someone upload where library requests are off.
  - Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (content ID, pre-check, HTTPS), `docs/rooms.md` (settings table, coupling), `docs/auth.md` (server guest settings coupling), `docs/api.md` (`contentId`, the dedupe response, `adjusted`). Tests: the Java content ID matches a known value computed the browser way (fixture with >1 piece and a short last piece; a JS test with Node's `crypto.subtle` gives the same hex); pre-check hit → item added/queued without bytes; miss/purged/failed → normal upload; guest quota counts a pre-check; no pre-check when the base URL isn't https; the coupling in both directions at room level, room guests and server guests, with the notice; an upload request refused when library requests are effectively off.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `e328e76`. **Content ID** `sha256c8m:<size>:<hex>` (size in the ID too, so a size mismatch is no hit): `library/ContentId` (incremental, `MessageDigest`) computed in `Uploads` alongside the SHA-256 as chunks arrive (and from the part file after a restart); stored in `items.content_id` (migration 0012, both databases, indexed) on insert, and filled in when a dedupe or re-ingest hits an item without one; completion dedupes on `content_sha256 OR content_id`. **Backfill:** `ContentIdBackfill`, a MIN_PRIORITY daemon platform thread at startup, one file at a time, for `source='upload'` items with `original_file` and no ID (not purged), only if the original's SHA-256 equals `content_sha256`. **Pre-check:** `POST /api/uploads {…, contentId?, addToLibrary?}` → on a ready/pending/processing upload-item hit `200 {uploadId, chunkSize, size, deduplicated: true, itemId, status}`. **Decision:** the hit still creates an `uploads` row, recorded as completed with the item, so the room flow is unchanged (`queue.add {uploadId}` applies the request rules and owner-mode suggestions) and `GET`/`complete` on it answer as for a finished upload; the size, free-space, permission and guest-quota checks run as for any start (a guest hit reserves a quota slot). **HTTPS:** `Main.uploadPrecheck(config)` (https `publicBaseUrl`) → `Uploads.precheck(true)`; the client (`web/src/contentid.ts`, `precheckAvailable()`) only hashes on an `https:` page in a secure context with `crypto.subtle` (pages share the base URL's origin, which CSRF requires, so no extra endpoint is needed to learn the server's scheme); new upload state `checking` with "Checking the file… N %"; hashing errors fall back to a normal upload. **Coupling:** room setting `uploadRequests` (default on; Manage room → "Allow uploads as requests"); `RoomSettings` reads any stored state with an upload switch on and its library switch off as upload off (constructor), `patchAdjusting` makes the companion change (`Rooms.updateAdjusting`, `PATCH /api/rooms/{id}` adds `adjusted`); a patch turning upload on and library off at once → `400 invalid_setting`. `queue.add {uploadId}` by non-moderators and guest `POST /api/uploads {roomId}` → `403 upload_requests_off` while off; `you.canUpload` false then. Server: `Settings.COUPLED` (`guests.canUpload` → `guests.canRequestLibrary`), `guestsCanUpload()` and `all()` read a breaking state as off; `PATCH /api/admin/settings` makes the companion change and lists `adjusted`. Web: `roomsettings.ts` `coupledChange`/`adjustedNotice` (DOM-free), used by Manage room and the admin page (notice in the saved/status line). Since libraryRequests off now turns uploads off, the M4 "libraryRequests" step's "uploads are unaffected" no longer holds (QueueOpsContract updated).
