- [x] Packager-side CBR padding instead of encoder CBR (added 2026-11-10, Siggi chose this over keeping `nal-hrd=cbr`; Siggi: "For the pre-rendered card that gets repeated every 2 seconds, the on-disk format shouldn't be padded with junk data, the padding should ideally be added before it gets sent out as a stream."). Why: `nal-hrd=cbr` writes the bitrate into the SPS (VUI HRD), so it's part of the avcC that must be identical across everything spliced into one stream: cards can't be stored unpadded, audio-only rooms (below) can't use a lower rate without other headers, and every bitrate change re-encodes everything.
  - **Encodes:** drop `nal-hrd=cbr` (and `vbv-init` if it no longer matters) from the shared argument constant; keep the rate **capped**: `-b:v R -maxrate R -bufsize <B>` (measure again which `B` keeps the cap tight; today R/2). No HRD in the SPS, so the bitrate leaves the avcC. Nothing on disk is padded (cards and mezzanines get smaller). Bump the encoding/args version so every item and card re-encodes once (from originals), as the presets step did; the reference card changes accordingly.
  - **Packager padding:** the packager inserts **H.264 filler data NAL units (type 12)** while it sends, so the program output's video runs at exactly the target rate T: a simple VBV-style model per output (bytes sent vs. T × elapsed media time) adds filler after each access unit when it's behind; a frame bigger than its share (an IDR) is paid back by the following frames' smaller padding. Because every encode obeys the cap R with buffer B, any T ≥ R can always be met; T < R can't (refuse it: the output rate must be ≥ the encode cap). Filler goes inside the access unit after the slice NALs (valid Annex B; `-c copy` to FLV keeps them, as checked on 2026-11-10). This is still not encoding (invariant: the packager never decodes or encodes): it adds padding NALs only. The `.ts`, HLS and RTMP outputs all get the padded stream. Optionally also MPEG-TS null packets for a constant mux rate: not needed (RTMP drops them); note it.
  - **Settings:** `output.videoBitrateKbps` becomes the encode cap and the default stream rate. New `output.audioOnlyBitrateKbps` (admin setting with config default, e.g. 800) for audio-only rooms (below; until that step exists it's stored and validated only). Changing the cap re-encodes (more quality needs new encodes); raising only the stream rate doesn't (say how that's exposed; simplest: stream rate = cap for normal rooms).
  - Update `CLAUDE.md` invariant 1 ("CBR at the configured `output.videoBitrateKbps` (cards padded with filler NALs)" → encodes capped at the configured rate; the packager pads the output to a constant rate; nothing on disk is padded). Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (rate control, cards), `docs/program-output.md` (padding), `docs/overlay.md` (overlay re-encodes use the same capped args). Tests: encodes have no HRD in the SPS and identical avcC across bitrates; cards on disk have no filler NALs; the packager's output is constant-rate per 2 s window within a small tolerance for a mixed session (videos, cards, a pause, idle) at T = R and at a lower T for cards only; RTMP copy keeps it.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `840d07a`. **Encodes:** `-b:v R -maxrate R -bufsize R/2`, `-x264-params open-gop=0:ref=4:chroma-qp-offset=…` (dropped `nal-hrd=cbr`, `vbv-init=1` and `-minrate`); `ARGS_VERSION` 3, so ids are `720p30-3000k-v3` and every item and card re-encodes once at the next start (existing mechanism). Bitrate is no longer in the avcC (verified: equal avcC at 800/1500/3000/4000 kb/s, `trace_headers` shows `nal_hrd_parameters_present_flag 0`); it stays part of the *encoding* id because it is the cap (changing it re-encodes, as the step says). **B measured again** (no HRD, 720p30, 3 Mb/s): R/2 keeps the run-ahead of the cumulative bytes over `R·t/8` at 23 KB (`testsrc2`), 30 KB (noisy fractal), 145 KB (cellular automaton), all within the 187.5 KB buffer, GOPs 70…93 % of the cap; 1 s let `testsrc2` run 100 KB ahead and single GOPs reach 106 %; 0.25 s cost quality (GOPs 57…87 %). Kept R/2. Still cards are now a few KB (`smptebars` 4 KB, `rgbtestsrc` 9 KB per 2 s) instead of ~750 KB. **Padding:** `output/CbrPadder` (one per output, between unit builder and muxer in `ProgramPipeline`; `OfflineHarness.run` takes an optional rate): cumulative model on AVCC sample bytes, a frame ends at the next frame's PTS (last frame: the unit boundary), filler NAL (type 12, ref_idc 0, 0xFF…, 0x80) appended after the slices when behind, split for 1/2-byte length prefixes; budget computed in whole seconds + rest so it can't overflow. `ProgramPipeline.Opened` gained `streamBitrateBps` (0 = unpadded); `Main` passes the output encoding's cap (normal rooms: stream rate = cap; a separate higher stream rate would only add padding, so no extra setting). No refusal of `T < R` was needed in code yet: normal rooms always use `T = R`, and the audio-only step uses its lower rate for cards only (it must not pass a rate below the cap for item video). **Settings:** `output.audioOnlyBitrateKbps` (config key + env `TOGETHERTUNES_OUTPUT_AUDIO_ONLY_BITRATE_KBPS` + admin setting, default 800, 500..25000, re-encodes nothing; stored and validated only until the audio-only rooms step). **Measured** (`CbrPaddingTest`): mixed session at T = R = 3 Mb/s with still cards: full units within ±0.8 % except the unit after a video's short last GOP (−2.3 %, paying back that GOP's IDR; the 0.3 s unit itself is +15 %); the up-to-one-audio-frame gap at a source switch is padded in the next unit (±0.5 %). Cards only at 800 kb/s: within ±0.6 %. FLV via `-c copy` keeps every filler NAL and the same rates. Moving "cards" (the harness's default `testsrc`/`mandelbrot` fillers) can't be held at `T = R` exactly: a looped 2 s GOP that starts with a full VBV buffer may exceed `R·2 s` every repeat; real cards are stills, so the test uses still fillers. MPEG-TS null packets not added (RTMP drops them; noted in docs). **Observation, not fixed (pre-existing, unrelated to padding):** `ffmpeg -v error -i out.ts -f null -` prints "non monotonically increasing dts to muxer" for a cards-only script (audio item, pause, play, seek, then a second audio item; same with padding off), although the TS timestamps are valid (`assertPackets`); `cardsOnlyBelowTheCap` therefore skips `assertDecodesClean`. Worth a look in the packager harness later. Docs: `library-and-ingest.md` (rate control row, why/buffer paragraphs, reference command, cards), `program-output.md` (new "Constant-rate padding" section, muxer filler line), `overlay.md`, `architecture.md` (config table, cards path), `auth.md` (settings), `README.md`; CLAUDE.md invariant 1 updated. Tests run: `CbrPadderTest`, `CbrPaddingTest`, `EncodeProfileTest`, `CardEncoderTest`, `ReferenceCardTest`, `EncodePresetsTest`, `FillerCardsTest`, `IngestPipelineTest`, `IngestPipelineSlowTest`, `RealTsMuxerTest`, `RealSourcesTest`, `OfflineHarnessTest`, `PackagerGoldenFileTest`, `PackagerTest`, `ProgramMediaTest`, `ProgramOutputsTest`, `RtmpPusherTest`, `ConfigLoaderTest`, `SqliteSettingsTest`, `AdminApiTest`, `MainEncodingTest`, `MainUploadPrecheckTest`, `PermissionTest`, `DtoIpLeakTest`, `SqliteIngestJobsTest`, `SqliteUploadsTest`, `SqliteYoutubeImportsTest`, `PostgresSettingsIT`.
- [x] Undo for media edits: keep previous encodes 48 h (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "For undo, in case a mistake is made, keep the previous encode for 48 hours (configurable in admin settings) so users can click an undo button to go back."). Every edit below (trims) and every re-encode publishes a new media version (versioned media step). Keep the previous version for `media.undoHours` (admin setting, default 48; 0 = no undo), instead of deleting it as soon as no play can still use it; the startup clean-up also keeps it while it's within the window. The item page shows **Undo** while a previous version is kept ("Undo the change from 14:32"), for whoever may edit the item: it switches `media_version` back (no re-encode; the trim settings return to what that version was made with, so store them per version) and the newer version becomes the kept "previous" one (so undo of an undo works within the window). Only one step back is needed (keep at most the previous version, or say if keeping more is as cheap). Kept versions count toward the free-space minimum; when free space is below the minimum, kept undo versions may be deleted early (oldest first) with a log line. Re-encodes made by the system (profile/cap changes, quality re-encodes) also keep the previous version for the window, but don't show Undo unless it's simple to allow (say which). Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md`, `docs/api.md`, `docs/auth.md` (setting). Tests: undo restores the old version and its trim settings, redo by undo, expiry after the window (fake clock), early deletion under the free-space minimum, permission.

Migration `0013_media_undo` (both databases): `media_versions` (per version: the `items` publish columns plus `edit_json`, the edit settings such as trims; rows only for the current and the kept version) and `media_undo` (an item's kept version, `switched_at`, `undoable`). Setting `media.undoHours` (default 48, 0..8760, 0 = off; Library group of the admin page). **Only one step back** (keeping more would cost a whole encode per step). **Undo does not switch to the old number:** it hard-links the kept version's files into a new `v<N+1>/` (copy if linking fails), so version numbers still only grow and versioned URLs stay immutable (switching `media_version` back would let a later publish reuse a deleted higher number); one transaction restores the kept version's `items` columns and `edit_json`, and makes the undone version the kept one (redo by undo). `MediaVersions.undo` / `undoableSince`; `Items.undo` (edit rule; audited `item.media_undone`); `POST /api/items/{id}/undo` → item page; `409 nothing_to_undo` (none, system re-encode, past window, off, not ready, or kept for another `output_profile`) / `item_busy` (job queued or running); the item page gets `undo: {changedAt}` (editors only) and shows "Undo the change from 14:32"; the rooms are told after an undo. **System re-encodes keep the previous version for the window but offer no Undo** (an encoding re-encode's undo would bring back files the program output can't splice; a quality re-encode's would only bring back the quick encode); a system re-encode after an edit replaces that edit's undo (one step back) — **note for the trim steps:** encode edits with the tier the item already has, so no quality re-encode follows and eats the Undo; edit jobs publish with `VersionChange(undoable = true, editJson)` (TODO in `IngestPipeline`). Until the trim steps exist, nothing in production creates an undoable change, so Undo can't show yet. Sweeper: kept versions wait for their plays and the window; while free space is below the minimum, kept versions whose plays are over are deleted early, oldest first, until the shortfall is covered (log line). Startup clean-up (`mediaVersions.cleanUpAtStartup()`) keeps the kept version within the window and hands it to the sweeper; forgets rows past it. Purge forgets the undo rows. - [x] Video start/stop trim with smart re-encoding (added 2026-11-10, Siggi; videos only). Per-item settings `videoStartMs`/`videoEndMs` (relative to the original; stored per media version), editable by whoever may edit the item's metadata. **Every encode comes from the original** (Siggi: "any new encode should always come from the original file"); a smart re-encode only re-encodes the parts that changed, from the original, and copies the rest as whole GOPs from the current version. The current encode's 2 s GOP grid in original time is `videoStartMs + 2 s × k`.
  - **End earlier:** no re-encode: cut the video after the last frame before the new end (no B-frames, so dropping trailing frames of the last GOP is valid; a short last GOP is normal at item ends).
  - **End later:** re-encode from the start of the current last GOP (it may have been cut short) to the new end, from the original (accurate seek, the same `fps=30` mapping so frames line up), and append those GOPs.
  - **Start later on the grid:** drop the leading GOPs; no re-encode. **Start earlier on the grid:** re-encode `[new start, old start)` from the original (a whole number of 2 s GOPs) and prepend. **Start anywhere else:** full re-encode from the original (the grid must restart at the new start: invariant 2).
  - **Snap to keyframes** tickbox (default on): snaps the start handle to the current encode's grid (both directions), so start changes avoid a full re-encode; the UI says when a choice needs a full re-encode ("Full re-encode: about N s").
  - **Audio:** always re-encode the whole audio track from the original for the new range (cheap), loudness measured again on that range, so the mezzanine's audio and `audio.m4a` match the new video range exactly (invariant 3: verify the audio duration rule still holds).
  - Splicing: rebuild `mezz.mp4` from the reused and new GOP byte ranges plus the new audio (remux with `-c copy` or the box writer; identical avcC is guaranteed by invariant 1, verify it as for any encode), then index, verify, publish as a new version (undo above). The verify step's "IDR every 2 s exactly" and avcC checks run on the result. If anything about the splice fails, fall back to a full re-encode from the original.
  - **UI** (provisional UI rules apply, but Siggi asked for this specifically): on the item page, a timeline under the player with draggable **start** and **end** handles, the playhead, and text fields for typing timestamps (`m:ss.mmm`); "Preview" plays the selection from the current version (seeking, no encode); **Save** starts the job and shows its progress (jobs page wording). Keep the timeline logic (handles, snapping, time parsing/formatting, "which kind of re-encode") in a DOM-free module with tests.
  - Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (trim, smart re-encode rules), `docs/api.md`, `docs/data-model.md`. Tests: each case (end earlier, end later, start later/earlier on grid, start off grid → full) produces a valid encode (avcC, IDR every 2 s from 0, audio length) whose frames match a full re-encode of the same range (compare decoded frame checksums at the seams or a PSNR threshold); fallback on failure; permission; the DOM-free timeline module.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `a609938`. **Job:** new kind `trim` (`IngestJob.Kind.TRIM`; doesn't own the item's status, so the item stays `ready`; re-encode tier; preemptible like a re-encode; claim order with new-item jobs), target settings in the new `ingest_jobs.edit_json` (migration `0014_trim_jobs`, both databases; `IngestJob.editJson`, kept by a retry). `IngestJobs.enqueueTrim`: a queued trim takes the new settings (latest wins); a queued `reencode`/`quality` job is marked done with a log line (they're queued again after the trim if still needed); a running job or a new item's ingest → `ItemBusy` (`409 item_busy`). **Settings:** `ingest/EditSettings` (`videoStartMs`, `videoEndMs`, plus `originalMs` recorded by the encode that applies a trim, for the timeline; other keys kept for the audio trim step). **Plan:** `ingest/TrimPlan` (pure; mirrored by `web/src/trim.ts`) on the current grid `videoStartMs + k/30 s`: smart iff the new start is a multiple of 2 s from the current one and something of the current encode is left; pieces REUSE/ENCODE as the step says (end later: from the current last GOP's start). **Smart path** (`IngestPipeline.smartTrim`): probe original, loudness on the range, audio encoded from the original (`-ss/-t` input options), encoded pieces from the original with the current version's preset (avcC + IDR check per piece; the result keeps `encode_preset`/`encode_quality`), reused pieces via `ffmpeg -ss <GOP start> -c copy -frames:v N` from the current `mezz.mp4` (checked frame by frame against the current index sizes), concat demuxer + new audio → `mezz.mp4` (`-c copy`), `audio.m4a` copied out, card/cover linked, index, the usual verify (avcC, IDRs every 2 s from 0, audio length vs `min(audio end, end) − start`) plus the frame sizes against the pieces; anything failing → log + full re-encode from the original. Smart also requires the current version to be for the current encoding with a readable index (else full). **Full encodes** of a trimmed item (off-grid, fallback, system `reencode`/`quality`) use `-ss/-t` before `-i` **and** `-t` as an output option (measured: with only the input `-t` the fps filter filled up to the end of the last source frame read, e.g. 197 frames for 6.513 s instead of 196); a re-encode from a previous encode (no original) doesn't apply the trim again. Trim jobs publish undoable versions with the new `edit_json`; system re-encodes keep the current one. **API:** `POST /api/items/{id}/trim {videoStartMs, videoEndMs?}` → `202` item page (`400 invalid_trim`/`unknown_field`, `409 not_a_video`/`item_not_ready`/`no_original`/`no_change`/`item_busy`; an end at/after the original's end = to the end; min 1 s), audited `item.trimmed`; the item page has `trim: {videoStartMs, videoEndMs?, originalMs}` for editors of ready videos with their original. **UI:** item page "Trim video" panel (timeline with handles, keyboard arrows, playhead, `m:ss.mmm` fields, Snap to keyframes default on, hint "No video re-encode…" / "Smart re-encode: about N s…" / "Full re-encode: about N s of video…" where N = seconds of video encoded, Preview = seek-and-play the part the current version has, Save); hidden while a job is queued/running; the forms re-render when the job ends or the version changes. Job kind text "Trim" (jobs page, item page). **Decisions:** purge forgets the trim (a refetched item starts untrimmed); trims need the kept original (no original → `409 no_original`). Not clicked through in a browser.

The variant is always encoded again for a new version (cheap) rather than linked. A re-encode from a previous encode (no original) drops a video's audio trim with a log line. - [x] Audio-only rooms (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "a new room setting to make it audio-only, where only audio tracks play. Still needs to be constant bitrate however, we may be able to use a lower bitrate for audio-only streams since video won't matter as much."). Room setting `audioOnly` (default off; owner, Manage room → Settings).
  - **Listeners:** every item plays audio-only (videos use their audio-only file from the audio trim step); the video/audio-only toggle is hidden; `mezz.mp4` is never requested.
  - **Program output:** every item uses `AudioItemSource` (its card, as for `presentAsAudio`) with the item's audio-only file, and the stream is padded to `output.audioOnlyBitrateKbps` (packager padding step) instead of the normal rate. Same avcC, so switching the setting never breaks the stream; only the padding target changes.
  - **Timeline:** a video's length in an audio-only room is its audio-only file's length. Switching the setting takes effect **from the next item**: the current entry keeps its variant and length until it ends (no jump), then the new mode applies to everything after it (including the preloaded next entry and the output's next-item preload).
  - Docs: `docs/rooms.md` (settings table, listen mode), `docs/program-output.md`. Setting in Manage room → Settings → Playback ("Audio-only room", with help text). **Program output:** `ProgramCue.Entry` gained `audioOnly`/`audioOnlyFile` (old constructor kept), `ProgramCue` the room's `audioOnly` (fillers); `ProgramMedia` plays an audio-only entry as `AudioItemSource` (variant: its own slot keyed `<item>#audio-only` reading `index-audio-only.bin`; otherwise `card.mp4` + `index.bin`'s audio); `TimelineFollower.audioOnlyRate()` says whether the unit just built is audio-only output, and the pipeline (and `OfflineHarness`) switches `CbrPadder` to `output.audioOnlyBitrateKbps` (read at output start) per unit (`bitrateBps(rate, atPts)`, debt carried over: measured up to ~17 KB less in the first card unit after a video, then ±0.6 %). - [x] MP3 stream output for Euro Truck Simulator 2 / American Truck Simulator radio (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "in addition to RTMP push, HLS, and TS, we add an MP3 stream (audio only) that works with Euro Truck Simulator 2 and American Truck Simulator."). ETS2/ATS play Shoutcast/Icecast-style MP3 over HTTP from `live_streams.sii` entries `stream_data[N]: "url|name|genre|lang|bitrate|favourite"`; community reports say 128 kb/s MP3 is the safe choice; HTTPS support is unclear (some HTTPS MP3 streams reported working, an HTTPS stream that worked in Winamp didn't) (sources: help.freakhosting.com ETS2 custom radio guide; Steam ETS2 discussions; gameplay.tips ETS2 radio guide; checked 2026-11-10).
  - **A fourth output kind `mp3`**, equal to the others: server switch `outputs.mp3.enabled` (admin, default on), room output row (`stream_outputs` kind `mp3`, one per room, token, rotate, on/off, migration for both databases), `.ts`-style lifecycle (starts the room's program output on the first listener, stops 60 s after the last), per-IP rate limit and per-token connection cap as for `.ts` (`LiveLimits`), tokens never logged in full (invariant 6), `estimatedDelayMs.mp3` room setting for remote mode (default e.g. 3000; measure).
  - **Encoding:** MP3 needs an audio encode, and the packager never encodes, so it's a **supervised ffmpeg subprocess per room** (like the v1 RTMP pusher, CLAUDE.md "Media tools"): a lease on the room's program output (`join=latest`), the program audio piped in (`-f mpegts -i pipe:0 -map 0:a -c:a libmp3lame -b:a 128k -ar 44100 -ac 2 -f mp3 pipe:1`; check `-write_xing 0` / no ID3 header so the stream starts with frames), its MP3 output fanned out to every listener of that room (one encoder however many listeners; per-listener bounded queues, slow listeners dropped as for `.ts`). Started with the first listener, stopped with the output; restarted with backoff if it dies (listeners get a short gap, not a disconnect, if possible). CPU: one real-time MP3 encode per room with listeners (small). Settings: `outputs.mp3.bitrateKbps` (admin, default 128; 64–320), sample rate fixed at 44.1 kHz (most compatible).
  - **Endpoint:** `GET`/`HEAD /live/{token}.mp3`: `200`, `Content-Type: audio/mpeg`, no `Content-Length`, close-delimited (as `.ts`), `icy-name` (room name), `icy-br`, `icy-pub: 0`, `Cache-Control: no-cache`. **ICY metadata** when the client sends `Icy-MetaData: 1`: `icy-metaint: 16000` and `StreamTitle='Artist - Title';` updated at item changes (as the item reaches the stream, i.e. with the output's delay, not the room's timeline), escaped/truncated safely; clients that don't ask get plain MP3. New listeners join at the current position (no start-of-item wait).
  - **Room UI:** the outputs panel lists MP3 with its URL and Copy, in generic words ("MP3 audio stream for internet-radio players"); **no game names or game-specific lines in the UI** (CLAUDE.md). The ETS2/ATS how-to (the `live_streams.sii` line `stream_data[N]: "<url>|<room name>|TogetherTunes|EN|128|0"`, the index and count, where the file is) goes into the external docs (M7 deployment notes / user guide). 
  - **HTTPS:** current ETS2 and ATS versions play `https://` streams (Siggi, 2026-11-10); older legacy versions may not. Use the normal `https://` URL; the docs mention only briefly that legacy game versions may need the proxy to serve `/live/*.mp3` over plain HTTP too (not a supported setup, no further work). **Correction (2026-11-10, manual check):** wrong: the game connected to port 80 over plain HTTP for an `https://` URL (`FMODStudio/2.03.11`, no TLS); see the insecure-endpoint step below.
  - Docs: `docs/program-output.md` (MP3 output), `docs/rooms.md` (outputs, setting), `docs/api.md` (endpoint, output kind, settings), `docs/auth.md` (server switch), `docs/README.md` (the outputs list). Tests: endpoint headers and close-delimited body, valid MP3 frames from the first byte (ffprobe), ICY metadata framing at exactly `metaint` bytes with the title change, two listeners share one encoder, encoder restart, rate limit/cap, rotate ends connections, server switch off.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `b10719b`. **New:** `output/Mp3Encoder` (one room's supervised `ffmpeg -analyzeduration 1000000 -f mpegts -i pipe:0 -map 0:a:0 -vn -c:a libmp3lame -b:a <k>k -ar 44100 -ac 2 -id3v2_version 0 -write_xing 0 -flush_packets 1 -f mp3 pipe:1`, a `join=latest` lease like the RTMP pusher, backoff 1/2/5/10/30 s, listeners stay connected across attempts; no new attempt while nobody listens), `Mp3Encoders` (one per room, started by the first listener, stopped **15 s** after the last left: **decision**, so a reconnecting player finds it running; the output then stops at the usual 60 s), `Mp3Frames` (cuts ffmpeg's output into whole Layer III frames, skips ID3/junk/false syncs), `IcyWriter` (blocks after exactly `metaint` = 16000 audio bytes; a title is repeated only when it changed, else the 0 byte; `'` → `’`, control chars → spaces, ≤ 250 chars), `LiveMp3Api` (`GET`/`HEAD /live/{token}.mp3`; shares `LiveTsApi`'s `/live/{file}` route, which hands `*.mp3` over). Listeners: 10 s queue (overflow → disconnected), a **2 s burst on join** (the encoder's output comes a unit at a time; **decision**). **Titles:** `TsUnit` gained `itemId` (from `ItemSource`, null for fillers); the encoder notes the title at the unit's start position and frames from that output position carry it, so it changes as the item reaches the stream; `Artist - Title` from `items` (title alone without artist; empty for fillers). `icy-name` = room name as printable ASCII (accents dropped); `icy-br`, `icy-sr: 44100`, `icy-pub: 0`. **Deviations:** `Cache-Control: no-store` (as `.ts`, from `Response.stream`) instead of `no-cache`; **no migration**: `stream_outputs.kind` is free text without a check, so `mp3` rows need none (noted in data-model.md); `HEAD` sends the headers close-delimited with no body (no `Content-Length: 0`, unlike `.ts`). Server settings `outputs.mp3.enabled` (default on, `Feature.OUTPUT_MP3`) and `outputs.mp3.bitrateKbps` (128, 64..320, read when each encode starts); room setting `estimatedDelayMs.mp3` = **3000 (not measured in the games**: units reach ffmpeg 1 s early, so it's mostly the player's buffer). Without ffmpeg: `GET` → `503 output_unavailable`, and the room page shows MP3 as `error` ("This server can't encode MP3: ffmpeg isn't configured"). Revalidation of MP3 connections runs with the `.ts` one on every reconcile. Web: "MP3 audio" in the outputs panel with the line "MP3 audio stream for internet-radio players. Audio only; the station title shows what is playing.", Copy, Rotate URL (confirm text now generic: "open connections end"), Turn on/off, Remove; remote mode prefers MP3 last; Manage room → Outputs has "Estimated MP3 delay (ms)"; `server-switches` has `outputs.mp3`. The ETS2/ATS how-to (`live_streams.sii` line, index/count, file location, HTTPS note) is in `docs/user-guide.md#internet-radio-mp3`. Note for M7's Docker image: its ffmpeg needs libmp3lame. Smoke-tested with the real server (SQLite, idle room): `curl` with `Icy-MetaData: 1` got the ICY headers and `StreamTitle='';` at byte 16000, ffprobe saw mp3 44100 Hz stereo 128 kb/s, decode clean, the token never in the log.
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; new `Mp3FramesTest`, `IcyWriterTest`, `Mp3EncoderTest` (real ffmpeg: valid MP3 from byte 0 + ffprobe, titles switching at 4 s and to empty for fillers, restart after a packager restart keeps the listener, a stalled listener dropped while another keeps going, 2 s burst for a late listener, close; plus a 24 s CBR-padded harness session through the encoder that decodes cleanly), `Mp3EncodersTest` (one encoder per room, linger stop, stop), `LiveMp3ApiTest` (headers, close-delimited, frames from the first byte, ICY framing at exactly 16000 with the title change and no repeats, two listeners one encoder, HEAD starts nothing, 404s, 503 without ffmpeg, revocation, per-token cap, per-IP limit, tokens not logged); updated `StreamOutputsApiTest` (mp3 URL, rotate, order, server switch), `OutputStatusFeedTest`, `RoomSettingsTest`, `RoomsApiTest`, `SettingsContract` (via `SqliteSettingsTest`), `PermissionTest`, `RolesContract`, `DtoIpLeakTest`, `outputs.test.mjs`, `remote.test.mjs`, `roomsettings.test.mjs`. Then the whole unit suite, `mvn -o test`: 1317 tests pass. No ITs (no schema change).

Have an option to automatically turn off after 10 minutes, or if it should remain always-on. Room owner can set it. Admin can force the auto-turn-off setting on." Siggi then chose: private rooms keep tokens; the 10 minutes start from an empty queue **or** no listeners, whichever comes first). Comes after the MP3 output step.
  - **URLs:** for **public and unlisted** rooms, the pull outputs use the room slug: `/live/{slug}.ts`, `/live/{slug}/index.m3u8` (and its segments), `/live/{slug}.mp3`. **Private rooms keep random token URLs** (as today, with Rotate) so their streams stay private. Changing visibility switches the scheme at once (to private: slug URLs stop and open slug connections end, tokens are issued; to public/unlisted: slug URLs start, token URLs stop). A slug change ends connections on the old slug (no redirect; VRChat players don't follow them reliably) and the panel shows the new URLs. Slugs and tokens can't collide (tokens are long random strings; slugs follow the slug rules: check). Rate limits and per-URL connection caps stay. Invariant 6 still applies to tokens (and RTMP keys); slugs aren't secrets. Rotate only appears for private rooms. Update `CLAUDE.md` invariant 6's wording if needed ("Stream URL tokens (private rooms) and RTMP keys …").

While on, an active output is turned **off** (like the owner pressing Turn off; stays off until the owner turns it on, with the "Stopped after N minutes …" notice and system audit as the RTMP idle stop does today) once **either** (a) the room has been idle (nothing playing, nothing queued) for the limit, **or** (b) for `.ts`/HLS/MP3, nobody has listened for the limit (no open `.ts`/MP3 connection and no HLS request; the timer also runs from the moment the output is turned on, so an unused output turns off too). RTMP uses (a) only (it has no listeners of its own). Each output has its own timer for (b); (a) is per room.

- [x] Short first GOP: every video start change is a smart re-encode (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: asked whether the first segment could be shorter than 2 s, like the last one, so the smart re-encoder needn't fully re-encode off-grid starts; "Let's do it"). **Relax invariant 2** to: the first GOP starts at 0 with an IDR and may be **shorter than 2 s** (at least one frame, at most 60 frames); after it, an IDR every 2 s exactly (60 frames), and the last GOP may be short as before. Update CLAUDE.md invariant 2 and `docs/` wording to match.
  - **Data:** the first GOP's frame count per media version (e.g. `media_versions.first_gop_frames` and `items.first_gop_frames`, default 60; migration for both databases; restored by undo). Fresh full encodes keep 60 (the normal grid from 0); only smart trims produce a short first GOP. The index (or what reads it) must not assume GOP k starts at frame 60·k: check `UnitBuilder`, the mid-item join/seek lookup (`ProgramMedia`, the room's join at a position), HLS segmenting, the item page's preview/seek, and anything else computing GOP positions from time; use the stored first-GOP length (or the index's IDR positions).
  - **Encode check:** the verify step ("IDR every 2 s from 0") becomes "IDR at 0, then at `first_gop_frames + 60·k`", all other checks unchanged. The packager emits the short first unit like a short last one (timestamps continuous, invariant 3; audio for the unit per `UnitBuilder`'s rules: check the unit's audio covers exactly its video span and the audio-frame spacing stays 1920 ticks).
  - **Trim planning** (`TrimPlan` and `web/src/trim.ts`): the current encode's full-GOP IDRs (after its first GOP) are at `videoStartMs + firstGop + 2 s·k` in original time. **One rule for any start change:** let `S` be the new start and `B` the first of those IDRs at or after `S` that the current encode still has (for a start earlier than the current start, the IDR that ends the current first GOP). If `S` is itself one of them, cut only. Otherwise re-encode `[S, B)` from the original as a short first GOP of `(B − S) mod 2 s` (a whole 2 s if 0) followed by whole 2 s GOPs, and keep the current GOPs from `B` on. The current short first GOP is never kept in the middle of a stream (only 2 s GOPs mid-stream). End earlier/later as now. Full re-encodes remain only as the fallback. The frame mapping (`fps=30` on the original's timeline) must line up at the seams as today (PSNR check).
  - **UI:** remove the "Snap to keyframes" tickbox and its snapping code (Siggi: not needed anymore); handles move freely (frame-accurate), and the hint shows "Smart re-encode: about N s" (or "No video re-encode") for every start change.
  - Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (trim rules), `docs/program-output.md` (first unit may be short), `docs/data-model.md`. Tests: `TrimTest` new cases (start later off grid, start earlier off grid, two successive off-grid trims, undo across them, a system re-encode afterwards gives a normal 60-frame first GOP), each with avcC, IDR positions, frame count, audio length and PSNR against a full encode of the range; `TrimPlanTest` and `trim.test.mjs` for the new plans; the packager plays an item with a short first GOP joined at the start and mid-item, with constant-rate padding and continuous timestamps (`UnitBuilderTest` or a program-output test); the verify step rejects a short GOP anywhere but first/last.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `b6cb115`. **Invariant 2** relaxed in CLAUDE.md and docs (README, library-and-ingest, program-output, overlay, data-model, api). **Data (deviation):** the first GOP is stored as `firstGopFrames` in the version's edit settings (`media_versions.edit_json`, absent = 60; `EditSettings.firstGopFrames`/`withFirstGop`) instead of new columns: no migration, undo restores it with the version for free, every full encode (`IngestPipeline.run`) drops it, an audio-only trim keeps it. The smart trim itself reads the current first GOP from the index (`IngestPipeline.firstGop`: the second IDR; 60 if none), which is authoritative; the item page's `trim` view gained `firstGopFrames` for `trim.ts`. **Plan** (`TrimPlan`, mirrored by `trim.ts`'s `estimate`): candidates = frames *f* + 60·*j* (and 0 when *f* = 60); *B* = the first with round(*B* − (*S* − curStart)·30/1000) ≥ 0, *h* that value; a head `ENCODE` piece of *h* frames from *S* with first GOP *h* mod 60 (60 if 0), then `REUSE` from *B* (first GOP *f* only when *S* = curStart), then the end-later tail from the last GOP start (*f* + 60·*k*). `Piece` is now `(source, from, frames, startUs, firstGop)` (µs on the original; REUSE `from` = current frame). Full only when nothing can be kept or the start is past the last keyframe. **Encode:** `EncodeProfile.videoArgs(encoding, preset, firstGopFrames)` changes only `-force_key_frames` to `expr:gte(n+60−f,n_forced*60)` (60 gives the old list exactly; checked with x264: IDRs at 0, 7, 67, 127 for *f* = 7 with `-g 60 -keyint_min 60`). **Verify:** `IngestVerifier.checkKeyframes(index, file, firstGop)` (IDR at 0 and *f* + 60·*k*, the IDR at *f* at *f*/30 s, later ones 2 s apart); the smart trim verifies each piece and the result with the plan's first GOP. **Pre-existing bug found and fixed:** the concat demuxer (ffmpeg 6.1.1) put the SPS/PPS in-band into the first frame of every piece, so the frame-size check failed and **every smart trim had silently fallen back to a full re-encode** (the old `TrimTest` couldn't tell, since the fallback is also valid); the splice now uses `-auto_convert 0`, and `TrimTest` fails on a fallback. The reuse copy seeks to the keyframe time rounded up to the µs (an off-grid keyframe like 7/30 s would otherwise seek to the one before). **Output:** nothing to change (units run IDR to IDR, joins use the index's keyframes, padding is per frame); Javadoc of `UnitBuilder`/`Unit`/`HlsSegmenter` updated. **UI:** "Snap to keyframes" and `snapToGrid`/`onGrid` removed; handles move freely, arrows step one frame; the hint's "full" line now says nothing can be kept. Also classified the previous step's `OutputAutoOff` records in `DtoIpLeakTest` (it failed on them). The relayed request in this run ("allow non-admins to see encodes for items in their own library", "prioritise items in a queue") is not part of this step and was not done.

- [x] Skip-frame cards: one keyframe plus 59 written "nothing changed" frames (added 2026-11-10, Siggi: "Is it possible to encode just a single frame, and then inject 59 frames that say 'nothing changed'?" → "Yes, add this"). **Problem found:** cards are encoded like video (`-b:v R -maxrate R`, R = 3000k), and x264's ABR keeps refining the still picture: real cover-art cards on Siggi's server measure 800–870 kb/s of video (a ~25 KB IDR, then P-frames growing from 0.8 to 3.9 KB), above the 800 kb/s `output.audioOnlyBitrateKbps` target, so an audio-only room's stream runs over its target and isn't constant-rate (the padder only adds). `AudioOnlyRoomOutputTest` passed because its test cards are tiny.
  - **Cards become:** x264 encodes **only the IDR** (one frame, same shared argument constant and preset table, so the SPS/PPS stay byte-identical: invariant 1; it can use the whole 2 s budget for that frame's quality, within the cap), and the other 59 frames are **P_Skip frames written by our own code** (not encoded: no pixels are processed): a P slice per frame with every macroblock skipped, `frame_num` counting up, `nal_ref_idc`/`dec_ref_pic_marking`, POC fields per the SPS (`pic_order_cnt_type`), `num_ref_idx` override, deblocking fields per the PPS, `slice_qp_delta` 0. **CABAC:** the PPS has `entropy_coding_mode_flag` 1 at our profile, so the slice data is `mb_skip_flag` = 1 for every macroblock (with `end_of_slice_flag`) through a small CABAC arithmetic encoder (context init per `cabac_init_idc` and the slice QP, H.264 §9.3); handle CAVLC too (`mb_skip_run` = all macroblocks) if the PPS ever says so. Parse the needed fields from our own SPS/PPS rather than hard-coding them, and write it in the ingest/media code at card creation time (the packager still never encodes or writes slices).
  - **Applies to** every card: item cards (audio items, audio-only rooms, `presentAsAudio`), the filler cards (idle, loading, paused) and the reference card; M5's overlay cards will reuse it where the overlay is static (note under M5). Card files keep their layout (one closed 60-frame GOP, IDR every 2 s), and `CardGop` and the packager need no change beyond what tests show.
  - **Verify** (at card creation, as the encode check does): decode the card with ffmpeg and check all 60 frames are pixel-identical to the IDR (frame checksums), the avcC equals the reference, 60 frames at 30 fps, one IDR. If writing skip frames fails for any reason, fall back to the current full x264 card encode with a log line, so a card is never missing.
  - **Re-encode:** bump the card format so existing cards are rebuilt (the encoding id's version or a card version: whatever makes `OutputAssets`/ingest regenerate filler cards and item cards without re-encoding whole videos; item cards are cheap). Siggi's server has 14 re-encodes queued; they should produce the new cards.
  - Docs: `docs/program-output.md` (cards), `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (card creation). Tests: the skip-frame writer against both 720p30 and 1080p30 SPS/PPS and each `X264Preset` (decodes with ffmpeg, pixel-identical, frame sizes tiny: e.g. < 100 bytes each); a card from real cover art (a detailed photo) is well under the audio-only rate (e.g. < 200 kb/s on average over the GOP); an audio-only room with real-cover-art cards streams at a constant 800 kb/s (extend `AudioOnlyRoomOutputTest` with a detailed cover); the fallback path; the filler cards rebuilt; splicing a card between video items still plays (existing packager tests).
  - **Done 2026-11-10.** `media.SkipFrames` parses the SPS/PPS (scaling lists, POC types 0/2 and 1 with always-zero deltas; refuses interlaced, slice groups, separate colour planes) and writes each frame: `nal_ref_idc` 2, `frame_num = i mod MaxFrameNum`, POC lsb `2i` for type 0, `num_ref_idx_active_override` to 1, empty `pred_weight_table` when `weighted_pred_flag` (x264 sets it at our presets), sliding window, `cabac_init_idc` 0, `slice_qp_delta` 0, `disable_deblocking_filter_idc` 1; CABAC slice data with ctxIdx 11 (m 23, n 33) and the §9.3.4 encoder (`end_of_slice_flag` by terminate, flush writes the stop bit), CAVLC as one `mb_skip_run`; emulation prevention applied. `CardEncoder`: the IDR comes from `-frames:v 1 … -f h264`, the GOP is remuxed with `-bsf:v setts=pts=N:dts=N:duration=1:time_base=1/30 -video_track_timescale 15360` (byte-identical layout to the old cards' time base, no "timestamps unset" warning), then `CardEncoder.check` decodes it (`-err_detect explode`, `framemd5`: 60 identical frames) and checks the packets (one IDR first, `n·512/15360`); any failure falls back to the full x264 card with a log line. Measured: skip frames 35 bytes (720p) / 67 bytes (1080p) in the MP4; a detailed photo-like cover card 113 kb/s at 720p (IDR 26 KB), 280 kb/s at 1080p (IDR 66 KB); with full x264 cards the same detailed-cover audio-only session ran ~+198 % over 800 kb/s.
  - **Card format bump:** `CardEncoder.CARD_VERSION` = 2. Filler cards: fingerprint `card v2`, so startup rebuilds them. Reference card: `reference.mp4` is now replaced by the fresh card at every start when the stored avcC is still reproduced (it was only written when missing). Items: new column `card_version` on `items` and `media_versions` (migration 0018, existing rows 1; part of `VERSION_COLUMNS`, so undo restores it). Full encodes record 2 (`published(…, cardVersion)`); smart trims and audio-trim-only publishes keep the stored value (they link the old card). At startup `IngestJobs.enqueueCardRemakes` queues a `reencode` job for every ready item of the current encoding with `card_version < 2` (deviation: no new job kind; it shows as a re-encode in the jobs page and its log says "card only"); `IngestPipeline.remakeCard` re-renders the card from `cover.jpg` and the current metadata, rebuilds `index.bin` (audio items) and `index-audio-only.bin` (videos with an audio trim), hard-links everything else into a new version, and switches with `IngestItems.cardRemade` (system change: previous version kept without Undo); incomplete files fall back to a full re-encode. Items of another encoding (Siggi's 14 queued re-encodes) get their full re-encode, which records card format 2.

- [x] Insecure (plain HTTP) endpoint for streaming only (added 2026-11-10, Siggi). **Found in the manual MP3 check:** ETS2's audio library (`FMODStudio/2.03.11` user agent) has no TLS: given an `https://` URL it connects to **port 80 over plain HTTP** (proxy log: `mbp.siggi.io (80): GET /live/sync-test.mp3`), so the "current versions support HTTPS" assumption was wrong. Siggi configured SimpleHttpProxy to proxy port 80 to TogetherTunes as well, and the stream then played. Siggi: "We should have an additional config field for insecure endpoint, it should redirect to HTTPS with a 301 for all requests except for streaming endpoints."
  - **Config:** `http.insecureBaseUrl` (config-only like `http.publicBaseUrl`, env `TOGETHERTUNES_HTTP_INSECURE_BASE_URL`; default unset = feature off; must be an `http://` URL, validated at startup with exit code 1 on a bad value; `config.example.json`, `--help`).
  - **Upstream fix (2026-11-10, SiggiHTTP session):** `isSecure()` now trusts `X-Forwarded-Proto` only from a trusted peer (loopback, Cloudflare, or `isIPTrusted`; same helper as `X-Forwarded-For`), installed in `1.11-SNAPSHOT` (commit `1a5aabb`). SiggiHTTP's defaults also trust 10/8, 172.16/12 and 192.168/16: make sure TogetherTunes decides "came over plain HTTP" with **its own `trustedProxies` config** (as it does for client IPs), e.g. by untrusting SiggiHTTP's default ranges that aren't in `trustedProxies` or by checking the peer against `trustedProxies` itself, so a LAN client that isn't a configured proxy can't claim HTTPS (or HTTP) with the header.
  - **Redirect:** when set, every request that arrived over plain HTTP (`Request` already knows: the connection, or `X-Forwarded-Proto` from a trusted proxy) gets **`301`** to the same path and query on `http.publicBaseUrl` (`Location`, empty body, `Connection: close` is fine), **except the streaming endpoints**, which are served as normal: `GET`/`HEAD` `/live/{slug}.mp3`, `/live/{slug}.ts`, `/live/{slug}/index.m3u8` and its segments, **for public and unlisted rooms only (slug URLs)**. Private rooms' token URLs are not served over HTTP (the token is a capability and would travel unencrypted: invariant 6): they get the 301 too, and nothing ever offers them as `http://`. WebSocket upgrades, `/api/*`, pages, `/media/*` all redirect. When `http.insecureBaseUrl` is unset, behaviour over plain HTTP stays as today (decide and note whether today's behaviour should already redirect; don't break local `http://localhost` testing setups where `publicBaseUrl` itself is `http://`: if `publicBaseUrl` is `http://`, never redirect).
  - **Outputs panel / API:** for public/unlisted rooms with `http.insecureBaseUrl` set, the outputs view gives each pull output an extra `insecureUrl` (`http://…` slug URL) and the panel shows it with Copy under the normal URL, in generic words ("For players that can't use HTTPS"; no game names: CLAUDE.md). Private rooms: no `insecureUrl`.
  - **Docs:** correct the MP3 step's HTTPS assumption in `docs/program-output.md` (MP3 output: some internet-radio players have no TLS and ignore the scheme; serve streaming over HTTP via `http.insecureBaseUrl` and the proxy), `docs/architecture.md`/config docs (the new key, what is and isn't served over HTTP), `docs/api.md` (`insecureUrl`). The external how-to (M7 user guide/deployment notes) notes that ETS2/ATS need the `http://` URL and the proxy must forward port 80. Note in CLAUDE.md's Commands/config section that `http.insecureBaseUrl` exists.

Record: migration 0019 `stream_outputs.token_cleartext_at` (both databases); `StreamOutputs.markTokenCleartext(kind, token)` sets it only for the current token of a private room's output of that kind and only once; `rotate` and `reissueTokens` clear it. `output/CleartextTokens` is called by `LiveTsApi`, `LiveMp3Api` and `LiveHlsApi` right after the key resolved (GET and HEAD) when `!request.isSecure()`; it remembers handled kind/key pairs in memory so HLS doesn't write per request (a failed write is retried); logs room and output id, never the token. **Decided:** the flag is recorded whenever a token arrives over plain HTTP, also without `http.insecureBaseUrl` (local `http://` setups then see the warning after first use: it's true there too).   - **Done 2026-11-10** at main `25f8e8b`. `Config.insecureBaseUrl` (`ConfigLoader.parseInsecureBaseUrl`: the `publicBaseUrl` rules plus `http://` only; bad value → exit 1), `Config.redirectPlainHttp()` = key set AND `publicBaseUrl` `https://` (with an `http://` public URL the key only logs a startup warning). `Router.filter(Filter)`: filters run for every request before the route result is used (so 404/405 paths redirect too); `http/PlainHttpRedirect` is that filter, installed by `Main` only when `redirectPlainHttp()`: `!request.isSecure()` → `301`, `Location` = `publicBaseUrl` + the raw request target (`Request.rawTarget()`, path and query as sent), `Content-Length: 0`; no `Connection: close` (SiggiHTTP manages keep-alive, a hand-set header would disagree with it). Exemption `output/PlainHttpStreams`: `GET`/`HEAD` of `/live/{key}.ts|.mp3`, `/live/{key}/index.m3u8`, `/live/{key}/seg/{file}` where the key is the slug of a non-private room (one `SELECT 1 FROM rooms WHERE slug = ? AND visibility <> 'private'`; a failed lookup redirects; whether the output is on is the stream handler's business, as over HTTPS). The Location may carry a private token (the client sent it); never logged. **Unset = no redirects** (decided: the server can't tell deliberate plain-HTTP setups from an HTTPS-only proxy, and for the latter port 80 never reaches it anyway). Outputs API: `insecureUrl` for ts/HLS/MP3 of public/unlisted rooms when redirecting is on; the panel shows it under the URL with "For players that can't use HTTPS:" and Copy (`outputs.ts` type, `outputspanel.ts`). `config.example.json` has `"insecureBaseUrl": null`; `--help` and the startup line mention it.

The proxy (SimpleHttpProxy) sets `X-Forwarded-Proto` to `http`/`https` per listener, so behind it HTTPS requests are not redirected.

- [x] Item pages ask for a missing `cover.jpg` (added 2026-11-10, found in the proxy log): the room page requests `/media/<id>/v/<n>/cover.jpg` for items whose media version has no `cover.jpg` (e.g. items `01a1c2fe-121a-74ee-ae2c-143f98971e63` and `01a1c337-8d2e-741d-95d2-53a49eac71e0` on Siggi's server, v2 and v3 have only `audio.m4a`, `card.mp4`, `index.bin`, `mezz.mp4`), getting 404s. Find out why (no cover expected for these items and the view still gives a cover URL, or a re-encode/version path — card-only re-encode, trim, undo, versioned media — that doesn't link `cover.jpg` into the new version although the item has one), fix it so views only give a `coverUrl` that exists and every new version carries the cover over, and repair existing versions on startup if a version path dropped it (relink from the newest version that has one, or regenerate). Tests for the cause found.
  - Done 2026-11-10 at main `2458bdf`. **Cause:** no cover was ever expected for those items. All four (`01a1c2fd…` click, `01a1c2fe-011a…` noise, `01a1c2fe-121a…` detrimental, `01a1c337…` justfortonight) are uploads whose source has no attached picture and came without a staged `cover.<ext>` (the first upload job's probe shows only the audio and video streams), so their versions never had a `cover.jpg`; every YouTube item's versions have one. Every version path already carries the cover over (full re-encode reuses the current `cover.jpg` as staged cover, smart trim, audio-trim-only and card-only link it, undo links all files of the kept version), so nothing dropped it and no startup repair was added. The bug was the views: `RoomViews.item` gave `coverUrl` for every ready item and `item.ts` always requested `cover.jpg` (hiding the image on error). **Fix:** new `library/Covers` (`exists(itemId, version)`: one `stat` of the version's `cover.jpg`, no cache, since version numbers may restart after a purge); `RoomViews.lookup` fills `ItemRow.hasCover` (wired through `RoomSocket.covers`, default `Covers.ALWAYS` for tests without a data dir) and `coverUrl` is given only then; `GET /api/items/{id}` gains `hasCover` (ready and the current version has the file) and the item page sets `src` only then. `docs/api.md` updated. Tests: `RoomViewsTest` (no `coverUrl` without a cover, `Covers` against a temp data dir), `ItemsContract#hasCoverFollowsTheCurrentVersionsCoverFile` (SQLite + `PostgresItemsIT`), `ItemsApiTest` expectation; ran `mvn package -DskipTests`, `-Dtest='RoomViewsTest,SqliteItemsTest,DtoIpLeakTest,WebBuildTest,ItemsApiTest,RoomSocketTest'` and `-Dit.test=PostgresItemsIT`.
- [x] Delayed start after a skip, so the stream doesn't lose the next item's start (added 2026-11-10, Siggi, from the VRChat skip check: "sometimes when skipping an item, it will skip the first bit of the next item"; chose the delayed start as an M4 step). **Cause:** a skip starts the next item on the timeline at once; the packager switches at its next unit boundary and joins the item at the timeline's position there (invariant 4), so the stream loses the first 0–2 s (plus LEAD) while web listeners hear it from 0.
  - **Fix on the timeline (it stays the only source of truth):** when an item is started **abruptly** (skip/next, jumping to a queue entry or "play now", starting playback from idle when the first item is added, and anything else that isn't a natural end of the previous item), the room schedules the new entry's start slightly in the future instead of now, so the program output reaches it at a unit boundary and plays it from 0:00 together with everyone else. Natural item ends, seeks and pause/play are unchanged.
  - **How far ahead:** only while the room's program output is running (any output with consumers or an RTMP push); otherwise start at once as today (no gap for web-only rooms). While it runs, use the earliest moment the output can start the item from 0: the start of the first unit it hasn't built yet (the packager knows it: next boundary + LEAD), plus a small margin, capped (e.g. at 4 s); if that can't be determined, a room setting `output.skipStartDelayMs` (default 3000, 0 disables the whole feature, owner-editable in Manage room → Settings, admin-capped if that fits the settings model) is used. Decide whether the setting is needed at all once the exact value is available, and note it.
  - **Between the skip and the start:** the timeline shows the new entry as current with a start time in the future (or a short "starting" state: pick whichever the sync engine, `RoomState` and the web player already handle best; a future start time with a negative position is the simplest if clients already clamp). Web clients show the new item's title at once and start its audio at the scheduled time (silence before); the program output plays the previous source or a filler until the unit at which the item starts (no Loading card flash if avoidable: the item is usually preloaded). Remote mode's delay estimates unaffected. A second skip during the gap skips again (same rule).
  - Docs: `docs/rooms.md` (timeline: abrupt starts), `docs/program-output.md` (timeline following: skips land at 0:00), `docs/api.md` if a setting or field is added. Tests: a skip while the output runs starts the next item at a future time and the output's first unit of it starts at item position 0 (frame/sample-exact, `ProgramOutputsRoomsTest` or a packager test with `ScriptedTimeline`), web clients and the output agree on the item's start; without a running output the skip is immediate; jump-to-entry and start-from-idle behave the same; a double skip; natural ends unchanged; the sync engine / roomclient tests for a future start (DOM-free modules).
  - **Done 2026-11-10** at main `7df9cab`. **Room:** `RoomTx.abruptStartAt()` asks the new `PackagerHook.itemStartAt(roomId, now)` (implemented by `ProgramOutputs`: the room's running packager's `Packager.nextItemStartServerMs(250)`, read under the cue feed's monitor only, never waits) and caps it at `RoomTx.MAX_START_DELAY_MS` (4 s); `now` without a running packager. `Timeline.startingAt(entryId, startServerMs)` (playing, anchor in the future, position 0). Abrupt: `Playback.advance(tx, SKIPPED)` (skip, vote-skip, failed/purged current), `startIfIdle` (`advance(tx, PLAYED, true)`: start from idle, play from idle), `QueueOps.previous` to an earlier entry while playing. Natural ends (`advanceIfDue`), seeks, pause/play and restarting the current entry via `previous` stay at `now`. There is no "jump to entry"/"play now" operation yet; a future one should use `abruptStartAt()` too. **Packager:** `nextItemStartServerMs(marginMs)`: the start of the unit the thread waits to build, or one unit (`UNIT_TICKS`) later while a unit is being built or due within the margin; empty when stopped. **Follower:** in `follow`, a playing cue whose position at the switch point is below −`START_EARLY_MS` (1 s, half a unit) and that isn't a natural advance (`playedOut == null`) keeps what plays (the skipped item or the filler; Loading if the skipped item ended in the gap); otherwise it starts from max(0, pos), and from 0 when the anchor position is 0 and pos < 100 ms (the switch point is up to one audio frame past the aimed boundary). **Web:** `listensync.msUntilStart(t, now)`; the listen player treats the entry as not playing (silent at 0) until then, schedules a tick at the start, and "Click to listen" doesn't start it early. Remote mode unchanged (future anchors are dated at arrival; the delayed position stays 0 until the start).
  - **Setting not added:** `output.skipStartDelayMs` isn't needed: while an output runs the exact start is always known (the packager's next unit), and without one starts are immediate. "Running" = the room's packager runs (it stops 60 s after its last consumer unless an RTMP push keeps it), so a skip within that minute after the last listener left is still put off (by at most ~3 s); judged harmless.
  - **Invariant 4:** the room now reads the output's schedule (when its next unit is); the output still never changes the timeline. Noted in `PackagerHook` and `docs/rooms.md`.
  - Existing test changed: `TimelineFollowerTest.outputBehindKeepsPlayingItsItemToTheEnd` used a future anchor to fake an output that started early; now anchored 0.9 s ahead (within `START_EARLY_MS`), same assertions.
  - Docs: `docs/rooms.md` (Room state: "Abrupt starts wait for the program output"; "Program output and the timeline" bullet), `docs/program-output.md` (Applying timeline changes: "Skips land at 0:00"). No API change (the `timeline` shape is the same; `anchorServerMs` may now be in the future).
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `TimelineFollowerTest` (new: `aDelayedSkipStartsTheNewItemFromZeroAtItsStartTime` (skipped item plays on, the new item's first unit has its first picture and AAC frame at the timeline's start time to the audio frame, contiguous), `delayedStartsOffTheBoundariesAndDoubleSkipsAndStartsFromIdle`, `aDelayedStartAfterTheSkippedItemEndsBridgesWithLoading`), `PackagerTest#nextItemStartIsTheFirstUnitNotBegun`, `ProgramOutputsRoomsTest#skipsWaitForTheRunningOutput` (cue carries the future start; immediate once the output stopped), `SqlitePlaybackTest` + `PostgresPlaybackIT` (`abruptStartsWaitForTheProgramOutput`: skip, double skip, pause in the gap, natural end immediate, cap, start from idle, no output immediate), `ListenJsTest` (`msUntilStart` unit tests in `listensync.test.mjs`), then the `rooms.**` and `output.**` packages plus `RemoteJsTest` and `WebBuildTest`: all pass.
  - Worth a look on the next VRChat skip check: skipping should now start the next item from its first second in the stream (the skipped item plays on for about 1–3 s first), with web listeners starting it at the same moment.

## M5 — QR overlay

- [x] Skip-frame cards with other ffmpeg versions (M4 follow-up, placed in M5 because Gate M4 had already passed; added 2026-11-10, found by Siggi deploying a demo in a Docker container with the **latest static build from FFmpeg-Builds** (github.com/BtbN/FFmpeg-Builds releases, the archive's sha256 `45eff5bb8fd14ea3e7df1ec5dd5c3af3f661770739e23d57b92bbb6cdc75d1d9`; find the matching release asset by that hash, likely a `master`/`n8.x` GPL Linux build). On startup: `WARNING: Skip-frame card failed for .reference-….mp4; encoding the whole GOP with x264 instead: the card decodes to 2 frames, not 60`. The fallback works, but full-x264 cards break the constant rate in audio-only rooms, so skip-frame cards must work on current ffmpeg.
  - **Reproduce** with that exact build in a Linux container (Docker is available locally; use the build's architecture, or the arm64/amd64 sibling from the same release if emulation is too slow, and say which). Find which part differs: the IDR-only encode (`-frames:v 1 … -f h264`), our written P_Skip slices (does the newer decoder reject or drop them, e.g. with `-err_detect explode`?), the remux (`-bsf:v setts=pts=N:dts=N:duration=1:time_base=1/30`, `-video_track_timescale 15360`: a changed `setts` or muxer behaviour giving duplicate timestamps would explain "2 frames"), or the check itself (`framemd5` frame counting, e.g. a changed default `-fps_mode`/vsync dropping frames). Fix the cause in a way that works on every version below, not by special-casing one (e.g. write the GOP's MP4 boxes ourselves with the existing box writer instead of an ffmpeg remux, or pass explicit `-fps_mode passthrough` to the check, whichever the cause calls for).
  - **Version matrix** (test in containers, the same `SkipFrameCardTest`-style checks: 60 pixel-identical frames, one IDR, reference avcC, timing): ffmpeg **6.1** (Ubuntu 24.04, as on Siggi's Mac), **5.1** (Debian 12), **7.x** (a release build), and the **latest FFmpeg-Builds** build above. Record the results in the step notes and `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (cards). A minimum supported ffmpeg version, if one emerges, goes into the deployment notes (M7) and is checked at startup (`ffmpeg -version`, a clear warning or error).
  - **Startup:** if the skip-frame card check fails for the reference card at startup, log once, clearly, that this ffmpeg can't make skip-frame cards and what that costs (audio-only rooms' cards run over the audio-only rate), instead of only the per-card warning.
  - **Done 2026-11-10** at main `3adaff1`. **Build:** the hash matches `ffmpeg-master-latest-linux64-gpl.tar.xz` of FFmpeg-Builds' `latest` release (Auto-Build 2026-11-10 12:39, `ffmpeg version N-128728-g49a3b1f259-20261110`); reproduced with that exact amd64 archive (emulated on the arm64 Mac) and its arm64 sibling. **Cause: the remux.** The IDR encode, our P_Skip slices and the check were fine (the Annex B GOP decodes to 60 identical frames on every version); current builds ignore `setts`' `time_base` option and read the expressions in the input time base, so `pts=N` put frame `n` at `n/15360 s` (packets 0, 1, 2, …), and `framemd5`'s default frame-rate handling kept 2 frames. A first fix with the expressions in the input time base (`setts=pts=N/(30*TB):…`, no `time_base`) worked on 6.1 and master but not on 5.1 (it wrote them as 1/15360 ticks: frame 1 at 40000/15360), so no `setts` form was trusted to work everywhere. **Fix (deviation from "remux" to "own writer", as the step suggested):** new `media.StillGopMp4` turns ffmpeg's one-frame MP4 (the IDR encode is now `-video_track_timescale 15360 -f mp4`) into the 60-frame card: every box copied byte for byte (whole `stsd`, so the avcC is this ffmpeg's own: invariant 1) except durations (`mvhd`/`tkhd`/`mdhd`/`elst`) and the sample tables (`stts` 60 × the IDR's duration, `stss` 1, `stsc`, `stsz`, `stco`), laid out `ftyp`, `mdat`, `moov`; it refuses fragmented files, more than one track/sample, a shifting edit list and unknown `stbl` boxes (→ fallback). `CardEncoder.skipFrameCard` replaces `skipFrameGop`/`remuxCommand`/`nalUnits`; `CardEncoder.check` adds `-fps_mode passthrough` (counts every decoded frame; the packet-time check catches bad timing). **Card format 3** (`CARD_VERSION`, filler fingerprint `card v3`, items' card-only re-encodes, overlay card names `-c3`) so servers whose format-2 cards fell back (Siggi's demo) get skip-frame cards; on Siggi's main server this queues card-only re-encodes once (cheap). **Startup:** `CardEncoder.encodeCard` returns whether it made a skip-frame card; `ReferenceCard.ensure` logs `noSkipFrameCardsMessage` once per process when the reference card fell back (what it costs: audio-only rooms over `output.audioOnlyBitrateKbps`).
  - **Version matrix** (`SkipFrameCardTest` 30, `CardEncoderTest` 7, `ReferenceCardTest` 8, run with `ffmpeg`/`ffprobe` wrappers that `docker run` the version on the same paths): 5.1.9 (Debian 12), 6.1.1 (Ubuntu 24.04; also macOS), 7.1.5 (Debian 13; FFmpeg-Builds' `latest` has no 7.x any more), FFmpeg-Builds n8.1.3, n9.0.2 and master, all arm64 natively, plus the reported amd64 master archive under emulation: **all pass**. Before the fix master failed with exactly the reported "2 frames" (the original remux was otherwise only tried on 6.1, where it worked). No minimum version emerged; the check's `-fps_mode` needs ≥ 5.1, and anything older falls back with the startup warning (for the M7 deployment notes: "ffmpeg 5.1 or newer"; no startup version check added since nothing tested needs one). **Also seen:** the avcC differs between ≤ 6.1 and ≥ 7.1 (one VUI byte; same for every image), so moving a library across that line gives the existing "no longer reproduces the reference" warning and re-encode, as for any encoder change. Jar smoke test with the reported amd64 build: fresh data dir, no warnings, reference + 3 filler cards are skip-frame cards (59 × 35 bytes), `/api/me` 200, stopped.
  - Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md#cards` (command, `StillGopMp4`, why not a remux, version matrix, `-fps_mode`, startup warning, format 3), `docs/data-model.md` (`card_version` 3).
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `SkipFrameCardTest` (card helper now via `skipFrameCard` and checks the avcC equals ffmpeg's one-frame MP4's; new: skip-frame card reported as such, fallback reported, check rejects frames 1 tick apart (the master symptom), a 2-frame MP4 refused, the startup message), `CardEncoderTest` (`idrCommand` → MP4), `ReferenceCardTest`, `SkipFramesTest`, the whole `ingest.**`, `media.**`, `output.**`, `overlay.**`, `DtoIpLeakTest` (the `StillGopMp4` records classified internal), `ProductNamesTest`, `MainTest`; the matrix above.
- [x] Colour description in the SPS must not depend on the ffmpeg version (M4 follow-up, added 2026-11-11, Siggi's demo container with the latest FFmpeg-Builds static build, see the skip-frame step above). Every YouTube ingest failed: `mezz.mp4: avcC differs from the 720p30-3000k-v3 reference card (expected …d40808050000…, got …d40404050000…)`. Decoded: identical SPS except the VUI colour description: the **reference card has `colour_primaries` 2 and `transfer_characteristics` 2 (unspecified)**, the video has 1 and 1 (BT.709); matrix 1, limited range, chroma location the same in both. On ffmpeg 6.1 (Siggi's Mac) both come out 1/1/1. So on newer ffmpeg the output options `-color_primaries bt709 -color_trc bt709` (in `EncodeProfile`) no longer win over the frames' own colour properties: the YouTube frames carry BT.709, the card's image source carries unspecified primaries/transfer (its scale filter sets only the matrix and range).
  - **Fix** so the SPS colour description is the same on every ffmpeg version and for every source: set the frame properties at the end of every filter chain that feeds x264 (mezzanines, cards, overlay re-encodes: `setparams=range=tv:color_primaries=bt709:color_trc=bt709:colorspace=bt709`, which exists since ffmpeg 4.4/5.0; check) **and** force them in x264 itself (`-x264-params …:colorprim=bt709:transfer=bt709:colormatrix=bt709:range=tv`, and chroma location if it isn't already pinned), keeping the existing output options. Keep it in the shared argument constant / `EncodeProfile` (invariant 1). On ffmpeg 6.1 the result must be byte-identical to today's avcC (so no encoding-version bump and no re-encodes on existing servers): assert that against the current reference avcC in a test.
  - **Verify** in containers on the same version matrix as the skip-frame step (6.1, 5.1, 7.x, the latest FFmpeg-Builds build): a BT.709-tagged source, an untagged source, a BT.601/full-range source and a card all give the reference avcC. Also check the other VUI fields (`video_format`, `chroma_loc`, timing) for the same kind of drift and pin whatever differs.
  - **Startup self-check** (cheap insurance for future ffmpeg changes): after the reference card exists, encode a 2-frame BT.709-tagged test clip and an untagged one through the video path and compare their avcC with the reference; on a mismatch log one clear error naming the differing SPS fields (decode them, as above) and refuse ingest with a clear job error ("this ffmpeg produces encodes that can't be spliced: …") instead of failing every job at the end.
  - Docs: `docs/library-and-ingest.md` (encode profile: colour description pinned in x264), CLAUDE.md invariant 1 if the wording should mention it. Tests: the args contain the pins; the avcC equality per source kind (local ffmpeg); the container matrix results in the notes; the startup self-check's mismatch path.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `ab0ad4d`. **Reproduced** (720p30, the card command and a mezzanine-style encode of a BT.709-tagged, an untagged, a full-range BT.601 and a Motion JPEG source): before the fix, 5.1.9 and 6.1.1 gave the reference everywhere; 7.1.5, FFmpeg-Builds n8.1.3, n9.0.2 and master (arm64, and the reported amd64 archive) gave `d40808` (primaries/transfer 2) for the card and untagged video, `d40404` for the tagged video, `d41818` (6, SMPTE 170M) for BT.601, and on 7.1 Motion JPEG also a chroma location. So the "avcC differs between ≤ 6.1 and ≥ 7.1" seen in the skip-frame step was this bug. **Fix** in `EncodeProfile`: `COLOUR_FILTER` = `setparams=range=tv:color_primaries=bt709:color_trc=bt709:colorspace=bt709` appended to `scaleFilter` (exists on 5.1; the overlay re-encoder's chain must end with it too, noted in the docs), `X264_COLOUR` = `colorprim=bt709:transfer=bt709:colormatrix=bt709:range=tv:chromaloc=0` appended to `-x264-params`, output options kept. Tried separately on 7.1: the x264 params alone fix every case; `setparams` alone leaves the Motion JPEG chroma location. With both, every version above gives the same avcC for every source and the card, and it is **byte-identical to the pre-fix 6.1 reference** (720p `…d40404…`, 1080p `…b501010140…`, asserted in `ReferenceCardTest#theReferenceAvcCIsUnchanged`), so no encoding-version bump and no library re-encodes. Filler and overlay cards are re-encoded once (their fingerprint includes the argument text; cheap). Other VUI fields (`video_format` 5, SAR, timing 1/60, bitstream restriction) didn't drift. **Startup self-check:** `ReferenceCard.ensure` (when the stored reference still matches) runs `checkVideoPath`: 2 frames each of a BT.709-tagged, an untagged and a full-range BT.601 lavfi clip through `scaleFilter` + `videoArgs` (reference preset); on a mismatch it logs one ERROR ("This ffmpeg produces encodes that can't be spliced: a BT.709-tagged video is encoded with a different SPS than the 720p30-3000k-v3 reference card: colour_primaries 1 (BT.709) instead of 2 (unspecified), …") and `spliceProblem()` is set; `IngestPipeline` then fails video jobs right after the probe (and smart trims up front) with that text, keeping the upload; audio items still work. A clip that ffmpeg can't encode only logs a warning (the check passes). Checked by hand that these lavfi clips reproduce the drift on 7.1 with the old args. New `media.AvcCFields` decodes an avcC (whole SPS incl. VUI and HRD; PPS as hex) and lists differing fields. **Siggi's demo container:** its stored reference is the 7.x "unspecified" one, so after updating it gets the existing "no longer reproduces the reference" warning; delete `data/cards/720p30-3000k-v3/` there (and re-add anything ingested so far, or let the re-encodes run) to get the new reference.
  - **Version matrix** (`EncodeProfileTest` 15, `ReferenceCardTest` 11, `CardEncoderTest` 7, `SkipFrameCardTest` 30, with `ffmpeg`/`ffprobe` wrappers that `docker run` each version on the same paths): 5.1.9, 6.1.1 (container and macOS), 7.1.5, FFmpeg-Builds n8.1.3, n9.0.2, master (arm64) all pass; the reported amd64 master archive (emulated) passes `EncodeProfileTest` and `ReferenceCardTest`. Two test-harness fixes found on the way: `colourTagsOfTheSourceDontChangeTheExtradata` made its interlaced MPEG-2 source with `-top 1`, which master no longer has (now `-vf setfield=tff`); on 5.1 only, `EncodeProfileTest`'s `checkStructure` counts one frame too many because ffprobe 5.1's CSV frame list prints an extra blank side-data line (pre-existing, unrelated; the encodes have the right frame count, checked with `nb_read_frames`), left as is since 5.1 isn't the test platform.

Java2D text needs FreeType, fontconfig and at least one font even when headless. **Fix:** at startup, before anything renders, check that text rendering works (e.g. measure and draw a string into a small `BufferedImage` with `java.awt.headless=true` set by `Main` if unset) and on failure exit with code 3 and a clear message naming the missing pieces and the install lines (Debian/Ubuntu `libfreetype6 fontconfig fonts-dejavu-core`, Alpine `freetype fontconfig font-dejavu`, Fedora `freetype fontconfig dejavu-sans-fonts`); no stack trace. **Bundle DejaVu Sans** (Siggi, 2026-11-10: regular and bold, from the official DejaVu release; licence text in the jar, e.g. `META-INF/fonts/DejaVu-LICENSE`) and load it with `Font.createFont`, so cards look the same on every host and only FreeType is a system requirement (verify in a container whether fontconfig is then still needed). **Admin setting for a preferred font** (Siggi): `cards.font` in the admin panel: the default is the bundled DejaVu Sans; the admin can pick an installed system font family (list `GraphicsEnvironment.getAvailableFontFamilyNames()`, plus the bundled one) or upload a TTF/OTF (stored in the data dir, validated with `Font.createFont`, size limit). **Fallback chain per character run** (`Font.canDisplay`): the admin's font → bundled DejaVu Sans → downloaded script fonts → any installed system font that has the character, so titles never render as boxes when some font has the glyphs. **Script fonts on demand** (Siggi, 2026-11-10: "download fonts on demand for other common scripts as well, and not just CJK", plus a "download all" option): a manifest in the jar maps Unicode scripts (`Character.UnicodeScript`) to Noto fonts from their official sources (notofonts / noto-cjk GitHub releases, SIL OFL): at least CJK (SC, TC, JP, KR: pick the variant from the script, kana → JP, hangul → KR, Han alone → the admin's preferred CJK variant, default JP), Thai, Devanagari and other Indic scripts, Arabic (fuller than DejaVu), Hebrew, Ethiopic, Khmer, Lao, Myanmar, Sinhala, Tibetan, plus Noto Emoji (monochrome) and Noto Sans Symbols/Symbols 2; each entry pinned to a URL **and sha256**, with its size. Fonts go to `<data>/fonts/` with their licence; only manifest URLs are ever fetched (no user-supplied URLs), with the JDK HTTP client, a size limit and the hash checked before use. **On demand:** when a card (item, filler or overlay) needs a script that no available font covers and `fonts.autoDownload` (admin setting, default **on**) is set, the card is rendered with what is available now, the font download is queued (one at a time, deduplicated, retried with backoff on failure), and when it lands the affected cards are re-rendered (card-only re-encodes for the items whose titles use that script; the font set is part of the card version key). **Admin panel → Fonts:** each manifest font with its scripts, size and status (not downloaded / downloading / installed / failed with the error), Download / Remove per font, **Download all** (shows the total size first, e.g. a few hundred MB with CJK), the auto-download switch, the preferred CJK variant, the preferred card font (above), and a list of characters seen in titles that no available font covers (so a missing script is visible without guessing). Offline servers: the admin can also upload a font file (above) or install system fonts; docs say so. Changing it regenerates the filler cards and queues card-only re-encodes (the card format/version key includes a font hash), like other card changes. API + settings docs (`docs/api.md`, `docs/auth.md` server settings). Docs: deployment requirements (M7 notes), `docs/architecture.md` if it lists runtime requirements. Tests: the startup check's message path (simulate the failure), a container run without fonts/fontconfig if a bundled font is added.

Also runnable alone: `java -cp TogetherTunes.jar io.siggi.togethertunes.fonts.TextRenderingCheck`. **Container finding (`HeadlessFontsIT`, Temurin 21 JRE image with packages purged):** the bundled font does *not* make fontconfig or a system font optional: Java's font manager starts through fontconfig even for `Font.createFont` fonts ("Fontconfig head is null" without fontconfig or without any font). - [x] Overlay renderer: ZXing QR (level M, quiet zone 4) for `{publicBaseUrl}/r/{slug}`, the text "Requests at {url}" below the QR code in the bottom corners and above it in the top corners (see `docs/overlay.md#the-overlay-image`), rounded semi-opaque panel; corner / heightPct / margin settings; `overlayHash`; regenerate on slug, settings or base URL change.

ZXing `com.google.zxing:core` 3.5.3 added (pre-approved). `overlay/OverlayRenderer`: `Spec` (URL `{publicBaseUrl}/r/{slug}`, corner, heightPct, margin, frame size, `RENDER_VERSION`), `render(spec)` → `Rendered(image, x, y, hash)`: the image is the **panel only** (ARGB, `rgba(0,0,0,176)` rounded rect, transparent corners) with its frame position, so cards draw it at (x, y) and the video encode uses `overlay=x:y`; position and size even (4:2:0). QR: `QRCodeWriter` level M, margin 4, one matrix pixel per module, drawn at whole pixels per module without AA, black on white. **Decided:** `heightPct` is the QR's white square (quiet zone included), rounded down to whole pixels per module, **at least 2 px per module** (1 px at 5% of 720p wouldn't survive scaling/compression), so neighbouring percentages can give identical images; `margin` is from both edges of the corner. Text "Requests at {url without scheme}" bold SansSerif at 16% of the QR side (≥ 3% of frame height), shrunk to fit the frame width, never cut; below the QR in `b*`, above in `t*`, aligned to the QR's edge. `overlayHash` = SHA-256 over frame size, position, size and ARGB pixels (16 hex), so it survives a PNG round trip and equal pixels keep caches. `overlay/Overlays`: `rooms/<roomId>/overlay.png` + `overlay.json` (spec key, x, y, size, hash; written atomically, metadata deleted first); `current(roomId, slug, settings)` reuses the in-memory or stored render when the spec matches (also across restarts) and re-renders otherwise, so a new `publicBaseUrl` after a restart, a new profile or a new render version take effect on first use; unreadable files are re-rendered. `Main`: `Overlays` (profile from `OutputAssets`) and the room edit listener calls `roomEdited` (renders at once when the room's and server's overlay are on). Nothing consumes the overlay yet (overlay cards and the encoder are later M5 steps).

Nothing calls `decide` yet: the scheduling step (current/next) is the consumer, and cards/encoder steps read the decision from there.
