per-file lock so concurrent outputs encode once. `forRoom(roomId, reference, overlays, lookups)` is the room's `ProgramMedia.OverlayCards`: `fillers()` → null unless server and room `overlay.enabled`, else the 3 overlaid fillers for the room's current overlay; `item(entry)` → `OverlayDecisions.decide(entryId)` (this is where the decision gets recorded, as the entry becomes current/next) and, for `cards`/`video`, the item card rendered from title/artist/album and the current version's `cover.jpg`. `lookupsFrom(db, dataDir, settings, decisions)` in production. **Packager:** `CardGop.repeat(Supplier)` picks the card at every GOP start (null → the plain card); `AudioItemSource`/`FillerSource`/`ItemSources.forItem` take that supplier. `ProgramMedia` (new constructor arg `OverlayCards`, null = none) makes and loads entry cards on its executor for current/next entries that play as audio (and on `item()`), and the room's filler cards on each cue (≥ 1 s apart) and every `FILLER_RECHECK_MS` (5 s) while a filler plays; loaded cards are cached by key (16). `ProgramPipeline.EncodedOpener.open(roomId)` (was no-arg) so `Main` builds each output's overlay for its room and encoding; `Overlays` is now created before the program outputs. **Decisions:** (1) never wait for an overlaid card: the plain card plays until it's loaded, then the overlay appears at the next 2 s GOP boundary (seamless: same avcC, closed GOPs); usually it is ready before the entry plays since it's made when the entry becomes next. (2) Filler cards follow the room's switches now (not a per-entry decision); item cards follow the entry's recorded decision. (3) Item cards are re-rendered and fingerprinted at each request (cheap) so an edited title/cover gives a new card under the same name; filler fingerprints are memoised per run. (4) A failure (encode, DB) is logged once, the plain card plays, retried after `RETRY_MS`. (5) Old cards (other overlayHash/version) stay until the LRU step's clean-up; room delete and purge clean-up is that step too. Also classified the overlay records in `DtoIpLeakTest` (incl. `OverlayRenderer$Spec/$Rendered` and `OverlayDecisions$1Row` from the previous two steps, which had been missed).

- [x] Overlay encoder pool (`overlay.workers`), separate from ingest, not persisted.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `7fb470f`. `overlay/OverlayPool`: its own virtual-thread workers (`overlay-worker-N`), jobs in memory only. `submit(key, HIGH|NORMAL, task)`: HIGH (current entry) before NORMAL (next), FIFO within a priority; one job per key (resubmitting a queued or running key returns it, a queued one moves up to a higher priority; its first task stays). `cancel(key)`: a queued job is dropped, a running one interrupted (the task also sees `Job.cancelled()`), ends `CANCELLED`. `Job.progress`/`currentProgress` for the ">50 % done" rule, `Job.end()` future with the final state (`DONE`/`FAILED`/`CANCELLED`, `failure()`); ended jobs aren't kept. `busy()` = running + queued ≥ workers (the scheduler then plays without the overlay; no preemption of a running job for a more urgent one). `resize` like ingest (grow at once, shrink after running jobs). `close()` cancels queued and running jobs and waits like `IngestQueue`. Started in `Main` with `overlay.workers` (setting, else config), closed in the shutdown hook, and `applyRuntimeSettings` now resizes it too. Not used by anything yet: the re-encoder and scheduling steps submit to it.
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `OverlayPoolTest` (new, 11: order, dedup and priority raise, busy, cancel queued/running/cooperative, failure, resize grow/shrink, before start, close), `MainEncodingTest` (`overlay.workers` applied at runtime), `MainTest`, `DtoIpLeakTest`, `ProductNamesTest`.
- [x] Music-video re-encoder: ffmpeg with profile constant from GOP `g0`; Annex-B reader, NAL splitter, access-unit grouping (`first_mb_in_slice`), SPS/PPS check vs reference (abort on mismatch), drop SPS/PPS/AUD/SEI, `gops.h264` + `gops.idx`, completion `BitSet`.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `83f6760`. `overlay/OverlayVideoEncoder.encode(tools, Request, OverlayGops, progress, log)`: `ffmpeg [-seek_timestamp 1 -ss <keyframe pts, floored to µs>us] -i mezz.mp4 -i overlay.png -filter_complex "[0:v][1:v]overlay=x:y,<COLOUR_FILTER>[v]" -map [v] -fps_mode passthrough <EncodeProfile.videoArgs(encoding, preset, firstGop)> -an -f h264 pipe:1` (`firstGop` = the mezzanine's own first-GOP length when starting at frame 0, else 60; preset = the caller's, i.e. `ingest.quickPreset`). Measured on ffmpeg 6.1.1: the floored keyframe time lands exactly on the keyframe, also for a file without edit list (video pts shifted by the AAC priming), since the time comes from the index's pts. stdout is read by the new `ToolCall.stdoutStream(handler)` (binary stdout on the reader thread, reads count as activity, a handler exception stops the tool, `OUTPUT_FAILED` with the cause; an early return drains the rest). New `media/AnnexBReader` (incremental start-code splitter, 3/4-byte codes, trailing zeros stripped, empty NALs skipped, 32 MiB NAL limit, garbage before the first start code rejected) and `media/AccessUnitGrouper` (new AU at a slice with `first_mb_in_slice` 0 or at AUD/SPS/PPS/SEI/14..18 after a slice).
  - **Checks, all aborting with `OverlayVideoEncoder.MismatchException`** (the item then plays without the overlay): every SPS/PPS must be byte-identical to the reference avcC's; SPS and PPS must come before the first frame; output frame *i* is mezzanine frame `gopStart(g0) + i`, and its IDR flag must equal the mezzanine's keyframe flag; more frames than the mezzanine, or fewer at the end, fail. So an off-by-one seek can't slip through (it shows as one frame too many or too few).
  - **Deviation from the design text: `gops.h264` holds AVCC samples, not Annex B** (4-byte length prefixes of the reference avcC's size, SPS/PPS/AUD/SEI dropped), i.e. exactly like `mezz.mp4`'s samples, so the substitution step can hand an overlay frame to the packager as a `VideoSample` one for one. `gops.idx`: 12-byte header (`TTOV`, version 1, the mezzanine's frame count), then 17-byte entries (frame int, offset long, size int, flags byte with 1 = IDR), big-endian; the frame's bytes are written before its entry, entries flushed at every GOP end.
  - **`overlay/OverlayGops`** (the per-job state the packager will read): GOPs are the mezzanine's (its keyframes from the index, so a smart trim's short first GOP is GOP 0); `isComplete(g)`, `completed()` (BitSet copy), `completeFrom(g)`, `offset(frame)`/`size(frame)` (valid once the frame's GOP is complete; stored before the GOP is published under the object's lock). `OverlayGops.load(dir, videoTrack)` reads an encode back (for the cache step): complete GOPs only, stops at a gap, a wrong IDR flag, bytes past the end of `gops.h264` or a torn last entry; another format or frame count is an `IOException`. The job directory is the caller's (the scheduling/cache steps decide `rooms/<roomId>/ov/…` and the key).
  - Docs: `docs/overlay.md#the-encode` and `#reading-the-output-java` (as implemented).
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `AnnexBReaderTest` (new: 3/4-byte start codes, one byte per read, trailing zeros and empty NALs, empty streams, data before the first start code, NAL limit, 20 random streams with NALs up to 150 KB across buffer and read boundaries), `AccessUnitGrouperTest` (new: x264 layout with in-band SPS/PPS/SEI, multi-slice pictures, SEI after a slice, filler/end-of-sequence, trailing non-slice NALs), `OverlayGopsTest` (new: GOP layout incl. short first GOP, completion, load with torn tail, missing bytes, gaps, wrong IDR flags, other format/frame count), `OverlayVideoEncoderTest` (new, real ffmpeg: 4.5 s mezzanine encoded from GOP 0, 1 and 2 and a short-first-GOP (20 frames) mezzanine from GOP 0 and 1: only GOPs ≥ g0 complete, progress per GOP, files read back the same, frames hold only slices with the mezzanine's IDR flags, the Annex B stream decodes to every frame with the red panel, and each frame is closer to the same mezzanine frame than to its neighbours (away from the panel); command (seek, filter, profile arguments); a reference SPS with another level aborts with nothing published; an interrupt stops a 20 s encode after GOP 0 was published), `ToolCallTest` (3 new `stdoutStream` cases).
  - **Preset setting (added 2026-11-10, Siggi):** admin setting `overlay.allowQualityPreset` (boolean, default **false**): by default overlay re-encodes of music videos use only the quick preset (`ingest.quickPreset`). When it's on, an overlay re-encode may use `ingest.qualityPreset` where there's time for it: the **next** item's re-encode (scheduled ahead); catch-up for the current item (e.g. after a skip mid-item, which must gain the overlay within ~4 s) stays on the quick preset. Same avcC either way (the preset table). If a quality-preset re-encode falls behind real time, the packager already plays original GOPs until overlay GOPs are complete, so nothing glitches; note whether it should then switch to the quick preset. **Audio-only content** (audio items and videos presented as audio) isn't affected: their overlay is on cards, which are encoded the same way as every other card. Docs: `docs/overlay.md`, `docs/auth.md`.
- [x] Packager per-GOP substitution (overlay GOP when complete, original otherwise).
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `cc933c3`. New `output/VideoOverlay` (`frameCount()`, `gop(keyframe)` → the GOP's AVCC samples if complete, else null; never waits for the encoder), implemented by `OverlayGops` (reads the GOP's frames from `gops.h264` with positional reads; null for an incomplete GOP or a frame that isn't a GOP start; missing bytes are an `IOException`). `VideoItemSource(itemId, index, data, Supplier<VideoOverlay>, startMs)` asks the supplier at **every keyframe** of the mezzanine and plays that whole GOP from the overlay if it gives one, the original GOP otherwise, so switches only happen at GOP boundaries (both ways; same avcC, timing and IDR positions). **Decided:** (1) an overlay GOP is read whole at its start (about one GOP, ~750 KB at 3 Mb/s), so a read failure falls back to the original GOP cleanly instead of splicing mid-GOP; original GOPs are still read sample by sample. (2) Safety checks per GOP: the overlay's frame count must be the mezzanine's (another media version → never used), the GOP must have exactly the mezzanine GOP's frame count, its first frame an IDR and no other (`AnnexB.isIdr` on the index's avcC). Any failure (those, or a read error) drops the overlay **for the rest of that play** (logged once as a warning, `VideoItemSource`), the original plays. (3) PTS come from the mezzanine's index either way, audio always from the item. `overlaidGops()`/`playingOverlay()` for tests and diagnostics. **Wiring:** `ItemSources.forItem(…, Supplier<VideoOverlay> videoOverlay, startMs)`; `ProgramMedia.OverlayCards.video(entry, itemDir, index)` (default null) is asked when an entry played as video starts playing, on the packager thread, with the version directory and index the play reads (so the scheduling/cache step keys the job on the media version); it returns a supplier that gives the job's `OverlayGops` once there is one. Nothing implements `video` yet: the scheduling step does (`OverlayCards.forRoom`). Also classified `OverlayVideoEncoder$Request` and `AccessUnitGrouper$AccessUnit` in `DtoIpLeakTest` (missed by the re-encoder step).
  - Docs: `docs/overlay.md#packager-substitution` (as implemented).
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `SourcesTest` (5 new: complete GOPs only and asked once per GOP, start mid-item, completion mid-GOP takes over at the next GOP, short first GOP, unusable overlays dropped: other frame count, read failure, short GOP, IDR at the wrong place or missing; `ItemSources` passes it only for video played as video), `ProgramMediaTest` (new: video played as video gets the re-encode with its version dir and index, overlay GOPs once complete; not asked for an entry played as audio), `OverlayGopsTest` (new: `gop()` reads complete GOPs incl. the short last one, null for incomplete/non-start/out of range, missing bytes or file → `IOException`), `OverlayVideoEncoderTest` (new, real ffmpeg: an encode from GOP 1 played through `VideoItemSource` over the mezzanine's index: GOP 0 original, GOPs 1–2 overlay, the spliced Annex B stream decodes all 135 frames with no decoder errors, panel exactly from frame 60, the mezzanine's picture before), `UnitBuilderTest`, `RealSourcesTest`, `RealTsMuxerTest`, `TimelineFollowerTest`, `PackagerGoldenFileTest`, `PackagerTest`, `ProgramOutputsTest`, `OverlayCardsTest`, `DtoIpLeakTest`, `ProductNamesTest`.
- [x] Scheduling: current (high) / next (normal); cancel when no longer next unless >50% done; busy pool → play without overlay.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `12d7389`. New `overlay/OverlayVideoJobs` (one per server, on the `OverlayPool`; `Session` per program output via the new `OverlayCards.forRoom(…, OverlayVideoJobs)`, wired in `Main`). `ProgramMedia.OverlayCards` gained `videoNext(entry, itemDir, index)` (the cue's next entry played as video, once its files are loaded: at every cue and after the load) and `videoWanted(entryIds)` (current, next and playing; sent when it changes, empty on close); `video(…)` now returns the session's `VideoOverlay` for the play.
  - **Decided:** (1) Next → NORMAL job from GOP 0. Current → HIGH job planned at the play's **first GOP request** (that's when the start is known): from GOP 0 for a play from the start, else from **g + 1** (GOP g is already being sent). (2) An existing encode is shared when it started at or before that GOP and is finished or at most 2 GOPs behind (`FRONTIER_SLACK_GOPS`); a queued one is raised to HIGH (new `OverlayPool.raise`, never creates a job), so the next entry's job simply continues when it plays. (3) Only entries whose recorded decision is `video` get a job; the decision, room and overlay image are looked up once per entry on the executor (retried after 5 s on failure). (4) A job no output wants any more is cancelled unless `progress > 0.5` (frames written from its start GOP); jobs are reference-counted across outputs. (5) Busy pool: nothing queued, the item plays without the overlay; wants without a job retry when any job ends, at cues (next) and every 5 s at a GOP start (playing entry, from the GOP after the one playing), so the overlay can appear later. No pre-emption. (6) The play's `VideoOverlay` gives a GOP from **any** encode of the same room/item/media version/overlayHash/encoding with that GOP complete, never waits. (7) `MismatchException` → that item/version/overlay/encoding isn't retried this run; other failures retried after 5 s. (8) **Directory per job:** `rooms/<roomId>/ov/<itemId>/<version>-<overlayHash>-<encoding>-g<g0>/` with `gops.h264`, `gops.idx` and the `overlay.png` written from the rendered panel (so hash and pixels can't diverge on a re-render); a new job for the same directory only starts after the old one ended and is marked unread (per-encode lock around reads). This deviates from the design's single `ov/<itemId>/` directory; the LRU step cleans up and reloads from these. (9) Finished encodes kept in memory (last 64) for repeats within a run; disk reuse across restarts is the LRU step's.
  - **Preset setting implemented here** (from the re-encoder step's "Preset setting" note, which wasn't implemented yet): admin setting `overlay.allowQualityPreset` (bool, default false, group overlay via the generic admin settings list). `Main.overlayPresets`: HIGH → `ingest.quickPreset`; NORMAL → `ingest.qualityPreset` if the setting is on. Chosen when the job starts, with its priority then (a queued next job raised to HIGH starts quick). A quality job already running keeps its preset; if it falls >2 GOPs behind the playing GOP, the play's retry starts a quick HIGH job from the next GOP when a worker is free.
  - Docs: `docs/overlay.md#scheduling-and-caching` (as implemented), `docs/architecture.md` (storage layout of `ov/`), `docs/auth.md` (overlay settings row).
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `OverlayVideoJobsTest` (new, fake encoder on a real pool: next ahead from GOP 0 with the normal preset, job directory and panel PNG, the play uses it, one lookup per entry, a repeat reuses the finished encode; mid-item play → HIGH job from the next GOP with the quick preset, a later play from the start gets a new job and reads GOPs from either encode; queued next raised to HIGH on play and starts quick; not wanted → cancelled at 40 %, kept at 60 %; shared between outputs until both give it up; busy pool → nothing queued, retried when the worker frees; non-`video` decision → no job; failed lookup retried only after 5 s; other failure retried after 5 s from the GOP after the one playing, mismatch never), `OverlayPoolTest` (new `raise` case), `ProgramMediaTest` (new: next video handed over once loaded and per cue, not for video played as audio, wanted set incl. the playing entry, sent on change, empty on close), `OverlayCardsTest` (new, real ffmpeg reference: forRoom's re-encode only for `video` decisions, with the room overlay's position/hash, the encoding and reference avcC), `MainEncodingTest` (new: `overlayPresets`), `SqliteSettingsTest` (39 keys, default false), `AdminApiTest`, `DtoIpLeakTest` (`OverlayVideoJobs$Plan` internal), `ProductNamesTest`, `MainTest`, `SourcesTest`, `TimelineFollowerTest`, `ProgramOutputsTest`, `ProgramOutputsRoomsTest`, `PackagerTest`, `UnitBuilderTest`, `RealSourcesTest`, `OverlayGopsTest`, `SqliteOverlayDecisionsTest`, `AudioOnlyRoomOutputTest`. Jar smoke test: starts, `/api/me` 200, stopped.
- [x] LRU cache (`overlay.cacheMaxBytes`) for `rooms/*/ov/` and room cards; delete on purge, room delete, `overlayHash` change; discard partials on restart.
  - **Media versions (added 2026-11-10):** items' files are versioned (`media/<itemId>/v<N>/`, M4 step "Versioned media files"): key overlay GOPs and item cards on the media version too, so a re-encoded item never splices overlay GOPs made from the old version.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `0c9670b`. New `overlay/OverlayCache` (one per server, built in `Main` before the outputs): entries are finished re-encode directories (new `done` marker file, written by `OverlayVideoJobs` after a complete encode) and room cards (mp4 + `.key`), counted together against `overlay.cacheMaxBytes` (setting re-read at every trim, config fallback). **LRU:** last use in memory (`used`: a GOP played from an encode, a card handed out; no I/O, so the packager can call it), persisted at most once a minute as the mtime of `done` / the card, so the order survives restarts. `trim` (on the executor, one at a time; after every new encode/card, when `wanted()` releases an encode, after an admin settings save) evicts oldest first until within the limit, skipping entries used in the last 60 s (`GRACE_MS`: a card just made is about to be loaded) and re-encodes in use (`OverlayVideoJobs.evict` refuses running ones and ones assigned to a current/next/playing entry; otherwise it forgets the encode (waiting for a read in progress) and deletes the directory under its lock). Cards are evicted under their per-file lock (`OverlayCards.evict`); always safe since outputs hold loaded cards in memory and a missing card is made again. **overlayHash change:** new `Overlays.hashListener` (told when a room's hash becomes known in a run or changes) → `roomOverlay`: that room's entries with another hash are stale and go at the next trim regardless of size/grace once unused (an old-hash encode finishing later too). **Purge:** new `Items.purgeListener` (after the files are deleted) → `OverlayVideoJobs.stopItem` (cancels the item's jobs, waits ≤10 s, forgets its encodes, releases its wants) → `itemPurged` deletes `ov/<itemId>/` and `item-<itemId>-…` cards in every room. **Room delete:** `Rooms.delete` now returns the room id; new `RoomsApi.deleteListener` → on a virtual thread `stopRoom` (cancel + no new jobs for that room), `Overlays.forget`, `roomDeleted` deletes `rooms/<roomId>/`. **Restart:** `start(roomIds)` deletes re-encode dirs without `done`, dot-prefixed temp files (cards' and `overlay.png`'s), cards without `.key` and `.key`s without card, and the dirs of rooms not in the database (skipped if the ids can't be read), then counts and trims. **Disk reuse across restarts** (left to this step by the scheduling step): when an entry is resolved, finished encodes of its item/version/overlayHash/encoding on disk are read back with `OverlayGops.load` (must be complete from its `g0`; otherwise deleted and redone). Also: a cancelled or failed encode's directory is now deleted when its job ends (unless a new job for it was already set up). **Decided:** small deletions (3 files) happen under the scheduler's lock, so a packager GOP lookup can wait for one; stale entries skip the grace period.
  - Docs: `docs/overlay.md` (cards section, `#scheduling-and-caching`), `docs/architecture.md` (storage layout `done` marker, `overlay.cacheMaxBytes` covers cards).
  - Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `OverlayCacheTest` (new: restart scan of partials, temp files, key/card orphans, deleted rooms, totals; LRU order with in-use and grace, 0 keeps nothing; added/used counting and throttled mtime persistence; stale hash entries incl. a late one; purge across rooms without touching `item10`; room delete; unsafe ids ignored), `OverlayVideoJobsTest` (4 new: finished encode marked and cached, evicted only when unused and past grace, the play then falls back to the original and a repeat re-encodes; cancelled/failed encode dirs deleted; reuse after a restart from disk with the partial discarded, unreadable one deleted and redone; stopItem/stopRoom interrupt jobs, forget encodes, no new jobs for a deleted room), `OverlayCardsTest` (cards counted, old-hash cards deleted on a slug change, evicted card made again), `OverlaysTest` (hash listener), `RoomsApiTest` (delete listener gets the id for a slug delete), `SqliteItemsTest` (purge listener), `OverlayGopsTest`, `OverlayPoolTest`, `DtoIpLeakTest`, `ProductNamesTest`, `MainTest`, `MainRouterTest`, `MainEncodingTest`, `ProgramMediaTest`, `ProgramOutputsRoomsTest`, `SqliteRoomsTest`, `SqliteRoomExecutorTest`, `ItemsApiTest`, `SqliteRetentionSweeperTest`, `SqliteReferencesTest`. New `output/NextVideoOverlayTest` (real chain: `OverlayCards.forRoom` with `OverlayVideoJobs` on a 1-worker `OverlayPool` running real `OverlayVideoEncoder` re-encodes of the room's rendered QR panel (quick preset), `ProgramMedia` feeding it, `OfflineHarness` writing a `.ts`). Script: audio item `a0` (decision `off`) with music video `v0` next, which starts at `a0`'s natural end; `w0` (a second item, copy of `video1`) next while `v0` plays, started by a skip 6 s later. Since the harness is unpaced, a wrapping `SourceFactory` waits (wall clock) before a video's first unit until the encode its "next" turn started is finished, and fails if the entry was never handed over as next; the encode directories must be one per item, `-g0`. Then ffmpeg extracts every output frame's panel region (grey) and ZXing must decode the room's URL from **every** `v0`/`w0` frame (incl. each one's first) and from **no** `a0`/filler frame; plus decodes clean and `assertPackets`. Measured here: each 10.3 s encode finished ~380 ms after the entry became next (the live output would have had 6.5 s / 6 s). **Observed:** `v0` starts at media 0 ms (the output moves on when the audio source ends); `w0` after the skip joins the timeline where it is when the skip reaches the output (`video2@2005ms`, GOP 1, about one unit plus `LEAD_MS` late, as designed), which the encode from GOP 0 covers. Not looked into: Siggi's report in this run that a skip sometimes "skips the first bit of the next item" in the live stream may be this same join-where-the-timeline-is behaviour (the output is up to ~2 s behind the timeline at a skip). Tests run: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `NextVideoOverlayTest`, `PackagerOverlayGoldenFileTest`.
- [x] A music video skipped to mid-item gains the overlay within 4 s with no stream glitch (ffmpeg/ffprobe).
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `aa0c8ee`. No production change needed. `OfflineHarness.run(…, realTime)` (new overload): with `realTime` each unit is built no earlier (wall clock) than the live packager would build it (`LEAD_MS` before it is due), so the overlay encoder races playback as it does live; output and timeline are unchanged. New `output/MidItemVideoOverlayTest` (same real chain as `NextVideoOverlayTest`: `OverlayCards.forRoom` + `OverlayVideoJobs` on a 1-worker pool with real quick-preset re-encodes, `ProgramMedia`, the harness in real time, ~24 s). Script: music video `v0` is the first entry and starts at 5 s (never next, so no encode exists: a pure catch-up job; the output joins at media 3989 ms, GOP 2, job from GOP 3), at 4 s it is seeked back to 0 (the output joins at 2005 ms, GOP 1, before the first job's start: a second catch-up job from GOP 2), then an audio item and the idle filler. Checks: exactly the two catch-up encodes (`-g2`, `-g3`); per play the QR code (ZXing on every decoded frame's panel region) appears within 4 s of output time from the play's first frame, on an IDR, and stays to the play's end; no QR code on audio/filler frames; `assertDecodesClean`, `assertPackets`. **Measured:** both plays gain the overlay at frame 120, **exactly 4000 ms** after their first frame.
  - **Why exactly 4 s (finding):** a play starts on a keyframe (GOP *g*). The unit of GOP *g*, built `LEAD_MS` ahead, already reads GOP *g*+1's keyframe (that's where the unit ends, `UnitBuilder.pendingVideo`), so `VideoItemSource` decides GOP *g*+1 in the same build that plans the catch-up job (`OverlayVideoJobs.Play.gop`: target *g*+1): *g*+1 can never come from that job, and the first overlay GOP is *g*+2, 4 s after the start (less only after a short first GOP). The job has 2 s of wall time to finish GOPs *g*+1 and *g*+2 (here it takes well under that). Not changed here: planning the catch-up target as *g*+2 (and the 5 s retry's "next GOP" likewise) would skip encoding a GOP that is never used and give a slow machine 2 s for one GOP instead of two, with the same 4 s on screen; a candidate follow-up if overlays show up late on slow servers.
  - Related to Siggi's report ("when skipping an item, it will skip the first bit of the next item"): here too a seek reaches the output at the next unit boundary and the output joins where the timeline is then (seek to 0 at 4 s → media 2005 ms), as designed (`TimelineFollower`); not looked into further in this step.
  - Tests run: `mvn test -Dtest='MidItemVideoOverlayTest,NextVideoOverlayTest,OfflineHarnessTest,PackagerOverlayGoldenFileTest'` (compiles Java and TypeScript).
- [x] *(manual)* Visual check of the mid-item overlay switch: no visible glitch.
  - **Checked by Siggi 2026-11-11:** a music video started without a ready overlay encode switched cleanly to the overlay version.
  - **Implemented, awaiting manual check (2026-11-11).** Nothing new to build. Automated coverage rerun at main `aa0c8ee`: `mvn test -Dtest='MidItemVideoOverlayTest,PackagerOverlayGoldenFileTest'` (both pass; `NextVideoOverlayTest` passed in the same run's reports): the switch lands on an IDR exactly 4 s after the play's first frame, the output decodes clean and timestamps are continuous. What only a person can check: that the switch from original GOPs to overlay GOPs looks seamless (no flash, smear, freeze, colour or brightness jump, stutter, or audio hiccup).
  - **Seen in the test log, not a glitch but worth knowing:** `VideoItemSource drop: Overlay for video1 not used for the rest of this play: made for 308 frames, the video has 309`, logged at the end of the play in `MidItemVideoOverlayTest` (the test still passes: the QR stays to the play's end it checks). If the overlay disappears for the last moment of a video during the manual check, this frame-count mismatch is the likely cause; worth a follow-up look.
  - **How to check:** build and run the server (`mvn package -DskipTests`, `java -jar target/TogetherTunes-1.0-SNAPSHOT.jar`). As admin, turn on `overlay.enabled` and a TS or HLS output; make sure the room owner holds `output.overlayVideo`. In the room's Settings, turn the QR overlay on. Open the TS/HLS URL in VLC (and, if convenient, the RTMP push in VRChat). Queue a **music video** as the first entry (or skip to it so it was never "next", so no encode exists yet), then seek it to the middle (e.g. 1:00). Watch the panel corner and the whole picture around 4 s after the jump appears in the stream: the QR panel should pop in cleanly on a frame boundary, with the video continuing smoothly underneath (no blockiness, green/grey frames, freeze, jump back/forward, or audio dropout). Repeat a couple of times (seek back, seek forward) and once with a fast-motion video. Tick `[x]` if no glitch is visible; otherwise note what was seen and when (seconds after the seek).

Checks: both entries' recorded decision is `cards`; no re-encode started (nothing under `rooms/<room>/ov/`); ZXing finds the room's QR code on **every** frame of the audio item's card and of the filler card and on **no** frame of the music video; `assertDecodesClean`, `assertPackets`. Measured: a0 225/225 frames with the QR code, v0 0/309, filler 120/120.

## M6 — OIDC and OAuth

- [x] Provider admin (`/api/admin/providers`), `client_secret_enc`, `allow_signup`, `claim_mapping_json`; `/api/auth/providers`.
  - **Several providers at once** (Siggi, 2026-11-11): any number of providers can be enabled together (e.g. Siggi's own OIDC provider and a Twitch one), each with its own login button, and a user can link several. Nothing may assume a single provider. Keep `docs/future-ideas.md#provider-gated-room-access-eg-twitch-subscribers` possible (one place that turns the token response into an identity; room request/vote checks in one server-side place), but don't build it. Tests: two providers enabled at once, logging in through each, and one user linking both.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `1c73b99`. New `auth.Providers` (service), `auth.ProvidersApi` (HTTP), `auth.ClaimMapping`. **Schema:** migration 0022 adds `oidc_providers.kind TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'oidc'` (`oidc` | `oauth2`, fixed at creation): a plain OAuth provider has no discovery, so its authorization/token endpoints live in `claim_mapping_json` next to the userinfo URL and claim paths, and an explicit kind is clearer than guessing from the mapping. **claim_mapping_json:** OIDC: optional, only `username`/`displayName`/`email` claim names (defaults `preferred_username`, `name`, `email`; subject is always `sub`); OAuth: required `authorizationEndpoint`, `tokenEndpoint`, `userinfoUrl`, `subject`, optional `username`/`displayName`/`email`. Paths are dot-separated names, a numeric name indexes an array (`data.0.id`); only the syntax is checked here, resolving them comes with the OAuth step. **Rules:** slug `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*` ≤ 32, lower-cased, unique (`409 provider_slug_taken`); issuer required for OIDC (kept exactly, compared with `iss` later), for OAuth optional (defaults to the authorization endpoint's origin; only names the provider); URLs `https`, or `http` only for literal loopback hosts / `localhost` (local fake IdP), no user info/fragment (issuer also no query); scopes RFC 6749 tokens, deduplicated, OIDC must include `openid` (default `openid profile email`, OAuth default none); `enabled` default on, `allowSignup` default off. **Client secret:** write-only, `SecretCipher` with context `oidc_providers.client_secret_enc:<id>`; API shows `hasClientSecret`; PATCH `clientSecret`: string replaces, `null` removes, absent keeps; audit only says `set`/`removed`/`none`; `Providers.clientSecret(id)` decrypts for the login flow. `Main` now passes the secret key (TODO removed). **Identities stay valid** (decision): while a provider has identities, its issuer (OIDC) or userinfo URL / subject path (OAuth) can't change and it can't be deleted (`409 provider_has_identities`: a new issuer could hand out an existing subject to someone else, and the FK cascade would delete identities and could lock out password-less accounts); disable it instead. **API:** `GET/POST /api/admin/providers`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/admin/providers/{id}` (*admin.providers*; provider JSON adds `redirectUri` = `{publicBaseUrl}/auth/oidc/{slug}/callback` and `identities` count; audited `admin.provider_created/updated/deleted`); `GET /api/auth/providers` (anyone) → enabled providers `{slug, displayName, allowSignup, startUrl}`, oldest first (creation order = button order). Nothing assumes one provider. **UI:** minimal "Login providers" tab on the admin page (one form per provider, claim mapping as JSON); login buttons on the login page come with the authorization step, whose `/auth/oidc/{slug}/start` the `startUrl` points to. **Tests:** `ProvidersContract` (SQLite `SqliteProvidersTest` + `PostgresProvidersIT`, 5 each: several providers at once with one user holding an identity at two of them, defaults/normalisation, validation, encrypted write-only secret bound to its row, identities pin the subject meaning and block delete); `AdminApiTest#twoProvidersEnabledAtOnce` (two enabled providers, OIDC + OAuth, both listed publicly with exact body, one user linked to both, disabling one) and `#providerAdmin` (secret keep/remove/set, validation, 409s, audit without the secret, permission); `PermissionMatrixTest` and `DtoIpLeakTest` rows for the 6 new routes; `InitialSchemaContract` gains `kind`. "Logging in through each" can only be tested once the login flow exists (authorization code / identity resolution steps), so those steps must cover it with two providers. Ran: `SqliteProvidersTest, AdminApiTest, PermissionMatrixTest, DtoIpLeakTest, MeApiTest, MainRouterTest, SqliteInitialSchemaTest, PermissionTest, ProductNamesTest, WebBuildTest, MainTest, SqliteMigrationTest, MigrationLoadingTest` and ITs `PostgresProvidersIT, PostgresInitialSchemaIT, SchemaEquivalenceIT, PostgresMigrationIT`; jar smoke test (create a provider, public list, secret not in the log).
- [x] Discovery + JWKS caching, refetch on unknown `kid`.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `ff125dc`. New `auth.OidcDiscovery` (service only; the authorization step wires it into `Main` and its start/callback routes). Adds `com.nimbusds:nimbus-jose-jwt` 10.10 (pre-approved; its own dependencies are all optional and left out). **Discovery:** `{issuer}/.well-known/openid-configuration` (a trailing `/` of the issuer dropped); the document's `issuer` must equal the configured one exactly (Discovery §4.3); `authorization_endpoint`, `token_endpoint`, `jwks_uri` required, `userinfo_endpoint` optional, each checked with `Providers.requireUrl` (https, http only on loopback); `id_token_signing_alg_values_supported` and `code_challenge_methods_supported` recorded (`Metadata.supportsS256()`: listed, or no methods listed). **JWKS:** only public signing keys kept (private parts dropped, `use` absent or `sig`, no `oct`). **Caching** per issuer (so an issuer change uses a new entry; `forget(issuer)` drops one): discovery 24 h, JWKS 1 h (keys the provider removed go away at the refresh), and a changed `jwks_uri` is fetched at once. **Unknown `kid`:** when no cached key matches and the token's `kid` isn't in the set (or it has none), the JWKS is refetched once, at most every 10 s per issuer (a known `kid` that just doesn't match the algorithm doesn't refetch). **Failures:** with a cached copy, a failed refresh keeps using it (logged) and retries after 10 s; without one, `DiscoveryException` is thrown. Redirects aren't followed, 15 s request timeout, 512 KiB body cap; fetches per issuer are serialised with a `ReentrantLock` (virtual-thread friendly), so concurrent logins share one fetch. `keySource(issuer)` is a Nimbus `JWKSource` for the ID-token step's `JWSVerificationKeySelector`.
  - Tests: new `OidcDiscoveryTest` (18, local fake IdP on `com.sun.net.httpserver`, injected clock): parsing and TTLs, invalid documents (issuer mismatch/missing, missing endpoints, non-https, user info, wrong types, bad JSON), HTTP errors / redirect not followed / oversized body / unreachable, JWKS filtering, JWKS TTL and key removal, unknown-`kid` refetch, its rate limit, no refetch for a known `kid`, `kid`-less tokens, stale copies during an outage, changed `jwks_uri`, 16 concurrent callers → one fetch each, and Nimbus's `DefaultJWTProcessor` verifying RS256/ES256 tokens after a rotation while refusing an unpublished key and a forged `kid`. Ran: `mvn package -DskipTests` (Nimbus in the shaded jar); `mvn test -Dtest='OidcDiscoveryTest,ProductNamesTest,SqliteProvidersTest,MainTest'`. All pass.
- [x] Authorization code + PKCE S256, `state`/`nonce` stored server-side.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `3c7a33b`. New `auth.OidcLogin`, registered through `ProvidersApi.loginFlow` (so `Main.router`'s signature is unchanged; `TestUploads.providersApi` wires it like `Main`). **Routes:** `GET /auth/oidc/{slug}/start?intent=login|link&next=/path` → `302` to the authorization endpoint (`response_type=code`, `client_id`, `redirect_uri`, `scope`, `state`, `nonce` (OIDC only), `code_challenge` + `S256`; an endpoint's own query is kept); `GET /auth/oidc/{slug}/callback` → takes the pending login, exchanges the code with the `code_verifier`, then calls a pluggable `Completion` with the provider, the pending login (nonce, intent, link user, next) and the token response. **The default completion redirects with `login_unavailable`**: the ID-token step and identity resolution plug in there (`OidcLogin.completion(...)` in `Main`). **Storage (decision):** in memory, not a table: keyed by the state's SHA-256, single-use (also when the callback fails), 10 min TTL, at most 10 000 (oldest dropped); a restart only means starting the login again. A state only works at its own provider's callback (mix-up). **Browser binding (addition):** the start sets `oidc_login` (random token, `Path=/auth/oidc/; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax`, `Secure` with https, 10 min, reused across tabs) and the pending login stores its hash; a callback without it is `login_browser_mismatch` (login CSRF). `intent=link` needs a logged-in user, and the same user at the callback (`link_session_changed`). OIDC needs S256 in discovery (or no methods listed), else `provider_unsupported`; plain OAuth2 providers get PKCE S256 too. **Token request:** form POST, `Accept: application/json`, no redirects, 15 s, 512 KiB cap; public client → `client_id` in the form; with a secret: OIDC `client_secret_basic` when listed or nothing listed (`OidcDiscovery.Metadata` gains `tokenEndpointAuthMethods`), else `client_secret_post`, else refused; OAuth2 `client_secret_post`. Needs `access_token`, `token_type` Bearer if present, `id_token` for OIDC; an `error` field fails it even with 200 (GitHub style). **Failures** redirect to `/login.html?error=<code>&next=…` or, for a link, `/account/?error=<code>`; codes listed in the Javadoc and `docs/auth.md`. Start and callback share a per-IP limit (20, then one per 3 s). Nothing secret is logged (provider error codes sanitised). **UI:** the login page lists the enabled providers as links (`startUrl` + `next`) and shows the error message for `?error=` (the account page's link buttons come with identity resolution).
  - Tests: new `OidcLoginTest` (15, fake IdP on `com.sun.net.httpserver`, real router): RFC 7636 test vector; the authorization URL and cookie; verifier ↔ challenge and the nonce reaching the completion; token form; single use and expiry; binding cookie missing/other; another provider's callback; two providers at once in one browser (OIDC + OAuth); OAuth (no nonce, endpoint query kept, `client_secret_post`); OIDC client auth basic / post-only / neither; no S256; unknown, disabled (also between start and callback) and unreachable providers; provider errors and 8 bad token responses; link with the same / no / another user; default completion; `next` sanitising; bounded store. `PermissionMatrixTest` and `DtoIpLeakTest` rows for the two routes (302 for everyone; the DTO sweep counts their redirects like `/r/`), and `DtoIpLeakTest` classifies the new records (plus `OidcDiscovery.Metadata`, missed in the previous step). Ran: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `mvn test -Dtest='OidcLoginTest,OidcDiscoveryTest,PermissionMatrixTest,DtoIpLeakTest,AdminApiTest,WebBuildTest,ProductNamesTest,MainRouterTest,MainTest,SqliteProvidersTest,MeApiTest'`; jar smoke test (unknown provider → 302, login page served).
- [x] ID-token validation via Nimbus: RS256/ES256 only by default, `iss`, `aud`, `exp`, `iat` ±60 s, `nonce`.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `8ed728f`. New `auth.IdTokenValidator`; `OidcLogin` runs it for OIDC providers right after the code exchange (it builds one from its discovery and clock), and `OidcLogin.Callback` gains `idToken` (the validated `IdToken(subject, claims)`, `null` for OAuth2), so identity resolution gets a checked subject. **Checks** (OIDC Core §3.1.3.7): a signed JWT (plain `alg=none` and encrypted refused); `alg` in the allowed set, default RS256 + ES256 (a constructor parameter can widen it to other RSA/EC/EdDSA algorithms; **HS* is refused outright**, since no setting exists to enable it and it would need the client secret as key: the "unless explicitly configured" of `auth.md` stays unbuilt); signature with Nimbus `DefaultJWTProcessor` + `JWSVerificationKeySelector` over `OidcDiscovery.keySource` (unknown `kid` → one rate-limited JWKS refetch); claims checked by hand against the injected clock (Nimbus's own claims verifier uses the wall clock): `iss` exact; `aud` contains the client ID; **added:** with several audiences `azp` is required, a present `azp` must be the client ID; `exp` required, expired once `exp + 60 s <= now`; `iat` required and within ±60 s of now; **added:** a present `nbf` with 60 s skew; `nonce` required and equal (constant time); `sub` required, non-empty. Failures → `id_token_invalid` (logged with the reason, never the token), or `provider_unreachable` when the keys can't be fetched. The login page shows its generic message for the new code. `docs/auth.md` step 3 updated.
  - Tests: new `IdTokenValidatorTest` (12, fake IdP with discovery + JWKS): RS256/ES256 accepted; `none`, HS256, RS512/PS256 (not allowed by default), a swapped payload, garbage refused; HS/empty sets rejected by the constructor; widening to RS512; rogue key / impostor with a published `kid`; unknown `kid` refetch after a rotation and a never-published `kid`; unreachable JWKS → `provider_unreachable`; `iss`, `aud`/`azp`, `exp` skew edges, `iat` ±60 s edges, `nbf`, `nonce` (other, missing, non-string, suffix), `sub`. `OidcLoginTest` now has its fake IdP sign real RS256 ID tokens with the login's nonce (JWKS endpoint added), checks the validated subject reaches the completion, and new `idTokenIsValidatedBeforeTheCompletion` (another login's nonce, unpublished key, stale `iat` → `id_token_invalid`, completion never called). `DtoIpLeakTest` classifies `IdTokenValidator$IdToken`. The later "Tests: bad nonce, bad aud, expired token, unknown kid" step is largely covered by these already. Ran: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `mvn test -Dtest='IdTokenValidatorTest,OidcLoginTest,OidcDiscoveryTest,DtoIpLeakTest,PermissionMatrixTest,ProductNamesTest,MainTest,MainRouterTest'`. All pass.
- [x] Identity resolution: login / link (`intent=link`, never by email) / signup / error; `/api/me/identities`.
  - Carried over from the provider admin step: test with **two providers enabled at once**, logging in through each, and one user linking both (the admin step could only test listing and identity rows).
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `4524808`. New `auth.Identities` (service), `auth.IdentityResolution` (the `OidcLogin.Completion`, wired in `Main` and `TestUploads.providersApi`), `auth.IdentitiesApi` (registered through `ProvidersApi.identities(...)`, so `Main.router`'s signature is unchanged). **One place for the provider's answer:** `IdentityResolution.identityOf(callback)` → `ProviderIdentity(subject, username, displayName, email)`; OIDC reads the validated ID token's `sub` and the claims named by the mapping (defaults `preferred_username`, `name`, `email`, string claims only); **plain OAuth2 still ends in `login_unavailable`** until the next step fills `identityOf` from the userinfo URL. **Login** by `(provider_id, subject)`: updates `last_login_at` (and `email` if sent), deletes the browser's old `sid` session, starts a cookie session, redirects to `next`; disabled account → `account_disabled`. **Sign-up (decision):** only the provider's `allow_signup` decides (`registration.mode` is for local registration; `invite` has no mechanism anyway), never before setup (`setup_required`); password-less account, default roles (never admin); username from the username claim, else the display name, filtered to the username alphabet, unique with a numeric suffix (`jane2`, shortened to fit 32), `user` as fallback; display name from the claim or the username. Unknown identity with sign-up off → `signup_disabled`. **Link:** `identity_in_use` (another account's), `provider_already_linked` (**decision:** one identity per provider per user; unlink first), re-linking the same identity is a no-op; back to `/account/?linked=<slug>`. Each resolution re-reads the provider in its transaction (disabled/issuer changed → `provider_unavailable`); sign-up and link take the account-creation lock and look the subject up again, so concurrent callbacks make one row; subject 1–255 chars (`provider_error` otherwise), email ≤ 320 without control characters (else not stored). **`/api/me/identities`** (users only, `401` otherwise): `GET` → `{items:[{id, provider:{slug, displayName, enabled}, email?, createdAt, lastLoginAt?}], hasPassword}` (no subject, no IP); `DELETE /{id}` → `204`, `404 identity_not_found`, **`409 last_login_method`** (added: no password and no other identity at an *enabled* provider). Audit: `auth.login` / `auth.login_failed` with `method: provider`, `provider` slug and `reason`; `account.register` (+ `auth.login`) for a sign-up; `auth.identity_linked` / `auth.identity_unlinked`; never the subject or email. **UI (minimal):** account page "Linked logins" (list, Remove, a link per enabled provider not yet linked, `?linked=`/`?error=` notice); the error texts moved from `login.ts` to a DOM-free `providers.ts` with the new codes. `docs/auth.md` step 4 and `docs/api.md` updated. Guest merge on provider login is left to the guest-merge step.

New `IdentityResolutionTest` (3, fake IdP serving **two OIDC providers at once** through the real `OidcLogin`): sign up through one, link the other, log in through each in fresh browsers to the same account, `signup_disabled` for an unknown subject with a shared email; `identity_in_use`, `provider_already_linked`, link without login, logging in replaces the browser's old session, disabled account; unlink over HTTP (404 for another user's, 204, 409 last method), anonymous 401. `PermissionMatrixTest` rows for both routes (DELETE succeeds with a linked login), `DtoIpLeakTest` sweeps `/api/me/identities` (with an identity written with an IP, removed and relinked) and lists `IdentityView` as a DTO (other new records internal). Ran: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `mvn verify -Dtest='SqliteIdentitiesTest,IdentityResolutionTest,OidcLoginTest,IdTokenValidatorTest,OidcDiscoveryTest,PermissionMatrixTest,DtoIpLeakTest,AdminApiTest,MeApiTest,MainRouterTest,MainTest,WebBuildTest,ProductNamesTest,SqliteAccountsTest,SqliteProvidersTest' -Dit.test='PostgresIdentitiesIT,PostgresAccountsIT,PostgresProvidersIT'` (163 + 31, all pass); jar smoke test (`/api/me/identities` 401 anonymous, account page and `providers.js` served, link start without login → `/account/?error=not_authenticated`).
- [x] Plain OAuth2 (Discord, GitHub) via userinfo URL + claim mapping.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `cb520d4`. **Userinfo request** (`OidcLogin.userinfo`, right after the code exchange for `oauth2` providers): `GET userinfoUrl` with `Authorization: Bearer <access_token>`, `Accept: application/json`, `User-Agent: TogetherTunes` (GitHub's API needs one) and **added** `Client-Id: <client id>` (public; Twitch Helix wants it); same client, no redirects, 15 s, 512 KiB cap; needs `200` and a JSON object or array. `OidcLogin.Callback` gains `userinfo` (JSON, `null` for OIDC). **Mapping** (`IdentityResolution.identityOf`, still the one place that reads a provider's answer; `ClaimMapping.resolve(root, path)` walks dot paths, digits index arrays): `subject` must be a non-blank string or an integer (**decision:** numbers in plain decimal, so GitHub's `583231`, `"583231"` and `583231.0` are one subject; fractions, booleans, objects refused); `username`/`displayName`/`email` strings only, else absent. Failures (request error, non-200, not JSON, too large, no usable subject) → new code **`userinfo_failed`** (message in `providers.ts`). **Added:** `Identities.checkProvider` also re-checks, for OAuth2, that the stored userinfo URL and subject path still equal the ones the login used (else `provider_unavailable`), so an admin edit during a login can't store a subject with another meaning. `login_unavailable` now only comes from the default completion. `docs/auth.md` step 5 has the details and GitHub/Discord/list-style mapping examples.
  - Tests: `OidcLoginTest` (fake IdP gains `/oauth/user`; OAuth callbacks carry the userinfo, Bearer token checked; new `oauthUserinfoFailuresNeverReachTheCompletion`: 401, 302 not followed, HTML, empty, bare number, oversized, unreachable → `userinfo_failed`). `IdentityResolutionTest` +2 (fake IdP serves plain OAuth2 under `/oauth/<slug>/`): `plainOauthProvidersThroughUserinfo` (GitHub-style numeric id sign-up with null name/email, headers, subject stored as `583231`, linking a nested `data.0.id` provider and an OIDC one to the same account, logging in through each in fresh browsers incl. the id as string / `583231.0`, another id → `octocat2`); `oauthUserinfoFailures` (11 bad answers + 3 bad nested ones → `userinfo_failed`, sign-up off → `signup_disabled`, subject path changed under a login → `provider_unavailable`). Ran: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `mvn test -Dtest='OidcLoginTest,IdentityResolutionTest,SqliteIdentitiesTest,SqliteProvidersTest,WebBuildTest,ProductNamesTest,DtoIpLeakTest,PermissionMatrixTest,IdTokenValidatorTest'` (72, pass); IT `PostgresIdentitiesIT` (7, pass).
- [x] Guest → account merge (`/api/me/merge-guest`), cookie mode only, in the browser holding the cookie; moves requests, queue entries and votes.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `1dc2aa4`. New `rooms.GuestMerge` (service + HTTP), registered by `RoomsApi.register` (it moves room data and needs the `RoomRegistry`; so `Main.router`'s signature and every test router stay as they were). **Routes:** `GET /api/me/merge-guest` → `{guest?: actor}` (the cookie guest this browser holds, if mergeable; **added** so the UI knows when to offer it); `POST` → `{guest, moved: {entries, votes}}` and clears the `gid` cookie (`Secure` per `request.isSecure()`). Users only (`401 not_authenticated`); a bearer request → `403 cookie_session_required` (GET: no `guest`); the guest is looked up by the request's `gid` cookie hash **and `kind = 'cookie'`**, so an IP-mode guest is never mergeable → `404 guest_not_found`; **decision:** a server-banned guest is refused (`403 guest_banned`, not offered). **What moves:** `room_requests` no longer exists (migration 0011 made requests queue entries with `via`/`added_by_name`), so "requests" are covered by moving **queue entries of every state** (current, queued, suggested, history); entries keep `added_by_name`. Votes and skip votes move; where the user already voted (or skip-voted) on the same entry, **the user's own vote stays** and the guest's is dropped (the `UNIQUE (entry_id, voter_user_id)` constraints). **How:** every room the guest has entries/votes/skip votes in (one `UNION` query) runs a mutation on its executor (`RoomRegistry.call`, loading it if needed): the live `RoomState` changes (`QueueEntry.withAddedByUser`, new `RoomState.mergeVoter`) and the SQL is persisted in the same commit, with the new event `RoomEvent.GuestMerged` (clients get the change through the normal snapshot diff). Up to 3 passes, in case a socket still open as the guest added something meanwhile; a room deleted meanwhile is skipped. **Afterwards:** the guest's `key_hash` is replaced by a random token's hash (the old cookie and any copy stop working), audit `guest.merged` (actor the user, target the guest, counts); the guest row stays (its uploads/items reference it). Not moved: uploads, items it added, room guest bans. **Web UI:** new DOM-free `guestmerge.ts`; `session.offerGuestMerge()` asks with `confirm()` right after a password login (unless a password change is due) or registration; the account page has a "Guest activity" section with a button (covers provider logins). `docs/api.md` and `docs/auth.md` updated.
  - Tests: new `GuestMergeContract` (`SqliteGuestMergeTest` + `PostgresGuestMergeIT`): a loaded playing room (guest's current entry, queued, suggested, played entries, votes incl. a clash with the user's own, skip votes incl. a clash, another guest's untouched data) and an unloaded room; checks counts, the live state, the database, the broadcast (`GuestMerged` only for touched rooms), key rotation, audit, equality after a reload, and that a second merge moves nothing. New `GuestMergeApiTest` (HTTP): offer/no offer (no cookie, bearer, anonymous), refusals (anonymous, bearer, no/unknown cookie), merge result + cleared cookie + live room, old cookie dead afterwards; IP-mode guest with a matching key hash and a banned guest never merged. `PermissionMatrixTest` rows (GET 401/401/200/200, POST 401/401/404/404: the cells hold no guest cookie next to `sid`), `DtoIpLeakTest` merges a second cookie guest into alice (sweep + POST) and classifies `GuestMerge$Moved`, `$CookieGuest`, `RoomEvent$GuestMerged`. Ran: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `mvn test -Dtest='WebBuildTest,ProductNamesTest,DtoIpLeakTest,PermissionMatrixTest,GuestMergeApiTest,SqliteGuestMergeTest,RoomsApiTest,MainRouterTest,MainTest,SqliteQueueControlTest,SqliteQueueOpsTest,SqliteRoomExecutorTest,RoomSocketTest,MeApiTest'` (135, pass); IT `PostgresGuestMergeIT` (pass). The browser flow (confirm after login, account-page button) wasn't clicked through in a real browser.
- [x] Tests: bad `nonce`, bad `aud`, expired token, unknown `kid` (against a local fake IdP).
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `581dc74`. The validator-level cases were already in `IdTokenValidatorTest` (previous ID-token step); this adds the end-to-end view. New `OidcLoginTest.badIdTokensAreRefusedAtTheCallback`: real logins through the router against the fake IdP (`com.sun.net.httpserver`), whose token endpoint now signs with a switchable key, publishes a switchable JWKS (fetch counter) and can tamper with the issued claims. Bad nonce (another login's, missing), bad aud (another client, none, several without `azp`, `azp` of another client), expired (2 min ago, exactly at the 60 s skew, no `exp`): each → `id_token_invalid` with the login's `next`, the state can't be retried, the completion never runs, no JWKS refetch. Unknown `kid` (never published) → one JWKS refetch then `id_token_invalid`; an immediate retry doesn't refetch (rate limit); an impostor key reusing the published `kid` fails the signature. A good login before, and after the IdP rotates in a new `kid` (accepted after one refetch). Ran: `mvn test -Dtest='OidcLoginTest,IdTokenValidatorTest'` (18 + 12, pass). Test-only change, so no `mvn package` beyond that compile.

The rest needs real client credentials and a browser.

(a) **OIDC** (e.g. your own OIDC provider, or Google): register a client with redirect URI `<publicBaseUrl>/auth/oidc/<slug>/callback` (shown on the provider's form in Admin → Providers, `/admin/#providers`), then add it there: kind OIDC, the issuer exactly as the provider's discovery document states it, client ID + secret, scopes `openid profile email`, enabled, allow sign-up. (b) **Plain OAuth** (e.g. GitHub: Settings → Developer settings → OAuth Apps, callback URL as above): kind OAuth2, scopes `read:user`, claim mapping as above (Discord: authorize `https://discord.com/oauth2/authorize`, token `https://discord.com/api/oauth2/token`, userinfo `https://discord.com/api/users/@me`, scopes `identify`, subject `id`, username `username`, display name `global_name`). (c) In a private window: `/login.html` shows a button per provider; sign up through each (lands logged in, username/display name taken from the claims); log out, log in again through each (same account, no second sign-up). (d) As one user, Account page (`/account/`): link the other provider (`intent=link`, comes back with `?linked=`), log out, log in through either: same account; unlink one (the last login method can't be removed). (e) Optional: as a cookie guest, request something in a room, then log in in the same browser and accept the guest merge offer. (f) Server log: no client secret, code or token in it. Passes when (c) and (d) work for at least one OIDC and one plain-OAuth provider.

- [x] Transition cards and `gapMs`.
  - **Done 2026-11-11** at main `4f19e88`. **Room:** after a **natural end** the next entry starts `RoomSettings.transitionGapMs()` later (`Timeline.startingAt(entry, now + gap)`, the delayed-start mechanism skips already use, so web clients need no change: the entry is current at once, silent at 0 until its start). Skips, `previous`, starts from idle and going idle get no gap. **Decision:** `transitionCard` makes the gap at least 2 s (one card GOP), so the toggle alone shows a 2 s card; `transitionGapMs()` = `max(gapMs, 2000)` while on, else `gapMs`. **Output:** `ProgramCue.Gap(ms, card)` (via `ProgramOutputs.Snapshot`); `TimelineFollower` plays a new `GapSource` (a card + the silence frame, cut to the gap's whole AAC frames; under one video frame = no gap) at `E_n` whether or not the room has advanced (it keeps it while the timeline is at the ended item or at the gap's entry as its natural advance, then starts the entry from 0; pause/seek/skip in the gap apply at the next boundary; > 3 s behind skips the gap and re-syncs). **What the gap shows:** with `transitionCard` the next item's card (audio item: its indexed card; video: its `card.mp4`, which `ProgramMedia` now loads after the item's files while the room shows transition cards, optional: a failure means the blank card, retried after 5 s, never fails the item; the overlaid version when the room's overlay is on); **decision:** otherwise, or until that card is loaded, a new **blank filler card** (`CardRenderer.Filler.BLANK` → `blank.mp4`, the filler background without icon/text, `FillerSource.Filler.GAP`; encoded at startup and overlaid per room like the other fillers). `ScriptedTimeline.gap(...)` for the harness. UI: help texts on the two settings (Manage room → Settings → Playback). Docs: new sections `program-output.md#transitions` and `rooms.md#transitions`, filler card lists in `library-and-ingest.md` and `architecture.md`.
  - Tests: `TimelineFollowerTest` +4 (gap then next item from 0 with the room advancing during it; gap ends before the room advances, advance adopted; room advanced first, 3 s gap across a unit boundary; pause in the gap, sub-frame gap left out), `ProgramMediaTest` +3 (blank vs next item's card, `card.mp4` loaded after the files and once, missing card doesn't stop the item and is retried after the backoff, overlaid transition card), `PlaybackContract.naturalEndsWaitForTheTransitionGap` (SQLite + PostgreSQL), `RoomSettingsTest.transitionGapIsAtLeastOneCardWhileTheCardIsOn`, new `TransitionGapOutputTest` (real encodes: video → card → audio → card.mp4 → video → blank gap → audio → idle; gap lengths 141/141/47 frames, contiguous, `assertPackets`, decodes clean); `CardRendererTest`/`OverlayCardsTest` adjusted for the fourth filler card; `DtoIpLeakTest` classifies `ProgramCue$Gap`, `ScriptedTimeline$SetGap`. Ran: `mvn package -DskipTests`; `mvn test -Dtest='RoomSettingsTest,TimelineFollowerTest,ProgramMediaTest,SqlitePlaybackTest,ProgramOutputsTest,ProgramOutputsRoomsTest,SourcesTest,UnitBuilderTest,PackagerTest,CardRendererTest,FillerCardsTest,OverlayCardsTest,DtoIpLeakTest,OfflineHarnessTest,WebBuildTest,ProductNamesTest,RoomsApiTest,MainEncodingTest,SqliteQueueControlTest,SqliteQueueOpsTest,SqliteRoomExecutorTest,TimelineTest,RoomSocketTest,PlayableFilesTest,TransitionGapOutputTest'` (all pass); IT `PostgresPlaybackIT` (22, pass). Not checked in a real browser or a real stream player (how the gap looks/sounds there).

## Notes / decisions log

Record decisions, deviations from `docs/`, and answers to open questions here (newest last), with the date.

- 2026-11-08: Plan created from the design docs. No implementation yet.
- 2026-11-08: HTTP stack is `io.siggi:SiggiHTTP:1.10`. Capability survey in "Before M0".
- 2026-11-08: Build tool is Maven, not Gradle (README open question 1 resolved).
- 2026-11-08: Remaining open questions resolved:
  - Default output profile **720p30**, configurable (`output.profile`).
  - **Audio uploads keep their original**, and the web player uses it (gain-matched to −14 LUFS, with fallback to `audio.m4a`). Video and YouTube originals are discarded. This replaces the former `ingest.keepOriginals` setting.
  - Libraries: Bouncy Castle, Nimbus, ZXing as planned; **Gson** for JSON; no connection-pool library (hand-written pool).
  - `.ts` framing: copy VRCDN. Observed on its stream: close-delimited body, `HEAD` → `200` + `Content-Length: 0`, SDT/PAT/PMT at the start, PAT/PMT about every 0.4 s, PIDs 0x100/0x101/0x102, joins mid-GOP with no burst. We copy all of it except the join point (`join=next` instead of mid-GOP). Details in `docs/program-output.md`.
  - WebSocket: **SimpleWebsocket**.
  - Client IP: leave SiggiHTTP's XFF handling as is; a future SiggiHTTP will require explicit trusted ranges.
  - Frontend in **TypeScript** (tsc via `frontend-maven-plugin`, no bundler).
  - Config file is **JSON**.
  - Coordinates `io.siggi:TogetherTunes`, package `io.siggi.togethertunes`.

The app runs on the **classpath, not as a JPMS module** (SiggiHTTP's `module-info.class` is dropped while shading).
