Packages under `io.siggi.togethertunes`: `http`, `auth`, `db`, `library`, `ingest`, `rooms`, `media`, `output`, `overlay`, `retention` (from `docs/architecture.md#code-layout-suggested`; the doc's `server/` level is the base package itself).
- 2026-11-08: Frontend build set up. `frontend-maven-plugin` 2.0.2 installs **Node v24.22.2** (LTS; npm is the bundled one) into `web/node/`, then runs `npm ci` and `npm run build` in `web/` during `generate-resources`. **TypeScript 7.0.2** (the native port; per-platform binaries come as optional deps, all recorded in `web/package-lock.json`) is pinned exactly. `web/tsconfig.json`: `strict` plus `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `noUnusedLocals/Parameters`, `noImplicitReturns`, `verbatimModuleSyntax`, `noEmitOnError`; target ES2022, `module` ES2022, source maps with inline sources. No bundler, so relative imports name the compiled file (`./x.js`). Layout: TS in `web/src/` compiles to `target/classes/web/js/`; static assets in `web/static/` are copied to `target/classes/web/` (Maven `<resources>` with `targetPath` `web`), so the jar holds the whole UI under `/web/` (`/web/index.html`, `/web/js/main.js`, ...). The static-file step decides how the server serves it (`returnFile` needs a `File`, so e.g. extract `/web/` from the jar on startup, or serve `target/classes/web/` in dev). `cd web && npm run watch` recompiles on change (with the downloaded Node on `PATH`). `WebBuildTest` checks the packaged output.
- 2026-11-08: Config loading done (`io.siggi.togethertunes.config`: `ConfigLoader`, `Config` record, `OutputProfile` enum with 720p30/1080p30 and their size and fps; **Gson 2.13.2**). Config file: `--config <file>` (or `-c`, `--config=`), else `TOGETHERTUNES_CONFIG`, else `./config.json` if it exists; the file is optional, so a config made only of env vars works. JSON keys can be nested or dotted, numbers may be strings, `null` means default, unknown or duplicate keys are errors (strict Gson reader). Env overrides: `TOGETHERTUNES_` + key with dots as `_` and camelCase as snake case (`http.publicBaseUrl` → `TOGETHERTUNES_HTTP_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`); blank counts as unset; `http.trustedProxies` comma-separated; unknown `TOGETHERTUNES_*` vars are only warnings (so later steps can add e.g. a secret-key variable). Validation collects every problem into one `ConfigException`; `Main` prints them and exits 1. Details: `publicBaseUrl` must be absolute http(s) with a host, no user info/query/fragment, and is stored lowercased without a trailing slash (a path prefix is allowed); `trustedProxies` are IPv4/IPv6 literals or CIDRs (no DNS, host bits must be zero, bare address = /32 or /128), stored normalised; sizes accept bytes or `KiB/MiB/GiB/TiB` (`4 GiB`); `data.dir` resolves against the working directory, not the config file's directory; `db.url` must be `jdbc:sqlite:` or `jdbc:postgresql:`; workers 1–256. Tools: a path must be an executable file, a bare name is resolved on `PATH` to an absolute path; a configured name not on `PATH` is an error, a missing default only a warning. `config.example.json` at the repo root; `/config.json` is git-ignored for local dev.
- 2026-11-08: SiggiHTTP integrated (`io.siggi.togethertunes.http`). `WebServer` owns the `ServerSocket` and accept loop (SiggiHTTP's own `listen()` is deprecated and uses platform threads) and hands each socket to `HTTPServer.handle`, built with `HTTPServerBuilder.setExecutor(newThreadPerTaskExecutor(virtual))`, so every connection and request handler runs on a virtual thread (tested: 200 concurrently blocked handlers). One `Router` is registered at `/` and answers everything itself: patterns with literal segments, `{name}` (one non-empty segment) and a trailing `{name...}` (rest, may be empty but needs the slash); paths are matched after SiggiHTTP's percent-decoding; among routes matching path *and* method the most specific wins (literal > param > rest, segment by segment), so a literal POST route doesn't hide a GET param route; no match → `404`, path matches but method doesn't → `405` + `Allow`, `OPTIONS` → `204` + `Allow`, `HEAD` runs the GET route. Errors use the `docs/api.md` body: `HttpError` (status, snake_case code, message) from handlers/hooks; other exceptions are logged and become `500 internal_error` with no details; a handler that writes nothing gets `404` (like SiggiHTTP's own rule, e.g. `returnFile` on a missing file); after the response is committed exceptions go to SiggiHTTP, which closes the connection. `Request` adapter: path params, query, headers, cookies, `clientIp()` (only for rate limits/guest IP mode/audit, never in responses), `body()`, `readJson` (requires `application/json`, 1 MiB default limit → 413, bad JSON → 400 `invalid_json`), typed attributes for hooks to pass data (e.g. the actor). `Response` writes through to SiggiHTTP (`json`, `text`, `empty`, `error`, `detach()` = `openResponse()`, `raw()` for `returnFile`/streaming). Hook chain: `Hook.handle(req, res, next)`, router-wide hooks (`Hooks.authenticate()`, `Hooks.csrf()`, set in `Main.router`) then the route's own (e.g. `Hooks.rateLimit("login")`), composed once at registration; all three are pass-through placeholders until M1. JSON via a shared `Json.GSON`; logging via `System.Logger`. `Main` now starts the server and blocks (exit 3 if it can't bind); SiggiHTTP's tmp dir is `java.io.tmpdir` until the data-dir step. Note: on `WebServer.close()` idle keep-alive handlers are interrupted and SiggiHTTP prints a harmless "Socket is closed" stack trace for each (seen in tests; not reported upstream).
- 2026-11-08: Range step: SiggiHTTP's `returnFile` mishandled suffix ranges, end positions past the file end and unsatisfiable ranges (no 416). Reported; fixed in SiggiHTTP 1.11-SNAPSHOT `e68dd28`, which TogetherTunes now needs. `RangeRequestTest` (7 tests) passes.
- 2026-11-08: Streaming responses done (needs SiggiHTTP 1.11-SNAPSHOT `4be3e04`+ for `closeDelimited()`). `Response.stream([status,] contentType, StreamBody.Framing.{CHUNKED|CLOSE_DELIMITED})` sets status, type and `Cache-Control: no-store`, picks the framing, turns buffering off and returns a `StreamBody` (an `OutputStream` that flushes every write). For `HEAD` it sends the headers at once and `headOnly()` is true (writes are discarded), so producers must check it and return. A client leaving surfaces as a write `IOException` in about 10–15 ms on loopback (writing every 5 ms); handlers can let it propagate, and SiggiHTTP ends the connection without logging. A detached close-delimited stream can be ended with `StreamBody.close()` from another thread. `StreamingResponseTest` (9 tests, raw sockets) covers header framing, chunk framing, keep-alive after a finite chunked body, HEAD, the detached case, detection time and that nothing is logged on stderr. Aborting a writer blocked on a stalled client from another thread is still open (M4).
- 2026-11-08: Request bodies streamed to disk. `Request.copyBody(out, maxBytes)` streams the raw body (Content-Length or chunked) with a 64 KiB buffer and returns the byte count; wrap `out` in a `DigestOutputStream` for the upload SHA-256. `Request.bodyToFile(path, position, maxBytes)` writes at an offset through a `FileChannel` (CREATE, WRITE, never truncates) for resumable `Content-Range` chunks; the caller validates the offset. Limits: a declared `Content-Length` over `maxBytes` → 413 before reading (and before the file is created); a chunked body → 413 once it passes the limit (bytes up to the limit stay written). A body that ends early (client gone, 60 s SiggiHTTP read timeout, fewer bytes than `Content-Length`) → 400 `incomplete_body`, not logged; received bytes stay in the file. Write errors (disk full) propagate → 500. `Request.contentLength()` added. `WebServer` now calls `setIgnoringMultipartFormData(true)` (SiggiHTTP would otherwise spool any multipart body to the tmp dir on every path before routing) and `setUploadLimit(0)` (its default 2 GB limit refused bigger `Expect: 100-continue` bodies with a plain-text 413; handlers own limits now, and SiggiHTTP still sends `100 Continue`). `RequestBodyTest` (10 tests, raw sockets): Content-Length and chunked bodies to file, keep-alive reuse, offsets, a 4 GiB + 12345 B body (no disk; about 1.3 s on loopback), early 413, Expect over 2 GiB, chunked over limit, truncated bodies, multipart unparsed. Reported to the SiggiHTTP session (non-blocking): `SubInputStream` reports a truncated Content-Length body as a normal EOF; `copyBody` checks the count itself.
- 2026-11-08: WebSocket support done (`io.siggi.togethertunes.http.WebSockets`, one instance per server holding the one `WebSocketAcceptor`). Settings: `setMaxMessageSize(65536)` **and `setMaxPayloadLength(65536)`**: SimpleWebsocket's per-frame limit defaults to 16 KiB, and browsers send each message as a single frame, so without it any 16–64 KiB message would close the socket with 1009 "Frame too big". Origin: `setAllowedOrigins(origin of publicBaseUrl)` (scheme, host, non-default port; a path prefix is dropped), case-insensitive; requests **without** `Origin` are accepted (`setAllowMissingOrigin(true)`, the default), since only browsers send it and only browsers attach a victim's cookies; bearer clients authenticate in their first message. `webSockets.handler(endpoint)` gives a GET route handler: a non-upgrade request → `426 upgrade_required` with `Upgrade: websocket`; a disallowed origin is checked before `accept()` (`isOriginAllowed`) → `403 origin_not_allowed` with the normal JSON error body, logged at INFO; an unsupported `Sec-WebSocket-Version` gets SimpleWebsocket's own 426. After the upgrade the request's handler thread (already a virtual thread) stays with the socket, renamed `ws-<n>`, and runs the blocking `read()` loop; `Endpoint.open(request, ws)` returns a `Session` (`onMessage` for text/binary only; pongs and close frames are skipped; `onClose(info)`). An exception from `open`/`onMessage` is logged and closes with 1011; an `IOException` just ends the socket. When a socket ends it's closed (1001 if the thread was interrupted, e.g. server shutdown, else 1000) and one INFO line is logged: `ws-<n> on <path> closed after <s> s: <getCloseInfo()>`. Route hooks run before the upgrade, so M1 auth can still reject with a plain HTTP error. Nothing is routed yet (the echo endpoint and `/ws` come in later steps). `WebSocketsTest` (15 tests): echo text/binary, origin variants (case, wrong scheme/port/host, `null`, suffix trick) → 403 with no socket opened, missing origin accepted, 426 for plain GET and for version 8, a 64 KiB incompressible message echoes and 64 KiB + 1 closes with 1009, two sockets on distinct virtual threads, close info logged (code and reason), peer vanishing → unclean, handler exception → 1011, server shutdown ends open sockets. Note for clients built on SimpleWebsocket (tests, later tools): their 16 KiB frame default applies to messages *from* the server too.
- 2026-11-08: Client IP step: SiggiHTTP's `isIPTrusted` matched only part of each range, never matched /0, and threw on IPv6 scope IDs. Reported; fixed in 1.11-SNAPSHOT `2a07447`, which TogetherTunes now needs. Our code clears SiggiHTTP's default private-range trust (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16) and trusts only `http.trustedProxies`; loopback and Cloudflare stay trusted inside SiggiHTTP until its 2.0. A test that expected 203.0.113.5 outside 128.0.0.0/1 was wrong (it only passed because of the bug) and was corrected.
- 2026-11-08: Static file serving done (`io.siggi.togethertunes.http.StaticFiles`, main `eb6cdeb`). The web UI is served at the **site root** (`/` = `index.html`, `/js/main.js`, `/css/main.css`, matching the URLs in `index.html`) as the catch-all route `GET /{path...}`, so every more specific route (API, media, live, ws) wins. Only a GET route is registered: the router answers `OPTIONS` itself (`204`, `Allow: GET, HEAD, OPTIONS`) and `PUT`/`DELETE`/`POST`/`PATCH` with `405`; the handler re-checks the method before `returnFile`. Side effect of a catch-all GET: a non-GET request to any unrouted path (e.g. `POST /api/unknown`) gets `405` instead of `404`. Files go through `returnFile(file, contentType)` (Range, ETag, Last-Modified, 304). Content types by extension (`charset=utf-8` on text, `text/javascript` for `.js`, `application/json` for `.map`, `application/octet-stream` for unknown) plus `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. Caching: `Cache-Control: no-cache` on every file, since names carry no content hash (no bundler); revalidation costs a 304. Add hashed/versioned names and long `max-age` later if it matters. Traversal: `.`/`..`/empty segments, backslashes, NUL and dot-files are refused, then the normalised path and the real path (symlinks resolved) must stay inside the root; anything else is a 404 (`returnFile` itself would happily serve a directory or follow `..`). A directory serves its `index.html`; without the trailing slash it redirects `308` to `path/`; no listings. In the jar, `StaticFiles.fromClasspath` extracts `/web/` into a fresh `web-*` directory under `java.io.tmpdir` (entry mtimes kept, so ETags are stable for a build) and deletes it on shutdown; from `target/classes` (IDE, tests) it serves in place. A hard kill leaves the extracted copy behind; the data-dir step should move it to `<data>/tmp` and clear stale copies on startup. `StaticFilesTest` (15 tests, raw sockets): content types, index/redirect, 304 by ETag and date, HEAD and Range, traversal (`..`, `%2e%2e`, `%5c`, `//`, `%00`, symlinks out, dot-files), `OPTIONS` (with and without a body), `PUT`, `DELETE`, `POST`, `PATCH` on existing, new and traversal paths leave the whole tree byte- and mtime-identical, the handler refuses non-GET methods even if mounted for them, and extraction from a jar. Smoke-tested with the packaged jar.
- 2026-11-08: `Dialect` done (`io.siggi.togethertunes.db`, main `69afe8b`). Sealed interface with `SqliteDialect` and `PostgresDialect` singletons; `Dialect.forJdbcUrl(url)`; `name()` is `sqlite`/`postgres` (the migrations subdirectory). Both databases accept the same upsert and `RETURNING` syntax (SQLite 3.24+/3.35+; sqlite-jdbc bundles a newer one, checked by a test), so those builders are shared defaults: `upsert(table, insertColumns, conflictColumns, updateColumns)` → `INSERT ... VALUES (?, ...) ON CONFLICT (...) DO UPDATE SET c = excluded.c` (no update columns → `DO NOTHING`; `insertOrIgnore` is that), `returning(sql, cols...)` appends `RETURNING ...` (run with `executeQuery`; an ignored insert returns no row). Builders accept only plain identifiers (`[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`), so they can't inject SQL. Case-insensitive search: `containsIgnoreCase(col)` → `<lower(col)> LIKE ? ESCAPE '\'`, bind `Dialect.containsPattern(term)` (folded, `%`/`_`/`\` escaped, wrapped in `%`); `equalsIgnoreCase(col)` → `<lower(col)> = ?`, bind `Dialect.foldCase(value)` (`toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT)`; no accent stripping). SQLite's own `lower()`/`LIKE` fold ASCII only, so `SqliteDialect.initConnection` registers a deterministic Java function `tt_lower()` on the connection; **the connection-handling step must call `dialect.initConnection(conn)` on every new connection** (PostgreSQL's is a no-op). PostgreSQL uses `lower()`, which is Unicode-aware for a UTF-8 database with a non-`C` ctype (true for the official image; document this for PostgreSQL deployments). New dependencies (pre-approved): `org.xerial:sqlite-jdbc` 3.54.1.0 (compile scope, for `org.sqlite.Function`), `org.postgresql:postgresql` 42.7.14, Testcontainers 2.0.5 (`testcontainers-postgresql`, test scope, via BOM). Tests: `DialectTest` (SQL generation, rejects bad identifiers), `DialectContract` run by `SqliteDialectTest` (in-memory) and `PostgresDialectIT` (`postgres:16-alpine`): upserts (single/composite key, set to NULL, ignore), `RETURNING` on upsert/ignored insert/UPDATE/DELETE, search with ASCII, Ö/Ó/Greek, literal wildcards, NULLs. Testcontainers prints an SLF4J "no binding" warning in ITs (harmless; no logging backend added).
- 2026-11-08: Connection handling done (`io.siggi.togethertunes.db.Database` + `ConnectionPool`, main `59d04c2`). All DB work goes through `db.read(c -> ...)` / `db.write(c -> ...)` (`SqlWork<T>`): one transaction per call on a pooled connection, commit when the callback returns, rollback when it throws; the callback must not close the connection or touch auto-commit/read-only. A nested `read`/`write` on the same thread joins the outer transaction (a nested read inside a write sees its uncommitted rows); `write` inside `read` throws `IllegalStateException`. **SQLite:** every connection gets `journal_mode=WAL` (set by the writer; a WARNING is logged if the file system refuses it), `foreign_keys=ON`, `busy_timeout=5000` and `dialect.initConnection` (`tt_lower`). Chose the **single write connection** (a pool of one, so in-process writers queue instead of hitting `SQLITE_BUSY`, no retry loop needed), with `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` so a lock held by another process is waited for up front; reads use up to 4 **read-only** connections that run concurrently with the writer and see the last commit. Keep write callbacks short (no network/file I/O inside). `jdbc:sqlite::memory:` (and `file::memory:`, `mode=memory`) uses the one connection for reads too. Missing parent directories of the SQLite file are created. Gotcha: `SQLiteConfig(Properties)` keeps and returns the very `Properties` object it's given, so every config gets its own copy. **PostgreSQL:** one pool (default 10) for reads and writes; `read` runs `READ ONLY` (SQLState 25006 on a write); `ApplicationName=TogetherTunes`; credentials in the URL (`?user=&password=`) or the `Properties` passed to `Database.open(url, props, settings)`. **Pool:** bounded by a fair `Semaphore` (borrow timeout 30 s → `SQLTransientConnectionException`), lazy opening, LIFO reuse, `isValid` check for connections idle > 10 s, 30 min max lifetime, connections that fail with SQLState `08*`/`57P*` or end up closed are discarded; `ReentrantLock`/`Semaphore` only (no `synchronized`, so waiting virtual threads don't pin). `Database.open` opens one connection right away (fail fast). `Main` opens the DB from `db.url` after loading the config (exit 3 with the driver's message if it fails) and closes it on shutdown (the SQLite writer closes last, which checkpoints and removes the WAL). Pool sizes aren't config keys yet (`Database.PoolSettings`). Tests: `ConnectionPoolTest` (9), `DatabaseContract` run by `SqliteDatabaseTest` (17, temp file: pragmas on writer and readers, single writer, read-modify-write serialised over 100 threads, readers not blocked by an open write and not seeing it, another process's lock waited for, in-memory mode, close removes WAL) and `PostgresDatabaseIT` (15, `postgres:16-alpine`: read-only tx, server-side connection count ≤ pool size via `pg_stat_activity`, reuse, recovery after `pg_terminate_backend`, connection killed mid-transaction is discarded, bad credentials fail at open). Smoke-tested with the jar: `data/db/media.db` created on start.
- 2026-11-08: UUIDv7 and time helper done (`io.siggi.togethertunes.db`, main `1f496ab`). `UuidV7.newId()` returns a canonical lowercase 36-char string (shared generator: `Clock.systemUTC()` + `SecureRandom`; `new UuidV7(clock, random)` for tests). RFC 9562 layout (48-bit ms, ver 7, 12-bit rand_a, variant 10, 62-bit rand_b). Monotonic per generator (RFC method 2): a new millisecond gets 74 fresh random bits; same or earlier clock reading reuses the last timestamp and adds a random step of 1..2^32 to the 74 bits, so IDs strictly increase (also across a backwards clock step) and a neighbour can't be guessed by +1; on overflow the timestamp borrows the next millisecond. Lock is a `ReentrantLock`. Lowercase canonical strings sort like the 128-bit values, so `ORDER BY id` on the `TEXT` column is creation order (tested in SQLite). `UuidV7.isValid(s)` accepts only canonical lowercase v7 strings (use it on IDs from URLs/bodies; invalid = not found); `timestampMs(...)` reads the embedded time. `EpochMs`: `now()`, `now(Clock)` (services that need controllable time take a `java.time.Clock`), `of(Instant)`/`toInstant`, saturating `plus(ms, Duration)`, `format` (ISO-8601 UTC for logs), and nullable `BIGINT` JDBC `get(rs, col)`/`set(ps, i, Long)`. Tests: `UuidV7Test` (12), `EpochMsTest` (4).
- 2026-11-08: Migration runner done (`io.siggi.togethertunes.db.MigrationRunner`, `Migration`, `SqlScript`; main `3ed4201`). Files live in **`src/main/resources/db/migrations/{sqlite,postgres}/`** (classpath `db/migrations/<dialect.name()>/`; currently only `.gitkeep`, the initial-schema step adds `0001_...`). Names must match `NNNN_name.sql` (4–9 digits, version ≥ 1, lower-case `[a-z0-9]` words joined by single underscores); any other non-hidden file in the directory is an error, hidden files are ignored. Loading works from `target/classes` and from the shaded jar (entries are listed through the jar, so directory entries aren't required). `MigrationRunner.migrate(db)` creates `schema_migrations (version INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, name TEXT NOT NULL, checksum TEXT NOT NULL, applied_at BIGINT NOT NULL)`, then applies pending files in version order, **each in its own `db.write` transaction together with its `schema_migrations` row**, so a failure rolls back that file completely (DDL included, on both databases) and earlier files stay applied; the error names the file and the failing statement. Checksum = SHA-256 of the file with line endings normalised to `\n`. Startup fails (`MigrationException`, `Main` exits 3) when an applied file was edited (checksum mismatch), an applied version has no file (database from a newer build), a new file is numbered below the highest applied version, two files share a version, or a file contains transaction control (`BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`END`/`ROLLBACK`/`SAVEPOINT`/`RELEASE`/`VACUUM`). Also don't use statements that can't run in a transaction (PostgreSQL `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY`, SQLite `PRAGMA foreign_keys`). Scripts are split into statements by `SqlScript` (JDBC drivers don't reliably run several per `execute`): `;` outside `'...'` (incl. `E'...'`), `"..."`, `` `...` ``, `--`/`/* */` comments and `$$`/`$tag$` dollar quotes; SQLite `CREATE TRIGGER ... BEGIN ... END` bodies (with `CASE ... END`) stay whole. Concurrent runners on one PostgreSQL database serialise on `pg_advisory_xact_lock` in every migration transaction and re-check the version inside it, so each file is applied once (tested with 4 runners); SQLite writes are already exclusive. `Main` runs `MigrationRunner.migrate(database)` right after opening the database. Tests: `SqlScriptTest` (7), `MigrationLoadingTest` (8: names, directory, classpath dir, jar without directory entries, SQLite/PostgreSQL file sets have identical names), `MigrationContract` run by `SqliteMigrationTest` (14, incl. trigger bodies, history kept across reopen) and `PostgresMigrationIT` (14, incl. plpgsql/`DO` dollar quoting and concurrent runners).
- 2026-11-08: Initial schema done (main `302230d`): `db/migrations/{sqlite,postgres}/0001_initial_schema.sql`, every table in `docs/data-model.md`. Column types: `TEXT`, `BIGINT`, `INTEGER`, and `REAL` (SQLite) = `DOUBLE PRECISION` (PostgreSQL) for `position`, `progress`, `loudness_lufs`, `original_gain_db` (PostgreSQL `REAL` is only 4 bytes, too coarse for fractional queue positions). All PKs `NOT NULL` (SQLite would otherwise allow NULL in a TEXT PK). Columns the doc doesn't mark NULL are `NOT NULL`, with these deliberate exceptions/choices: `items.duration_ms` and `items.metadata_json` nullable (unknown before probing), `oidc_providers.client_secret_enc` and `claim_mapping_json` nullable (public clients / pure OIDC), `sessions.user_agent`/`ip`, `audit_log.target`/`details_json`, `user_identities.last_login_at` nullable. Booleans have `CHECK (x IN (0, 1))` and defaults (`enabled` 1, everything else 0); counters/anchors/`version` default 0. Enums are plain `TEXT` (checked in code, per the doc). Actor CHECK `(a IS NULL) <> (b IS NULL)` on `items`, `queue_entries`, `votes`, `skip_votes`. Deviations: `room_requests` uses **at most one** actor (its user/guest FKs are `ON DELETE SET NULL`, `by_name` keeps the name, as the doc's comment implies); `audit_log` has no FKs and both actor columns may be NULL (anonymous failed logins; the log outlives users/guests). FK deletes: rows owned by a room (members, guest bans, queue entries and their votes, requests, outputs) cascade with the room; rows owned by a user (credentials, identities, sessions, user_roles, library entries, playlists, shares, memberships, votes) cascade with the user; `items` are never cascaded (rows are kept forever, invariant 7), so FKs to items are `NO ACTION`; `rooms.current_entry_id` and `room_requests.entry_id` are `SET NULL`; `items.added_by_*`, `queue_entries.added_by_*` and `rooms.owner_id` are `NO ACTION` (deleting users/guests isn't designed yet; guest merge moves those rows first). On PostgreSQL the circular `rooms.current_entry_id` FK is added with `ALTER TABLE` after `queue_entries` (named `rooms_current_entry_id_fkey`, same as an inline one would be); SQLite declares it inline. `username` uniqueness is plain (case-sensitive) `UNIQUE`; case-insensitive/NFKC clash checks happen in code. Indexes on FK and lookup columns (sessions by user/expiry, items by sha256 and (status, last_referenced_at), queue by (room, state, position), requests by (room, at), shares by (to_user, created_at), etc.). Tests: `InitialSchemaContract` run by `SqliteInitialSchemaTest` and `PostgresInitialSchemaIT` (7 each): exact table/column sets vs the doc, actor checks, FK enforcement, `youtube_id` unique, room deletion cascades and keeps items, current entry / request entry set NULL, deleted guest keeps request history, double precision of positions. The full cross-dialect equivalence test is still its own step.
- 2026-11-08: Settings storage done (new package `io.siggi.togethertunes.settings`, main `07aa7ad`). `Settings(db)` / `Settings(db, config)` over the `settings (key, value_json)` table. Each setting is a `SettingKey<T>` constant on `Settings` (name, Java type, default, JSON codec with validation); `Settings.KEYS` lists all 18 in doc order. Typed accessors (`registrationMode()`, `guestsEnabled()`, `guestsUploadQuota()`, `retentionUnreferencedDays()`, `outputsHlsEnabled()`, ...), plus generic `get(key)`, `set(key, value)` (upsert; validates, `IllegalArgumentException` with a readable message), `reset(key)` (deletes the row, so the setting follows its default), `all()` (every effective value in one query) and `setJson(name, jsonText)` for the admin API (strict JSON, unknown names rejected). No cache: every read hits the DB, so changes are seen at once and calls inside `db.read`/`db.write` join that transaction (a rolled-back `set` leaves nothing). A stored row that is malformed or fails validation logs a WARNING and reads as the default; rows with unknown keys are ignored. Stored forms: booleans/ints as JSON literals, enums as their lowercase wire name (`"open"`, `"cookie"`), `guests.uploadQuota` as `{"perHour":3,"maxItemBytes":524288000}` (record `UploadQuota`; per guest and per IP is the enforcing code's job), `ytdlp.cookiesFile` as a string or `null` (empty string = null). **Defaults the docs didn't give, chosen here:** `registration.mode` = `closed` (safe default; the first-user setup page works regardless; there is no invite table yet, so `invite` needs design before it does anything), `ytdlp.enabled` = `true` (users still need `library.youtube`), `ytdlp.cookiesFile` = none. Documented ones: guests per the guest-policy table, `retention.unreferencedDays` 14 (0..36500, 0 = never), all three outputs on, `overlay.enabled` off. `ingest.maxDurationSeconds` is both a config key and a setting: `Settings(db, config)` takes the config value as the setting's default, and the admin UI can override it (`Main` should construct it that way once something uses it). **Default roles are not a setting**: they are `roles.is_default` (already in the schema). Tests: `SettingsContract` (8) run by `SqliteSettingsTest` and `PostgresSettingsIT`: documented defaults, round trips and stored JSON, upsert/reset, validation on set and via `setJson`, bad/unknown stored rows, rollback of a `set` inside an outer transaction, config-supplied default.
- 2026-11-08: Secret key handling done (new package `io.siggi.togethertunes.secrets`, main `91319b4`). `ServerSecret.load(dataDir, env)`: env var **`TOGETHERTUNES_SECRET_KEY`** (base64 of 32 bytes, standard or URL-safe, padding optional) wins and then no file is read or created; otherwise `<data>/secret.key` (one line of base64), generated on first start with mode 0600 (data dir created if needed; written to a temp file and hard-linked into place, rename fallback, so no half-written key and concurrent starts agree on one key). A malformed key (env or file, wrong length, empty file) is a startup error (exit 3), never silently replaced; a file readable by group/others is only a warning. `SecretCipher` (AES-256-GCM): `encrypt(plaintext, context)` / `decrypt(stored, context)`, null passes through; stored form is URL-safe unpadded base64 of `0x01 version || 12-byte random nonce || ciphertext+16-byte tag`; the context, by convention `"<table>.<column>:<row id>"` (e.g. `oidc_providers.client_secret_enc:<id>`), plus the version byte is the AAD, so a ciphertext moved to another row/column fails. Wrong key/context/tampering → `SecretDecryptionException` (message never contains the value). No key rotation yet (the version byte leaves room). `Main` loads the key right after config and prints its source; the cipher isn't passed anywhere yet (TODO for the provider admin / stream outputs steps). `ConfigLoader` knows the variable (`SECRET_KEY_ENV`) so it isn't warned about as unknown. Tests: `SecretCipherTest` (10), `ServerSecretTest` (10, incl. 16 concurrent first starts).
- 2026-11-08: Cross-dialect schema equivalence test done (main `bdb2efe`). `SchemaEquivalenceIT` runs `MigrationRunner.migrate` on a fresh SQLite file and on `postgres:16-alpine`, reads both catalogs into a dialect-neutral `SchemaSnapshot` (test code; SQLite via `sqlite_master` and `pragma_table_info`/`foreign_key_list`/`index_list`/`index_info`, PostgreSQL via `pg_class`/`pg_attribute`/`pg_attrdef`/`pg_constraint`/`pg_index`/`pg_trigger` in `current_schema()`) and fails with one `only in SQLite:` / `only in PostgreSQL:` line per difference. Compared: tables (incl. `schema_migrations`), views, triggers; columns with normalised type (`REAL` = `DOUBLE PRECISION` = `double`), nullability (PK columns count as NOT NULL, since SQLite reports `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY` as nullable) and default (PostgreSQL casts stripped); primary keys in order; foreign keys with referenced columns and ON DELETE/ON UPDATE; unique constraints and unique indexes by column list (names differ between dialects); non-unique indexes by name and columns; partial-index flag; and for each CHECK constraint only the set of columns it uses (PostgreSQL rewrites the expressions, e.g. `IN (0, 1)` → `= ANY (ARRAY[0, 1])`; `InitialSchemaContract` tests their behaviour). Not compared: collations, `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`, the CHECK expressions themselves. A second test proves a nullability difference is reported; also checked by hand that changing an FK action, dropping a CHECK and dropping an index in the PostgreSQL migration each fail the test.
- 2026-11-08: Data dir layout: `DataDir.create` (root package) makes `<data>/` plus `db/`, `media/`, `cards/`, `rooms/`, `tmp/` before the secret key is loaded, follows symlinks (e.g. `media/` on another disk) and fails startup (exit 3) if any is not a writable directory. The HTTP server's temp files and the extracted web UI (`tmp/web-*`, deleted on shutdown) now live in `<data>/tmp` instead of `java.io.tmpdir`. Stale `tmp/web-*` dirs after a crash aren't swept yet; leave that to the M2 tmp/upload cleanup.

No login needed (M1 hooks will apply router-wide). Test page `/test/ws-echo.html` (`web/static/test/ws-echo.html`, `web/src/test/ws-echo.ts` → `/js/test/ws-echo.js`, `web/static/css/test.css`; linked from `/`): connect / close (1000), optional connect on load (remembered in localStorage), send text, 16 KiB text, 1 KiB / 64 KiB / 64 KiB + 1 binary, a burst of 100, periodic send every N s; every echo is checked against what was sent (in order); the log (also mirrored to the console with a `[ws-echo]` prefix) shows open time, negotiated extensions, close code with its name, reason, `wasClean`, open and idle duration and unanswered messages, and the status line shows sent/echoed/pending/mismatch counts and the idle timer. - 2026-11-08: M0 browser test done with a new unattended page, `/test/ws-auto.html` (`web/src/test/ws-auto.ts`; main `f0d3253`). Click Start and it runs ten checks in about 7 minutes, then shows a results table and POSTs the report to `/test/ws-echo/report`, which logs it on one line (`WebSocket test report: {...}`). The echo socket remembers the server-side close info of sockets opened with `?tag=<tag>` (last 500, in memory; `GET /test/ws-echo/closes?tag=`), so the page checks what the server saw for each close. The closed-tab check opens a popup from the Start click; the reload check carries the run over the reload in sessionStorage. Test routes need no login; revisit whether `/test/*` should be disabled or admin-only before the first release. **Frame limit fix:** `setMaxPayloadLength` is now `MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD` = 65536 + 1024, because Chrome and Firefox deflate every message and 64 KiB of random bytes becomes a 65557-byte frame; the message limit (65536, decompressed) is unchanged. `WebSocketsTest.compressedFramesOfFullSizeMessagesAreAccepted` sends such a frame over a raw socket (SimpleWebsocket's own client sends incompressible data uncompressed, so it couldn't catch this). On a 1009 close the server logs `remote=1006, clean=false`: SimpleWebsocket fails the connection without waiting for the peer's close frame, which RFC 6455 allows, so this isn't reported upstream. Also: both test scripts now end with `export {}` so tsc treats them as modules (two global scripts clashed on shared names).
- 2026-11-08: Actor resolution done (main `2caf7cb`; `io.siggi.togethertunes.auth`: `Actor` sealed interface with `User(userId, sessionId, via COOKIE|BEARER)`, `Guest(guestId, identifiedBy, banned)` and `ANONYMOUS`, `ActorResolver`, `Tokens`, `GuestKeys`). `Hooks.authenticate()` is gone: the router-wide hook is `ActorResolver.hook()` (it needs the database), and handlers call `ActorResolver.actor(request)`. **Lazy:** the hook only captures the credentials; the actor is resolved on the first `actor()` call and memoised for the request, so static files never touch the database (also in IP mode). **Order:** an `Authorization: Bearer` header decides alone (cookies ignored, so such a request is never cookie-authenticated); an unknown, malformed, expired, cookie-kind or disabled-user token is `401 invalid_token`, not anonymous. Other schemes are ignored. Then `sid` (live `cookie` session of an enabled user; a stale `sid` is ignored and resolution goes on), then, only if `guests.enabled`, the `gid` cookie (`cookie` guest), then in IP mode the `ip` guest keyed by the client IP. A `gid` cookie is honoured in both modes ("changing the mode doesn't migrate existing guests"); in cookie mode IP guests aren't resolved. Banned guests resolve with `banned=true` (enforcement is left to the actions). `Actor.cookieAuthenticated()` is there for the CSRF step. **Formats** (later steps must use them): tokens are 32 random bytes, base64url without padding (43 chars; anything else is rejected without a query); `*_hash` columns hold lowercase-hex SHA-256 of the token text. IP key: IPv4 dotted (IPv4-mapped IPv6 counts as IPv4), IPv6 the first four groups in lowercase hex without leading zeros + `::/64` (e.g. `2001:db8:1:2::/64`); `key_hash` = SHA-256 hex of that key. Non-literals never reach `InetAddress.getByName` (no DNS). Resolution is read-only: no sliding `expires_at`/`last_seen_at` updates, no `ip_last`, no guest creation; those belong to the Sessions, guest-mode and lazy-guest steps. `Main.router` now takes the `ActorResolver`. Tests: `ActorResolverContract` (SQLite + `PostgresActorResolverIT`, 14 each), `ActorHookTest` (real server: cookies, bearer, 401 only where the actor is used, IP mode via `X-Forwarded-For` from loopback), `TokensTest`, `GuestKeysTest`. `mvn verify`: 296 unit + 73 integration tests pass.
- 2026-11-08: Setup flow done (main `ced70d1`). `GET /api/setup` → `{"needed": bool}` (no-store); `POST /api/setup` `{username, password, displayName?}` → `201 {"user": {id, username, displayName, roles}}` while no account exists, else `409 setup_complete`; rate-limit hook bucket `setup` (placeholder). Audited as `account.setup` (actor = the new user, `ip` = client IP, details `{username, roles}`). **Pulled forward from the next steps, because setup needs them:** `auth.Passwords` (Argon2id via Bouncy Castle `bcprov-jdk18on` 1.86, lightweight API only; m=64 MiB, t=3, p=1, 16-byte salt, 32-byte hash, PHC string `$argon2id$v=19$m=…,t=…,p=…$salt$hash` in unpadded base64; `verify` uses the parameters in the hash, constant-time compare, rejects malformed or absurd parameters; `needsRehash` for the login step; length 10–1024 code points, `Passwords.problem`), checked against hashes from the reference `argon2` CLI. `auth.BuiltinRoles.ensure` creates `admin` (builtin) and `user` (builtin, `is_default`) on startup with insert-or-ignore by name (UUIDv7 ids, so roles are looked up by name); permissions for them come with the Roles step (admin = all, implicitly). `auth.Accounts.createLocal` is the shared account creation for the Local accounts step too: **the account that makes `users` non-empty gets `admin`, whichever way it's created**, every account gets the `is_default` roles; all creations are serialised by `UPDATE roles … WHERE name = 'admin'` first (row lock on PostgreSQL, READ COMMITTED, so the following "any users?" check sees the winner; SQLite has one writer), tested with 6 racers and a widened race window (fails without the lock). Rules: usernames 3–32 of `A–Z a–z 0–9 . _ -`, starting with a letter or digit, stripped, unique case-insensitively (`lower(username)`), stored as typed; display names stripped, whitespace collapsed, 1–64 code points, no control/format/private-use/unassigned characters except ZWJ, default the username. Errors: `400 invalid_username` / `invalid_display_name` / `invalid_password`, `409 username_taken` / `setup_complete`. Passwords are hashed outside the transaction. `auth.AuditLog.write` inserts `audit_log` rows (write-only; reading stays with `IpAccess`). **Web:** `/setup.html` (`web/src/setup.ts`, form with repeat-password check) and `web/src/api.ts` (fetch helper, `ApiError`); `main.ts` sends `/` to `/setup.html` while setup is needed. **Not done here (later steps):** setup doesn't log the new admin in (Sessions step: set `sid` from `POST /api/setup`); registration must refuse while setup is needed or just go through `Accounts.createLocal` (Local accounts step); anyone who reaches a fresh server first becomes admin (as in Jellyfin), no setup token. Tests: `PasswordsTest` (7), `AccountsContract` (SQLite + `PostgresAccountsIT`, 11 each), `MainRouterTest` (setup flow over HTTP, setup page), `WebBuildTest`. `mvn verify`: 317 unit + 84 integration tests pass. Smoke-tested with the shaded jar.
- 2026-11-08: Local accounts done (main `c24a4e4`; `auth.AuthApi`, `auth.Sessions`, `Accounts.login`). `POST /api/auth/register` `{username, password, displayName?}` → `201 {"user": …}` + `sid` cookie (registering logs you in); `open` only: **`invite` → `403 registration_invite_only`**, because nothing in the design defines invites (no table, no endpoint), so invite mode currently behaves like `closed` for self-registration; `closed` → `403 registration_closed` (the default); no account yet → `409 setup_required` (the first account always comes from `/api/setup`). Validation and the admin-for-first-account lock as in `Accounts.createLocal`; audited `account.register`. `POST /api/auth/login` `{username, password}` → `200 {"user": …}` + `sid`; username matched case-insensitively after strip; unknown user, account without local password and wrong password all → `401 invalid_credentials` (same message) and all cost one Argon2 derivation (dummy hash at current params, computed once); right password on a disabled account → `403 account_disabled`. Overlong input (username > 32, password > 2048 chars) is refused without hashing it. On success the hash is replaced when `Passwords.needsRehash` (parameters differ), with `UPDATE … WHERE hash = <old>` so a concurrent password change wins. Audit: `auth.login` (details `{method:"password", rehashed?}`), `auth.login_failed` (no actor, target = user id if known, details `{username (≤32 + "…"), reason: unknown_user|no_password|wrong_password|disabled}`): the Rate limiting step can build on these. A login from a browser that already has a `sid` deletes that old session. `POST /api/auth/logout` → `204`, deletes the session the request is authenticated with (cookie or bearer; bearer logout doesn't touch a cookie), also a stale `sid`'s row, and clears the cookie (`Max-Age=0`) when one was sent; audited `auth.logout`; idempotent. Rate-limit hook buckets `login` and `register` (placeholders). **Pulled forward from the Sessions step (minimum needed for login):** `Sessions.create` (cookie or bearer kind, 30-day `expires_at`, user agent truncated to 512 chars, `ip` stored), `delete`, `deleteByToken`; `Created.toString()` redacts the token. Cookie: `sid=…; Path=/; Max-Age=2592000; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax` plus **`Secure` only when `publicBaseUrl` is https** (`Config.secureCookies()`), so local http testing works in every browser; the Sessions step still owns sliding expiry, bearer tokens and the session list. No web UI yet (the "Minimal web UI" step). Tests: `AccountsContract` +7 (login, same answer for wrong/unknown, no local password, disabled, rehash on parameter change, documented defaults, sessions create/resolve/delete; SQLite + `PostgresAccountsIT`, 18 each), `MainRouterTest` +2 (registration modes over HTTP, login/logout with cookie flags). `mvn verify`: 326 unit + 91 integration tests pass. Smoke-tested with the shaded jar.
- 2026-11-08: Sessions done (main `25912fa`; `auth.Sessions`, `auth.SessionsApi`, `AuthApi` token endpoint). **Sliding expiry:** `ActorResolver` (no longer read-only for users) calls `Sessions.touch` when a session is used and `last_seen_at` is at least `TOUCH_INTERVAL_MS` = 60 s old: `last_seen_at = now`, `expires_at = now + 30 days`, as a conditional `UPDATE … WHERE last_seen_at = <read value>` so concurrent requests write once. Applies to cookie and bearer sessions. When a `cookie` session slid, the lazy actor lookup also re-sends `sid` (same token, `Max-Age=2592000`) if the response isn't committed, so the browser cookie slides with the row; static files never resolve the actor, so they neither slide nor set cookies. `ActorResolver` now takes `secureCookies` (`Main` passes `config.secureCookies()`). New `Response.setCookie`: a later cookie of the same name replaces an earlier one (also via `header`/`addHeader("Set-Cookie", …)`), so e.g. a logout that slid first sends only the clearing cookie. Cookie format lives in `Sessions.cookie(...)`. **Bearer tokens:** `POST /api/auth/token` → `201 {token, tokenType:"Bearer", sessionId, expiresAt, user}`, `no-store`, no cookie. With `{username, password}` it goes through `Accounts.login` (same errors, same `auth.login`/`auth.login_failed` audit, rate-limit bucket `login`); with no body or `{}` it's issued to the already-authenticated user (cookie or bearer; for accounts without a password, e.g. OIDC later), else `401 not_authenticated`. Audited `auth.token` (`{via: password|session}`). Logging out with a bearer token revokes it. **`/api/me/sessions`** (users only, else `401 not_authenticated`): `GET ?cursor=&limit=` (1–200, default 50, else `400 invalid_limit`) → `{items:[{id, kind, createdAt, lastSeenAt, expiresAt, userAgent?, current}], nextCursor?}`, live sessions newest first (UUIDv7 id order, cursor = last id); **no IP** (`SessionView` has no ip/token/hash component, tested). Nulls are left out as everywhere (no `nextCursor` on the last page). `DELETE /api/me/sessions/{id}` → `204` (own sessions only, else `404 not_found`; revoking the current cookie session clears `sid`), audited `auth.session_revoked`. **Added:** `DELETE /api/me/sessions` → `200 {revoked: n}` revokes every other session ("log out everywhere else"), audited `auth.sessions_revoked`. **Cleanup:** creating a session deletes that user's expired rows; `Sessions.deleteExpired()` runs once at startup (periodic cleanup can go with retention). **Setup now logs the new admin in** (`POST /api/setup` sets `sid`), as left open by the setup step. `Accounts.findEnabled(userId)` added. Tests: `SessionsContract` (SQLite + `PostgresSessionsIT`, 8 each: hashing, sliding for cookie and bearer incl. expiry after 30 idle days, conditional touch, paging, no-IP view, revoke ownership, revoke others, cleanup), `MainRouterTest` +4 (setup logs in, bearer tokens, list/revoke over HTTP, cookie re-sent after a minute and not before; logout sends only the clearing cookie), shared `TestClock`. `mvn verify`: 338 unit + 99 integration tests pass. Smoke-tested with the shaded jar (setup cookie, token via password, bearer session list, revoke others).

- 2026-11-08: CSRF done (main `d4a3660`; `http.Csrf`). `Hooks.csrf()` placeholder replaced by `Csrf.hook()` (router-wide, after `ActorResolver.hook()`); `Main` builds one `Csrf(publicBaseUrl, [sid, gid])` and passes it to `WebSockets` (constructor now `WebSockets(Csrf)`; `originOf` moved to `Csrf.originOf`). **Rule** (stricter than "cookie-authenticated", documented in `docs/auth.md`): for every method but GET/HEAD/OPTIONS without an `Authorization: Bearer` header, a present `Origin` must equal the `publicBaseUrl` origin (case-insensitive, exact; `null`, other scheme/port/host, paths and lists never match), and a request carrying a `sid` or `gid` cookie must send `Origin`. So cross-site writes are refused even without cookies (login CSRF, and IP-mode guests, whose client IP is an ambient credential the old `Actor` comment overlooked), and the check needs no actor lookup or database (cookie presence, not the resolved actor; a stale `sid` without `Origin` is refused too, which only affects non-browser clients). Requests with neither `Origin` nor credential cookies (curl, scripts) pass. Bearer requests are exempt: the resolver ignores cookies then, and a cross-site page can't set `Authorization` without a CORS preflight, which we never grant. WebSocket upgrades: `Csrf.checkUpgrade` (same, no bearer exemption) runs before the acceptor's own `setAllowedOrigins` check, so an upgrade with `sid`/`gid` but no `Origin` is now `403`. Refusals: `403 origin_not_allowed`, logged at INFO (method, path, reason; no cookie values). **Consequence for local testing:** open the UI at exactly `publicBaseUrl` (CLAUDE.md updated). Tests: `CsrfTest` (9, real server: all state-changing methods with `sid`/`gid`/mixed cookies, missing/mismatching/lookalike origins, no-cookie cross-site refused, no-Origin no-cookie passes, safe methods unchecked, bearer exempt, Basic not), `WebSocketsTest` +1 (no Origin with `sid`/`gid` → 403, other cookies fine; logger now the package logger), `MainRouterTest` +2 (logout CSRF keeps the session, cross-site login refused, bearer with stray cookie works, WebSocket with cookie and no Origin refused); test helpers now send the `publicBaseUrl` Origin like a browser. `mvn verify`: 350 unit + 99 integration tests pass. Smoke-tested with the shaded jar (cross-site setup 403, same-origin setup 201, cookie logout without Origin 403, with Origin 204).
- 2026-11-08: Browser baseline step done (main `eb5c415`). `web/tsconfig.json`: `target` stays **ES2022** (class fields, `#` members and top-level await stay native; all fine in Safari 15.0), `lib` lowered from ES2023 to **ES2021** + DOM + DOM.Iterable, so tsc rejects `.at()`, `Object.hasOwn` and `findLast` (checked with a scratch file: TS2550 for each). No existing TypeScript needed changes. tsc can't catch class static blocks (valid ES2022 syntax) or `structuredClone` (DOM lib), so `WebBuildTest.compiledJavaScriptStaysWithinBrowserBaseline` scans every compiled `web/js/**/*.js` for static blocks, `.at(`, `Object.hasOwn`, `structuredClone` and `findLast`/`findLastIndex` (with a self-test of the patterns). Documented in `docs/architecture.md#browser-baseline`. `mvn verify`: 351 unit + 99 integration tests pass.
- 2026-11-08: The iPod touch 7 is on iOS 15.1 as well, and no 15.0 device is available. The baseline stays Safari 15.0 (the build rules are the same as for 15.1), but 15.1 is the oldest version actually tested.
- 2026-11-08: Rate limiting done (main `0d3ffa2`). **Login backoff** (`auth.LoginThrottle`, in memory, used by `Accounts.login`, so `/api/auth/login` and the password form of `/api/auth/token` share it): failures counted per IP key (IPv4 address, IPv6 /64, via the new `http.IpKeys`, which `GuestKeys` now delegates to) and per username (case-insensitive). Per username 5 free failures, per IP 20 (shared NATs); after that each failure locks the key for 1 s × 2^(n−1), capped at 15 min, from the latest failure; a key with no failure for 1 h is forgotten. Every outcome but success counts (wrong password, unknown user, no password, disabled). An attempt counts from the moment it starts (before Argon2), and success takes it back, so parallel attempts can't slip past; success clears the username's count but only takes its own attempt back from the IP (the IP's earlier failures still count; restoring the IP's previous failure time so a success never starts a lock). A locked key refuses even the right password with `429 rate_limited` + `Retry-After` (seconds, rounded up) without checking it; refused attempts aren't counted. Audit: failures stay `auth.login_failed`; the **first** refusal of each lock writes `auth.login_throttled` (no actor; `details` `username`, `scope` `ip`/`username`, `retryAfterMs`) so hammering a locked key can't flood `audit_log`. Known trade-off: anyone can delay a known username's logins (at most 15 min at a time). Memory bounded (100k keys per kind, LRU). Constants, not settings (no config key added). **Generic limiter** `http.RateLimiter`: keyed token bucket (burst, refill interval; bounded LRU of 65 536 keys) with `acquireForIp`/`checkIp`; `Hooks.rateLimit(RateLimiter)` (replacing the placeholder `rateLimit(String)`) applies it per route before the body is read, ready for `/live/`. `auth.AuthRateLimits` holds the account routes' limiters: setup 5 then 1/12 s, register 5 then 1/min, login+token (one shared bucket) 10 then 1/6 s. `HttpError` gained an optional `retryAfterSeconds` that `Response.error` sends as `Retry-After`. Tests: `RateLimiterTest` (6), `LoginThrottleTest` (10), `RouterTest` (hook: 429 + Retry-After, shared bucket, handler not run), `AccountsContract` +1 (SQLite and PostgreSQL: username and IP backoff, single audit row per lock), `MainRouterTest` +1 (HTTP 429 with `Retry-After: 1`, token shares the backoff). `mvn verify`: 369 unit + 100 integration tests pass.
- 2026-11-08: Roles and permissions done (main `e2308c4`; `auth.Permission`, `auth.Feature`, `auth.Permissions`, `auth.Roles`). **Permissions:** enum with the 13 wire names from `docs/auth.md` (stored in `role_permissions.permission`; unknown stored names, e.g. after a downgrade, grant nothing). `admin` (`builtin = 1`, name `admin`) has **no** `role_permissions` rows and holds every permission by definition, so permissions added later are the admin's at once. **`user` starts with `library.upload`, `library.youtube` and `rooms.create`** (the docs give no defaults; seeded only when `BuiltinRoles.ensure` creates the row, so admin edits stick). **Union:** `Permissions.held(userId)` = union over the user's roles; disabled or unknown users, guests and anonymous hold nothing; no cache, so role changes apply on the next request. **Feature switches** (`auth.Feature`: `ytdlp.enabled`, `outputs.{rtmp,ts,hls}.enabled`, `overlay.enabled`; guest policy switches stay with the guest code): `library.youtube` belongs to `ytdlp.enabled`, `output.overlayVideo` to `overlay.enabled`. `Permissions.require(actor, p)` checks feature first (`403 feature_disabled`, admins and anonymous included), then account (`401 not_authenticated`), then permission (`403 forbidden`); `requireFeature`, `allowed`, `effective` (held minus switched-off features), and per-route `hook(Permission)` / `hook(Feature)`. **Roles service** (for the admin API, which does the auditing): list (admin, user, then by name), create, rename, set permissions, set default, delete, user role assignment (`setUserRoles` replaces the set). `admin`: no rename/delete/permission/default change → `409 role_immutable` (can still be granted/revoked); `user`: no rename/delete → `409 role_builtin`, permissions and default flag editable. Role names: trimmed, whitespace collapsed, 1–32 of letters/digits/space/`._-` starting with a letter or digit (`400 invalid_role_name`), unique case-insensitively in Java (`409 role_name_taken`). **Last admin:** taking `admin` from the last enabled admin → `409 last_admin`. Name changes and assignments lock the admin role row (`BuiltinRoles.lockAdminRow`, now shared with `Accounts`). Other errors: `404 role_not_found`, `404 user_not_found`, `400 unknown_role`, `400 unknown_permission` (`Roles.parsePermissions`). Not wired into `Main` yet (nothing uses it until the admin API step). `mvn verify`: 391 unit + 118 integration tests pass.
- 2026-11-08: Admin API + pages done (main `9d2fd14`; `auth.AdminApi`, `auth.Users`, `web/src/admin.ts`, `/admin/`). **Routes** (all `no-store`, all audited with the acting user; full shapes in the `AdminApi` Javadoc and `docs/api.md#admin`): users (`admin.users`) `GET/POST /api/admin/users` (list by username ignoring case, `q` = substring of username or display name, cursor = last lowercased username, `limit` 1–200), `GET/PATCH /api/admin/users/{id}` (`displayName`, `disabled`), `POST …/{id}/password`, `GET/PUT …/{id}/roles` (`{roleIds}`); roles (`admin.roles`; `GET /api/admin/roles` also with `admin.users`, for the role picker) `GET/POST /api/admin/roles` (list not paginated, plus every permission with description and feature state), `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/admin/roles/{id}` (`name`, `isDefault`), `GET/PUT …/{id}/permissions`; settings (`admin.settings`) `GET/PATCH /api/admin/settings` (`{items:[{key, group, type, value?, default?, options?}]}`; PATCH is all-or-nothing in one transaction, `null` resets to the default; `400 unknown_setting` / `invalid_setting`). **Temporary passwords:** new migration `0002_password_must_change` (both dialects) adds `password_credentials.must_change`. An admin can create a user (`POST /api/admin/users`, the only way to add accounts while registration is `closed`, the default) or reset a password; both set `must_change`, and a reset logs the user out everywhere (except the admin's own session when resetting their own). `user` JSON from setup/login/register/token now has `mustChangePassword`. **Added `POST /api/me/password`** `{currentPassword, newPassword}` → `204` (`Accounts.changePassword`): checks the current password through the login backoff (same keys), `403 wrong_password`, `400 password_unchanged`, `409 no_password`; clears `must_change` and deletes the user's other sessions; audited `auth.password_changed` / `auth.password_change_failed`. The flag is advisory on the server (no route is blocked while it is set); the login UI (Minimal web UI step) must force the change. **Disabling:** deletes the user's sessions; `409 cannot_disable_self`, `409 last_admin` (last enabled `admin` holder). **No escalation (beyond `docs/`, now in `docs/auth.md`):** granting/taking a role, creating/deleting a role, changing its permissions or default flag need the actor to hold every permission involved; only `admin` holders grant/take `admin`; and a user can't manage (disable, reset, change roles or name of) another user holding the `admin` role or any permission the actor lacks. Without this, `admin.users` could reset the admin's password and `admin.roles` could give its own role everything. Renaming roles is unrestricted. **Pages:** `/admin/` (linked from `/`), tabs Users (search, paging, detail panel with display name, role checkboxes, temporary password, disable/enable; create form), Roles (one card per role with name, default flag, permission checkboxes with feature-off notes, delete) and Settings (grouped typed fields, "Default" per setting, PATCH of changed values only). No login page yet: use it after `/setup.html` (which logs the admin in). Tests: `UsersContract` (SQLite + `PostgresUsersIT`, 7 each: listing/paging/search incl. literal `_`/`%`, display name, disabling, self/last-admin, temporary passwords, own password change, its throttling), `AdminApiTest` (6: 401/403 on all 16 routes for anonymous and a plain user, users flow incl. temporary password → login → change, roles incl. built-in rules, the escalation rules, settings incl. atomicity/reset/audit, page loads), `InitialSchemaContract` knows `must_change`, `MainRouterTest` user JSON. `mvn verify`: 405 unit + 125 integration tests pass. Smoke-tested with the shaded jar (setup, create user, settings GET/PATCH, `/admin/` and `/js/admin.js` 200, temporary-password login reports `mustChangePassword: true`).
- 2026-11-08: Lazy guest creation + `/api/me` done (main `8e3a51d`; `auth.Guests`, `auth.MeApi`). **Lazy creation:** `Guests.requireIdentity(request, response)` is what every identity-requiring action calls first: a user or existing guest is returned as is; an anonymous request becomes a new guest, `ActorResolver.actor` is updated for the rest of the request (new package-private `ActorResolver.setActor`), and cookie mode sets `gid` (`Path=/; Max-Age=31536000; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax`, `Secure` with an https `publicBaseUrl`). Cookie mode stores SHA-256 of a fresh 256-bit token; IP mode keys by `GuestKeys.ipKeyHash` (IPv4 / IPv6 /64) and returns the existing guest of that key, and a lost insert race (unique `key_hash`) falls back to the winner's row, so concurrent requests from one address get one guest. Both modes write `ip_last` at creation (read only by the future `IpAccess`); `guest.created` and `guest.renamed` go to `audit_log` with `actor_guest_id`. Guests disabled → `403 guests_disabled`. **Routes:** `GET /api/me` → `{kind:"user", id, username, displayName, roles, mustChangePassword, permissions}` (effective permissions, for the UI's admin link), `{kind:"guest", id, name, nameChosen, customNames}` or `{kind:"anonymous", guestsEnabled, customNames}` (`customNames` = `guests.allowCustomNames`, so the UI knows whether to offer a name field). `POST /api/me/guest {name?}` (body optional) → `201` when created, `200` when the request already is a guest or its IP already has one (a given `name` then renames); users get `409 already_logged_in`. `PATCH /api/me/guest {name}` renames (`403 custom_names_disabled`, `403 guest_banned`, `400 invalid_name`; anonymous `401 identity_required`, users `409 not_a_guest`). `PATCH /api/me` is the same for a guest, and `{displayName?}` for a user (absent: unchanged, blank: the username). **Left to the following guest steps:** generated names are a placeholder `Guest NNNN` (`Guests` takes a `Supplier<String>`; the word lists and uniqueness come next); custom names are only trimmed/collapsed (Unicode whitespace), 2–32 code points, no control/format characters (char whitelist, NFKC clash check and blocklist come in the custom-names step); the `gid` cookie isn't re-sent on use yet and `last_seen_at`/`ip_last` aren't updated after creation (cookie/IP mode step); no `identityTicket` in the `POST` response yet. Tests: `MeApiTest` (HTTP, incl. a test route using `requireIdentity`), `GuestsContract` (SQLite + PostgreSQL, incl. 16 concurrent IP creations → one row).
- 2026-11-08: Guest cookie and IP mode done (main `7effb18`; `ActorResolver`, `Guests.touch`). Most of the mode logic (token/hash, IP key IPv4 full / IPv6 /64, `key_hash` = SHA-256, `ip_last` at creation, one row per IP under races) already came with actor resolution and lazy creation; this step adds **use tracking**. When `ActorResolver` resolves a guest (cookie or IP, banned ones too) it calls `Guests.touch`: `last_seen_at = now` at most once per `Guests.TOUCH_INTERVAL_MS` = 60 s, and `ip_last` = the request's client IP **as soon as it differs** (so it really is the last IP; in IP mode the full address, e.g. a rotated IPv6 interface ID, while the key stays the /64; a request without a client IP keeps the old value). The `UPDATE` is conditional on the `last_seen_at`/`ip_last` that were read, so concurrent requests write once. **Sliding `gid`:** whenever a cookie guest's row was touched, the lazy actor lookup re-sends `gid` (same token, `Max-Age=31536000`, same flags) if the response isn't committed, like `sid`; so the cookie is renewed at most about once a minute (or on an IP change). Static files never resolve the actor, so they neither touch nor set cookies; a user with a stray `gid`, or any request while guests are disabled, doesn't touch the guest. Guests have no server-side expiry (the row lives on; "active guest" for name uniqueness can use `last_seen_at`). Tests: `ActorResolverContract` +3 (SQLite + PostgreSQL), `MeApiTest` +2 (cookie re-sent after a minute and not before, not on static files, `ip_last` follows `X-Forwarded-For`; IP mode keeps one guest across IPv6 interface IDs in a /64, other /64 or IPv4 is anonymous). `mvn verify`: 425 unit + 132 integration tests pass.
- 2026-11-08: Generated guest names done (main `880b8b2`; `auth.GuestNames`, `Guests.generatedName`). **Lists:** `src/main/resources/io/siggi/togethertunes/auth/guest-name-adjectives.txt` (306) and `guest-name-nouns.txt` (304, animals and nature/music things), one lower-case ASCII word per line (`#` comments and blank lines allowed), title-cased on load; a malformed or repeated word fails at startup. No word is in both lists (no "Swift Swift"); words were picked to be friendly (no body/insult-ish words). `GuestNames` draws with a `SecureRandom`; it replaces the `Guest NNNN` placeholder as the default `Supplier` of `Guests`. **Active guest** = `last_seen_at` within the last 30 days (`Guests.ACTIVE_MS`; `last_seen_at` is kept current by `touch`); banned guests and guests with chosen names count too. **Uniqueness:** up to 5 draws (`NAME_DRAWS`), each checked case-insensitively (`Dialect.equalsIgnoreCase`) against active guests' `display_name`; then the 5th draw gets the **smallest free number from 2** among active guests (`Quiet Walrus 2`; only plain numeric suffixes count, e.g. `02` or `Fan` don't). The choice runs inside the insert's write transaction: SQLite writes are serialised, and PostgreSQL takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` (key "TTguests"), so concurrent creations get distinct names (tested with 16 concurrent creations in both dialects). Only names generated at creation are checked; uniqueness isn't enforced by a DB constraint (an inactive guest may share a name with a new one) and there's no index on `display_name`/`last_seen_at` (no migration; add one if guest tables grow large). Custom names are not checked against guests here (the custom-names step checks them against accounts). Tests: `GuestNamesTest` (3), `GuestsContract` +5 (SQLite + PostgreSQL). `mvn verify`: 432 unit + 137 integration tests pass.
- 2026-11-08: Custom guest names done (main `6699a37`; `auth.GuestNameRules`, setting `guests.nameBlocklist`). **Form** (`GuestNameRules.normalise`, `400 invalid_name`): the name is NFKC-normalised first and **stored in that form** (full-width or styled letters become plain ones), whitespace trimmed and collapsed to single spaces, then 2–32 code points; allowed are letters of any script, combining marks (only on a letter/digit, at most 3 per base character, against "Zalgo"), decimal digits, space and the punctuation `.,'’-_!?&():;#+~` (`’` because phones type it); at least one letter or digit. Not allowed: emoji, symbols, `@ / \ " < > * $ % = | [] {}`, control/format/private-use characters. **Account clash** (`409 name_taken`): the name's key (NFKC, `’`/`‘` → `'`, collapsed whitespace, `toUpperCase(ROOT).toLowerCase(ROOT)` so `ß` = `ss`, NFKC again) equals the key of any `users.username` or `users.display_name`, disabled accounts included. Checked in Java by scanning `users` (SQL can't do NFKC; few accounts on a self-hosted server), at create and rename time only, so a guest whose name an account takes later keeps it. Custom names aren't checked against other guests (docs don't ask for it). **Blocklist** (`400 name_blocked`): new server setting `guests.nameBlocklist`, default `[]`, a new setting type `stringList` (`settings.StringList`; JSON array of strings, entries trimmed/collapsed, blank and duplicate entries dropped, at most 2000 entries of 64 code points); the admin settings page edits it as a textarea, one entry per line. A name is blocked if its key with only letters/digits/marks kept contains such a compacted entry (so `R.U.D.E` matches `rude`; substring, so entries must be specific; an entry without letters or digits matches nothing). Generated names aren't checked against either rule. `docs/auth.md#names` and its settings table updated. Tests: `GuestNameRulesTest` (5), `MeApiTest` +2 (HTTP: clash with username/display name incl. full-width and spacing, blocklist on create and rename), `SettingsContract` and `AdminApiTest` (blocklist setting, `stringList` type). `mvn verify`: 440 unit + 137 integration tests pass.
- 2026-11-08: Guest badge done (main `f7c2838`; `auth.ActorRef`, `auth.ActorRefs`, `web/src/actor.ts`). **`ActorRef`** is the one actor form for every response and message: record `(kind, id, name, isGuest)`, serialised by `Json.GSON` as exactly `{kind, id, name, isGuest}`; `kind` is `user`/`guest` only (anonymous is never an actor ref) and the constructor rejects an `isGuest` that doesn't match `kind`. `name` is a user's `display_name` (never the username, which is a login name) or a guest's name. No IP and no other guest detail (identification mode, ban flag) is in it. Factories: `user`, `guest`, `of(Account|UserView|GuestView)`, and `fromColumns(userId, userName, guestId, guestName)` for rows with a user/guest actor pair joined to `users`/`guests` (later queue entries, requests, votes). **`ActorRefs(db)`** looks one up by id (`user`, `guest`, `of(Actor)`; static `Connection` variants for use inside a transaction): disabled users and banned guests still resolve (history shows them), unknown ids and anonymous give `null`; reads only `id`/`display_name`. **`/api/me`**: the user and guest forms gain `isGuest` (and the user form `name` = `displayName`), so they are supersets of the actor ref; anonymous has no `isGuest`. **UI:** `web/src/actor.ts` exports the `ActorRef` type and `actorLabel(actor)` (name as text plus a `Guest` badge, classes `.actor`, `.badge.badge-guest`, styled in `main.css`); nothing shows other actors yet, so it is first used by the room UI. Tests: `ActorRefTest` (exact JSON keys, factories, badge/kind check), `ActorRefsContract` (SQLite + `PostgresActorRefsIT`), `MeApiTest` (isGuest on user/guest/anonymous), `WebBuildTest` (actor.js and badge CSS packaged). `mvn verify`: 446 unit + 139 integration tests pass.
- 2026-11-08: WebSocket identity tickets done (main `cda8eba`; `auth.IdentityTickets`). Every successful `POST /api/me/guest` (`201` created, and `200` for an existing cookie or IP guest, incl. the rename case) adds `identityTicket` to the guest JSON: a fresh 256-bit token (`Tokens`, 43 chars), a new one per call; earlier tickets stay valid until used or expired. `GET /api/me` and refused requests carry none. **Store:** in memory only (a restart drops open sockets anyway), keyed by SHA-256 of the ticket, value = guest id + expiry (no IP); `TTL_MS` = 60 s; bounded to 65 536 outstanding tickets (oldest dropped); expired ones swept on each issue/redeem. **`redeem(ticket)`** (for the M3 socket handler) removes the ticket on the first attempt and returns `{guestId, expiresAt}` or `null` (malformed, unknown, expired, used); the caller must re-check the guest (e.g. banned since). `Main` creates one `IdentityTickets` and passes it to `MeApi` (new constructor parameter); the M3 WebSocket handler must share that instance. Tickets are capabilities: never logged. Tests: `IdentityTicketsTest` (7: single use, distinct, 60 s boundary, sweep, malformed/unknown, bound, concurrent redeem), `MeApiTest` +1 (HTTP: 201/200 tickets, single use, expiry, IP mode, none for users). `mvn verify`: 453 unit + 139 integration tests pass.
- 2026-11-08: `IpAccess` + admin guests/audit/session-IP APIs done (main `73b3ad5`; `auth.IpAccess`, `auth.AuditEntries`, `auth.AdminActivityApi`, `Guests.list`). **`IpAccess(db, permissions)`** is the only class that selects `sessions.ip`, `guests.ip_last` or `audit_log.ip`, and checks `ip.view` against the acting `Actor` on every call: `ipsOfGuests(actor, ids)` / `ipsOfAuditEntries(actor, ids)` return `id → ip` (rows without an IP left out; **an empty map without `ip.view`**, so callers just omit `ip`; IN-lists chunked by 500), `sessions(actor, userId)` throws `401`/`403` without it. General listings carry no IP: `Guests.list(q, cursor, limit)` → `GuestListItem(GuestView, lastSeenAt)`, `AuditEntries.list(action, actor, cursor, limit)` → `AuditEntryView(id, at, ActorRef actor, action, target, detailsJson)` (actor joined to `users`/`guests`; a deleted actor shows as none); the API adds `ip` from `IpAccess`. **Guest touch no longer reads `ip_last`:** `ActorResolver` selects `IpAccess.GUEST_IP_UNCHANGED` (`CASE WHEN ip_last = ? THEN 1 ELSE 0 END`, bound to the request IP) and `Guests.touch` takes that boolean; its `UPDATE` sets `ip_last = COALESCE(?, ip_last)` conditional on `last_seen_at` only (a same-millisecond race can write twice, harmless). **Routes** (`no-store`): `GET /api/admin/guests?q=&cursor=&limit=` and `GET /api/admin/audit?action=&actor=&cursor=&limit=` need **`admin.users`** (docs named no permission; the audit log is mostly account activity), `GET /api/admin/ip/sessions/{userId}` needs only **`ip.view`** (`404 user_not_found`, live sessions newest first, not paginated). Guests are ordered by `last_seen_at` desc (cursor `lastSeenAt_id`; a guest seen while paging can move up a page), audit by `at` desc then id (cursor `at_id`); `limit` 1–200, default 50; `400 invalid_limit` / `invalid_cursor`. `docs/api.md#admin` and `docs/auth.md#ip-address-policy` updated. **No admin UI for these yet** (the Minimal web UI step covers users/roles/settings; guest/audit tabs can come with it or later). Tests: `IpAccessContract` (SQLite + `PostgresIpAccessIT`, 6: ip.view only, admin implicit, guests/anonymous/other permissions get nothing, chunking, session IPs, guest paging/search, audit paging across equal timestamps/filters/actors, touch without reading), `IpAccessGuardTest` (scans `src/main/java`: any `+`-joined string-literal run containing `SELECT` that names `ip`/`ip_last` fails outside `IpAccess.java`, with a self-test), `AdminApiTest` +1 (HTTP: helpdesk role without IPs, ip.view-only role, admin with IPs, 401 for guests/anonymous, paging and errors; the three routes added to the permission sweep). `mvn verify`: 463 unit + 145 integration tests pass.
- 2026-11-08: DTO IP-leak test done (main `a9feea5`; `DtoIpLeakTest`, plus `Router.routes()`, which lists routes as `"METHOD pattern"`). Four parts. **Classification:** the test walks the main classes and requires every record to be listed either as a DTO (it reaches clients directly or field by field: `ActorRef`, `Accounts.Account`, `Users.UserView/RoleRef/Page`, `Sessions.SessionView/Page/Created`, `Guests.GuestView/GuestListItem/Page/Created`, `AuditEntries.AuditEntryView/Page`, `Roles.Role`, `EchoEndpoint.Closed`, `StringList`, `UploadQuota`) or as internal, with a reason. Request-body records, `Actor.*`, `ActorResolver.Credentials` (which holds the client IP) and similar records are internal. A new record fails the test until someone classifies it, and so does a stale entry. `IpAccess.SessionIp` is the one ip.view-only DTO. **Field names:** no general DTO may have an IP-like component (`ip`, `ips`, `…Ip(s)`, `ip…`, `…addr`, `…address(es)`, `…forwarded…`, `…host`). **Instances:** the real services build every DTO type from a database seeded with IPv4 and IPv6 data (sessions, IP-mode and cookie guests, audit rows, all written with IPs). Each instance is serialised with `Json.GSON`, its JSON strings and keys are scanned, and the test fails if any DTO type never got built. As the control, `IpAccess.sessions` returns the seeded IPs. **Endpoints:** every route of `Main.router` gets a 2xx at least once while seeding, which runs through `X-Forwarded-For` (the loopback peer is trusted) from documentation-range IPs: setup, register, a failed login, logins, token, password change, session revoke, logout, the admin user and role APIs, settings, a cookie guest's renames, the echo socket and its report. Then every GET route is called (the test checks that the sweep covers all of them) as anonymous, cookie guest, IP-mode guest (IPv6), cookie user, bearer user, and an admin.* user without `ip.view`. All headers and bodies are scanned: JSON bodies string by string, other bodies as raw text. Any `ip` key is also a failure. The built-in admin (`ip.view`) is the control: IPs may appear only under `ip` keys on `/api/admin/guests`, `/audit` and `/ip/sessions`, and the seeded IPs must show up there. **Detector:** an IPv4 dotted quad with octets ≤ 255; IPv6 candidates need two or more colons and must parse as an IPv6 literal (`InetAddress.getByName`, which never does a DNS lookup for a string with a colon), with zone and v4-mapped forms accepted. Its own tests check that it ignores UUIDs, user agents, times, version strings, tokens and dates. SiggiHTTP reports IPv6 clients uncompressed (`2001:db8:1234:5678:0:0:0:42`), so the scan also looks for those literal forms. A mutation check (`/api/me/sessions` echoing `clientIp`) made the test fail as expected. When later milestones add DTO records or routes (rooms, library, WebSocket messages), they have to be classified, produced and swept here, and the test fails until they are. WebSocket message payloads aren't covered yet, because the only socket is the echo.
- 2026-11-08: The M6 manual check (login with a real OIDC and a plain-OAuth provider) no longer blocks the M6 gate: it can stay `[?]` while M7 goes ahead, and is tracked again under "Before the first release". Every other M6 step still blocks the gate.
- 2026-11-08: Permission-matrix test done (`PermissionMatrixTest`, main `3cc2576`). One row per route with the exact status for anonymous / guest (cookie and IP mode) / user / admin; every check uses a fresh caller and targets; it fails if a route has no row, and 401/403 must carry an error code. Blocked twice on SiggiHTTP: (1) after an early 401/403 without reading the body it advertised keep-alive and then closed the socket (fixed in 66a4e74: drains up to 64 KiB, else `Connection: close`); (2) that fix made `Connection: close` + unread body end in a TCP RST (fixed in 6a3fa36 with a lingering close: shutdown output, discard input up to 2 s / 1 MiB). TogetherTunes now needs SiggiHTTP 1.11-SNAPSHOT 6a3fa36 or later.
- 2026-11-08: Minimal web UI done (main `445e33b`; `web/src/session.ts`, `login.ts`, `register.ts`, `password.ts`, `account.ts`). **Gate:** every page that needs an account calls `requireUser()` first (anonymous → login with `?next=`, `mustChangePassword` → `/password.html?next=`); the change-password page hides the nav while the password is temporary. The front page only applies the password gate (anonymous and guests see Log in / Register). **No API change:** `registration.mode` stays private, so the Register link always shows and a closed server answers "Registration is closed". The password-change gate is client-side only; the server does not refuse other API calls while `must_change` is set (not required by the docs).
- 2026-11-09: Rooms CRUD: ownership is transferred with `PUT /api/rooms/{id}/members/{userId}` `{role:"owner"}`; new rooms default to `unlisted` + `vote`; room changes are owner-only while deleting is also allowed with `rooms.moderateAny`. Documented in `docs/api.md#rooms` and `docs/rooms.md`.
- 2026-11-09: Timeline step: until the control-mode step, the next entry is chosen by pinned, then queue `position`, then `added_at`. `queue_entries.played_at` is set when an entry **starts** playing. Resume after restart keeps the stored anchor when the computed position is still inside the entry (wall-clock time passed while down counts as played), else restarts the entry from 0. A current entry whose item isn't `ready` at resume is marked `skipped`. Not-ready entries (`pending`/`processing`/`failed`/`purged` items, or no duration) stay `queued` and are skipped over.
- 2026-11-10: Safari sync fixes (main `94434e3`): listen mode picks a sync mode per engine: rate nudges (quantised ±3/±5 %, with hysteresis) in Chromium/Gecko, seek-only with a learnt seek lead in WebKit, overridable with `?sync=`. New `POST /api/diagnostics` (anyone, 256 KiB, rate-limited per IP, logged on one line, no IPs, token-like URL parameters masked) and in-memory diagnostics rings on the room page. Design in `docs/rooms.md#listen-mode` and `docs/api.md`.
- 2026-11-10: Safari seek-mode follow-up (main `cc039be`): seek landings are judged on the settled drift, not at `seeked` (WebKit holds the clock ~300 ms after it); the seek lead learns only from seeks into buffered data; the seek-mode backoff is for scattering seeks only and capped at 8 s; a converged element (last seek settled within ±60 ms) seeks only above 80 ms. Documented in `docs/rooms.md#listen-mode`.
- 2026-11-10: iOS 15 follow-ups (main `5a8a3e2`): explicit link colours via custom properties (light + `prefers-color-scheme: dark`) instead of engine defaults/`LinkText`; `WebBuildTest` checks the shipped CSS against the Safari 15.0 baseline like the JS. Listen mode: no seek/start within the last 1 s of an item (`END_GUARD_MS`); a seek stuck for 5 s reloads the element instead of re-seeking; the unlock gesture doesn't seek over a pending seek (both from iPod report `f552z8`).
- 2026-11-10: Rooms react to item status changes (main `72dea8f`): new `Database.afterCommit` hook, `ingest.ItemStatusListener` fired by `IngestJobs` and `Items.purge`, `RoomRegistry` notifies each room with the item queued/suggested/current (`Playback.itemsChanged`, new `RoomEvent.EntryItemsChanged`); a current entry whose item failed or was purged is skipped; startup also starts idle rooms that have a ready queued entry.
